The rigorous verdict is:

- No explicit joint scaling \(\eta(n)\to0\) is currently proved sufficient for normalized-ReLU \(L=2\) loss Euler dynamics.
- At initialization, \(n\eta\) is exactly the spatial gate-boundary threshold:
  \[
  \frac{N_n(b)}{n\eta}\to b\sqrt{\frac2\pi}
  \quad\text{if }n\eta\to\infty,
  \]
  while \(n\eta\to\lambda\) gives a Poisson limit and \(n\eta\to0\) gives no gates in the slab with high probability.
- This is not a pathwise convergence criterion. At attracting gates, Euler chattering instead produces the exact Young measure
  \[
  (1-\lambda)\delta_0+\lambda\delta_1,\qquad
  \lambda=\frac p{p-q},
  \]
  independently of spatial boundary-layer counts.
- Softplus smoothing is not equivalent to hard Euler: it produces second gate moment \(\lambda^2\), while hard Euler produces \(\lambda\). The loss tangent kernel can distinguish them.
- The unresolved bridges are fixed-mesh hard-indicator reused-adjoint identification, growing-program concentration, dynamic small-ball estimates, joint multigate occupation measures, square-tail control, and uniqueness of the resulting generalized flow.

\[

\[

remains unproved because it requires a discrete covariant-Schur theorem. Thus:

- finite-n ODE then width: rigorously negative;
- width first then mesh: rigorously negative;
- simultaneous ODE-consistent diagonals above: rigorously negative;
- arbitrary under-resolved diagonals: still open, with no positive scaling currently proved.

\[

on a high-probability initialization event, where

\left\|\frac W{\sqrt n}\right\|_{\rm op}\right\}.
\]

The proposed derivation is algebraically correct, with three important qualifications:

- The learning-rate ratio \(n:1:n^{-1}\) is the raw-coordinate form of the original \(\mu\)P update. It is not a competing optimizer scaling.
- The initialization is fundamentally different: \(b_{ji}=W_0(\beta_j,\alpha_i)/n\) is a correlated graphon/neuron-embedded model, whereas the original Gaussian model has \(b_{ji}=B_{ji}/\sqrt n\) with independent \(B_{ji}=O(1)\).
- The condition \(nh_n\to0\) is unnecessary for the finite-type structured theorem; \(h_n\to0\) suffices. It only forces the raw first-layer learning rate itself to vanish.

All displayed gradients, normalized updates, the exact \(Z\)-correction, \(K_n\), \(\Gamma_n\), and the \(L_k\) Taylor coefficients check out. The finite-type convergence and global-existence proof also work after explicitly requiring positive type masses and weighted norms.

The structured witness relies on correlations absent from the original model: \(b_{ji}\) shares \(\alpha_i\) with \(a_i\), and \(c_j\) shares \(\beta_j\) with the corresponding middle row. This creates coherent \(O(1)\) sums. With independent centered \(b_{ji}\) of variance \(n^{-2}\), instead \(Z_j=O_{\mathbb P}(n^{-1/2})\).

Consequently, \(h_n=n^{-3}\) is valid for that finite-type graphon model, but it establishes nothing about the original iid-Gaussian OMFP dynamics. It also fails the currently proved—very nonsharp—Gaussian Euler/ODE consistency condition involving the exponential factor.

One wording should be weakened: the row-projection example proves that the ordinary entrywise empirical law is not a closed state descriptor. It does not, by itself, rule out every autonomous formulation along the special iid trajectory; an enriched Gaussian operator/response state is precisely what OMFP supplies.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 21018 | 2026-08-25T21:05:44.874Z | /root/compare_omfp_scalings ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/compare_omfp_scalings
Payload:
The proposed result is accurate for the stated finite-type neuron-embedded model, but it is not a scaling result for the original iid-Gaussian MFP model.

The key comparison is:

\[
b=\frac{W_{\rm raw}}{\sqrt n},
\qquad
c=\frac{a_{\rm out}}n.
\]

Under this reparameterization, the original μP mobility becomes exactly

\[
(\eta_a,\eta_b,\eta_c)=(nh,h,h/n).
\]

So the learning-rate powers are the same. What changes is the initialization:

\[
\operatorname{Var}(b_{ji})
=
\begin{cases}
\Theta(n^{-1}),&\text{original iid MFP},\\
\Theta(n^{-2}),&\text{nested kernel}.
\end{cases}
\]

The nested model uses the coherent field

\[
\frac1n\sum_iW_0(\beta_j,\alpha_i)A_0(\alpha_i)^2,
\]

whereas the original uses a CLT field

\[
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_iW_{ji}A_i^2.
\]

For iid centered \(b_{ji}\) with variance \(n^{-2}\),

\[
\mathbb E[Z_j^2\mid A]
=
\frac{\sigma^2}{n^2}\sum_iA_i^4
=
O_{\mathbb P}(n^{-1}),
\]

so the hidden preactivation collapses. Nondegeneracy at variance \(n^{-2}\) comes entirely from the nested dependence.

The explicit witness is arithmetically valid, but it is highly structured:

\[
W_{ji}=v_j\left(x_i^2-\frac52\right)
\]

is rank one; \(W_{ji}\) is coupled to \(A_i^2\), and \(C_j\) is coupled to \(v_j\). Despite all one-coordinate marginals being centered, these dependencies produce

\[
F_0=\frac12,
\]

rather than the original \(F_0\to0\). Thus it is neither iid nor Gaussian and does not test the original centered-iid model.

For finite types, the claimed Euler-to-ODE theorem and global norm identities are valid. In fact, \(h_n\to0\) suffices; \(nh_n\to0\) is unnecessary except to force the nominal raw number \(\eta_{a,n}\) itself to vanish. Consequently \(h_n=n^{-3}\) proves convergence for the nested finite-type model only.

For original centered-iid weights, variance \(n^{-1}\) is required, recovering the original MFP regime. A one-edge empirical law cannot close that dynamics; row/column Gaussian fields, overlaps, and response information are necessary. Fixed-horizon OMFP remains meaningful, but the proved quadratic initial layer still excludes a classical continuous initialized width-first flow and every currently covered ODE-consistent diagonal. Arbitrary deliberately under-resolved diagonals remain open.

Full audit: [INDEPENDENT_COMPARISON.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_scaling_comparison/INDEPENDENT_COMPARISON.md).

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 21154 | 2026-08-26T13:18:50.482Z | /root/arctan_continuum_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root

Payload:
Audit result: PASS. No flaw was found in the finite algebra, autonomous IDE, Gaussian-envelope well-posedness, kernel identity, or signed physical-time reduction.

The exact reconstruction is saved at [CONTINUUM_AND_CLOCK_AUDIT.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_arctan_mfp_reaudit/CONTINUUM_AND_CLOCK_AUDIT.md).

The decisive identities are

\[
A'=Y,\qquad r'=Q,\qquad G'=B\otimes X,
\]

for \(r=u+u^3/3\), and

\[
F'(s)=K(s)
=
\mathbb E Y^2+\mathbb E B^2\,\mathbb E X^2
+\mathbb E[c(r)^2Q^2]\ge0.
\]

The continuum uniqueness proof correctly avoids false ambient \(L^2\)-Lipschitzness. Its essential estimate is

\[
\|a[h(z)-h(\tilde z)]\|_2
\le
C\delta\sqrt{\log(e/\delta)},
\qquad
\delta=\|z-\tilde z\|_2,
\]

whose reciprocal integral diverges. Gaussian cutoff removal is also valid because the factor \(e^{C_SM}\) is dominated by the clipping tail \(e^{-M^2/4}\).

For full MSE, the signed clock is

\[
\dot s=2\eta e,\qquad e=y-F(s),
\]

so

\[
e(t)=y\exp\!\left(-2\eta\int_0^tK(s(\tau))\,d\tau\right).
\]

This covers negative labels: \(e\) retains its sign and feature time simply runs backward. Consequently,

\[
F(s(t))+e(t)=y,\qquad
\dot{\mathcal L}=-4\eta K\mathcal L.
\]

The only qualification is that uniqueness is proved in the reachable Gaussian-envelope class, not for arbitrary ambient \(L^2\) states. The overall width theorem still depends on the separate pointed-action source, finite-program identification, and square-uniform-integrability arguments; nothing in Sections 2, 5, 6, or 8 breaks those bridges.

Audit result: the source/adjoint/fixed-Euler bridge is mathematically sound, with these required clarifications:

- Cite Tensor Programs III, Theorem E.15 directly for singular limiting Gram matrices. Moore–Penrose conditioning alone is insufficient.
- Add the straightforward rational-to-real extension for arbitrary mesh \(h\).
- Specify that the common triangular-array coupling is the one under which the imported almost-sure master theorem applies.

The exact reused-adjoint construction passes:

\[
\langle \Gamma p,q\rangle
=\lim_n\langle G_np_n,q_n\rangle_n
=\lim_n\langle p_n,G_n^\top q_n\rangle_n
=\langle p,\Gamma^\dagger q\rangle,
\]

and the spectral norm bound makes both maps continuous, hence \(\Gamma^\dagger=\Gamma^*\).

The fixed Euler reduction also passes exactly:

\[
G^kX^k
=
G_0X^k+\sum_{\ell<k}hB^\ell
\langle X^\ell,X^k\rangle,
\]

\[
(G^k)^*B^k
=
G_0^*B^k+\sum_{\ell<k}hX^\ell
\langle B^\ell,B^k\rangle.
\]

This is a chronological finite two-sided program with no hidden algebraic cycle. Singular Gram matrices do not affect the finite-rank trace-norm passage.

Thus this part of the arctangent theorem is not broken. It presently imports the finite two-sided Tensor Programs master theorem; a literally self-contained MFP reproduction would need to reprove its adaptive Gaussian-conditioning and singular-core rewrite argument.

  \]

The Section 7 bridges themselves check out:

1. At fixed readout cutoff and feature-time horizon, the transformed vector field is dimension-free locally Lipschitz in
   \[
   L_R^2\oplus L_C^2\oplus\mathfrak S_1.
   \]
   Hence its transformed-coordinate Euler scheme has \(O(h)\) global error uniformly in width.

2. Every fixed auxiliary mesh is one finite reused-\(G_0,G_0^*\) program because
   \[
   q^k=\sum_{\ell<k}hB^\ell\otimes X^\ell.
   \]
   Thus the fixed-program theorem applies before \(h\downarrow0\).

3. With
   \[
   T_R(Q)=\sup_s\left\langle Q(s)^2
   \mathbf1_{\{|Q(s)|>R\}}\right\rangle_n,
   \]
   the proof obtains
   \[
   T_R(Q)
   \le
   4D_h^2+
   2\left(\frac2R\right)^\varepsilon
   \max_k\langle|Q_k^h|^{2+\varepsilon}\rangle_n.
   \]
   One first fixes a sufficiently small auxiliary \(h\), then sends \(R\to\infty\). No moment bound uniform in the number of mesh steps is used.

4. The resulting square-tail estimate correctly controls
   \[
   \|c(r)Q-c(r^h)Q^h\|_2,
   \]
   so the raw weighted kernel term
   \[
   \langle c(r)^2Q^2\rangle
   \]
   converges uniformly in time.

5. Cutoff removal is valid because
   \[
   D_{n,M}
   \le C_S e^{C_SM}
   \|A_{0,n}-\operatorname{clip}_M A_{0,n}\|_2,
   \]
   while the Gaussian clipping tail is bounded by
   \[
   C(1+M)e^{-M^2/4}.
   \]
   The Gaussian decay dominates the exponential stability loss. The same tail inequality transfers square uniform integrability from the cutoff trajectory to the uncut one.

The decisive scope distinction is the update being discretized. Section 7 uses the auxiliary Euler update in the natural coordinate

\[
r^+=r+hQ.
\]

Actual gradient descent updates the original parameter by

\[
u^+=u+h\frac{Q}{1+u^2}.
\]

Since \(r=u+u^3/3\), its exact transformed update is

\[
r^+
=
r+hQ
+h^2\frac{uQ^2}{(1+u^2)^2}
+\frac{h^3Q^3}{3(1+u^2)^3}.
\]

Therefore the fixed-mesh program used in Section 7 is not the transformed actual GD program. Controlling the accumulated correction after taking width first requires mesh-uniform reachable \(L^4/L^6\), or an equivalent response-tail estimate, for \(Q\). That estimate is not proved.

So the earlier arctangent result was not broken: it rigorously proves the width limit of the continuous finite-width ODE. What remains open is whether the width-first discrete OMFP trajectories converge to that IDE as the learning-rate mesh vanishes.

Decisive verdict: **the simultaneous-diagonal argument is mathematically sound after two necessary corrections**. The defect estimate and the choice \(\eta_n=n^{-2}\) are valid. As literally phrased, however, it must not claim cross-width operator-norm convergence of \(B_n\), and it must acknowledge that \(\operatorname{Var}(W_3)=n^{-4}\) changes the initialization.

Using normalized vector norms and

\[
f_n=\langle C,Y\rangle_n,\qquad C=nW_3,
\]

the block learning rates \((n,1,1/n)\) give

\[
C^+=C-2\eta eY,\qquad
B^+=B-2\eta e\,D\otimes H,
\]

\[
A^+=A+\eta q(A)R,
\qquad
R=-2eB^*D,
\qquad q(a)=\frac1{1+a^2}.
\]

For \(X=\Theta(A)=A+A^3/3\),

\[
X^+
=
X+\eta R
+\eta^2Aq(A)^2R^2
+\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(A)^3R^3.
\]

There is no missing \(n\) or factor \(2\). Since

\[
\sup_a|a q(a)^2|=\frac9{16\sqrt3},
\qquad
0<q\le1,
\qquad
\|R\|_\infty\le\sqrt n\,\|R\|_2,
\]

the exact one-step defect satisfies

\[
\|\mathcal E_\eta\|_2
\le
\frac9{16\sqrt3}\eta^2\sqrt n\,\|R\|_2^2
+\frac13\eta^3n\,\|R\|_2^3.
\]

Thus, whenever \(\sup_{k\eta\le T}\|R_k\|_2\le R_T\),

\[
\|\mathcal E_\eta\|_2
\le C_T(\eta^2\sqrt n+\eta^3n).
\]

A dimension-free stability factor \(1+L_T\eta\) then gives, after at most \(T/\eta\) steps,

\[
\sup_{t\le T}
d(U_n^{\mathrm{GD}}(t),U_n^{\mathrm{flow}}(t))
\le
C_T\bigl(\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n\bigr).
\]

Consequently \(\eta_n=n^{-2}\) works. More generally, the displayed argument works whenever

\[
\eta_n\sqrt n\longrightarrow0.
\]

For the proposed initialization, the constants really are width-independent. Indeed,

\[
\operatorname{Var}(C_{0,i})
=n^2\operatorname{Var}(W_{3,i})=n^{-2},
\]

so with probability tending to one,

\[
\|C_0\|_\infty\le1,\qquad
\|C_0\|_2\le1,\qquad
\|B_0\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le3.
\]

Writing \(a=\pi/2\), one has

\[
|e|\le |y|+a\|C\|_2
\]

and

\[
\|C_{k+1}\|_2
\le
(1+2a^2\eta)\|C_k\|_2+2a|y|\eta.
\]

Discrete Gronwall bounds \(\|C_k\|_2\), after which direct summation bounds \(\|C_k\|_\infty\), \(\|B_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\), and

\[
\|R_k\|_2
\le
2|e_k|\,\|B_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|C_k\|_2
\]

uniformly in \(n\).

The required topology correction is:

\[
B=B_0+q,\qquad
d(U,\widetilde U)
=
\|X-\widetilde X\|_2+
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2+
\|q-\widetilde q\|_1.
\]

The quantity \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}\) is a uniform size bound, not a cross-width distance. Ginibre matrices do not converge across widths in operator norm. Fixed auxiliary meshes converge through the reused-\(B_0,B_0^*\) MFP action laws and their Gram data; the trained part \(q\) is a finite rank-one sum. Dimension-free Euler approximation then removes that auxiliary mesh.

The limit is nontrivial for \(y\ne0\). At the limiting initialization \(C_0=0\), one has \(D_0=0\), hence \(\dot X(0)=\dot B(0)=0\), but

\[
\dot C(0)=2yY_0.
\]

Therefore

\[
\dot f(0)
=
2y\,\mathbb E\!\left[\arctan(Z_0)^2\right]\ne0.
\]

Finally, this is not exactly the initialization in the earlier arctangent theorem. There \(C_0\sim N(0,1)\), corresponding to raw \(\operatorname{Var}(W_3)=n^{-2}\), whereas the new \(n^{-4}\) choice makes \(C_0\to0\). For the original initialization, the Lipschitz constant is not dimension-free because \(\|C_0\|_\infty=O_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt{\log n})\). Nevertheless, \(\eta_n=n^{-2}\) still survives the resulting factor \(e^{C_T\sqrt{\log n}}=n^{o(1)}\), so the canonical initialization can also be recovered with an explicitly \(n\)-dependent stability estimate.

Precise audit:

- \(C_0\) degenerates:
  \[
  \mathbb E\|C_0\|_n^2=n^{-2},\qquad
  \|C_0\|_\infty=O_{\mathbb P}\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}n\right).
  \]
  The correct limiting state has \(C(0)=0\). A cyclic construction generated only from \(C_0\) collapses. It must instead be generated from \(A_0\) or \(H_0=\arctan A_0\) and the nondegenerate \(B_0/B_0^*\) action.

- The dynamics is nevertheless nontrivial:
  \[
  e(0)=-1,\qquad \dot C(0)=2G_0,
  \]
  while initially \(\dot X(0)=\dot B(0)=0\).

- Every fixed Euler mesh is a valid finite two-sided Tensor Program. Because its initial Grams are singular, the proof must invoke Tensor Programs III, Theorem E.15, not rely solely on Yang–Hu Theorem 7.4 or Moore–Penrose notation.

- Countable simultaneous convergence is valid only for a predetermined rational program language under an almost-sure common coupling. It gives pointwise convergence for each fixed program depth, not uniform convergence for depth \(Tn^2\).

- The operator realization is valid only after using
  \[
  \|B_{0,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\to2
  \]
  to prove null-space preservation and boundedness, followed by exact finite transpose duality to obtain the genuine adjoint.

- Mesh Cauchy and the semigroup can be proved independently. Indeed,
  \[
  e'=-2eK,\qquad
  K=\|G\|_2^2+\|D\|_2^2\|H\|_2^2+
  \|c(X)B^*D\|_2^2\ge0,
  \]
  so \(C\) remains pointwise bounded on compact times. This gives a locally Lipschitz vector field in the \(L^2\)-plus-trace-class state and a unique autonomous IDE. The proof must display these estimates; fixed-mesh Tensor Program convergence alone does not imply them.

- The claimed \(\eta_n=n^{-2}\) actual-GD diagonal remains unproved. Fixed-depth convergence cannot be evaluated at \(k_n=Tn^2\). Moreover, raw \(A\)-Euler updates are not Euler updates in the natural coordinate:
  \[
  X^+
  =
  X+\eta v+\eta^2Aq(A)^2v^2
  +\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(A)^3v^3.
  \]
  Uniform accumulated \(L^4/L^6\)-type control is required.

Finally, this altered initialization is not the earlier arctangent theorem’s model: the earlier normalized readout is \(O(1)\), whereas here \(C_0\to0\). Thus the altered proof cannot by itself reproduce the original result.

The audited correction is decisive:

- With \(C=nW^{(3)}\) and \(\operatorname{Var}(W^{(3)}_i)=n^{-4}\),

\[
\operatorname{Var}(C_i)=n^{-2},
\qquad
C_0\to0
\]

strongly. The limiting readout is not Gaussian.

- Under \((\lambda_1,\lambda_2,\lambda_3)=(n,1,n^{-1})\), the finite equations are correctly scaled:

\[
\dot C=-2eG,\qquad
\dot B=-2e(D\otimes H),\qquad
\dot A=-2e\,q(A)B^{\mathsf T}D.
\]

- At finite width, expansions must retain \(C_{0,n}\), \(D_{0,n}\), and \(f_{0,n}\). Only after \(n\to\infty\) are

\[
C(t)=2tG_0+O(t^2),\qquad
D(t)=2tU+O(t^2),
\]

and

\[
B(t)-B_0=2t^2(U\otimes H_0)+o(t^2),
\qquad
A(t)-A_0=2t^2q(A_0)\Xi+o(t^2)
\]

valid.

- Initially,

\[
K_3(0)=\mathbb E[\arctan(Z)^2]>0,
\qquad
K_2(0)=K_1(0)=0.
\]

Thus only the readout block is active at \(t=0\). Both hidden blocks become strictly active at order \(t^2\):

\[
K_2(t)=4t^2\mathbb E[H^2]\mathbb E[U^2]+o(t^2),
\]

\[
K_1(t)=4t^2\mathbb E[q(A)^2\Xi^2]+o(t^2)>0.
\]

The reused-adjoint law is

\[
\Xi=\mu H+\sqrt{\mathbb E U^2}\,\zeta,
\qquad
\mu=\mathbb E U'(Z),
\]

which proves the last coefficient is strictly positive.

Finally, a continuum proof should use \(R=A+A^3/3\). Direct ambient-\(L^2\) Picard theory in \(A\) is unjustified because it produces products of two merely \(L^2\) fields.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 21769 | 2026-08-26T15:17:18.859Z | /root/hostile_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/hostile_audit
Payload:
Verdict: the scaling is algebraically consistent and properties 3–7 are locally nondegenerate, but it does not by itself yield the required theorem. Any proposed closure using only node/path laws, an ordinary sampled-edge law, or a scalar replacement of the reused middle matrix is false. The unresolved burden is properties 1–2.

Let
\[
a=z^{(1)},\quad B=W^{(2)},\quad q=nW^{(3)},\quad
u=\phi(a),\quad b=Bu,\quad v=\phi(b),
\]
\[
D=\operatorname{diag}\phi'(a),\quad p=q\odot\phi'(b),\quad
r=\langle q,v\rangle_n-1,
\]
where \(\langle x,y\rangle_n=n^{-1}x^\top y\). For the proposed variances and learning-rate scalers, continuous gradient flow is exactly
\[
\dot q=-2rv,\qquad
\dot B=-\frac{2r}{n}pu^\top,\qquad
\dot a=-2rD B^\top p.
\]
Consequently, with
\[
S_n=\langle u^2\rangle_n,\qquad
C_n=BD^2B^\top,\qquad H_n=S_nI+C_n,
\]
one has
\[
\dot b=-2rH_np,
\]
and
\[
K_n^{(1)}=\langle p,C_np\rangle_n,\quad
K_n^{(2)}=S_n\langle p^2\rangle_n,\quad
K_n^{(3)}=\langle v^2\rangle_n.
\]
Thus
\[
K_n=\langle v^2\rangle_n+\langle p,H_np\rangle_n,\qquad
V_{n}^{(1)2}=4r^2K_n^{(1)},\qquad
V_{n}^{(2)2}=4r^2\langle H_np,H_np\rangle_n.
\]

The first reused-matrix correction already appears at second order. Let \(X\sim N(0,1)\),
\[
U=\arctan X,\quad s=\mathbb E U^2,\quad G\sim N(0,s),
\]
\[
F(G)=\arctan(G)\frac1{1+G^2},\quad
\tau=\mathbb E F(G)^2,\quad
\alpha=\frac{\mathbb E[GF(G)]}{s},
\quad D(X)=\frac1{1+X^2}.
\]
At the limiting initialization, \(q=0,r=-1\), and
\[
\dot q(0)=2v,\qquad \dot p(0)=2F,\qquad
\ddot a(0)=4DB^\top F,\qquad
\ddot b(0)=4H_0F.
\]

The exact Gaussian row regression
\[
B_j=\frac{b_j}{\|u\|^2}u+\xi_j
\]
gives
\[
(X_i,(B^\top F)_i)\Longrightarrow
\bigl(X,\alpha U+\sqrt{\tau}\,Z\bigr),
\qquad Z\sim N(0,1),\ Z\perp X.
\]
In particular,
\[
c_1:=\lim_n\langle D^2(B^\top F)^2\rangle_n
=\tau\,\mathbb E D^2+\alpha^2\mathbb E[D^2U^2]>0.
\]
The independent Gaussian term is essential. A deterministic transport \(a(t)=A_t(a(0))\), or a centered-Gaussian ansatz omitting the response mean \(\alpha U\), is wrong.

This also quantitatively refutes scalarization:
\[
\lim_n\langle F,C_nF\rangle_n
=\tau\mathbb E D^2+\alpha^2\mathbb E[D^2U^2],
\]
whereas the independence replacement \(C_n\rightsquigarrow \mathbb E[D^2]I\) gives only the first term. Moreover, if \(m_1=\mathbb E D^2\), \(m_2=\mathbb E D^4\), then
\[
\frac1n\operatorname{tr}C_n\to m_1,\qquad
\frac1n\operatorname{tr}C_n^2\to m_1^2+m_2,
\]
so
\[
\frac1n\|C_n-m_1I\|_F^2\to m_2>0.
\]

Ordinary edge laws do not close the dynamics either. At a positive time choose \(w\perp u\), \(\langle w^2\rangle_n=1\), and put
\[
R=\frac{\gamma}{n}pw^\top.
\]
Then
\[
(B+R)u=Bu,
\]
so all current node preactivations, node laws, output, \(K^{(2)}\), and \(K^{(3)}\) are unchanged. Also
\[
\frac1{n^2}\sum_{ji}(\sqrt n\,R_{ji})^2
=\frac{\gamma^2\langle p^2\rangle_n}{n}\to0,
\]
and its spectral effect has finite rank. Nevertheless,
\[
(B+R)^\top p-B^\top p
=\gamma\langle p^2\rangle_n w,
\]
which changes \(\dot a\) and \(K^{(1)}\) at order one. The actual update of \(B\) generates precisely such \(n^{-1}\) outer-product terms. Thus a two-scale rooted/traffic or cavity-response state is mandatory.

Conditional on establishing that enriched joint limit, the remaining required properties have favorable strict small-time expansions. With
\[
\kappa=\mathbb E[\arctan(G)^2],\qquad c=s\tau+c_1,
\]
one obtains
\[
K^{(1)}(t)=4c_1t^2+o(t^2),\qquad
K^{(2)}(t)=4s\tau t^2+o(t^2),
\]
\[
K^{(3)}(t)=\kappa+4ct^2+o(t^2),\qquad
K(t)=\kappa+8ct^2+o(t^2),
\]
and
\[
f(t)=2\kappa t+o(t),\qquad
L(t)=1-4\kappa t+o(t).
\]
Also,
\[
V^{(1)2}(t)=16c_1t^2+o(t^2),
\]
while, writing \(\gamma_2=\lim\langle H_0F,H_0F\rangle_n\),
\[
V^{(2)2}(t)=16\gamma_2t^2+o(t^2),\qquad
\gamma_2\ge s^2\tau>0.
\]
The squared displacements are respectively
\[
4c_1t^4+o(t^4),\qquad 4\gamma_2t^4+o(t^4).
\]
Thus all layerwise integrals, both feature displacements, loss decrease, and kernel movement are strictly positive on every sufficiently short interval. Hidden variances and the nonlinear projection residuals are positive at \(t=0\); Wasserstein continuity would preserve them locally. For a nondegenerate Gaussian, \(\arctan z\) cannot agree almost surely with an affine function, so the initial residuals are strictly positive.

The square reused matrix is genuinely singular asymptotically. Taking a unit vector orthogonal to the first \(n-1\) rows of \(B\) shows
\[
s_{\min}(B)\le |N(0,1/n)|\to0.
\]
Since \(0<D^2\le I\),
\[
C_n\le BB^\top,
\]
so \(C_n\) has no uniform inverse. Any proof using an inverse of this matrix, or inverses of increasingly singular time-Gram matrices, fails. This does not destroy the dynamics because
\[
H_n=S_nI+C_n\ge S_nI,
\]
and \(S_n\to s>0\) locally.

The choice \(\eta_{0,n}=n^{-2}\) is not a fatal diagonal issue. In \((a,B,q)\), discrete GD is exactly explicit Euler for the displayed ODE. On \(\|B(0)\|_{\rm op}=O(1)\), \(\|q(0)\|_n=o(1)\), boundedness of \(\arctan,\phi',\phi''\) gives \(n\)-uniform finite-time bounds and a uniform local Lipschitz constant, hence an \(O_T(\eta_{0,n})\) Euler error. This direct estimate should replace separate width and gradient-flow limits.

However, normalized \(L^2\) bounds alone do not prove path-\(W_2\) tightness or uniform integrability: the measures
\[
(1-n^{-1})\delta_0+n^{-1}\delta_{\sqrt n}
\]
are the standard counterexample. A proof needs uniform \(2+\varepsilon\) path moments or delocalization estimates for all reused-matrix messages.

A viable repair would require all of the following:

- a finite-depth two-sided Gaussian-conditioning/tensor-program theorem, including transpose/Onsager corrections and singular Gram matrices handled by Hilbert projections rather than inverses;
- an \(n\)-uniform Picard approximation, followed by fixed-depth width convergence and then Picard-depth convergence;
- a current, restartable rooted-traffic or conditional Gaussian-operator state that records the invisible rank-one alignments; a Volterra equation retaining the past is not by itself property 2;
- uniform \(2+\varepsilon\) path estimates and temporal equicontinuity to obtain path-\(W_2\), pass \(B^\top p\), \(C_np\), \(H_np\), kernels, and velocities;
- uniqueness of that enriched autonomous evolution, giving full-sequence rather than merely subsequential convergence.

Therefore the scaling survives the adversarial algebraic and local-positivity tests, but an affirmative resolution is not valid unless this enriched closure and lifting theorem is actually proved. Failure to supply it leaves the task unresolved; it is not a counterexample to the scaling itself.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 21793 | 2026-08-26T15:19:14.204Z | /root/meanfield_closure ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/meanfield_closure
Payload:
Use \(\phi(u)=\arctan u\) and
\[
\sigma_{1,n}=1,\qquad \sigma_{2,n}=n^{-1/2},\qquad
\sigma_{3,n}=n^{-2},
\]
\[
\lambda_n^{(1)}=n,\qquad \lambda_n^{(2)}=1,\qquad
\lambda_n^{(3)}=n^{-1},\qquad \eta_{0,n}=n^{-2}.
\]
Then \(A_j:=nW_j^{(3)}\) has variance \(n^{-2}\) and hence converges to the zero normalized readout, while every variance remains positive. Also all three products \(\eta_{0,n}\lambda_n^{(\ell)}\) tend to zero.

### 1. Exact normalized dynamics and the natural coordinate

Put
\[
M_n=W^{(2)},\quad U_i=W_i^{(1)},\quad
H_i=\phi(U_i),\quad V=M_nH,
\]
\[
D_j=A_j\phi'(V_j),\quad
F_n=\langle A,\phi(V)\rangle_{2,n},\quad
\rho_n=2(1-F_n),
\]
where both finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces use
\[
\langle x,y\rangle_{r,n}=\frac1n x^\top y.
\]
Write
\[
\Gamma_n=\frac{G_n}{\sqrt n},\qquad G_{ji}\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}N(0,1),
\qquad M_n=\Gamma_n+Q_n.
\]
For \(d\in\mathbb R^n\), \(h\in\mathbb R^n\), use the normalized rank-one operator
\[
d\otimes h:x\longmapsto d\,\langle h,x\rangle_{1,n},
\]
whose matrix is \(dh^\top/n\). The gradient-flow equations are exactly
\[
\dot U=\rho_n\phi'(U)(\Gamma_n^*+Q_n^*)D,\qquad
\dot A=\rho_n\phi(V),\qquad
\dot Q_n=\rho_nD\otimes H. \tag{1}
\]

Let
\[
\Theta(u)=u+\frac{u^3}{3},\qquad X=\Theta(U),\qquad
\vartheta=\Theta^{-1},\qquad
\psi=\phi\circ\vartheta .
\]
Since \(\Theta'(u)=1+u^2=1/\phi'(u)\),
\[
\dot X=\rho_n(\Gamma_n^*+Q_n^*)D. \tag{2}
\]
Moreover, \(\vartheta\) and \(\psi\) are globally Lipschitz, \(\psi\) is bounded, and
\[
\psi'(x)=\frac1{(1+\vartheta(x)^2)^2}.
\]

The middle-layer perturbation is trace class:
\[
Q_n(t)=\int_0^t\rho_n(s)D(s)\otimes H(s)\,ds,\qquad
\|d\otimes h\|_{\mathfrak S_1}=\|d\|_2\|h\|_2. \tag{3}
\]

### 2. Two-sided pointed Gaussian-action theorem

The following is the precise fixed-depth result needed here.

**Lemma (two-sided Gaussian-action master theorem).**  
Let \(\Gamma_n=G_n/\sqrt n\), with \(G_n\) iid standard Gaussian, independent of iid seed vectors having moments of every order. Consider a fixed finite directed acyclic bipartite program whose vector nodes are formed using:

1. seed vectors and constants;
2. finite linear combinations and empirical scalar products;
3. coordinatewise \(C^1\) functions whose functions and derivatives have polynomial growth;
4. calls \(z\mapsto\Gamma_nz\) from side 1 to side 2 and \(d\mapsto\Gamma_n^*d\) in the reverse direction.

Then, jointly for all nodes, the empirical laws on each side converge in probability in \(W_p\), for every fixed \(p<\infty\), to deterministic laws. All empirical scalar products converge. Degenerate limiting Gram matrices are allowed.

The limiting program terms form two abstract \(L^2\)-probability modules \(\mathcal H_1,\mathcal H_2\). On the quotient by zero \(L^2\)-norm, the formal action calls induce adjoint bounded operators
\[
\Gamma:\mathcal H_1\to\mathcal H_2,\qquad
\Gamma^*:\mathcal H_2\to\mathcal H_1.
\]
The pointed object consisting of the two modules, the seed variables, and \(\Gamma,\Gamma^*\) is deterministic and unique up to pointed isometric probability-module isomorphism.

**Proof.** Order action calls topologically. Conditional on all prior calls, every new input vector is fixed and the conditional law of \(G_n\) is Gaussian on the affine subspace determined by the previous linear observations. If \(H\) and \(D\) collect previous inputs on the two sides, the residual is
\[
P_D^\perp\,\widetilde G_n\,P_H^\perp,
\]
with \(\widetilde G_n\) an independent Gaussian matrix. Thus a new action is the sum of:

- a conditional mean in the spans of previous opposite-side vectors, with coefficients determined by empirical Gram matrices;
- a fresh Gaussian action on the orthogonal residual of the new input.

Conditional Gaussian concentration gives convergence of bounded Lipschitz empirical tests. Polynomial tests follow by truncation and conditional Gaussian moment estimates. Nonlinear and scalar nodes then follow by the ordinary law of large numbers and uniform moment bounds.

If a limiting Gram matrix is singular, orthogonalize only directions with eigenvalue at least \(\varepsilon\). The discarded action has \(L^2\)-norm at most
\[
\|\Gamma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\varepsilon};
\]
first let \(n\to\infty\), then \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\). Hence no rank-stability assumption is needed.

For completeness, a \(1/4\)-net of each Euclidean unit sphere has at most \(9^n\) points, and
\[
\Pr(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}>10)
 \le 2\cdot 81^n e^{-100n/8}\le 2e^{-8n}.
\]
Consequently, on every formal program term \(z\),
\[
\|\Gamma z\|_2
=\lim_n\|\Gamma_nz_n\|_{2,n}
\le 10\lim_n\|z_n\|_{2,n}.
\]
This defines the bounded extension; finite-dimensional adjointness passes to the limit. The deterministic limits of all finite programs give uniqueness of the pointed action object. ∎

This theorem avoids a non-autonomous two-time DMFT representation: the static Gaussian disorder survives as the canonical, width-independent pointed operator \(\Gamma\).

### 3. Autonomous limiting IDE

On the canonical Gaussian-action space, let
\[
X_0=\Theta(U_0),\qquad U_0\sim N(0,1),\qquad A_0=0,\qquad Q_0=0.
\]
Define
\[
U=\vartheta(X),\qquad H=\psi(X),
\]
\[
V=(\Gamma+Q)H,\qquad D=A\phi'(V),
\]
\[
F=\mathbb E_2[A\phi(V)],\qquad \rho=2(1-F).
\]
The limiting evolution is
\[
\dot X=\rho(\Gamma^*+Q^*)D,\qquad
\dot A=\rho\phi(V),\qquad
\dot Q=\rho D\otimes H. \tag{4}
\]
This is autonomous and restartable: at any \(\tau\), the current pointed state
\[
(\Gamma;X_\tau,A_\tau,Q_\tau)
\]
is initial data for exactly the same equation. No finite array or trajectory history occurs.

The edge representation is
\[
B_t(\omega_2,\omega_1)
=\int_0^t\rho(s)D_s(\omega_2)H_s(\omega_1)\,ds,
\]
and \(Q_t\) is its integral operator. Thus the normalized middle-layer update is also explicitly determined.

### 4. Well-posedness

Let \(b=\pi/2\) and \(\gamma=\|\Gamma\|\). Work in
\[
\mathcal H_1\times(\mathcal H_2\cap L^\infty)\times
\mathfrak S_1(\mathcal H_1,\mathcal H_2).
\]
All Nemytskii maps in (4) are locally Lipschitz there because
\(\vartheta,\psi,\phi,\phi'\) are globally Lipschitz and bounded. Also
\[
\|Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le \|Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}.
\]
If \(a(t)=\|A_t\|_\infty\) and \(q(t)=\|Q_t\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\), then
\[
|\rho(t)|\le 2(1+ba(t)),
\]
\[
a'(t)\le b|\rho(t)|,
\qquad
q'(t)\le |\rho(t)|\,a(t)b,
\]
\[
\|\dot X_t\|_2
\le |\rho(t)|(\gamma+q(t))a(t).
\]
These estimates exclude finite-time blowup.

Picard iteration is a contraction on a sufficiently short interval on every bounded ball: the rank-one estimate
\[
\|d\otimes h-\widetilde d\otimes\widetilde h\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le \|d-\widetilde d\|_2\|h\|_2
 +\|\widetilde d\|_2\|h-\widetilde h\|_2
\]
supplies the only non-immediate term. Iterating short intervals and using the preceding a priori bounds gives a unique global \(C^1\) solution of (4). The Hilbert-space chain rule gives
\[
\dot U=\phi'(U)\dot X,\qquad
\dot H=\phi'(U)^2\dot X,
\]
so \(U,H,V,D\) are \(H^1\)-paths.

### 5. Fixed mesh and removal of the mesh

For a mesh \(\Delta\), explicit Euler applied to (4) has
\[
Q^k=\Delta\sum_{r<k}\rho_rD^r\otimes H^r.
\]
Hence
\[
Q^kH^k
=\Delta\sum_{r<k}\rho_rD^r\langle H^r,H^k\rangle_1,
\]
\[
Q^{k*}D^k
=\Delta\sum_{r<k}\rho_rH^r\langle D^r,D^k\rangle_2.
\]
Therefore every fixed-mesh iterate is a finite two-sided Gaussian program. The master theorem identifies its full joint empirical law with the Euler discretization of (4), along the full sequence \(n\to\infty\).

On the event \(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le10\), the finite-width and limiting vector fields have a dimension-independent Lipschitz constant on every compact time interval. The usual Euler remainder estimate, proved directly from
\[
S(t_{k+1})-S(t_k)-\Delta\mathcal F(S(t_k))
=\int_{t_k}^{t_{k+1}}
   [\mathcal F(S(s))-\mathcal F(S(t_k))]\,ds,
\]
gives
\[
\sup_{t\le T}\|S_n(t)-S_n^\Delta(t)\|
 +\|S_n-S_n^\Delta\|_{H^1(0,T)}
 \le C_T\Delta, \tag{5}
\]
uniformly in \(n\) on that event. The same estimate holds in the limiting action space. Hence fixed-mesh identification followed by \(\Delta\downarrow0\) proves the joint continuous-time limit.

There is one discrete-GD defect from the coordinate change. If
\[
P_k=(\Gamma_n^*+Q_k^*)D_k,\qquad
\delta U_k=\eta_{0,n}\rho_k\phi'(U_k)P_k,
\]
then the exact polynomial identity
\[
\Theta(U_k+\delta U_k)-\Theta(U_k)
=\eta_{0,n}\rho_kP_k
 +U_k\delta U_k^2+\frac{\delta U_k^3}{3}
\]
shows that the defect \(R_k\) obeys
\[
|R_k|\le C_T\bigl(\eta_{0,n}^2|P_k|^2
+\eta_{0,n}^3|P_k|^3\bigr),
\]
because \(\sup_u|u|\phi'(u)^2<\infty\). Uniform \(L^2\) bounds and
\(\|P_k\|_\infty\le\sqrt n\,\|P_k\|_{2,n}\) give
\[
\sum_{k<T/\eta_{0,n}}\|R_k\|_{2,n}
\le C_T\bigl(\eta_{0,n}\sqrt n+\eta_{0,n}^2n\bigr)
\longrightarrow0.
\]
With \(\eta_{0,n}=n^{-2}\), the same bound after division by
\(\eta_{0,n}\) tends to zero in \(L^2(0,T;\mathcal H_{1,n})\), so the discrete velocity error also vanishes.

### 6. Path-Wasserstein convergence and square-tail transfer

Use \(H^1(0,T)\) for the hidden paths and include the action fields
\[
P=(\Gamma^*+Q^*)D,\qquad
V=(\Gamma+Q)H
\]
in the state. At fixed \(\Delta\), the master theorem gives \(W_2\) convergence of the linearly interpolated finite tuple. Estimate (5) supplies the coupling bound
\[
W_2^2(\mu_n,\mu_n^\Delta)
\le \frac1n\sum_i
 \|Z_{i,n}-Z_{i,n}^\Delta\|_{H^1(0,T)}^2
\le C_T\Delta^2+o_n(1).
\]
The analogous limiting bound holds between \(\mu^\Delta\) and \(\mu\). Thus
\[
W_2(\mu_n,\mu)
\le W_2(\mu_n,\mu_n^\Delta)
 +W_2(\mu_n^\Delta,\mu^\Delta)
 +W_2(\mu^\Delta,\mu)
\longrightarrow0
\]
by first fixing \(\Delta\), letting \(n\to\infty\), and then sending \(\Delta\downarrow0\).

This also supplies the required uniform integrability. Indeed, \(W_2\)-convergence yields couplings \(Z_n,Z\) with
\(\mathbb E\|Z_n-Z\|_{H^1}^2\to0\); splitting on
\(\{\|Z\|_{H^1}>R/2\}\) and
\(\{\|Z_n-Z\|_{H^1}>R/2\}\) shows
\[
\lim_{R\to\infty}\limsup_n
\int_{\{\|z\|_{H^1}>R\}}\|z\|_{H^1}^2\,d\mu_n(z)=0.
\]
Consequently all quadratic velocity, kernel, and update terms pass to the limit.

A convenient formal topology is the pointed-action \(W_2\) topology: enumerate all finite causal extensions formed from the state using coordinatewise Lipschitz maps, scalar products, \(\Gamma,\Gamma^*\), and finite-rank operators; require joint \(W_2\) convergence of their path laws and convergence of the finite-rank nuclear norms. This topology is restartable and determines every future action of the vector field.

Finally, the limiting observable formulas are
\[
K^{(1)}
 =\mathbb E_1[\phi'(U)^2P^2],\qquad
K^{(2)}
 =\mathbb E_2[D^2]\,\mathbb E_1[H^2],\qquad
K^{(3)}
 =\mathbb E_2[\phi(V)^2],
\]
\[
\dot U=\rho\phi'(U)P,
\]
\[
\dot V
 =\rho D\,\mathbb E_1[H^2]
   +(\Gamma+Q)\bigl(\phi'(U)^2\dot X\bigr).
\]
Thus the pointed-action limit determines both hidden path laws, both hidden velocity energies, all three layer updates, the kernel, output, and loss.

Tensor-Programs audit verdict: the cited theorem does justify the fixed-mesh width limit, including reuse of \(\Gamma_n\) and \(\Gamma_n^\top\), moment scalars, and singular limiting Gram matrices. It does not justify the countable probability-space/operator construction as written. Thus this part of the proposed proof is incomplete, though I found a plausible rigorous repair rather than a fatal obstruction.

The exact primary result is [Yang–Hu, supplement, Theorem G.4, Setup G.2, Assumption L.4](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/yang21c/yang21c-supp.pdf), based on [Yang, Tensor Programs III, Theorem E.15](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.10685). It says, for a fixed finite Tensor Program:

- initial matrices have iid \(N(0,\sigma_W^2/n)\) entries;
- initial vector coordinates are iid copies of a fixed jointly Gaussian vector;
- initial scalar sequences converge almost surely to deterministic limits;
- both \(Wv\) and \(W^\top v\) are permitted, with arbitrary reuse;
- coordinate nonlinearities and moment integrands involving vector arguments must be jointly pseudo-Lipschitz in all vector and scalar arguments;
- empirical averages of every fixed pseudo-Lipschitz test converge almost surely, and program scalars converge;
- no rank-stability assumption is needed in this pseudo-Lipschitz version.

Consequently:

1. Reuse of \(\Gamma_n,\Gamma_n^\top\) is genuinely covered. The theorem tracks their dependence through its correction term; no gradient-independence approximation is being made.

2. Fixed-mesh Euler is expressible as a finite program. Indeed,

\[
Q_k=-2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rD_r\otimes H_r
\]

gives

\[
(\Gamma_n+Q_k)H_k
 =
\Gamma_nH_k
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rD_r\langle H_r,H_k\rangle_n,
\]

and

\[
(\Gamma_n^\top+Q_k^\top)D_k
 =
\Gamma_n^\top D_k
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rH_r\langle D_r,D_k\rangle_n.
\]

Thus only MatMul, coordinate maps, and Moment operations occur for fixed \(T/\delta\).

3. The regularity hypotheses hold. The maps \(\Theta(u)=u+u^3/3\), \(J=\Theta^{-1}\), \(\arctan\), \(q(s)=(1+s^2)^{-1}\), scalar-vector multiplication, \((c,s)\mapsto cq(s)\), and all inner-product integrands are pseudo-Lipschitz of finite degree. Tests such as \(|z|^p\), \(p\ge1\), are also pseudo-Lipschitz.

4. Singular Grams and zero fields are allowed. The claim that this version needs no separate rank-stability hypothesis is correct.

5. The \(n\)-dependent readout can be handled exactly, more cleanly than the proposed stability replacement. Write

\[
C_{0,n}=\frac1nR_{0,n},\qquad R_{0,n}\sim N(0,I_n).
\]

Use \(R_{0,n}\) as an initial Gaussian vector and \(a_n=1/n\to0\) as an initial scalar, then generate \(C_{0,n}=a_nR_{0,n}\) by a pseudo-Lipschitz Nonlin operation. This fits Setup G.2 exactly.

There are nevertheless two material defects.

First, “continuous scalar operations” is too broad. Arbitrary continuity is allowed only for pure-scalar Moment calls. Any operation mixing coordinate variables with scalar parameters must be jointly pseudo-Lipschitz. The particular recursion satisfies this stronger condition, so this is repairable wording.

Second, and much more importantly,

> “Quotient … and complete them. This produces two probability \(L^2\)-spaces”

is false as stated. Hilbert completion produces only an abstract Hilbert space. It supplies neither a probability-space realization nor the coordinatewise nonlinear structure needed by the limiting ODE. The finite-program theorem also does not itself provide a countable operator action.

A repair requires a separate lemma:

- Construct one countable, sequential, typed program closed under rational linear combinations, \(\Gamma,\Gamma^\top\), the required fixed nonlinearities, and a universal countable dense family of bounded-Lipschitz cylinder maps.
- Apply Theorem G.4 to every finite prefix. A countable intersection gives simultaneous convergence, while the recursively defined master laws form consistent finite-dimensional distributions.
- Use Kolmogorov extension to obtain actual probability spaces \((\Omega_R,\mu_R)\) and \((\Omega_L,\mu_L)\). The cylinder family can be chosen dense in the corresponding \(L^2\)-spaces.
- Prove independently, by a Gaussian net bound and Borel–Cantelli, that eventually
  \[
  \|\Gamma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le 10.
  \]
  Then Theorem G.4 applied to squared norms yields
  \[
  \|Z^{\Gamma v}\|_{L^2(\mu_L)}
  \le 10\|Z^v\|_{L^2(\mu_R)}
  \]
  on the dense program algebra.
- Define \(\Gamma_0Z^v=Z^{\Gamma v}\). The inequality makes this well-defined and bounded. Exact finite-dimensional duality passes to the limit:
  \[
  \langle\Gamma_0v,w\rangle_{L^2(\mu_L)}
  =
  \langle v,\Gamma_0^*w\rangle_{L^2(\mu_R)}.
  \]
  Extend both maps by density.

That would rigorously produce the required probability \(L^2\)-action. The proposed proof does not currently contain this argument.

Finally, Theorem G.4 supplies no continuous-time, uniform-in-time, or growing-program conclusion. The auxiliary-mesh approximation and uniform stability estimates must carry all of that burden independently.

So the precise conclusion is: the Tensor-Program citation is appropriate for each fixed mesh and has no apparent fatal hypothesis mismatch, but the proposed proof is not valid as stated because its countable law/action bridge is asserted rather than proved. That gap appears repairable along the route above.

Adversarial audit verdict: the finite-width scaling mechanism and the small-time nondegeneracy calculation survive scrutiny, but the submitted text is not yet a rigorous proof of the theorem as stated. Its principal unresolved obligations are in the continuum lifting/topology argument, not in the scaling algebra.

What checks out:

- The rescaling \(c=nW^{(3)}\), the exact GD equations, and all three kernel formulas are correct.
- With \(R=-2eM^\top D\),

  \[
  u^+=u+\eta q(u)R
  \]

  and the cubic identity

  \[
  \Theta(u+\eta q(u)R)-\Theta(u)
  =
  \eta R+\eta^2u q(u)^2R^2+\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(u)^3R^3
  \]

  is exact.
- The bounds

  \[
  \|R^2\|_n\le \sqrt n\,\|R\|_n^2,\qquad
  \|R^3\|_n\le n\,\|R\|_n^3,\qquad
  \sup_u |u|q(u)^2=\frac9{16\sqrt3}
  \]

  give precisely the accumulated defect

  \[
  O_T(\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n).
  \]

  For \(\eta=n^{-2}\), this is \(O_T(n^{-3/2})\).
- Uniform finite-width stability is available on the high-probability event
  \(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}\le10\), \(\|C_{0,n}\|_n,\|C_{0,n}\|_\infty\le1\). In particular, for \(a=\pi/2\),

  \[
  \|C_{k+1}\|_n
  \le (1+2a^2\eta)\|C_k\|_n+2a\eta.
  \]

  This successively bounds \(e_k\), \(\|C_k\|_\infty\), \(\|Q_k\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\), \(\|M_k\|_{\rm op}\), and \(\|R_k\|_n\), uniformly for \(k\eta\le T\). A standard perturbed-Euler Grönwall argument then proves the stated GD/flow rate.
- The path estimates (21)–(23) are correct. With the uniform velocity-energy bound, they can yield \(W_2(C([0,T]))\) convergence.
- The small-time expansion is algebraically correct:

  \[
  C=2tG+o(t),\quad D=2tY+o(t),\quad
  X=X_0+2t^2\Xi+o(t^2),
  \]

  \[
  Q=2t^2Y\otimes H_0+o(t^2),\quad
  S=Z+2t^2\mathcal RY+o(t^2).
  \]

  Consequently, all coefficients in (27)–(29) are correct. The adjoint identity proves \(d_0>0\), and \(\mathcal R\ge mI\) proves \(\mathcal RY\ne0\). Thus, if the limiting flow and convergence theorem are established, the feature learning, kernel movement, loss decrease, variance, and nonlinearity conditions follow on a sufficiently short interval.

The proof gaps are:

1. **The stated action topology is too strong for the estimates proved.**

   It includes arbitrary sums and products of probes, while the Euler error is only controlled in \(L^2\) and trace norm. \(L^2\)-closeness does not propagate through arbitrary polynomial products. A rare-coordinate perturbation can have vanishing \(L^2\)-norm but a nonvanishing squared or higher-product probe.

   This can likely be repaired by replacing the topology with a countable closure of the probes actually required by the vector field, using bounded/truncated coordinate maps and recording the relevant second moments separately. Alternatively, one must prove a uniform hierarchy of higher-moment error estimates, which the submission does not do.

2. **Fixed-mesh moment convergence is not uniform as the mesh vanishes.**

   A fixed-program theorem may give every \(p\)-moment for each fixed \(\delta\), but its program length is \(T/\delta\). Nothing stated controls those moments uniformly as \(\delta\downarrow0\). Therefore “the same approximation gives square-tail tightness” does not follow immediately.

   A repair is possible for the necessary fields via a two-scale argument:

   - compare the flow with one fixed coarse Euler mesh;
   - use fixed-program \(p>2\) bounds at that coarse mesh;
   - transfer square-tail control using (23);
   - send the coarse mesh to zero.

   This must be carried out uniformly in time and in probability.

3. **Velocity and kernel convergence do not follow from path-\(W_2\) convergence.**

   Uniform path convergence permits high-frequency derivative defects. Here derivative convergence should instead be proved from the exact vector fields. The delicate fields are

   \[
   r=M^*D,\qquad p=q(U)^2r,\qquad
   A=\|H\|_2^2D+Mp.
   \]

   The map \((U,r)\mapsto q(U)^2r\) is not globally Lipschitz on \(L^2\times L^2\). It requires square uniform integrability of \(r\) and a truncation argument. Only after proving this uniformly in time can one pass \(Mp\), \(V^{(2)}\), and \(K^{(1)}\). The submitted single paragraph is an outline, not the required proof.

4. **The actual output initialization should be removed before applying the fixed-program theorem.**

   Since

   \[
   \|C_{0,n}\|_n=O_{\mathbb P}(n^{-1}),\qquad
   \|C_{0,n}\|_\infty=O_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt{\log n}/n),
   \]

   stability permits comparison with a finite-width system initialized at \(C_0=0\). That comparison should precede the theorem invocation unless the exact external theorem explicitly permits the width-dependent degenerate Gaussian seed \(n^{-1}\xi\).

5. **Several probability quantifiers are suppressed.**

   Bounds such as (15), (19), moment tails, and temporal equicontinuity hold on high-probability initialization events, not deterministically. A complete proof must choose those events, show their probabilities tend to one, and preserve the order

   \[
   \text{coarse mesh fixed}\;\to\;n\to\infty
   \;\to\;\text{tail cutoff}\to\infty
   \;\to\;\text{mesh}\downarrow0.
   \]

6. **The small-time expansions need a short strong-topology justification.**

   Nemytskii maps are not automatically Fréchet differentiable \(L^2\to L^2\). Here the expansions are repairable using pointwise differentiability, bounded derivatives, \(L^2\)-convergence of the scaled increments, and square uniform integrability, but that argument is absent.

Thus I found no contradiction in the proposed scaling or local dynamics, and the finite-width/GD and positivity portions are repairable. But the affirmative theorem is not validly proved as written. Its ultimate status remains conditional on:

- a correct external fixed-program theorem with all hypotheses verified;
- a valid construction of the pointed two-sided action;
- the missing two-scale uniform-integrability and vector-field convergence argument.

Without those, the correct conclusion is “promising construction with substantial proof gaps,” not an established affirmative resolution.

The pointed-action/IDE part is not rigorous as written, although I found no internal contradiction in the proposed limiting ODE. Its main defects appear repairable, but the repairs are substantial lemmas rather than cosmetic edits.

1. The claimed GNS step is incomplete.

“Quotient the program fields by zero \(L^2\)-norm and complete” produces abstract Hilbert spaces. It does not produce probability \(L^2\)-spaces with pointwise operations. On a bare Hilbert completion, expressions such as

\[
J(X),\qquad \arctan(MH),\qquad Cq(S)
\]

are undefined. Arbitrary Hilbert-space unitary equivalence also does not preserve these nonlinear operations.

A repair is available:

- Construct a countable, two-sorted universal program algebra \(\mathscr A_R,\mathscr A_L\), closed under rational linear combinations, \(\Gamma,\Gamma^\top\), empirical moments, and a countable dense class of bounded Lipschitz cylindrical maps.
- Apply the fixed-program master theorem to every finite prefix. Countability gives consistent finite-dimensional laws simultaneously.
- Use Kolmogorov extension to realize the right and left fields on standard probability spaces \((\Omega_R,\mathbb P_R)\), \((\Omega_L,\mathbb P_L)\).
- Include enough compactly supported rational piecewise-linear cylindrical functions that their spans are dense in
  \[
  L^2_R=L^2(\Omega_R),\qquad L^2_L=L^2(\Omega_L).
  \]
  These are admissible pseudo-Lipschitz program nonlinearities.
- Define \(\Gamma\) first on this dense program algebra.

For a program field \(v\), finite-width operator norm control gives

\[
\|\Gamma_n v_n\|_n\le \|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}\|v_n\|_n.
\]

The Gaussian tail bound \(\Pr(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}>10)\le 2e^{-cn}\), together with convergence of both empirical norms, implies

\[
\|\Gamma v\|_{L^2_L}\le 10\|v\|_{L^2_R}.
\]

Thus zero-norm equivalence is respected and \(\Gamma\) extends uniquely to all of \(L^2_R\). The same applies to the formal transpose. Passing

\[
\langle \Gamma_n v_n,w_n\rangle_n
=
\langle v_n,\Gamma_n^\top w_n\rangle_n
\]

to the limit on dense algebras proves that the transpose extension is exactly \(\Gamma^*\).

This repairs the probability structure, functional calculus, bounded operator, and adjoint. The original text does not prove these points.

2. The trace-class middle-layer equation is sound after that repair.

For \(D\in L^2_L\), \(H\in L^2_R\),

\[
(D\otimes H)v=D\,\mathbb E_R[Hv]
\]

is rank one and

\[
\|D\otimes H\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
=
\|D\|_2\|H\|_2.
\]

Hence a continuous solution satisfies

\[
Q(t)=-2\int_0^t e(s)D(s)\otimes H(s)\,ds
\in\mathfrak S_1.
\]

There is no trace-class obstruction here.

3. The Picard argument stated in the solution is not valid as a direct Banach-ODE invocation.

The proposed metric uses only \(L^2\) for \(C\), but the estimate

\[
\|Cq(S)-\widetilde Cq(\widetilde S)\|_2
\le
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2+
\|\widetilde C\|_\infty
\operatorname{Lip}(q)\|S-\widetilde S\|_2
\]

requires a uniform \(L^\infty\) bound. The vector field is therefore not locally Lipschitz on an open subset of

\[
L^2_R\times L^2_L\times\mathfrak S_1.
\]

Merely saying that Picard iterates have a common \(L^\infty\) envelope does not justify the theorem.

This is repairable by doing the contraction on the closed complete path set

\[
\left\{
(X,C,Q):
\sup_{t\le \tau}\|(X,C,Q)(t)-(X_0,C_0,Q_0)\|\le R,\ 
|C(t,\omega)|\le B\ \text{a.e.}
\right\}
\]

with the \(C_t(L^2\times L^2\times\mathfrak S_1)\) metric. On such a set, writing \(a=\pi/2\),

\[
\|H-\widetilde H\|_2\le \|X-\widetilde X\|_2,
\]

\[
\|S-\widetilde S\|_2
\le
(\|\Gamma\|+R)\|X-\widetilde X\|_2
+a\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1},
\]

and the displayed estimate for \(D-\widetilde D\), followed by the rank-one trace-norm estimate, gives a genuine contraction for sufficiently small \(\tau\). The integral map preserves the \(L^\infty\) envelope because \(G\) is bounded. This supplies existence and uniqueness.

4. The global bounds are correct once the chain rule is supplied.

For the repaired local solution, a pointwise/Fubini Sobolev chain rule gives

\[
\dot H=q(U)^2\dot X=-2e\,q(U)^2M^*D
\]

and hence

\[
\dot F=-2eK,\qquad
K=\|q(U)M^*D\|_2^2+\|H\|_2^2\|D\|_2^2+\|G\|_2^2.
\]

Since \(e(0)=-1\),

\[
e(t)=-\exp\!\left(-2\int_0^tK(s)\,ds\right),
\qquad |e(t)|\le1.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\|C(t)\|_\infty\le 2at,
\qquad
\|D(t)\|_2\le2at,
\]

\[
\|\dot Q(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\le4a^2t,
\qquad
\|Q(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\le2a^2t^2,
\]

and, with \(b=\|\Gamma\|\),

\[
\|\dot X(t)\|_2
\le4at\bigl(b+2a^2t^2\bigr).
\]

These bounds exclude finite-time blowup and justify global continuation. Thus the global-well-posedness claim is repairable and appears correct.

5. “Law-level” and “restartable” require a definition absent from the solution.

A bare pointed Hilbert operator is not enough because arbitrary unitaries do not preserve pointwise nonlinearities. The correct state is the isomorphism class of a two-sorted probability-operator object

\[
(\Omega_R,\mathbb P_R;\Omega_L,\mathbb P_L;M,X,C),
\]

where \(M:L^2_R\to L^2_L\) is bounded, under measure-space/Koopman isomorphisms intertwining \(M\) and carrying \(X,C\) to their counterparts. The evolution is equivariant under precisely these isomorphisms.

It is cleaner to use the current operator

\[
M_t=\Gamma+Q_t
\]

rather than \((\Gamma,Q_t)\). Then

\[
\dot M=-2eD\otimes H,
\]

and the future depends only on the current \((M_t,X_t,C_t)\). No history or externally prescribed trajectory is retained. Under this operator-law interpretation, the construction is autonomous and restartable and is not tautological: the universal action is constructed once from initialization, while the path is subsequently obtained from the autonomous IDE.

There remains an interpretive risk: this is a maximally rich operator-valued law, not an ordinary neuron or edge empirical measure. The prompt is broad enough to allow genuinely necessary conditional/edge laws, so it may be admissible, but the original solution does not establish that its undefined “pointed action” meets the stipulated meaning of “law-level mean field.”

6. The solution also did not explicitly establish the “gradient flow” characterization.

This too is repairable. In the original variable \(U=J(X)\), on the affine Hilbert space

\[
L^2_R\times(\Gamma+\mathfrak S_2)\times L^2_L,
\]

the derivatives of \(F\) are

\[
\nabla_UF=q(U)M^*D,\qquad
\nabla_MF=D\otimes H,\qquad
\nabla_CF=G.
\]

Thus the curve satisfies

\[
\dot U=-\nabla_U (F-1)^2,\qquad
\dot M=-\nabla_M (F-1)^2,\qquad
\dot C=-\nabla_C (F-1)^2.
\]

The trajectory happens to remain in the stronger affine class \(\Gamma+\mathfrak S_1\).

Verdict for this subsystem: the IDE itself, its trace-class structure, energy identity, and global bounds appear mathematically viable. But the proof as stated is not valid at the requested resolution standard because the probability/operator state, nonlinear functional calculus, Picard theorem, and law-level equivalence are missing. Those gaps have plausible rigorous repairs as above. Whether the full theorem survives still depends on the separate fixed-program-to-continuous-time and exact-GD convergence audit.

The repair closes the main uniform-integrability obstruction for the fields actually needed by \(K\) and \(V\), provided three details are added.

For fixed coarse \(\delta\), take \(P_n^\delta\) piecewise constant at its finitely many Euler mesh states. Then

\[
\sup_{t\le T}
\left\langle
P_n(t)^2\mathbf 1_{\{|P_n(t)|>R\}}
\right\rangle_n
\le
2\sup_{t\le T}
\left\langle
P_n^\delta(t)^2
\mathbf 1_{\{|P_n^\delta(t)|>R/2\}}
\right\rangle_n
+
4\sup_{t\le T}\|P_n(t)-P_n^\delta(t)\|_n^2.
\]

Fixed-\(\delta\) Tensor-Program \(p>2\) moments control the first term; the second is \(O(\delta^2)\). Thus, in probability,

\[
\lim_{R\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}
\sup_{t\le T}
\left\langle
P_n(t)^2\mathbf 1_{\{|P_n(t)|>R\}}
\right\rangle_n=0,
\]

because the limsup is bounded by \(C\delta^2\) for every \(\delta>0\). No moment estimate uniform in \(\delta\) is required. The same inequality transfers this uniform integrability from the flow to arbitrary fine Euler approximations.

This permits the truncation argument for \(q(U_n)^aP_n\), \(a=1,2\). For the reused operator term, one must explicitly use

\[
\|M_np_n-M_n^\delta p_n^\delta\|_2
\le
\|M_n-M_n^\delta\|_{\rm op}\|p_n\|_2
+
\|M_n^\delta\|_{\rm op}\|p_n-p_n^\delta\|_2,
\]

where \(p_n=q(U_n)^2P_n\). The first term follows from the trace-norm state estimate; the second follows from the new uniform integrability. At fixed \(\delta\), \(M_n^\delta p_n^\delta\) is a finite-program probe. This closes

\[
K^{(1)}=\|q(U)P\|_2^2
\]

and the continuous-flow second-layer velocity

\[
\dot S=-2e\bigl(\|H\|_2^2D+M(q(U)^2P)\bigr).
\]

Remaining additions:

- Establish uniform-time joint \(W_2\) convergence of \((U_n,P_n)\), not merely separate marginal convergence. The fixed-mesh triangle should provide it because \(P=M^*D\) is \(L^2\)-Lipschitz on the bounded stability region.
- Repeat the argument for polygonal exact-GD derivatives. Inside a GD step,

  \[
  \dot u=-2e_kq(u_k)P_k,
  \]

  \[
  \dot s=-2e_k\left[
  D_k\langle H_k,h(t)\rangle_n
  +
  M(t)\bigl(q(u(t))q(u_k)P_k\bigr)
  \right].
  \]

  Endpoint and intra-step errors must be shown to vanish using the same uniform-integrability argument. Otherwise only flow velocities, not the requested exact-GD \(V_n^{(\ell)}\), have been handled.
- State all tail limits in probability; the empirical tail averages are random.

Subject to these additions and a valid fixed-program/action theorem, I see no remaining fatal analytic gap for kernel and velocity convergence. This repair still does not establish the overstrong topology containing arbitrary polynomial products; that topology should be restricted to the necessary recursively generated bounded/truncated probes.

Conditional on the fixed-finite-program Tensor-Program theorem applying as claimed, the repaired construction closes. I do not see a remaining fatal mathematical issue in the probability/action/IDE/restartability subsystem.

The repair must be stated with the following precision.

1. Use a canonical countable two-sorted infinite program, constructed by dovetailing all admissible operations. Every finite prefix is a legal finite Tensor Program. Include:

   - the initial fields and constants;
   - rational linear combinations;
   - \(\Gamma,\Gamma^\top\);
   - moment operations;
   - a countable class of rational compactly supported Lipschitz cylindrical maps dense in every \(L^2(\mathbb R^k,\mu)\).

   Fixed-prefix convergence gives consistent finite-dimensional laws. Kolmogorov realization produces standard countably generated probability spaces \(\Omega_R,\Omega_L\). Algebraic and nonlinear program identities hold almost surely because their squared residuals have limiting expectation zero.

2. Bounded cylindrical fields are dense in the resulting full \(L^2_R,L^2_L\). For every such right field \(v\), include \(\Gamma v\) as a left field, and conversely for \(\Gamma^\top w\). The finite-dimensional inequality and Gaussian operator-norm tail give

\[
\|\Gamma v\|_2\le B\|v\|_2
\]

on the dense algebra. Hence \(\Gamma\) is well-defined modulo limiting zero norm and extends uniquely to full \(L^2_R\). Finite duality on dense algebras proves that the transpose extension is \(\Gamma^*\). This fully supplies the functional calculus missing from a bare Hilbert/GNS completion.

3. Formulate the ODE on

\[
L^2_R\times L^2_L\times (M_0+\mathfrak S_1),
\]

but perform the local fixed-point argument on a closed path set satisfying a uniform \(L^\infty\) envelope for \(C\). That set is complete in the uniform \(L^2/L^2/\mathfrak S_1\) metric. The integral map preserves the envelope because \(G=\arctan S\) is bounded, and it is contractive there because

\[
\|Cq(S)-\widetilde Cq(\widetilde S)\|_2
\le
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2+
B\,\operatorname{Lip}(q)\|S-\widetilde S\|_2.
\]

Every solution starting from bounded \(C_0\) automatically lies in such an envelope, so uniqueness is not restricted to an artificially small solution class. The energy identity then yields the global bounds and continuation.

4. Define law-states modulo probability-algebra, equivalently Koopman, isomorphisms—not arbitrary Hilbert unitaries. If \(U_R,U_L\) are the Koopman maps, require

\[
\widetilde M U_R=U_LM,\qquad
\widetilde X=U_RX,\qquad
\widetilde C=U_LC.
\]

Koopman maps preserve expectations and pointwise nonlinear functional calculus, and

\[
U_L(D\otimes H)U_R^{-1}
=
(U_LD)\otimes(U_RH).
\]

Therefore the IDE is equivariant and descends to these isomorphism classes. Taking the current \(M_t\), rather than the decomposition \(\Gamma+Q_t\), as state makes restartability immediate: the future uses only \(M_t,X_t,C_t\).

The remaining caveats are external to this repaired subsystem:

- The fixed-program theorem’s exact applicability must still be verified.
- The finite-width processes must still be proved to converge uniformly in time to this operator-law flow.
- The prompt may or may not intend to admit such a maximally rich two-sorted operator law as “mean field.” Mathematically it is a genuine deterministic law object, not a retained finite array or trajectory, but that semantic admissibility should be acknowledged explicitly.

The repaired route is a legitimate use of Theorem G.4. I found no hidden incompatibility from infinite-language nesting, program order, singular Gram matrices, or the symbolic pseudoinverse. The crucial point is never to apply G.4 to an infinite program: apply it only to finite prefixes of one countable sequential program.

A rigorous implementation needs these details.

1. Build the universal language in stages. Starting from the typed seeds, at stage \(m+1\) add:

\[
\text{rational linear combinations},\quad
\text{a countable dense bounded-Lipschitz cylinder algebra},\quad
\Gamma v,\ \Gamma^\top w,
\]

for objects constructed by stage \(m\), together with the particular pseudo-Lipschitz maps used by all rational-mesh Euler schemes. Dovetailing gives one countable instruction sequence in which every object appears at a finite line.

2. Apply G.4 separately to every finite prefix and every test in a countable convergence-determining family. The intersection of these probability-one events still has probability one.

3. The limiting finite-dimensional laws are consistent. Extending a prefix leaves all old finite-\(n\) variables literally unchanged. Hence the same empirical bounded-Lipschitz test has both the old-prefix limit and the restriction of the extended-prefix limit. Since these limits are deterministic, they coincide. This uniqueness argument also resolves the apparent fact that the \(Z^{\rm dot}\) decomposition can depend on program order: the decomposition may change, but the law of every previously computed variable cannot.

4. G.4/E.15 requires no rank stability. Every prefix contains only finite covariance matrices, and the no-rank-stability theorem handles their possibly singular limiting pseudoinverses. No infinite-dimensional pseudoinverse is ever formed.

5. Kolmogorov extension applies to the consistent countable family. To obtain full probability \(L^2\)-spaces, not merely abstract Hilbert completions, use the canonical coordinate realization and its right/left sigma-fields. A universal countable family of rational, compactly supported, piecewise-linear cylinder functions is dense in \(L^2\): finite-coordinate conditional expectations converge in \(L^2\), and such functions are dense for every finite-dimensional Borel probability law.

6. Define the action first on the dense program algebra. Exact finite-\(n\) linear identities, tested through squared norms using G.4, prove linearity modulo \(L^2\)-zero classes. The eventual Gaussian bound

\[
\|\Gamma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le 10
\]

gives

\[
\|Z^{\Gamma v}\|_2\le10\|Z^v\|_2,
\]

so the action is well-defined and extends uniquely. Exact finite-\(n\) duality and convergence of product moments yield

\[
\langle \Gamma v,w\rangle_L
=
\langle v,\Gamma^*w\rangle_R.
\]

7. For every fixed coarse mesh, expanding the learned matrix into its finite rank-one sum makes the entire recursion a fixed finite Tensor Program. Induction identifies its master-law MatMul variables with the operator action just constructed.

8. Mesh removal is analytic, not a Tensor-Program invocation. On high-probability sets with bounded \(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}\), normalized initial \(L^2\)-norm, and initial readout \(L^\infty\)-norm, the vector fields have width-independent local Lipschitz and growth bounds in

\[
L_R^2\times L_L^2\times\mathfrak S_1.
\]

Thus finite-width and limiting Euler errors are \(O_T(\delta)\). Restrict initially to a countable rational mesh family and extend to arbitrary compact times by continuity.

Two technical cautions remain:

- Convergence of finite-rank \(Q\) across widths must be expressed through its factors and their Gram moments; there is no canonical cross-width trace-norm difference.
- Transferring mesh error to unbounded weighted probes requires the fixed-mesh higher-moment convergence plus a tail truncation argument. Raw \(L^2\) error alone is insufficient.

Subject to those explicit lemmas, the repaired construction is compatible with G.4 and avoids any growing-depth Tensor-Program claim.

## Lemma package: fixed-mesh width limit

Let

\[
\langle v,w\rangle_n=\frac1n v^\top w,\qquad
\|v\|_n^2=\langle v,v\rangle_n,
\]

and

\[
a\otimes_n b:v\longmapsto a\langle b,v\rangle_n.
\]

Let \(\Gamma_n\) have iid \(N(0,1/n)\) entries. Let \(U_n,R_n\) be independent standard Gaussian vectors, independent of \(\Gamma_n\), and set

\[
a_n=\frac1n,\qquad
X_{0,n}=\Theta(U_n),\qquad
C_{0,n}=a_nR_n,\qquad Q_{0,n}=0,
\]

where

\[
\Theta(u)=u+\frac{u^3}{3},\qquad
J=\Theta^{-1},\qquad
\Psi(x)=\arctan J(x),\qquad
q(s)=\frac1{1+s^2}.
\]

Thus \(C_{0,n}\) has exactly the law of the rescaled readout in the proposed construction.

### Lemma 1: Specialized Tensor-Program theorem

Consider a fixed finite program whose vector variables lie in copies of \(\mathbb R^n\), whose initial data are \(\Gamma_n,U_n,R_n\), and whose instructions are:

1. \(v\mapsto\Gamma_nv\) or \(w\mapsto\Gamma_n^\top w\);
2. coordinatewise application of a fixed function to finitely many existing vectors and scalar variables;
3. creation of a scalar
   \[
   \frac1n\sum_{\alpha=1}^n
   \varphi(v^1_\alpha,\ldots,v^k_\alpha;\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_\ell).
   \]

Suppose every initial scalar sequence converges almost surely to a deterministic finite limit. Assume:

- if \(k=0\), the pure-scalar function is continuous;
- if \(k>0\), the function is jointly pseudo-Lipschitz in all vector-coordinate and scalar arguments.

Then, for every fixed finite family of generated vectors \(v^1_n,\ldots,v^m_n\) and every pseudo-Lipschitz \(\zeta:\mathbb R^m\to\mathbb R\),

\[
\frac1n\sum_{\alpha=1}^n
\zeta(v^1_{n,\alpha},\ldots,v^m_{n,\alpha})
\longrightarrow
\mathbb E\zeta(Z^{v^1},\ldots,Z^{v^m})
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\]

Every generated scalar converges almost surely to its deterministic master scalar.

This is exactly [Yang–Hu, Setup G.2, Assumption L.4 and Theorem G.4](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/yang21c/yang21c-supp.pdf), whose no-rank-stability basis is [Yang, Theorem E.15](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.10685). In particular:

- arbitrary finite reuse of both \(\Gamma_n\) and \(\Gamma_n^\top\) is allowed;
- limiting Gram matrices may be singular;
- no rank-stability assumption is imposed;
- the conclusion is for a fixed finite program, not for a program whose length grows with \(n\).

The theorem applies to the functions above:

- \(\Theta\) is pseudo-Lipschitz of degree \(2\);
- \(J\) is globally \(1\)-Lipschitz because
  \[
  J'(x)=\frac1{1+J(x)^2};
  \]
- \(\Psi,\arctan,q\) are bounded and globally Lipschitz;
- \((c,s)\mapsto cq(s)\) and \((c,s)\mapsto c\arctan s\) are pseudo-Lipschitz;
- finite products, scalar-vector multiplication, and polynomial combinations are pseudo-Lipschitz of finite degree;
- \(a_n\to0\), and \(C_{0,n}=a_nR_n\) is generated by the jointly pseudo-Lipschitz map \((r;a)\mapsto ar\).

Thus the \(n\)-dependent readout is covered exactly; it need not first be discarded by a stability argument.

### Lemma 2: Every fixed Euler scheme is a finite Tensor Program

Fix \(\delta>0\) and an integer \(K\). Define, for \(0\le k<K\),

\[
H_{k,n}=\Psi(X_{k,n}),
\]

\[
S_{k,n}=(\Gamma_n+Q_{k,n})H_{k,n},
\]

\[
G_{k,n}=\arctan S_{k,n},
\qquad
D_{k,n}=C_{k,n}q(S_{k,n}),
\]

\[
F_{k,n}=\langle C_{k,n},G_{k,n}\rangle_n,
\qquad
e_{k,n}=F_{k,n}-1,
\]

and

\[
X_{k+1,n}
=
X_{k,n}
-2\delta e_{k,n}(\Gamma_n+Q_{k,n})^\top D_{k,n},
\]

\[
C_{k+1,n}=C_{k,n}-2\delta e_{k,n}G_{k,n},
\]

\[
Q_{k+1,n}
=
Q_{k,n}-2\delta e_{k,n}D_{k,n}\otimes_n H_{k,n}.
\tag{1}
\]

Induction gives

\[
Q_{k,n}
=
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_{r,n}D_{r,n}\otimes_n H_{r,n}.
\tag{2}
\]

Consequently,

\[
S_{k,n}
=
\Gamma_nH_{k,n}
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_{r,n}D_{r,n}
\langle H_{r,n},H_{k,n}\rangle_n,
\tag{3}
\]

and

\[
(\Gamma_n+Q_{k,n})^\top D_{k,n}
=
\Gamma_n^\top D_{k,n}
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_{r,n}H_{r,n}
\langle D_{r,n},D_{k,n}\rangle_n.
\tag{4}
\]

Equations (3)–(4) express the whole \(K\)-step scheme using only MatMul, Nonlin and Moment operations. Therefore, for fixed \((\delta,K)\), (1) is a finite Tensor Program satisfying Lemma 1.

No learned matrix is submitted to the Tensor-Program theorem: the learned part is always expanded as the finite rank-one sum (2).

## Construction of the limiting probability/operator state

### Lemma 3: A countable sequential universal program exists

Use two formal sorts:

- right variables, on which \(\Gamma\) acts;
- left variables, on which \(\Gamma^\top\) acts.

The sorting is only bookkeeping; each finite typed program is a valid ordinary square Tensor Program.

Construct countable sets \(\mathcal V_R,\mathcal V_L\) in stages. Begin with the Gaussian right seed \(U\), Gaussian left seed \(R\), constants, and \(a_n=1/n\). At each stage add:

1. rational linear combinations of existing same-sort variables;
2. the fixed maps \(\Theta,J,\Psi,\arctan,q\) and the finite products needed by the Euler recursion;
3. every member of a fixed countable dense family of bounded Lipschitz cylinder functions of existing same-sort variables;
4. \(\Gamma v\) for every existing right variable \(v\);
5. \(\Gamma^\top w\) for every existing left variable \(w\);
6. all variables and moment scalars occurring in the first \(K\) steps for every \(\delta\in\mathbb Q_{>0}\) and \(K\in\mathbb N\).

Dovetail these additions. Every expression has finite dependency depth, so the resulting countable acyclic collection has a sequential enumeration in which every dependency precedes its use. Every finite prefix is a valid finite Tensor Program.

The bounded cylinder family may be chosen as rational compactly supported piecewise-affine functions on rational triangulations, together with constants. It is countable and uniformly dense on compact sets in \(C_c(\mathbb R^m)\) for every \(m\).

### Lemma 4: The prefix master laws are projectively consistent

For a finite set \(I\subset\mathcal V_R\cup\mathcal V_L\), choose a prefix containing all variables in \(I\), and let \(\mu_I\) be the joint law of their Tensor-Program master variables.

This definition is independent of the chosen prefix.

Indeed, suppose one prefix extends another. Every old finite-\(n\) vector is literally unchanged by the extension. For every bounded Lipschitz \(\zeta\), the corresponding empirical average is therefore identical in the two programs for every \(n\). Lemma 1 says that this one sequence converges to both proposed deterministic expectations. Hence the expectations agree. Bounded Lipschitz functions determine Borel probability measures on finite-dimensional Euclidean space, so the marginal laws agree.

This argument also resolves program-order concerns in the symbolic \(Z^{\rm dot}\) construction. Adding unused earlier calculations can change a Gaussian conditioning decomposition, but cannot change the law of an unchanged finite-\(n\) vector.

Since the family \((\mu_I)\) is projectively consistent and the index set is countable, Kolmogorov extension gives a deterministic probability measure \(\mathbb P\) on

\[
\Omega=\mathbb R^{\mathcal V_R\cup\mathcal V_L}.
\]

Write \(Z^v\) for the coordinate associated with \(v\), and define

\[
\mathscr F_R=\sigma(Z^v:v\in\mathcal V_R),
\qquad
\mathscr F_L=\sigma(Z^w:w\in\mathcal V_L),
\]

\[
\mathcal H_R=L^2(\Omega,\mathscr F_R,\mathbb P),
\qquad
\mathcal H_L=L^2(\Omega,\mathscr F_L,\mathbb P).
\]

This is the missing probability-space construction. Abstract Hilbert completion alone would not provide the required coordinatewise nonlinear structure.

### Lemma 5: The program cylinder classes are dense

Let \(\mathcal D_R\) be the rational linear span of the chosen bounded Lipschitz right-cylinder variables, and define \(\mathcal D_L\) analogously. Then

\[
\overline{\mathcal D_R}^{\,L^2}=\mathcal H_R,
\qquad
\overline{\mathcal D_L}^{\,L^2}=\mathcal H_L.
\]

To prove the right statement, enumerate the right coordinate variables as \((Y_j)_{j\ge1}\) and let

\[
\mathscr F_m=\sigma(Y_1,\ldots,Y_m).
\]

For \(X\in L^2(\mathscr F_R)\), martingale convergence gives

\[
\mathbb E[X\mid\mathscr F_m]\longrightarrow X
\quad\text{in }L^2.
\]

Every \(\mathscr F_m\)-measurable \(L^2\) function is an \(L^2\) function on \(\mathbb R^m\) under the finite-dimensional coordinate law. Continuous compactly supported functions are dense in this \(L^2\)-space, and the selected rational piecewise-affine family is uniformly dense in \(C_c\). Thus elements of \(\mathcal D_R\) approximate \(X\). The left proof is identical.

### Lemma 6: The Gaussian action extends to a bounded operator

There is a numerical \(c>0\) such that

\[
\Pr(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}>10)\le 2e^{-cn}.
\tag{5}
\]

For completeness, take \(1/4\)-nets of both Euclidean unit spheres, each of cardinality at most \(9^n\). The net inequality gives

\[
\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}
\le
2\max_{x,y\text{ in the nets}}|y^\top\Gamma_nx|.
\]

For fixed unit \(x,y\),

\[
y^\top\Gamma_nx\sim N(0,1/n),
\]

so a union bound yields

\[
\Pr(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}>10)
\le
2\cdot81^n e^{-25n/2},
\]

which is (5). Borel–Cantelli implies that, almost surely,

\[
\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}\le10
\quad\text{for all sufficiently large }n.
\tag{6}
\]

For \(v\in\mathcal D_R\), define provisionally

\[
\Gamma_0 Z^v:=Z^{\Gamma v}.
\tag{7}
\]

This is well-defined on \(L^2\)-equivalence classes. If \(Z^v=Z^{\widetilde v}\) in \(L^2\), Lemma 1 and (6) give

\[
\begin{aligned}
\|Z^{\Gamma v}-Z^{\Gamma\widetilde v}\|_2^2
&=
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\|\Gamma_n(v_n-\widetilde v_n)\|_n^2\\
&\le
100\lim_{n\to\infty}
\|v_n-\widetilde v_n\|_n^2\\
&=
100\|Z^v-Z^{\widetilde v}\|_2^2=0.
\end{aligned}
\]

Exact finite-\(n\) linearity, followed by Lemma 1 applied to squared differences, proves that \(\Gamma_0\) is linear. The same calculation gives

\[
\|\Gamma_0v\|_{\mathcal H_L}
\le10\|v\|_{\mathcal H_R}.
\]

Density therefore gives a unique bounded extension

\[
\Gamma_\infty:\mathcal H_R\longrightarrow\mathcal H_L,
\qquad
\|\Gamma_\infty\|\le10.
\tag{8}
\]

Similarly, the formal reverse action extends to a bounded map

\[
\Gamma_\infty^\dagger:\mathcal H_L\longrightarrow\mathcal H_R.
\]

For \(v\in\mathcal D_R,w\in\mathcal D_L\), the product test is pseudo-Lipschitz, so Lemma 1 and exact finite-\(n\) duality give

\[
\begin{aligned}
\langle\Gamma_\infty v,w\rangle_{\mathcal H_L}
&=
\lim_n\langle\Gamma_nv_n,w_n\rangle_n\\
&=
\lim_n\langle v_n,\Gamma_n^\top w_n\rangle_n\\
&=
\langle v,\Gamma_\infty^\dagger w\rangle_{\mathcal H_R}.
\end{aligned}
\]

Density implies

\[
\Gamma_\infty^\dagger=\Gamma_\infty^*.
\tag{9}
\]

Thus the master laws determine a genuine bounded two-sided operator action, not merely separate coordinate laws.

## Identification of every rational-mesh Euler state

### Lemma 7: Master-law Euler recursion

Fix \(\delta\in\mathbb Q_{>0}\). On the probability spaces above, set

\[
X_0=\Theta(U),\qquad C_0=0,\qquad Q_0=0.
\]

For \(k\ge0\), define

\[
H_k=\Psi(X_k),
\qquad
S_k=(\Gamma_\infty+Q_k)H_k,
\]

\[
G_k=\arctan S_k,
\qquad
D_k=C_kq(S_k),
\]

\[
F_k=\mathbb E[C_kG_k],
\qquad
e_k=F_k-1,
\]

and

\[
X_{k+1}
=
X_k-2\delta e_k(\Gamma_\infty+Q_k)^*D_k,
\]

\[
C_{k+1}=C_k-2\delta e_kG_k,
\]

\[
Q_{k+1}
=
Q_k-2\delta e_kD_k\otimes H_k,
\tag{10}
\]

where

\[
(D\otimes H)v=D\,\mathbb E[Hv].
\]

Each \(Q_k\) is finite-rank and trace-class.

For every \(k\), the Tensor-Program master variables of the finite-width recursion (1) equal the fields in (10).

The proof is induction. At step \(k\), (3) and the Moment rule give

\[
Z^{S_k}
=
\Gamma_\infty H_k
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_rD_r\,\mathbb E[H_rH_k]
=
(\Gamma_\infty+Q_k)H_k.
\]

Similarly, (4) and (9) give

\[
Z^{(\Gamma_n+Q_{k,n})^\top D_{k,n}}
=
(\Gamma_\infty+Q_k)^*D_k.
\]

The Nonlin rule identifies \(H_k,G_k,D_k,X_{k+1},C_{k+1}\), while the Moment rule identifies \(F_k,e_k\). This proves the induction.

There is no scalar or pseudoinverse mismatch: the pseudoinverse occurs only inside the theorem’s proof/master-law description, and Theorem E.15 covers singular limiting covariance matrices.

## Convergence topology

Enumerate all pairs \((\mathbf v_r,p_r)\), where \(\mathbf v_r\) is a finite tuple of same-sort universal probe variables and \(p_r\in\mathbb N\). Let

\[
\mu_{n,r}
=
\frac1n\sum_{\alpha=1}^n
\delta_{(v^1_{n,\alpha},\ldots,v^{m_r}_{n,\alpha})},
\]

and let \(\mu_r\) be the law of

\[
(Z^{v^1},\ldots,Z^{v^{m_r}}).
\]

Enumerate the generated scalars as \((\theta_j)\). Define

\[
d_{\rm act}
=
\sum_{r\ge1}2^{-r}
\bigl(1\wedge W_{p_r}(\mu_{n,r},\mu_r)\bigr)
+
\sum_{j\ge1}2^{-j}
\bigl(1\wedge|\theta_{j,n}-\theta_j|\bigr).
\tag{11}
\]

For each fixed tuple, Lemma 1 gives weak convergence using a countable bounded-Lipschitz determining class. It also gives

\[
\int\|x\|^{p_r}\,d\mu_{n,r}
\longrightarrow
\int\|x\|^{p_r}\,d\mu_r,
\]

because \(x\mapsto\|x\|^{p_r}\) is pseudo-Lipschitz. Weak convergence plus convergence of the \(p_r\)-moment implies \(W_{p_r}\)-convergence. Truncating the series (11) then proves

\[
d_{\rm act}\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}
\tag{12}
\]

for every fixed rational-mesh finite horizon.

This topology determines:

- every forward and adjoint action on the dense cylinder domain;
- all finite joint laws and all polynomial moments of every generated field;
- \(X_k,C_k,H_k,S_k,G_k,D_k\);
- \(F_k,e_k\), output and loss;
- all kernel blocks obtained from their squared-norm Moment variables;
- every finite-rank learned operator \(Q_k\).

For the last point, \(Q_k\) is determined by finitely many factors \(D_r,H_r,e_r\). Its trace norm is a continuous function of their two finite Gram matrices and coefficient matrix, so those trace norms also converge, although no cross-width operator subtraction is asserted.

## Conclusion

There is no unrepairable Tensor-Program hypothesis mismatch. The fixed-mesh width limit is rigorous once the probability realization and bounded action lemmas above are supplied. What this package does not provide—and what G.4 cannot provide—is continuous-time or growing-depth convergence. Mesh removal must be obtained separately from width-uniform analytic stability and Euler-error estimates.
