Payload:
Conditional on a valid fixed-mesh two-sided master theorem and a valid pointed-action limit, the probabilistic/path part can be repaired completely. I found no further fatal gap in that portion.

The essential proof is:

1. On events of probability tending to one, uniformly bound \(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}\), \(\|C_n\|_n\), \(\|C_n\|_\infty\), \(|e_n|\), \(\|Q_n\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\), \(\|M_n\|_{\rm op}\), and \(\|M_n^*D_n\|_n\) on every fixed \([0,T]\).

2. Use the exact natural-coordinate defect and perturbed-Euler Grönwall to obtain

   \[
   \sup_{t\le T}d(Y_n^{\rm GD}(t),Y_n^{\rm flow}(t))
   =O_{\mathbb P}(n^{-3/2}).
   \]

3. For a fixed coarse Euler mesh, apply the fixed-program result to the joint tuple

   \[
   (U,H,S,C,D,P),\qquad P=M^*D.
   \]

   Flow/Euler stability and a triangle argument then give uniform-time joint empirical \(W_2\) convergence of this tuple.

4. Prove square uniform integrability of \(P\) in probability with

   \[
   \operatorname{Tail}_R(P)
   \le
   2\operatorname{Tail}_{R/2}(P^\delta)
   +4\|P-P^\delta\|_2^2.
   \]

   Fixed-\(\delta\) \(p>2\) moments handle the first term; the second is \(O(\delta^2)\). This justifies passing \(q(U)P\) and \(q(U)^2P\) in \(L^2\).

5. Pass the reused-operator term through

   \[
   \|Mp-M^\delta p^\delta\|_2
   \le
   \|M-M^\delta\|_{\rm op}\|p\|_2
   +
   \|M^\delta\|_{\rm op}\|p-p^\delta\|_2,
   \qquad p=q(U)^2P.
   \]

   This establishes the second-layer velocity field.

6. Compute exact polygonal-GD velocities a.e.:

   \[
   \dot u=-2e_kq(u_k)P_k,
   \]

   \[
   \dot s=-2e_k\left[
   D_k\langle H_k,h(t)\rangle_n
   +
   M(t)\bigl(q(u(t))q(u_k)P_k\bigr)
   \right].
   \]

   The same uniform-integrability argument proves convergence to the flow velocities in \(L^2([0,T];H_n)\), and hence \(V_n^{(\ell)2}\to V^{(\ell)2}\) in \(L^1(0,T)\).

7. Fixed-mesh joint \(W_2\) convergence of hidden values pushes through piecewise-linear interpolation. Inequality (22) gives flow/Euler path error \(O(\delta)\) in mean squared sup norm. For exact GD versus flow, it gives

   \[
   O\!\left(\frac{T}{\eta}n^{-3}+\eta\right)=O(n^{-1}).
   \]

   Thus both empirical hidden path laws converge in probability in \(W_2(C([0,T]))\), which supplies tightness and path-second-moment uniform integrability.

8. The preceding joint convergence yields uniform convergence of every kernel block, output, and loss. The small-time expansions then establish all positivity, displacement, nonlinearity, kernel-movement, and loss-decrease properties simultaneously for every \(0<T<T_*\), after choosing one sufficiently small \(T_*>0\).

The remaining serious uncertainties are external to this repaired lifting argument:

- whether the cited Tensor-Program theorem really has the exact transpose-reuse, degeneracy, moment, and fixed-program conclusions claimed;
- whether the proposed pointed Gaussian action is rigorously constructed and identified by that theorem;
- whether the topology is restricted to the necessary recursively generated bounded/truncated probes. The submitted topology containing arbitrary polynomial products is not justified by these \(L^2\) estimates.

Thus the convergence/path/positivity portion is salvageable, but it does not by itself validate the overall affirmative theorem.

Payload:
Below is a deterministic analytic core that is valid both on the finite normalized spaces and on the repaired probability-operator limit. It also identifies what this core does not prove.

## 1. Normalized finite-width dynamics

Let

\[
\langle v,w\rangle_n=\frac1n v^\top w,\qquad
\|v\|_n^2=\langle v,v\rangle_n,
\]

and regard matrices as operators on this normalized Hilbert space. Their operator, Hilbert–Schmidt, and trace norms are the usual singular-value norms. Define

\[
(a\otimes_n b)v=a\langle b,v\rangle_n=\frac1n ab^\top v.
\]

Then

\[
\|a\otimes_n b\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
=\|a\|_n\|b\|_n.
\]

Set

\[
u=W^{(1)},\qquad M=W^{(2)},\qquad c=nW^{(3)},
\]

\[
h=\arctan u,\qquad s=Mh,\qquad g=\arctan s,
\]

\[
q(r)=\frac1{1+r^2},\qquad d=c\odot q(s),
\]

\[
f=\langle c,g\rangle_n,\qquad e=f-1.
\]

Direct differentiation gives

\[
\nabla_u f=\frac1n q(u)\odot M^\top d,\qquad
\nabla_Mf=\frac1n dh^\top,\qquad
\nabla_{W^{(3)}}f=g.
\]

For

\[
\lambda_1=n,\qquad \lambda_2=1,\qquad \lambda_3=\frac1n,
\]

one exact GD step of duration \(\eta=\eta_{0,n}\) is therefore

\[
u^+=u-2\eta e\,q(u)\odot M^\top d,
\tag{1}
\]

\[
M^+=M-2\eta e\,d\otimes_n h,
\tag{2}
\]

\[
c^+=c-2\eta e\,g.
\tag{3}
\]

The corresponding continuous finite-width flow is given by the same right-hand sides with \((v^+-v)/\eta\) replaced by \(\dot v\).

The kernel blocks are exactly

\[
K_n^{(1)}
=\|q(u)\odot M^\top d\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(2)}
=\|h\|_n^2\|d\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(3)}
=\|g\|_n^2.
\]

Consequently, along the continuous flow,

\[
\dot f=-2eK_n,\qquad
\frac d{dt}e^2=-4e^2K_n.
\tag{4}
\]

All normalizations here are exact.

## 2. The transformed first-layer coordinate

Let

\[
\Theta(r)=r+\frac{r^3}{3},\qquad J=\Theta^{-1},\qquad x=\Theta(u).
\]

Since

\[
\Theta'(r)=1+r^2=q(r)^{-1},
\]

the continuous equation becomes

\[
\dot x=-2eM^\top d.
\tag{5}
\]

Define

\[
\Psi(x)=\arctan J(x).
\]

The useful global bounds are

\[
|\,\arctan r\,|\le a:=\frac\pi2,
\qquad
\operatorname{Lip}(\arctan)=1,
\]

\[
\operatorname{Lip}(J)\le1,
\qquad
\Psi'(x)=q(J(x))^2,
\qquad
\operatorname{Lip}(\Psi)\le1,
\]

\[
\operatorname{Lip}(q)
=\sup_r\frac{2|r|}{(1+r^2)^2}
=\frac{3\sqrt3}{8},
\tag{6}
\]

and

\[
\sup_r |r|q(r)^2=\frac{3\sqrt3}{16}.
\tag{7}
\]

Write \(M=\Gamma+Q\), where \(\Gamma=M(0)\). In the variables \(Y=(x,c,Q)\),

\[
H=\Psi(x),\quad
M=\Gamma+Q,\quad
S=MH,\quad
G=\arctan S,\quad
D=Cq(S),
\]

\[
F=\langle C,G\rangle,\qquad e=F-1,
\]

the vector field is

\[
\mathcal V_x=-2eM^*D,\qquad
\mathcal V_C=-2eG,\qquad
\mathcal V_Q=-2eD\otimes H.
\tag{8}
\]

Here and below the same formulas apply on normalized finite spaces and on the limiting probability spaces.

## 3. Continuum operator ODE

Let

\[
\mathcal H_R=L^2(\Omega_R,\mathbb P_R),\qquad
\mathcal H_L=L^2(\Omega_L,\mathbb P_L),
\]

and let \(\Gamma:\mathcal H_R\to\mathcal H_L\) be bounded. Take

\[
X\in\mathcal H_R,\qquad
C\in\mathcal H_L\cap L^\infty,\qquad
Q\in\mathfrak S_1(\mathcal H_R,\mathcal H_L).
\]

The state norm is

\[
\rho(Y,\widetilde Y)
=
\|X-\widetilde X\|_2+
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2+
\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}.
\tag{9}
\]

Suppose

\[
\|\Gamma\|_{\rm op}\le B,\qquad
\|Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1},\|\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\le R,
\]

\[
\|C\|_\infty,\|\widetilde C\|_\infty\le c_*.
\]

Put

\[
m=B+R,\qquad E=1+ac_*,
\]

\[
s_*=m+a,
\]

\[
d_*=1+c_*\operatorname{Lip}(q)s_*,
\]

\[
e_*=a+c_*s_*,
\]

\[
r_*=md_*+c_*.
\]

Then

\[
\|H-\widetilde H\|_2\le\|X-\widetilde X\|_2,
\]

\[
\|S-\widetilde S\|_2
\le
m\|X-\widetilde X\|_2+
a\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1},
\]

\[
\|D-\widetilde D\|_2\le d_*\rho(Y,\widetilde Y),
\]

\[
|e-\widetilde e|\le e_*\rho(Y,\widetilde Y),
\]

\[
\|M^*D-\widetilde M^*\widetilde D\|_2
\le r_*\rho(Y,\widetilde Y).
\]

The rank-one estimate gives

\[
\|D\otimes H-\widetilde D\otimes\widetilde H\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
a\|D-\widetilde D\|_2+
c_*\|X-\widetilde X\|_2.
\]

Thus (8) is Lipschitz, relative to the \(L^\infty\)-bounded stratum, with for example

\[
L(B,R,c_*)
=
2Er_*+2mc_*e_*
+2Es_*+2ae_*
+2E(ad_*+c_*)+2ac_*e_*.
\tag{10}
\]

It also satisfies

\[
\|\mathcal V(Y)\|
\le
A(B,R,c_*):=
2E(mc_*+a+ac_*).
\tag{11}
\]

A direct Banach-space Picard theorem on an open \(L^2\) set is not applicable, because the Lipschitz estimate uses \(\|C\|_\infty\). The correct argument is a contraction on the closed complete path set having:

\[
\sup_{t\le\tau}\rho(Y(t),Y(0))\le r,
\qquad
\sup_{t\le\tau}\|C(t)\|_\infty\le c_*.
\]

The integral map preserves the \(L^\infty\) constraint because \(G\) is bounded. Taking \(\tau\) small enough that it preserves the state-radius constraint and \(L\tau<1\) gives a unique local solution. Any \(L^2\)-solution starting from \(C_0\in L^\infty\) automatically satisfies an \(L^\infty\) envelope from its integral equation, so uniqueness holds among all solutions of the stated regularity.

## 4. Energy identity and global existence

Let \(U=J(X)\). The pointwise/Fubini chain rule gives

\[
\dot H
=
q(U)^2\dot X
=
-2e\,q(U)^2M^*D.
\]

Since \(\dot M=\dot Q\),

\[
\dot S
=
-2e\left[
\|H\|_2^2D+
M\bigl(q(U)^2M^*D\bigr)
\right].
\]

Therefore

\[
\begin{aligned}
\dot F
&=\langle\dot C,G\rangle
+\langle C,q(S)\dot S\rangle\\
&=-2e\left[
\|G\|_2^2+
\|H\|_2^2\|D\|_2^2+
\|q(U)M^*D\|_2^2
\right].
\end{aligned}
\tag{12}
\]

Thus, with \(K\) equal to the bracket,

\[
\dot e=-2Ke,\qquad
e(t)=e(0)\exp\left(-2\int_0^tK(r)\,dr\right).
\tag{13}
\]

Let \(E_0=|e(0)|\) and \(c_0=\|C(0)\|_\infty\). Then

\[
\|C(t)\|_\infty\le c_0+2aE_0t,
\tag{14}
\]

\[
\|D(t)\|_2\le c_0+2aE_0t,
\]

\[
\|Q(t)-Q(0)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2E_0a(c_0t+aE_0t^2).
\tag{15}
\]

Consequently \(M(t)\) remains bounded on every finite interval, and

\[
\|\dot X(t)\|_2
\le
2E_0\|M(t)\|_{\rm op}
(c_0+2aE_0t).
\tag{16}
\]

These bounds exclude finite-time blowup and give global existence.

In the original \(U\)-coordinate, the equations are the Hilbert gradient equations for

\[
\mathcal L=(F-1)^2
\]

on

\[
L^2_R\times(\Gamma+\mathfrak S_2)\times L^2_L:
\]

\[
\nabla_UF=q(U)M^*D,\qquad
\nabla_MF=D\otimes H,\qquad
\nabla_CF=G.
\]

The solution remains in the stronger affine class \(\Gamma+\mathfrak S_1\).

## 5. Uniform explicit-Euler approximation

The same estimates are dimension-free. Suppose

\[
\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}\le B,\qquad
\|C_{0,n}\|_\infty\le c_0.
\]

For either explicit Euler or the \(C,Q\) components of exact GD, the bounded activation gives

\[
|e_k|\le1+a\|C_k\|_\infty
\]

and hence

\[
\|C_{k+1}\|_\infty
\le
(1+2a^2\delta)\|C_k\|_\infty+2a\delta.
\]

Thus, for \(k\delta\le T\),

\[
\|C_k\|_\infty
\le
\bar c_T:=
\left(c_0+\frac1a\right)e^{2a^2T}-\frac1a.
\tag{17}
\]

Put

\[
\bar E_T=1+a\bar c_T,
\]

\[
\bar R_T=2a\bar E_T\bar c_TT.
\tag{18}
\]

Then

\[
\|Q_k\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\le\bar R_T,
\qquad
\|M_k\|_{\rm op}\le B+\bar R_T.
\tag{19}
\]

Therefore all exact flows and all Euler iterates with sufficiently small \(\delta\) lie in a common region on which (10)–(11) give constants \(L_T,A_T\) independent of \(n\).

Let \(Y(t)\) be the exact flow and \(Y_k^\delta\) its explicit Euler scheme. The local truncation error is

\[
\left\|
Y(t_{k+1})-Y(t_k)-\delta\mathcal V(Y(t_k))
\right\|
\le
\frac12L_TA_T\delta^2.
\]

Discrete Grönwall yields

\[
\max_{k\delta\le T}
\rho(Y(t_k),Y_k^\delta)
\le
\frac{A_T}{2}(e^{L_TT}-1)\delta.
\tag{20}
\]

Polygonal or piecewise-constant interpolation adds at most \(A_T\delta\). This proves a uniform \(C_T\delta\) finite-width Euler error and the same \(C_T\delta\) error for the limiting operator ODE.

There is no cross-width trace-norm comparison in (20); it is a within-space estimate, separately at each \(n\) and in the limit.

## 6. Exact transformed-GD defect

For exact GD let

\[
R=-2eM^\top d.
\]

Then (1) is

\[
u^+=u+\eta q(u)R.
\]

Because \(\Theta\) is cubic, coordinatewise,

\[
x^+
=
x+\eta R+
\eta^2u q(u)^2R^2+
\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(u)^3R^3.
\tag{21}
\]

Thus exact GD is a perturbed explicit-Euler scheme for (8), with defect only in the \(x\)-component:

\[
\zeta
=
\eta^2u q(u)^2R^2+
\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(u)^3R^3.
\]

Using (7),

\[
\|R^2\|_n
\le
\|R\|_\infty\|R\|_n
\le
\sqrt n\,\|R\|_n^2,
\]

\[
\|R^3\|_n
\le
\|R\|_\infty^2\|R\|_n
\le
n\|R\|_n^3.
\]

Therefore

\[
\|\zeta\|_n
\le
\frac{3\sqrt3}{16}\eta^2\sqrt n\,\|R\|_n^2
+
\frac13\eta^3n\,\|R\|_n^3.
\tag{22}
\]

From (17)–(19),

\[
\|R_k\|_n
\le
2\bar E_T(B+\bar R_T)\bar c_T
=:\bar r_T.
\tag{23}
\]

Comparing exact GD with the finite-width flow and combining the local flow truncation with (22) gives, at all grid points \(k\eta\le T\),

\[
\begin{aligned}
\rho(Y_k^{\rm GD},Y_n^{\rm flow}(k\eta))
\le e^{L_TT}T\bigg[
&\frac12L_TA_T\eta\\
&+\frac{3\sqrt3}{16}\eta\sqrt n\,\bar r_T^2
+\frac13\eta^2n\,\bar r_T^3
\bigg].
\end{aligned}
\tag{24}
\]

Hence

\[
\sup_{k\eta\le T}
\rho(Y_k^{\rm GD},Y_n^{\rm flow}(k\eta))
\le
C_T\left(\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n\right).
\tag{25}
\]

For \(\eta=n^{-2}\), this is \(O_T(n^{-3/2})\).

If the parameters themselves are polygonally interpolated, then between adjacent grid points

\[
\left\|\frac{u_{k+1}-u_k}{\eta}\right\|_n
\le\bar r_T,
\]

and the corresponding \(M,c\) slopes are also uniformly bounded. Since \(J\) is \(1\)-Lipschitz, (25) therefore extends to uniform-in-time convergence of the actual interpolated \(u,M,c\) paths, with an additional \(O_T(\eta)\) term. The original proof needed this interpolation observation.

## 7. Vanishing output initialization

Here

\[
C_{0,n}=nW^{(3)}(0)
\]

has iid \(N(0,n^{-2})\) entries. Hence

\[
\mathbb E\|C_{0,n}\|_n^2=n^{-2},
\]

and, for every fixed \(\varepsilon>0\),

\[
\Pr(\|C_{0,n}\|_\infty>\varepsilon)
\le
2n\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^2n^2}{2}\right).
\tag{26}
\]

Thus \(C_{0,n}\to0\) in both normalized \(L^2\) and \(L^\infty\), in probability. Comparing the finite flow initialized at \(C_{0,n}\) with the one initialized at zero, the Lipschitz estimate gives

\[
\sup_{t\le T}\rho(Y_n^{C_{0,n}}(t),Y_n^0(t))
\le
e^{L_TT}\|C_{0,n}\|_n.
\tag{27}
\]

Therefore replacing the vanishing finite initialization by the limiting \(C_0=0\) is justified.

## 8. Deterministic diagonal bridge

For each fixed auxiliary mesh \(\delta\),

\[
\begin{aligned}
d_{\rm act}(Y_n^{\rm flow},Y^{\rm flow})
\le{}&
d_{\rm act}(Y_n^{\rm flow},Y_n^\delta)\\
&+d_{\rm act}(Y_n^\delta,Y^\delta)
+d_{\rm act}(Y^\delta,Y^{\rm flow}).
\end{aligned}
\]

The first and third terms are \(O_T(\delta)\) by (20). The middle term is the fixed-finite-program probabilistic step. Sending \(n\to\infty\) first and then \(\delta\downarrow0\) proves flow convergence, provided that fixed-program step and the chosen action topology have been established.

Equation (25) then transfers this convergence to exact GD along the single diagonal \(\eta=n^{-2}\). This is a genuine uniform joint-diagonal estimate, not an uncontrolled composition of a width limit and a later gradient-flow limit.

## 9. Restartability

Use the current middle operator \(M_t\), not its historical decomposition \(\Gamma+Q_t\), as part of the state. At a restart time \(\tau\), solve in the affine space

\[
M_\tau+\mathfrak S_1
\]

with initial state \((X_\tau,C_\tau,M_\tau)\). Equations (8) depend only on that current state. Under Koopman isomorphisms of the two probability spaces intertwining \(M_\tau\), all Nemytskii maps, expectations, adjoints, and rank-one updates are equivariant. Uniqueness therefore makes the evolution well-defined on operator-law isomorphism classes.

## Remaining gaps

I find no fatal defect in this deterministic analytic core after the \(L^\infty\)-envelope and interpolation corrections above. It does not itself prove:

- applicability of the cited Tensor-Program theorem to every fixed auxiliary Euler program;
- construction and admissibility of the full operator-action law;
- convergence of fixed-mesh empirical action laws;
- uniform integrability and Wasserstein path convergence;
- passage of every kernel and velocity observable.

Those are probabilistic lifting obligations. They cannot be inferred from the deterministic estimates alone.

Second-round verdict:

- Uniform-time convergence in the originally defined \(d_{\rm act}\): not proved and generally not implied by the available \(L^2\) estimates. Arbitrary products of unbounded probes are the obstruction. Replace it with a countable required-probe topology generated by \(M,M^*\), the actual bounded coordinate maps and multipliers, the bounded current \(C\), and separately recorded \(L^2\) norms. Then truncate the metric to finitely many probes, choose the auxiliary mesh, and finally send \(n\to\infty\).

- Square uniform integrability of \(P=M^*D\): valid after writing the probability quantifiers explicitly. For piecewise-constant coarse Euler \(P^\delta\),

  \[
  \sup_t\langle P^2\mathbf1_{\{|P|>R\}}\rangle_n
  \le
  2(2/R)^{p-2}\max_k\langle|P_k^\delta|^p\rangle_n
  +4C_T^2\delta^2.
  \]

  Choose \(\delta\), then \(R\), then \(n\). This proves the conventional asymptotic-UI statement and transfers UI to fine Euler and exact-GD fields.

- Passage of \(M(q(U)^2P)\): valid. First prove \(p-p^\delta\to0\) for \(p=q(U)^2P\) using truncation and \(P\)-UI, then use

  \[
  \|Mp-M^\delta p^\delta\|_2
  \le
  \|M-M^\delta\|_{\rm op}\|p\|_2+
  \|M^\delta\|_{\rm op}\|p-p^\delta\|_2.
  \]

- Exact polygonal-GD second-layer velocity: the formula is exact:

  \[
  \dot s=
  -2e_k\left[
  D_k\langle H_k,h(t)\rangle_n+
  M(t)\{q(u(t))q(u_k)P_k\}
  \right].
  \]

  State convergence, intra-step \(O(\eta)\) motion, operator bounds, and \(P\)-UI give \(L^2\)-in-time convergence to the flow velocity. Consequently \(V_n^{(2)2}\to V^{(2)2}\) in \(L^1(0,T)\). The same proof handles \(V^{(1)}\).

- Hidden path \(W_2(C([0,T]))\): valid. Fixed-mesh joint \(W_2\) convergence pushes through linear interpolation. Inequality (22) gives flow/Euler squared sup-path error \(O(\delta)\), while exact-GD/flow error is

  \[
  O\!\left((T/\eta)n^{-3}+\eta\right)=O(n^{-1}).
  \]

  This supplies path tightness and second-moment uniform integrability.

- Small-time positivity: valid. The strong expansions have strictly positive leading coefficients. Choose one \(T_*>0\) so that all signs, positive variances, and positive regression residuals hold simultaneously for every \(0<t<T_*\). Then every \(0<T<T_*\) satisfies the required integral, displacement, kernel-movement, nonlinearity, and loss conditions.

Thus the probabilistic/path/kernel argument can be completed. The remaining topology must not be advertised as controlling arbitrary polynomial products. Apart from that correction, the unresolved issues lie in the external fixed-program theorem and the pointed-action construction, not in these six lifting steps.

No fatal flaw emerged. The architecture is sound after four minimal corrections.

1. Use either two separate Kolmogorov marginal spaces or one common realization with two sub-\(\sigma\)-fields. If using one space, never use cross-sort pointwise products: the dynamics only uses expectations within each sort and the operator pairing. The operator-domain class must contain a countable dense family of bounded-Lipschitz cylinder functions, not merely the linear span of named coordinate probes. For example, \(\operatorname{span}\{1,Z\}\) is not dense in \(L^2\) of a Gaussian variable, so omitting cylinder closure would be a real counterexample.

2. State projective consistency as a lemma proved from finite-\(n\) identity and uniqueness of deterministic limits. Although the symbolic \(Z^{\rm dot}\) decomposition can change when unused computations are inserted earlier, the actual vector and all its joint empirical statistics remain identical. Applying G.4 to both finite writings forces the same limiting law. Only finite prefixes and a countable determining test family are used; no infinite pseudoinverse arises.

3. Encode the width-dependent readout exactly as

\[
C_{0,n}=a_nR_n,\qquad a_n=1/n,\qquad R_n\sim N(0,I_n).
\]

Setup G.2 permits \(a_n\to0\), and \((a,r)\mapsto ar\) is jointly pseudo-Lipschitz. For every fixed mesh and finite horizon, G.4 applies to \(\|z\|^p\) for each integer \(p\ge1\). A countable intersection gives simultaneous moment convergence. Countable bounded-Lipschitz convergence gives weak convergence, and weak convergence plus convergence of the \(p\)-th radial moment gives \(W_p\), particularly \(W_2\). This says nothing uniform in the mesh.

4. Remove construction artifacts from the restart state:

- The Gaussian \(R\) used to encode the vanishing readout is auxiliary and disappears when \(a_n\to0\); marginalize it out of the limiting action.
- Store \(Q_t\) only as the current trace-class operator, not as its historical rank-one decomposition.
- Treat \(U_0\), the universal program syntax, and past Euler probes only as proof devices, not named state components.

The restart state should be the isomorphism class

\[
(\mathcal H_R,\mathcal H_L,\Gamma;\,X_t,C_t,Q_t),
\]

or its cyclic reduction under \(\Gamma,\Gamma^*,Q_t,Q_t^*\) and the required coordinate maps. This contains the current pointed fields and the genuinely necessary quenched edge action, but no past trajectory. Given this state, the next Euler step and the limiting vector field are autonomous.

Thus the exact minimal corrections are: dense cylinder closure, an explicit projective-consistency lemma, exact scalar encoding of \(C_{0,n}\), and removal of \(R\) and past rank-one factors from the final state. With those changes, none of the four red-team targets falsifies the construction.

The deterministic core survives a second hostile audit. I found no fatal scaling, norm, chain-rule, stability, or diagonal-limit error. The following qualifications are essential.

## 1. Banach-space well-posedness is valid only with the envelope argument

The vector field is well-defined already for \(C\in L^2\), because \(q(S)\) is bounded. It is not locally Lipschitz on an open \(L^2\)-neighborhood: the estimate for

\[
D=Cq(S)
\]

requires an \(L^\infty\) bound on one readout field. The correct contraction space is therefore

\[
C\!\left([0,\tau];
L^2_R\times L^2_L\times\mathfrak S_1
\right)
\]

intersected with

\[
\sup_{t\le\tau}\|C(t)\|_\infty\le B_C.
\]

This subset is closed in the uniform \(L^2/L^2/\mathfrak S_1\) metric: for each fixed \(t\), an \(L^2\)-limit of functions bounded by \(B_C\) is also bounded by \(B_C\) almost everywhere. The integral map preserves the envelope because

\[
|\dot C|\le 2|e|\,|\arctan S|
\le 2a(1+aB_C),\qquad a=\pi/2.
\]

The local Lipschitz estimates then close exactly. Moreover, any solution starting from \(C_0\in L^\infty\) automatically has such an envelope from its integral equation, so uniqueness is not restricted to a specially selected class.

Restartability is therefore certified for actual trajectory states, all of which have \(C_\tau\in L^\infty\). It has not been proved for arbitrary restarts with only \(C_\tau\in L^2\), nor is that needed.

## 2. The energy chain rule is valid, but should not be justified by a false Nemytskii claim

A nonlinear Nemytskii map is generally not Fréchet differentiable \(L^2\to L^2\). That fact does not invalidate the energy calculation.

The constructed solution is \(C^1\) into \(L^2_R\times L^2_L\times\mathfrak S_1\). Since

\[
X(t)=X(0)+\int_0^t\dot X(s)\,ds,
\]

Fubini gives, after choosing representatives, an absolutely continuous scalar path \(t\mapsto X(t,\omega)\) for almost every \(\omega\). The ordinary scalar chain rule then yields

\[
\frac d{dt}\Psi(X)
=
\Psi'(X)\dot X
=
q(U)^2\dot X
\]

almost everywhere, with the derivative in \(L^1_tL^2_\omega\) because \(|\Psi'|\le1\). The same argument applies to \(G=\arctan S\). The operator product rule for \(S=MH\) is valid because \(M\) is absolutely continuous in \(\mathfrak S_1\), hence in operator norm, and \(H\) is absolutely continuous in \(L^2\).

Thus

\[
\dot F=-2e\left(
\|G\|_2^2+
\|H\|_2^2\|D\|_2^2+
\|q(U)M^*D\|_2^2
\right)
\]

is rigorous. All signs and factors are correct.

## 3. Continuous global bounds pass every factor check

Writing \(E_0=|e(0)|\) and \(c_0=\|C(0)\|_\infty\),

\[
|e(t)|\le E_0,
\]

\[
\|C(t)\|_\infty\le c_0+2aE_0t,
\]

\[
\|\dot Q(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2E_0a(c_0+2aE_0t),
\]

and hence

\[
\|Q(t)-Q(0)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2E_0a(c_0t+aE_0t^2).
\]

No factor of \(2\), \(n\), or \(a\) is missing. These estimates, together with

\[
\|\dot X(t)\|_2
\le
2E_0\|M(t)\|_{\rm op}(c_0+2aE_0t),
\]

give global continuation.

## 4. Explicit-Euler stability is dimension-free

For either explicit Euler or the \(C,Q\) components of exact GD,

\[
\|C_{k+1}\|_\infty
\le
(1+2a^2\delta)\|C_k\|_\infty+2a\delta.
\]

Therefore

\[
\|C_k\|_\infty
\le
\left(c_0+\frac1a\right)e^{2a^2k\delta}-\frac1a.
\]

The resulting trace-class estimate

\[
\|Q_k-Q_0\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2a\,\bar E_T\bar C_T\,T
\]

is also correct. These bounds do not use a discrete energy inequality, which exact GD does not possess automatically.

On the resulting common bounded region, the exact flow–Euler error is \(O_T(\delta)\) in

\[
\|X-\widetilde X\|_2+
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2+
\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}.
\]

This is a within-space estimate. It must not be interpreted as a trace-norm comparison between operators acting on different widths.

## 5. The transformed exact-GD defect and numerical constants are correct

The layer scalings give exactly

\[
u^+=u-2\eta e\,q(u)M^\top d,
\]

\[
M^+=M-2\eta e\,d\otimes_n h,
\]

\[
c^+=c-2\eta e\,g.
\]

With

\[
R=-2eM^\top d,
\]

the exact cubic identity is

\[
\Theta(u+\eta q(u)R)
=
\Theta(u)+\eta R
+\eta^2u q(u)^2R^2
+\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(u)^3R^3.
\]

The numerical extrema are

\[
\operatorname{Lip}(q)=\frac{3\sqrt3}{8},
\qquad
\sup_u |u|q(u)^2=\frac{3\sqrt3}{16}.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\|\zeta_k\|_n
\le
\frac{3\sqrt3}{16}\eta^2\sqrt n\,\|R_k\|_n^2
+
\frac13\eta^3n\,\|R_k\|_n^3.
\]

After \(T/\eta\) steps this produces

\[
O_T(\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n).
\]

Adding the ordinary Euler truncation gives

\[
O_T(\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n).
\]

For \(\eta=n^{-2}\), the three terms are

\[
n^{-2},\qquad n^{-3/2},\qquad n^{-3},
\]

so the exact-GD/flow error is \(O_T(n^{-3/2})\). The macroscopic step conditions also hold:

\[
\eta\lambda_1=n^{-1},\qquad
\eta\lambda_2=n^{-2},\qquad
\eta\lambda_3=n^{-3}.
\]

The actual polygonal interpolation of \(u,M,c\) differs from its grid values by \(O_T(\eta)\), because all three discrete slopes are uniformly bounded in their respective normalized norms. Thus the node estimate extends to the requested interpolated paths.

## 6. The kernel and gradient normalizations are correct

The finite gradients satisfy

\[
\nabla_u f=\frac1n q(u)M^\top d,\qquad
\nabla_Mf=\frac1n dh^\top,\qquad
\nabla_{W^{(3)}}f=g.
\]

Hence

\[
\lambda_1\|\nabla_uf\|_{\ell^2}^2
=
\|q(u)M^\top d\|_n^2,
\]

\[
\lambda_2\|\nabla_Mf\|_F^2
=
\|H\|_n^2\|D\|_n^2,
\]

\[
\lambda_3\|\nabla_{W^{(3)}}f\|_{\ell^2}^2
=
\|G\|_n^2.
\]

There is no missing \(n\)-factor.

## 7. It is genuinely a Hilbert gradient flow, with one important metric distinction

The gradient-flow coordinates are the original \(U,M,C\), not the transformed \(X\). The relevant Hilbert space is

\[
L^2_R\times(\Gamma+\mathfrak S_2)\times L^2_L.
\]

The middle-layer gradient metric is Hilbert–Schmidt. The trace norm is a stronger regularity and convergence norm used to prove closure; it is not the gradient metric.

For

\[
F(U,M,C)
=
\left\langle
C,\arctan(M\arctan U)
\right\rangle_L,
\]

the Fréchet derivatives are

\[
\nabla_UF=q(U)M^*D,
\qquad
\nabla_MF=D\otimes H,
\qquad
\nabla_CF=G.
\]

Although \(U\mapsto\arctan U\) need not be Fréchet \(L^2\to L^2\), the scalar functional \(F\) is Fréchet differentiable. The required remainder estimate follows by truncation: for fixed \(P\in L^2\), the pointwise Taylor remainder \(r_h\) obeys

\[
|r_h|\le 2|h|,
\]

and, splitting into \(\{|h|\le\delta\}\) and \(\{|h|>\delta\}\),

\[
\frac{|\langle P,r_h\rangle|}{\|h\|_2}
\le
\omega(\delta)\|P\|_2
+
2\|P\,1_{\{|h|>\delta\}}\|_2.
\]

For fixed \(\delta\), the second term vanishes as \(\|h\|_2\to0\); then \(\delta\downarrow0\). Applying this to the inner and outer activations proves the displayed derivative formulas.

Therefore

\[
\dot U=-\nabla_U(F-1)^2,\qquad
\dot M=-\nabla_M(F-1)^2,\qquad
\dot C=-\nabla_C(F-1)^2.
\]

Moreover,

\[
\|\nabla F\|^2
=
K^{(1)}+K^{(2)}+K^{(3)},
\]

so the energy identity is the genuine Hilbert gradient-flow dissipation identity.

Final verdict: no fatal defect remains in the deterministic analytic portion after explicitly using the closed \(L^\infty\)-envelope contraction, the pointwise/Fubini chain rule, the Hilbert–Schmidt gradient metric, and the interpolation correction. The unresolved risks remain in the probabilistic/action-law lifting, not in these deterministic estimates.

Yes. The scalar output is Fréchet differentiable on the full Hilbert affine space

\[
\mathcal X
=
L^2_R\times(\Gamma+\mathfrak S_2(\mathcal H_R,\mathcal H_L))
\times L^2_L,
\]

not merely at \(C\in L^2\cap L^\infty\). Consequently, the IDE is genuinely a Hilbert gradient flow in the original \(U,M,C\) coordinates.

Let

\[
\phi=\arctan,\qquad q=\phi',
\]

\[
H=\phi(U),\qquad S=MH,\qquad G=\phi(S),\qquad D=Cq(S),
\]

\[
F(U,M,C)=\langle C,G\rangle_L.
\]

This is defined for every \(C\in L^2_L\), because \(G\) is bounded.

## Scalar truncation lemma

Let \((\Omega,\mu)\) be a probability space, let \(\varphi\in C^1(\mathbb R)\), and suppose \(\varphi'\) is bounded and uniformly continuous. For fixed \(z,p\in L^2(\Omega)\), define

\[
R_z(h)
=
\varphi(z+h)-\varphi(z)-\varphi'(z)h.
\]

Then

\[
\langle p,R_z(h)\rangle=o(\|h\|_2)
\qquad
\text{as }\|h\|_2\to0.
\tag{1}
\]

Indeed, let

\[
\omega(\delta)
=
\sup_{\substack{x\in\mathbb R\\ |t|\le\delta}}
|\varphi'(x+t)-\varphi'(x)|.
\]

Uniform continuity gives \(\omega(\delta)\to0\). On \(\{|h|\le\delta\}\), the integral form of the scalar remainder gives

\[
|R_z(h)|\le\omega(\delta)|h|.
\]

If \(L=\|\varphi'\|_\infty\), then globally

\[
|R_z(h)|\le2L|h|.
\]

Hence, with \(A_h=\{|h|>\delta\}\),

\[
\begin{aligned}
|\langle p,R_z(h)\rangle|
&\le
\omega(\delta)\int |ph|
+
2L\int_{A_h}|ph|\\
&\le
\left[
\omega(\delta)\|p\|_2+
2L\|p1_{A_h}\|_2
\right]\|h\|_2.
\end{aligned}
\]

For fixed \(\delta>0\),

\[
\mu(A_h)\le\frac{\|h\|_2^2}{\delta^2}\longrightarrow0.
\]

Absolute continuity of the integral of \(p^2\) therefore implies

\[
\|p1_{A_h}\|_2\longrightarrow0.
\]

After dividing by \(\|h\|_2\), first let \(h\to0\), then \(\delta\downarrow0\). This proves (1).

For \(\varphi=\arctan\), the hypotheses hold because \(q\) is bounded and globally Lipschitz.

## Joint Fréchet expansion

Fix \((U,M,C)\in\mathcal X\). Let the perturbation be

\[
(h,B,k)\in
L^2_R\times\mathfrak S_2\times L^2_L,
\]

and put

\[
\varepsilon
=
\|h\|_2+\|B\|_{\mathfrak S_2}+\|k\|_2.
\]

For the inner activation, write

\[
\phi(U+h)
=
H+q(U)h+r_h.
\tag{2}
\]

Since \(\phi\) is \(1\)-Lipschitz,

\[
\|\phi(U+h)-H\|_2\le\|h\|_2,
\qquad
\|r_h\|_2\le2\|h\|_2.
\tag{3}
\]

The perturbed second preactivation is

\[
\begin{aligned}
S'
&=(M+B)\phi(U+h)\\
&=S+M(q(U)h)+BH
+\underbrace{Mr_h+B(\phi(U+h)-H)}_{=:W}.
\end{aligned}
\]

Thus

\[
\Delta S:=S'-S=L_1+W,
\]

where

\[
L_1=M(q(U)h)+BH
\]

is the linear part. Since

\[
\|B\|_{\rm op}\le\|B\|_{\mathfrak S_2},
\qquad
\|H\|_2\le\pi/2,
\]

we have

\[
\|\Delta S\|_2=O(\varepsilon).
\tag{4}
\]

Expand the outer activation:

\[
\phi(S+\Delta S)
=
G+q(S)\Delta S+r_S(\Delta S).
\tag{5}
\]

Using \(D=Cq(S)\),

\[
\begin{aligned}
F(U+h,M+B,C+k)-F(U,M,C)
={}&
\langle D,L_1\rangle_L+\langle k,G\rangle_L\\
&+\langle D,Mr_h\rangle_L\\
&+\langle D,B(\phi(U+h)-H)\rangle_L\\
&+\langle C,r_S(\Delta S)\rangle_L\\
&+\langle k,\phi(S+\Delta S)-G\rangle_L.
\end{aligned}
\tag{6}
\]

The first line is the candidate derivative. Each remaining term is \(o(\varepsilon)\):

1. By the truncation lemma with \(p=M^*D\),

\[
\langle D,Mr_h\rangle_L
=
\langle M^*D,r_h\rangle_R
=
o(\|h\|_2).
\]

2. By (3),

\[
\left|
\langle D,B(\phi(U+h)-H)\rangle
\right|
\le
\|D\|_2\|B\|_{\mathfrak S_2}\|h\|_2
=O(\varepsilon^2).
\]

3. By the truncation lemma applied at \(S\), with fixed \(p=C\), and using (4),

\[
\langle C,r_S(\Delta S)\rangle
=
o(\|\Delta S\|_2)
=
o(\varepsilon).
\]

4. Since \(\phi\) is \(1\)-Lipschitz,

\[
\left|
\langle k,\phi(S+\Delta S)-G\rangle
\right|
\le
\|k\|_2\|\Delta S\|_2
=O(\varepsilon^2).
\]

Therefore (6) is a genuine joint Fréchet expansion.

## Gradient

The linear part satisfies

\[
\begin{aligned}
\langle D,L_1\rangle_L
&=
\langle D,M(q(U)h)\rangle_L+\langle D,BH\rangle_L\\
&=
\langle q(U)M^*D,h\rangle_R
+
\langle D\otimes H,B\rangle_{\mathfrak S_2}.
\end{aligned}
\]

Hence

\[
\nabla_UF=q(U)M^*D,
\]

\[
\nabla_MF=D\otimes H,
\]

\[
\nabla_CF=G.
\tag{7}
\]

All three belong to their claimed Hilbert spaces:

\[
q(U)M^*D\in L^2_R,
\qquad
D\otimes H\in\mathfrak S_2,
\qquad
G\in L^2_L.
\]

The gradient is also continuous. The only nontrivial product is \(Cq(S)\): if \(C_n\to C\) and \(S_n\to S\) in \(L^2\), then

\[
\|(C_n-C)q(S_n)\|_2\le\|C_n-C\|_2,
\]

while

\[
\|C(q(S_n)-q(S))\|_2\to0
\]

by convergence in measure, boundedness of \(q(S_n)-q(S)\), and dominated convergence with weight \(C^2\). Thus \(F\in C^1(\mathcal X)\).

## Gradient-flow verdict

For

\[
\mathcal L(U,M,C)=(F(U,M,C)-1)^2,
\]

\[
\nabla\mathcal L
=
2e\bigl(q(U)M^*D,\ D\otimes H,\ G\bigr).
\]

The operator IDE in the original variables is

\[
\dot U=-2e\,q(U)M^*D,
\]

\[
\dot M=-2e\,D\otimes H,
\]

\[
\dot C=-2e\,G.
\]

Therefore

\[
(\dot U,\dot M,\dot C)=-\nabla\mathcal L.
\]

So the answer is **yes**: it is a genuine Hilbert gradient flow on

\[
L^2_R\times(\Gamma+\mathfrak S_2)\times L^2_L.
\]

The fact that \(M(t)-\Gamma\) actually remains trace class is stronger regularity. The trace norm is used for well-posedness and convergence estimates; the middle-layer gradient metric itself is Hilbert–Schmidt.

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER

Payload:
For the three-hidden-layer extension, the small-time coefficients and positivity are correct, including strict bottom-layer backpropagation through the two Gaussian operators. But the extension introduces a serious unresolved well-posedness problem.

Let

\[
h_1=\arctan U_0,\quad Z_2=\Gamma_2h_1,\quad h_2=\arctan Z_2,
\quad Z_3=\Gamma_3h_2,\quad h_3=\arctan Z_3,
\]

and set

\[
m_r=\|h_r\|_2^2,\qquad
y_3=h_3q(Z_3),\qquad
b_2=\Gamma_3^*y_3,
\]

\[
y_2=q(Z_2)b_2,\qquad
p_1=\Gamma_2^*y_2,
\]

\[
\nu_3=\|y_3\|_2^2,\qquad
\nu_2=\|y_2\|_2^2,\qquad
d_1=\|q(U_0)p_1\|_2^2.
\]

The bottom coefficient is strictly positive. Put \(v=Z_2q(Z_2)\). Since \(\Gamma_3\) is independent of the \(\Gamma_2\)-generated pair \((v,h_2)\), conditional Gaussian regression gives

\[
\langle v,b_2\rangle
=
\frac{\langle v,h_2\rangle}{m_2}
\mathbb E[Z_3\arctan(Z_3)q(Z_3)]>0.
\]

Both factors are positive because \(z\arctan(z)q(z)>0\) for \(z\ne0\). Hence

\[
\langle h_1,p_1\rangle
=
\langle Z_2,y_2\rangle
=
\langle v,b_2\rangle>0.
\]

Thus \(p_1\ne0\), and \(q(U_0)>0\) almost surely implies \(d_1>0\). This argument correctly handles the reused transpose; it does not falsely treat \(\Gamma_3^*y_3\) as independent.

Define

\[
\mathcal R_2
=
m_1I+\Gamma_2M_{q(U_0)^2}\Gamma_2^*,
\]

\[
\mathcal R_3
=
m_2I+
\Gamma_3M_{q(Z_2)}
\mathcal R_2
M_{q(Z_2)}\Gamma_3^*.
\]

Then

\[
\langle y_2,\mathcal R_2y_2\rangle=m_1\nu_2+d_1,
\]

\[
\langle y_3,\mathcal R_3y_3\rangle
=m_2\nu_3+m_1\nu_2+d_1.
\]

Both operators are strictly positive because \(\mathcal R_r\ge m_{r-1}I\).

For any sufficiently regular strong solution,

\[
U(t)=U_0+2t^2q(U_0)p_1+o_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

\[
S_2(t)=Z_2+2t^2\mathcal R_2y_2+o_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

\[
S_3(t)=Z_3+2t^2\mathcal R_3y_3+o_{L^2}(t^2).
\]

The four kernel blocks therefore satisfy

\[
K^{(1)}=4d_1t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
K^{(2)}=4m_1\nu_2t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
K^{(3)}=4m_2\nu_3t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
K^{(4)}
=
m_3+
4(d_1+m_1\nu_2+m_2\nu_3)t^2+o(t^2),
\]

and hence

\[
K(t)
=
m_3+
8(d_1+m_1\nu_2+m_2\nu_3)t^2+o(t^2).
\]

The hidden speeds are

\[
V_1^2=16d_1t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
V_2^2=16\|\mathcal R_2y_2\|_2^2t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
V_3^2=16\|\mathcal R_3y_3\|_2^2t^2+o(t^2).
\]

All leading coefficients are strictly positive. Moreover, \(F(0)=0\) and

\[
L'(0)=-4m_3<0.
\]

All three initial hidden marginals are nondegenerate Gaussians, so the affine-regression residual of \(\arctan\) is strictly positive. Wasserstein continuity preserves positive variance and residual on a common interval \((0,T_*)\).

For polygonal exact GD, during step \(k\),

\[
\dot S_2
=
-2e_k\left[
D_{2,k}\langle H_{1,k},H_1(t)\rangle+
M_2(t)\{q(U(t))q(U_k)P_{1,k}\}
\right],
\]

and

\[
\begin{aligned}
\dot S_3=-2e_k\bigl[&
D_{3,k}\langle H_{2,k},H_2(t)\rangle\\
&+M_3(t)q(S_2(t))
\{D_{2,k}\langle H_{1,k},H_1(t)\rangle
+M_2(t)(q(U(t))q(U_k)P_{1,k})\}\bigr].
\end{aligned}
\]

Passing these velocities requires an inductive uniform-integrability hierarchy for \(B_2\), \(P_1\), the \(S_2\)-velocity field, and finally the multiplier entering \(S_3\).

The fatal issue is limit well-posedness. The map

\[
(S_2,B_2)\longmapsto q(S_2)B_2
\]

is well-defined but not locally Lipschitz from \(L^2\times L^2\) to \(L^2\), because

\[
(q(S_2)-q(\widetilde S_2))\widetilde B_2
\]

is a product of two \(L^2\) quantities. Here \(B_2=\Gamma_3^*D_3\) is generally unbounded even when \(D_3\) is bounded. Uniform integrability gives sequential convergence but not Picard uniqueness or restartable well-posedness. A new \(L^p\), graph-norm, or structured uniqueness theorem is required.

Thus the coefficients and all conditional positivity claims are verified, but the three-hidden-layer extension is not yet a rigorous autonomous mean-field theorem.

Payload:
A natural three-hidden-layer scaling exists algebraically, but the two-hidden-layer proof does not rigorously extend in the stated \(L^2/\mathfrak S_1\) action topology. A new unbounded-multiplier obstruction prevents the required well-posedness and dimension-free Euler estimate. Thus I cannot certify the requested three-hidden-layer theorem.

## Exact finite-width scaling and equations

For

\[
U=W^{(1)}\in\mathbb R^n,\qquad
M_2=W^{(2)}\in\mathbb R^{n\times n},
\]

\[
M_3=W^{(3)}\in\mathbb R^{n\times n},\qquad
C=nW^{(4)}\in\mathbb R^n,
\]

define

\[
H_1=\phi(U),\qquad
S_2=M_2H_1,\qquad
H_2=\phi(S_2),
\]

\[
S_3=M_3H_2,\qquad
H_3=\phi(S_3),
\]

\[
F_n=\langle C,H_3\rangle_n,\qquad e=F_n-1,
\]

with \(\phi=\arctan\), \(q=\phi'\), and

\[
D_3=Cq(S_3),
\qquad
P_2=M_3^\top D_3,
\qquad
D_2=q(S_2)P_2.
\]

The natural initialization is

\[
\sigma_{1,n}^2=1,\qquad
\sigma_{2,n}^2=\sigma_{3,n}^2=\frac1n,\qquad
\sigma_{4,n}^2=\frac1{n^4},
\]

and the learning-rate scalers are

\[
\lambda_1=n,\qquad
\lambda_2=\lambda_3=1,\qquad
\lambda_4=\frac1n.
\]

Direct differentiation gives

\[
\nabla_UF_n
=
\frac1n q(U)M_2^\top D_2,
\]

\[
\nabla_{M_2}F_n
=
\frac1nD_2H_1^\top,
\]

\[
\nabla_{M_3}F_n
=
\frac1nD_3H_2^\top,
\]

\[
\nabla_{W^{(4)}}F_n=H_3.
\]

Thus exact GD with macroscopic step \(\eta=\eta_{0,n}\) is

\[
U^+
=
U-2\eta e\,q(U)M_2^\top D_2,
\tag{1}
\]

\[
M_2^+
=
M_2-2\eta e\,D_2\otimes_n H_1,
\tag{2}
\]

\[
M_3^+
=
M_3-2\eta e\,D_3\otimes_n H_2,
\tag{3}
\]

\[
C^+
=
C-2\eta e\,H_3.
\tag{4}
\]

Every \(n\)-factor is accounted for.

The four kernel blocks are

\[
K_n^{(1)}
=
\|q(U)M_2^\top D_2\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(2)}
=
\|D_2\|_n^2\|H_1\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(3)}
=
\|D_3\|_n^2\|H_2\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(4)}
=
\|H_3\|_n^2.
\]

For the continuous finite-width flow,

\[
\dot F_n=-2e\sum_{\ell=1}^4K_n^{(\ell)}.
\]

## Formal three-sort operator gradient flow

Let

\[
\mathcal H_1=L^2(\Omega_1),\quad
\mathcal H_2=L^2(\Omega_2),\quad
\mathcal H_3=L^2(\Omega_3),
\]

with bounded initial actions

\[
\Gamma_2:\mathcal H_1\to\mathcal H_2,\qquad
\Gamma_3:\mathcal H_2\to\mathcal H_3.
\]

Take

\[
M_2=\Gamma_2+Q_2,\qquad
M_3=\Gamma_3+Q_3,
\]

with \(Q_2,Q_3\) trace class. Using

\[
X=\Theta(U),\qquad
\Theta(u)=u+\frac{u^3}{3},
\]

the formal autonomous system is

\[
\dot X=-2eM_2^*D_2,
\tag{5}
\]

\[
\dot M_2=-2eD_2\otimes H_1,
\tag{6}
\]

\[
\dot M_3=-2eD_3\otimes H_2,
\tag{7}
\]

\[
\dot C=-2eH_3.
\tag{8}
\]

In the original \(U\)-coordinate, this is formally the negative Hilbert gradient flow of

\[
\mathcal L=(F-1)^2,
\]

on

\[
\mathcal H_1
\times(\Gamma_2+\mathfrak S_2)
\times(\Gamma_3+\mathfrak S_2)
\times\mathcal H_3.
\]

Indeed,

\[
\nabla_UF=q(U)M_2^*D_2,
\]

\[
\nabla_{M_2}F=D_2\otimes H_1,
\]

\[
\nabla_{M_3}F=D_3\otimes H_2,
\]

\[
\nabla_CF=H_3.
\]

The scalar functional is \(C^1\); the truncation argument used for two hidden layers can be iterated through both nonlinear compositions. The problem is not differentiability of \(F\), but regularity of its gradient vector field.

## Energy identity and a priori global bounds

If a sufficiently regular solution exists, then

\[
\dot F=-2eK,
\]

where

\[
K=
\|q(U)M_2^*D_2\|_2^2
+\|D_2\|_2^2\|H_1\|_2^2
+\|D_3\|_2^2\|H_2\|_2^2
+\|H_3\|_2^2.
\]

Hence \(|e(t)|\le E_0:=|e(0)|\).

Let \(a=\pi/2\) and

\[
c_T=\|C(0)\|_\infty+2aE_0T.
\]

Then

\[
\|D_3(t)\|_2\le c_T.
\]

If

\[
m_{3,T}
=
\sup_{t\le T}\|M_3(t)\|_{\rm op},
\]

then

\[
\|D_2(t)\|_2
\le
\|M_3(t)^*D_3(t)\|_2
\le m_{3,T}c_T.
\]

Moreover,

\[
\|Q_3(t)-Q_3(0)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2E_0ac_TT,
\]

so \(m_{3,T}<\infty\), and then

\[
\|Q_2(t)-Q_2(0)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2E_0a\,m_{3,T}c_TT.
\]

Finally,

\[
\|\dot X(t)\|_2
\le
2E_0\|M_2(t)\|_{\rm op}m_{3,T}c_T.
\]

Thus finite-time blowup is excluded for any solution. These are only a priori bounds; they do not establish existence or uniqueness.

## Dimension-free discrete boundedness

Explicit Euler and exact GD retain dimension-free norm bounds. As before,

\[
\|C_{k+1}\|_\infty
\le
(1+2a^2\eta)\|C_k\|_\infty+2a\eta.
\]

Consequently, for \(k\eta\le T\),

\[
\|C_k\|_\infty
\le
\bar c_T
:=
\left(\|C_0\|_\infty+\frac1a\right)e^{2a^2T}-\frac1a.
\]

Writing \(\bar E_T=1+a\bar c_T\),

\[
\|Q_{3,k}\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2a\bar E_T\bar c_TT,
\]

and, with

\[
\bar m_{3,T}
=
\|\Gamma_3\|_{\rm op}
+
2a\bar E_T\bar c_TT,
\]

\[
\|Q_{2,k}\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2a\bar E_T\bar m_{3,T}\bar c_TT.
\]

Therefore

\[
\|R_k\|_n
\le
2\bar E_T\bar m_{2,T}\bar m_{3,T}\bar c_T,
\qquad
R_k:=-2e_kM_{2,k}^\top D_{2,k}.
\]

So boundedness is dimension-free.

## Exact transformed-GD defect

Equation (1) has the form

\[
U^+=U+\eta q(U)R.
\]

Therefore the same exact identity holds:

\[
X^+
=
X+\eta R+
\eta^2Uq(U)^2R^2+
\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(U)^3R^3.
\]

Thus

\[
\|\zeta_k\|_n
\le
\frac{3\sqrt3}{16}\eta^2\sqrt n\,\|R_k\|_n^2
+
\frac13\eta^3n\,\|R_k\|_n^3.
\]

If a dimension-free flow-stability estimate were available, accumulated transformed-coordinate error would again be

\[
O_T(\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n),
\]

and \(\eta=n^{-2}\) would make it vanish. Also,

\[
\eta\lambda_1=n^{-1},\quad
\eta\lambda_2=\eta\lambda_3=n^{-2},\quad
\eta\lambda_4=n^{-3}.
\]

The raw scaling and defect therefore work.

## Fatal analytic obstruction

The two-hidden-layer local Lipschitz argument relied on

\[
D=Cq(S),
\]

where \(C\) is uniformly bounded. Here the earlier backpropagated field is

\[
D_2=q(S_2)P_2,
\qquad
P_2=M_3^*D_3.
\]

Although \(P_2\in L^2\), it is generally not in \(L^\infty\). For two states,

\[
\begin{aligned}
D_2-\widetilde D_2
={}&q(S_2)(P_2-\widetilde P_2)\\
&+\bigl(q(S_2)-q(\widetilde S_2)\bigr)\widetilde P_2.
\end{aligned}
\]

The second term cannot be bounded by

\[
C\|S_2-\widetilde S_2\|_2
\]

unless \(\widetilde P_2\in L^\infty\).

This is not merely a missing estimate. Vary \(Q_2\) by a rank-one perturbation so that

\[
\delta Q_2\,H_1=y
\]

for arbitrary \(y\in L^2(\Omega_2)\). Differentiating \(D_2\) in this direction gives

\[
\delta D_2
=
q'(S_2)P_2\,y
+
q(S_2)M_3^*
\left[
Cq'(S_3)M_3(q(S_2)y)
\right].
\tag{9}
\]

The second term defines a bounded operator \(L^2\to L^2\), because \(C\in L^\infty\), \(M_3\) is bounded, and \(q,q'\) are bounded. The first term is multiplication by

\[
q'(S_2)P_2.
\]

It is bounded on \(L^2\) if and only if

\[
q'(S_2)P_2\in L^\infty.
\]

In the Gaussian action generated by the second middle matrix, \(P_2=M_3^*D_3\) generically has an unbounded Gaussian innovation once \(D_3\neq0\). There is no \(L^\infty\) bound. Thus the gradient vector field is not locally Lipschitz in

\[
L^2
\oplus\mathfrak S_1
\oplus\mathfrak S_1
\oplus L^2.
\]

At finite width, the corresponding deterministic bound is only

\[
\|P_2\|_\infty
\le
\sqrt n\,\|P_2\|_n,
\]

which yields a Lipschitz constant growing at least as badly as \(O(\sqrt n)\). Standard Euler Grönwall would then contain \(e^{C\sqrt nT}\), so it does not give a dimension-free \(O(\delta)\) estimate or make the \(n^{-2}\) diagonal valid.

## Verdict

The exact scaling, updates, kernels, formal operator gradient equations, energy identity, a priori bounds, and cubic GD defect all extend.

The required theorem does not presently extend rigorously because:

- local existence and uniqueness of the continuum IDE are not proved;
- the vector field is not locally Lipschitz in the natural \(L^2/\mathfrak S_1\) topology;
- the dimension-free Euler error fails by the available deterministic estimates;
- therefore the exact-GD/flow diagonal limit with \(\eta=n^{-2}\) is unproved.

A successful extension would need a stronger graph, weighted-moment, Gaussian-Sobolev, or full action topology controlling multiplication by \(M_3^*D_3\), together with uniqueness and uniform Euler stability in that topology. No such argument is contained in the two-hidden-layer construction.

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/tp_scope_audit
Payload:
The three-hidden-layer lift is rigorous at every fixed mesh, including two independently reused Gaussian matrices and both adjoints. The obstruction appears only at uniform mesh removal: the two-hidden-layer \(L^2\times\mathfrak S_1\) Lipschitz argument does not extend. A genuine unbounded-backprop multiplier enters, and finite uniform-integrability information alone does not repair stability or uniqueness.

## 1. Fixed-mesh model

Let the three neuron populations carry normalized inner products \(\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{r,n}\), \(r=1,2,3\). Let

\[
\Gamma_{2,n}:\mathbb R^n_1\to\mathbb R^n_2,\qquad
\Gamma_{3,n}:\mathbb R^n_2\to\mathbb R^n_3
\]

be independent matrices with iid \(N(0,1/n)\) entries. Set

\[
M_{2,k,n}=\Gamma_{2,n}+Q_{2,k,n},\qquad
M_{3,k,n}=\Gamma_{3,n}+Q_{3,k,n}.
\]

For \(\phi=\arctan\), \(q=\phi'\), and \(\Psi=\phi\circ\Theta^{-1}\), define

\[
H_{1,k,n}=\Psi(X_{k,n}),
\]

\[
S_{2,k,n}=M_{2,k,n}H_{1,k,n},
\qquad
H_{2,k,n}=\phi(S_{2,k,n}),
\]

\[
S_{3,k,n}=M_{3,k,n}H_{2,k,n},
\qquad
G_{k,n}=\phi(S_{3,k,n}),
\]

\[
D_{3,k,n}=C_{k,n}q(S_{3,k,n}),
\]

\[
B_{3,k,n}=M_{3,k,n}^{\top}D_{3,k,n},
\qquad
D_{2,k,n}=q(S_{2,k,n})B_{3,k,n},
\]

\[
A_{1,k,n}=M_{2,k,n}^{\top}D_{2,k,n},
\]

\[
F_{k,n}=\langle C_{k,n},G_{k,n}\rangle_{3,n},
\qquad e_{k,n}=F_{k,n}-1.
\]

The Euler updates are

\[
X_{k+1,n}=X_{k,n}-2\delta e_{k,n}A_{1,k,n},
\]

\[
C_{k+1,n}=C_{k,n}-2\delta e_{k,n}G_{k,n},
\]

\[
Q_{2,k+1,n}
=
Q_{2,k,n}
-2\delta e_{k,n}
D_{2,k,n}\otimes_n H_{1,k,n},
\]

\[
Q_{3,k+1,n}
=
Q_{3,k,n}
-2\delta e_{k,n}
D_{3,k,n}\otimes_n H_{2,k,n}.
\tag{1}
\]

Consequently,

\[
Q_{2,k,n}
=
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_{r,n}D_{2,r,n}\otimes_nH_{1,r,n},
\]

\[
Q_{3,k,n}
=
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_{r,n}D_{3,r,n}\otimes_nH_{2,r,n}.
\tag{2}
\]

Every learned-matrix action is therefore a finite sum of Moment and coordinatewise operations. For example,

\[
M_{3,k,n}^{\top}D_{3,k,n}
=
\Gamma_{3,n}^{\top}D_{3,k,n}
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_{r,n}H_{2,r,n}
\langle D_{3,r,n},D_{3,k,n}\rangle_{3,n},
\tag{3}
\]

with analogous formulas for \(M_2^\top D_2,M_2H_1,M_3H_2\).

## 2. Exact Tensor-Program scope

[Yang–Hu, Setup G.2, Assumption L.4 and Theorem G.4](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/yang21c/yang21c-supp.pdf), based on the no-rank-stability [Tensor Programs III, Theorem E.15](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.10685), permits:

- finitely many mutually independent Gaussian matrix seeds;
- arbitrary finite reuse of each matrix and its transpose;
- coordinatewise jointly pseudo-Lipschitz maps;
- empirical Moment scalars;
- singular limiting covariance and Gram matrices.

Thus a fixed \((\delta,K)\) recursion (1) is a valid finite Tensor Program. All relevant maps are pseudo-Lipschitz:

\[
(s,b)\mapsto q(s)b
\]

is not globally Lipschitz as a map between \(L^2\)-spaces, but it is a pseudo-Lipschitz finite-dimensional coordinate map, which is all G.4 requires.

The theorem gives, for every fixed mesh and horizon:

- joint empirical convergence of every finite collection of forward and backward probes;
- convergence of every generated scalar;
- convergence of every fixed polynomial moment;
- consequently \(W_p\)-convergence of every finite probe tuple for each fixed finite \(p\).

It gives no estimate uniform in \(K\), \(\delta\), or continuous time.

## 3. Three-population cyclic action

Construct countable typed probe sets \(\mathcal V_1,\mathcal V_2,\mathcal V_3\) in stages. Close each population under a universal countable bounded-Lipschitz cylinder family and rational linear combinations, and add

\[
v\in\mathcal V_1\Longrightarrow
\Gamma_2v\in\mathcal V_2,
\]

\[
w\in\mathcal V_2\Longrightarrow
\Gamma_2^\top w\in\mathcal V_1,\qquad
\Gamma_3w\in\mathcal V_3,
\]

\[
z\in\mathcal V_3\Longrightarrow
\Gamma_3^\top z\in\mathcal V_2.
\]

Dovetailing gives one countable sequential program. Apply G.4 only to finite prefixes. As in the two-population construction, unchanged finite-\(n\) variables force projective consistency of the prefix master laws, regardless of changes in their symbolic Gaussian-conditioning decompositions.

Kolmogorov extension gives probability algebras

\[
(\Omega_r,\mathscr F_r,\mathbb P_r),\qquad
\mathcal H_r=L^2(\Omega_r,\mathscr F_r,\mathbb P_r),
\quad r=1,2,3.
\]

The chosen bounded cylinder classes are dense in their respective \(L^2\)-spaces.

Gaussian net bounds and Borel–Cantelli give, simultaneously,

\[
\|\Gamma_{2,n}\|_{\rm op}\le10,\qquad
\|\Gamma_{3,n}\|_{\rm op}\le10
\]

eventually almost surely. Hence the formal actions extend to bounded operators

\[
\Gamma_2:\mathcal H_1\to\mathcal H_2,
\qquad
\Gamma_3:\mathcal H_2\to\mathcal H_3.
\]

Exact finite-\(n\) dualities and product-moment convergence give

\[
\langle\Gamma_2v,w\rangle_{\mathcal H_2}
=
\langle v,\Gamma_2^*w\rangle_{\mathcal H_1},
\]

\[
\langle\Gamma_3w,z\rangle_{\mathcal H_3}
=
\langle w,\Gamma_3^*z\rangle_{\mathcal H_2}.
\]

The independence of the two matrices is encoded in the joint Tensor-Program master laws. No additional probabilistic independence assertion about the resulting deterministic operators is needed.

The limiting fixed-mesh state is

\[
(\Gamma_2,\Gamma_3;X_k,C_k,Q_{2,k},Q_{3,k}),
\]

where

\[
Q_{2,k}\in\mathfrak S_1(\mathcal H_1,\mathcal H_2),
\qquad
Q_{3,k}\in\mathfrak S_1(\mathcal H_2,\mathcal H_3).
\]

Induction using (2)–(3) identifies every finite-width master variable with the corresponding Euler field. Both adjoint actions are identified by the constructed Hilbert adjoints.

For restartability, only the current trace-class operators \(Q_{2,k},Q_{3,k}\) belong to the state, not their historical rank-one decompositions. Auxiliary vanishing-readout Gaussian marks are marginalized out.

## 4. Required-probe topology

Enumerate every finite same-population tuple of cyclic probes and every integer \(p\ge1\). Define

\[
d_{\rm act}
=
\sum_j2^{-j}
\bigl(1\wedge W_{p_j}(\mu_{n,j},\mu_j)\bigr)
+
\sum_\ell2^{-\ell}
\bigl(1\wedge|\theta_{\ell,n}-\theta_\ell|\bigr).
\]

Here the \(\theta_\ell\) enumerate Moment scalars. At every fixed rational mesh and finite horizon,

\[
d_{\rm act}\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\]

This determines:

- every forward preactivation and activation;
- \(D_3,B_3,D_2,A_1\);
- both Gaussian actions and both adjoints on their dense cyclic domains;
- \(Q_2,Q_3\) through their finite factor and Gram data;
- output, loss, hidden velocities, and every kernel block.

For example, the middle parameter kernels are determined by

\[
K^{(2)}=\|H_1\|_2^2\|D_2\|_2^2,
\qquad
K^{(3)}=\|H_2\|_2^2\|D_3\|_2^2.
\]

## 5. Finite nested uniform-integrability hierarchy

At a fixed mesh, G.4 supplies every level needed:

- \(C,H_1,H_2,G,D_3\) are bounded at each finite step;
- \(B_3=M_3^*D_3\) has all finite master moments;
- \(|D_2|\le |B_3|\);
- \(A_1=M_2^*D_2\) has all finite master moments;
- every weighted field needed in \(\dot S_2,\dot S_3\) is another fixed Tensor-Program probe and has all finite moments.

For passage through the nonlinear backprop product, the relevant elementary lemma is:

> If \(S_n\to S\) in probability, \(B_n\to B\) in \(L^2\), and \(\{B_n^2\}\) is uniformly integrable, then
> \[
> q(S_n)B_n\to q(S)B
> \quad\text{in }L^2.
> \]

Indeed,

\[
\|q(S_n)(B_n-B)\|_2\le\|B_n-B\|_2,
\]

while boundedness of \(q\), convergence in probability of \(q(S_n)\), and uniform integrability of \(B^2\) handle the remaining term by truncation.

Thus a finite UI hierarchy is enough to identify all terms along a sequence already known to converge. It also supports path-law identification once uniform temporal tightness has independently been proved.

It does not provide stability of two nearby solutions.

## 6. Fatal gap in the proposed uniform mesh-removal argument

The natural state norm would be

\[
\|X\|_2+\|C\|_2+
\|Q_2\|_{\mathfrak S_1}+
\|Q_3\|_{\mathfrak S_1}.
\tag{4}
\]

The vector field is not locally Lipschitz in (4).

The obstruction is

\[
D_2=q(S_2)B_3,\qquad B_3=M_3^*D_3.
\]

For nonzero times, \(B_3\) is generally unbounded. At small time,

\[
D_3(t)=2t\,\phi(S_3(0))q(S_3(0))+o(t),
\]

and \(\Gamma_3^*D_3(t)\) has a nondegenerate Gaussian component plus its transpose-correlation correction. Hence \(B_3(t)\notin L^\infty\).

Choose a state for which \(B_3\) is unbounded on a set where \(|q'(S_2)|\ge c>0\). Let

\[
E_m\subseteq
\{|B_3|\ge m,\ |q'(S_2)|\ge c\}
\]

have positive measure, and let \(h_m=\varepsilon_m1_{E_m}\), with \(\varepsilon_m\downarrow0\). Taylor’s formula gives, after choosing \(\varepsilon_m\) sufficiently small,

\[
\frac{
\|(q(S_2+h_m)-q(S_2))B_3\|_2
}{
\|h_m\|_2
}
\ge \frac c2\,m.
\tag{5}
\]

This is an admissible trace-class state perturbation. If \(H_1\ne0\), set

\[
\Delta Q_2
=
\frac{h_m\otimes H_1}{\|H_1\|_2^2}.
\]

Then

\[
\Delta Q_2H_1=h_m,
\qquad
\|\Delta Q_2\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
=
\frac{\|h_m\|_2}{\|H_1\|_2}.
\]

The induced change of \(B_3\) itself is only \(O(\|h_m\|_2)\): with \(C\in L^\infty\),

\[
\|B_3(S_2+h_m)-B_3(S_2)\|_2
\le C_{\rm state}\|h_m\|_2.
\]

Therefore it cannot cancel (5). The \(D_2\) component, and hence the \(Q_2\)- and \(X\)-components of the vector field, have unbounded local Lipschitz ratio.

So the earlier claimed width-independent estimate

\[
\sup_{t\le T}\|Y_n(t)-Y_n^\delta(t)\|
\le C_T\delta
\]

does not follow in the norm (4), and is generally false as a consequence of a uniform local-Lipschitz bound.

Finite UI does not fix this: it proves continuity of the product along convergent sequences, but supplies neither a quantitative stability modulus nor uniqueness of the limiting integral equation. G.4 supplies no uniform-in-mesh Orlicz tail bound, no \(L^p\)-operator bound for the reused Gaussian matrices, and no continuous-time theorem.

## 7. Consequence for path convergence

Conditional on a valid uniform stability/uniqueness theorem, path convergence would follow from

\[
\frac1n\sum_i
\sup_{s,t\in I}|Z_i(t)-Z_i(s)|^2
\le
|I|\int_I V_n(t)^2\,dt
\]

plus the finite nested UI hierarchy. But neither this estimate nor fixed-mesh Tensor-Program convergence supplies the missing stability theorem.

Therefore:

- the two-matrix, three-population fixed-mesh probabilistic lift is rigorous;
- its countable cyclic action and required-probe topology are sound;
- all fixed-mesh moments and nested product identifications are covered by the exact external theorem;
- uniform mesh removal, uniqueness, and hence Wasserstein path convergence remain unresolved in the proposed \(L^2/\mathfrak S_1\) architecture.

Repair would require a new weighted/Orlicz stability theory, an Osgood-type uniqueness estimate with verified uniform tails, or another structure-specific argument. Tensor Programs alone do not supply it.

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER

Payload:
For a fixed dataset, the finite-width algebra and scaling extend cleanly, but the single-sample well-posedness proof generally does not. The obstruction is the lack of one scalar natural coordinate that removes all first-layer activation derivatives simultaneously.

I use the averaged loss convention

\[
L_n=\frac1m\sum_{a=1}^m(f_n^a-y_a)^2.
\]

For the unaveraged loss, remove every factor \(1/m\).

## Exact finite-width equations

Let \(x_a\in\mathbb R^d\), and write the first-layer row vectors as \(w_i\in\mathbb R^d\). Define

\[
U_i^a=w_i\cdot x_a,\qquad
H_i^a=\arctan U_i^a,
\]

\[
S_j^a=\sum_iM_{ji}H_i^a,\qquad
G_j^a=\arctan S_j^a,
\]

\[
C=nW^{(3)},\qquad
f_n^a=\langle C,G^a\rangle_n,\qquad
e_a=f_n^a-y_a.
\]

Set

\[
D^a=Cq(S^a),\qquad
P^a=M^\top D^a,\qquad
A^a=q(U^a)P^a,
\]

where \(q(z)=(1+z^2)^{-1}\).

The same scaling as for one sample is natural:

\[
\sigma_{1,n}^2=1,\qquad
\sigma_{2,n}^2=\frac1n,\qquad
\sigma_{3,n}^2=\frac1{n^4},
\]

\[
\lambda_1=n,\qquad
\lambda_2=1,\qquad
\lambda_3=\frac1n.
\]

A different fixed, width-independent first-layer variance such as \(1/d\) only changes the initial covariance.

For each sample,

\[
\nabla_{W^{(1)}}f_n^a
=
\frac1nA^a x_a^\top,
\]

\[
\nabla_Mf_n^a
=
\frac1nD^a(H^a)^\top,
\]

\[
\nabla_{W^{(3)}}f_n^a=G^a.
\]

Thus exact full-batch GD is

\[
W^{(1)+}
=
W^{(1)}
-\frac{2\eta}{m}
\sum_a e_aA^ax_a^\top,
\tag{1}
\]

\[
M^+
=
M-\frac{2\eta}{m}
\sum_a e_aD^a\otimes_nH^a,
\tag{2}
\]

\[
C^+
=
C-\frac{2\eta}{m}
\sum_a e_aG^a.
\tag{3}
\]

Every \(n\)-factor cancels exactly as in the one-sample construction.

## Kernel matrix and energy

The full-batch kernel is now an \(m\times m\) matrix. Its layer blocks are

\[
K_{ab}^{(1)}
=
(x_a\cdot x_b)\,
\langle A^a,A^b\rangle_n,
\]

\[
K_{ab}^{(2)}
=
\langle D^a,D^b\rangle_n
\langle H^a,H^b\rangle_n,
\]

\[
K_{ab}^{(3)}
=
\langle G^a,G^b\rangle_n.
\]

Each block is positive semidefinite, since it is a Gram matrix of parameter gradients. Along continuous full-batch gradient flow,

\[
\dot f_a
=
-\frac2m\sum_bK_{ab}e_b,
\]

and therefore

\[
\dot L_n
=
-\frac4{m^2}e^\top K e
\le0.
\tag{4}
\]

## Formal continuum flow

The first-sort law now carries a random vector \(W\in\mathbb R^d\). For each sample,

\[
U^a=W\cdot x_a,\qquad
H^a=\arctan U^a.
\]

Let \(M:\mathcal H_1\to\mathcal H_2\) be the middle action and \(C\in\mathcal H_2\). Define

\[
S^a=MH^a,\quad
G^a=\arctan S^a,\quad
D^a=Cq(S^a),
\]

\[
P^a=M^*D^a,\qquad
A^a=q(U^a)P^a.
\]

The formal operator gradient flow is

\[
\dot W
=
-\frac2m\sum_a e_aA^ax_a,
\tag{5}
\]

\[
\dot M
=
-\frac2m\sum_a e_aD^a\otimes H^a,
\tag{6}
\]

\[
\dot C
=
-\frac2m\sum_a e_aG^a.
\tag{7}
\]

This is the Hilbert gradient equation for the averaged loss on

\[
L^2(\Omega_1;\mathbb R^d)
\times(\Gamma+\mathfrak S_2)
\times L^2(\Omega_2).
\]

The scalar loss is \(C^1\), and (4) remains valid formally.

There are dimension-free a priori bounds. Since the loss decreases,

\[
\left(\frac1m\sum_a e_a(t)^2\right)^{1/2}
\le
\left(\frac1m\sum_a e_a(0)^2\right)^{1/2}.
\]

Because every \(G^a\) is bounded by \(\pi/2\), this bounds \(\|C(t)\|_\infty\) linearly on compact intervals. It then bounds all \(D^a\), the trace-class motion of \(M\), and the \(L^2\)-size of \(\dot W\). These are no-blowup estimates, not a uniqueness proof.

## Why the natural coordinate fails generically

Let

\[
\mathsf G_{ab}=x_a\cdot x_b
\]

be the input Gram matrix. From (5),

\[
\dot U^a
=
-\frac2m\sum_b
\mathsf G_{ab}\,e_b\,q(U^b)P^b.
\tag{8}
\]

For one sample, \(X=\Theta(U)\), with

\[
\Theta'(u)=q(u)^{-1},
\]

removes \(q(U)\). Applying the same transform componentwise now gives

\[
\dot X^a
=
-\frac2m\sum_b
\mathsf G_{ab}e_b
\frac{q(U^b)}{q(U^a)}P^b.
\tag{9}
\]

Only the diagonal terms simplify. Every off-diagonal Gram entry leaves a ratio \(q(U^b)/q(U^a)\), which can be unbounded.

This is not repaired by treating all \(U^a\) as independent trainable variables: their true GD metric is the input Gram matrix \(\mathsf G\). Replacing it by independent optimization would change GD.

A general coordinate transformation that eliminates \(\mathsf G\,\mathrm{diag}(q(U))\) also fails the usual integrability conditions unless the effective Gram structure is diagonal or otherwise degenerate. Orthogonal inputs and a single effective input are special cases; a generic dataset is not.

## The analytic obstruction

In the raw first-layer vector field, the problematic quantity is

\[
A^a=q(U^a)P^a,\qquad P^a=M^*D^a.
\]

Although \(P^a\in L^2\), it is generally unbounded. For two nearby states,

\[
A^a-\widetilde A^a
=
q(U^a)(P^a-\widetilde P^a)
+
\bigl(q(U^a)-q(\widetilde U^a)\bigr)\widetilde P^a.
\]

There is no estimate of the form

\[
\|A^a-\widetilde A^a\|_2
\le
C\bigl(\|U^a-\widetilde U^a\|_2+\cdots\bigr)
\]

unless \(\widetilde P^a\in L^\infty\). Infinitesimally, the derivative contains multiplication by

\[
q'(U^a)P^a,
\]

which is not a bounded operator on \(L^2\) when that field is unbounded.

For a generic nonorthogonal dataset, the natural-coordinate cancellation cannot remove all these multipliers. Hence the vector field is not locally Lipschitz in the two-hidden-layer state norm

\[
L^2(\Omega_1;\mathbb R^d)
\oplus\mathfrak S_1
\oplus L^2(\Omega_2).
\]

Consequences:

- the earlier Picard uniqueness argument does not extend;
- the dimension-free \(O(\delta)\) Euler estimate is unavailable;
- exact GD is Euler in the raw parameters, but no dimension-free GD/flow stability estimate follows;
- choosing \(\eta=n^{-2}\) does not by itself repair this gap.

At finite width, the deterministic replacement is only

\[
\|P^a\|_\infty\le\sqrt n\,\|P^a\|_n,
\]

which gives width-growing Lipschitz constants. A stronger weighted-moment or graph topology might succeed, but that requires a new proof.

## Decisive failure of universal activity

No affirmative theorem can hold for every arbitrary fixed dataset without data nondegeneracy assumptions.

Take two identical nonzero inputs,

\[
x_1=x_2=x\ne0,
\]

with conflicting labels

\[
y_1=1,\qquad y_2=-1.
\]

The network outputs are identical: \(f_1=f_2=f\). Therefore

\[
L
=
\frac12\bigl((f-1)^2+(f+1)^2\bigr)
=
f^2+1.
\]

In the proposed scaling, the limiting readout initialization is \(C_0=0\), hence \(f(0)=0\). Thus

\[
\nabla L(0)=2f(0)\nabla f(0)=0,
\]

and the limiting gradient flow is stationary for all time.

This example has nonzero inputs and can have nondegenerate, genuinely nonlinear hidden laws, yet:

- both hidden-layer displacements vanish;
- the first two parameter-block kernels vanish because \(C=D=0\);
- the loss remains equal to \(1\);
- no feature learning occurs.

So any “all fixed finite datasets” version of the required active-properties theorem is false.

Verdict: the scaling, exact updates, kernel matrix, and energy law extend. Generic finite datasets destroy the scalar natural-coordinate mechanism, leaving an unresolved \(L^2\) well-posedness and Euler-stability obstruction. Moreover, universal feature-learning/activity claims are decisively false without assumptions on the dataset and residual forcing.

No: all seven properties cannot hold for arbitrary finite datasets.

The universal counterexample is a single sample with \(x=0\). In a bias-free network,

\[
z_i^{(1)}(0,t)=W_i^{(1)}(t)\cdot0=0
\]

for every width and time. Hence the first hidden variance, velocity, displacement, and first-layer kernel contribution are all identically zero, independently of activation and scaling.

For the zero-output arctan construction, there are additional nonzero-input counterexamples:

- One sample with \(y=0\) is stationary.
- Two identical inputs with labels \(+1,-1\) have cancelling full-batch gradients at \(F(0)=0\).
- Because the bias-free arctan network is odd, the distinct inputs \(x,-x\) with equal labels \(+1,+1\) also cancel: the constrained predictor satisfies \(F(-x)=-F(x)\), and \(F=0\) already minimizes the two-sample loss.

For a finite dataset \((x_a,y_a)_{a=1}^m\), define at initialization

\[
U_a=w\cdot x_a,\quad H_a=\arctan U_a,\quad
S_a=\Gamma H_a,\quad G_a=\arctan S_a,
\]

and

\[
R=\sum_a y_aG_a,\qquad
Y_a=q(S_a)R,\qquad
\Xi_a=\Gamma^*Y_a.
\]

Then

\[
C(t)=2tR+o(t),\qquad
D_a(t)=2tY_a+o(t),\qquad
P_a(t)=2t\Xi_a+o(t).
\]

Put

\[
W_*=\sum_b y_bq(U_b)\Xi_bx_b,
\qquad
T_*=\sum_b y_bY_b\otimes H_b,
\]

\[
A_a=x_a\cdot W_*,
\qquad
B_a=T_*H_a+\Gamma(q(U_a)A_a).
\]

The hidden expansions are

\[
U_a(t)=U_a+2t^2A_a+o_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

\[
S_a(t)=S_a+2t^2B_a+o_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

so

\[
V_{1,a}^2(t)=16t^2\mathbb E A_a^2+o(t^2),
\qquad
V_{2,a}^2(t)=16t^2\mathbb E B_a^2+o(t^2).
\]

Writing \(\mathbf K^{(\ell)}\) for the samplewise kernel matrices,

\[
y^\top\mathbf K^{(1)}(t)y
=
4t^2\|W_*\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^d)}^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
y^\top\mathbf K^{(2)}(t)y
=
4t^2\|T_*\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
y^\top\mathbf K^{(3)}(0)y=\|R\|_2^2.
\]

Moreover,

\[
y^\top[\mathbf K^{(3)}(t)-\mathbf K^{(3)}(0)]y
=
4t^2\bigl(\|W_*\|^2+\|T_*\|^2\bigr)+o(t^2).
\]

Thus

\[
L'(0)=-4\|R\|_2^2.
\]

Consequently:

- \(R\ne0\) is necessary and sufficient for immediate loss decrease in this zero-output construction.
- \(W_*\ne0\) and \(T_*\ne0\) are clean leading-order conditions for both hidden parameter blocks to be active.
- Per-sample feature learning requires \(\mathbb EA_a^2>0\) and \(\mathbb EB_a^2>0\); aggregate feature learning requires the corresponding sums over samples to be positive.
- Every sample for which positive first-hidden variance is required must satisfy \(x_a\ne0\).
- A sufficient output nondegeneracy condition is positive definiteness of the initial output-kernel Gram matrix on the label/residual vector.

For multiple samples, the correct hidden empirical laws are vector-valued path laws in

\[
W_2\!\left(C([0,T];\mathbb R^m)\right).
\]

For fixed \(m\), the previous fixed-mesh, uniform-integrability, and path-tightness arguments extend componentwise. The loss identity becomes

\[
\dot L=-4e^\top\mathbf K e.
\]

Hence block activity must be measured by \(\int e^\top\mathbf K^{(\ell)}e\,dt>0\); positive trace of a kernel block is insufficient.

So a multi-sample theorem is feasible only under explicit input, residual-kernel, block-gradient, and feature-displacement nondegeneracy assumptions. It cannot hold for arbitrary finite input-label configurations.

Payload:
For a fixed finite dataset, the fixed-mesh Tensor-Program/operator construction remains valid without additional assumptions. The obstruction is analytic: for generic correlated inputs, the first-layer multi-sample gradient reintroduces an unbounded multiplier that the single-sample natural coordinate eliminated. The original uniform \(L^2\times\mathfrak S_1\) mesh-removal proof therefore does not close.

## Fixed-mesh equations

Let the dataset be \((x^a,y^a)_{a=1}^m\), with fixed \(m\) and fixed input dimension. Write

\[
G_{ab}=\langle x^a,x^b\rangle.
\]

For one first-layer neuron, let

\[
U^a=\langle W^{(1)},x^a\rangle,\qquad H^a=\phi(U^a).
\]

For the middle and output layers, set

\[
S^a=(\Gamma_n+Q_n)H^a,\qquad
G^a_{\rm out}=\phi(S^a),
\]

\[
D^a=Cq(S^a),\qquad
A^a=(\Gamma_n+Q_n)^\top D^a,
\]

where \(\phi=\arctan\), \(q=\phi'\), and

\[
f^a=\langle C,G^a_{\rm out}\rangle_n.
\]

For squared empirical loss, write \(\chi_a=\partial_{f^a}L\). Up to the harmless fixed normalization by \(m\), gradient flow is

\[
\dot U^b
=
-\sum_{a=1}^m
G_{ba}\chi_a q(U^a)A^a,
\tag{1}
\]

\[
\dot C=-\sum_a\chi_aG^a_{\rm out},
\]

\[
\dot Q=-\sum_a\chi_aD^a\otimes_nH^a.
\tag{2}
\]

At Euler step \(k\),

\[
Q_{k,n}
=
-\delta\sum_{r<k}\sum_{a=1}^m
\chi_{r,a}D^a_{r,n}\otimes_nH^a_{r,n}.
\tag{3}
\]

Hence

\[
(\Gamma_n+Q_{k,n})H^b_{k,n}
=
\Gamma_nH^b_{k,n}
-\delta\sum_{r<k,a}
\chi_{r,a}D^a_{r,n}
\langle H^a_{r,n},H^b_{k,n}\rangle_n,
\]

and

\[
(\Gamma_n+Q_{k,n})^\top D^b_{k,n}
=
\Gamma_n^\top D^b_{k,n}
-\delta\sum_{r<k,a}
\chi_{r,a}H^a_{r,n}
\langle D^a_{r,n},D^b_{k,n}\rangle_n.
\]

Thus, for fixed \((m,\delta,K)\), the complete recursion is a finite Tensor Program.

## Tensor-Program hypotheses

The initial mark

\[
(U^1_0,\ldots,U^m_0)
\]

is jointly Gaussian with covariance proportional to \(G\). [Yang–Hu, Setup G.2 and Theorem G.4](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/yang21c/yang21c-supp.pdf) permits an arbitrary fixed multivariate Gaussian initial-coordinate law. Singular covariance is allowed by the no-rank-stability version, [Tensor Programs III, Theorem E.15](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.10685).

The shared reuse of \(\Gamma_n,\Gamma_n^\top\) across every sample is allowed. Since \(m\) and the number of mesh steps are fixed, all sample fields, outputs, residual scalars, cross-sample moments, and kernels lie in one finite program.

Consequently, at every fixed mesh:

- all joint sample-mark empirical laws converge;
- every fixed polynomial moment converges;
- all forward and backward fields converge jointly;
- every output, residual, loss, and kernel-matrix entry converges;
- the same countable cyclic action construction produces
  \[
  \Gamma:\mathcal H_R\to\mathcal H_L,\qquad \Gamma^*:\mathcal H_L\to\mathcal H_R.
  \]

For example, the three kernel matrices are determined by the probes

\[
K^{(1)}_{ab}
=
G_{ab}\,
\mathbb E\!\left[
q(U^a)A^a\,q(U^b)A^b
\right],
\]

\[
K^{(2)}_{ab}
=
\mathbb E[H^aH^b]\,
\mathbb E[D^aD^b],
\]

\[
K^{(3)}_{ab}
=
\mathbb E[G^a_{\rm out}G^b_{\rm out}].
\]

There is no fixed-mesh theorem mismatch.

## Why the single-sample natural coordinate fails generically

Let

\[
\Theta'(u)=\frac1{q(u)},\qquad X^b=\Theta(U^b).
\]

Equation (1) becomes

\[
\dot X^b
=
-\sum_a
G_{ba}\chi_a
\frac{q(U^a)}{q(U^b)}A^a.
\tag{4}
\]

For one sample, the ratio is \(1\). For correlated distinct samples, it is a nonconstant random multiplier. Since \(A^a=\Gamma^*D^a+Q^*D^a\) generally has an unbounded Gaussian component, (4) is not locally Lipschitz in the natural \(L^2\)-state topology.

Tracking \(U\) directly does not help: the problematic map is then

\[
(U^a,A^a)\longmapsto q(U^a)A^a.
\]

At a state where \(A^a\) is unbounded and \(q'(U^a)\ne0\) on a set of positive measure, choose sets

\[
E_r\subseteq
\{|A^a|\ge r,\ |q'(U^a)|\ge c\}
\]

and perturb the underlying first-layer mark by \(h_r=\varepsilon_r1_{E_r}\). Taylor expansion gives

\[
\frac{
\|(q(U^a+h_r)-q(U^a))A^a\|_2
}{
\|h_r\|_2
}
\ge \frac c2 r
\]

for sufficiently small \(\varepsilon_r\). Thus the local Lipschitz constants diverge.

Uniform integrability can prove convergence of this product along a sequence already known to converge, but it does not give a quantitative stability estimate or uniqueness. Hence the earlier width-uniform Euler estimate cannot simply be reused.

## Exact cancellation condition

In vector notation, (1) is

\[
\dot U=-G\,D_q(U)V,
\qquad
D_q(U)=\operatorname{diag}(q(U^1),\ldots,q(U^m)),
\tag{5}
\]

where \(V_a=\chi_aA^a\).

For the componentwise transform \(X=\Theta(U)\),

\[
\dot X
=
-D_q(U)^{-1}G D_q(U)V.
\]

It becomes a constant-coefficient expression precisely when

\[
G D_q(U)=D_q(U)G
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{6}
\]

Entrywise,

\[
G_{ab}\bigl(q(U^b)-q(U^a)\bigr)=0.
\tag{7}
\]

For \(q(u)=(1+u^2)^{-1}\), if \(G_{ab}\ne0\), condition (7) requires

\[
(U^a)^2=(U^b)^2
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\]

A centered jointly Gaussian pair satisfying this is supported on one of the two lines \(u^b=\pm u^a\). Therefore

\[
U^b=\pm U^a\quad\text{almost surely},
\]

which, under isotropic first-layer initialization, is equivalent to

\[
x^b=\pm x^a.
\]

Thus (6) holds exactly when the dataset can be partitioned into blocks \(B_r\) such that

\[
x^a=\varepsilon_a v_r,\qquad
\varepsilon_a\in\{-1,1\},\qquad a\in B_r,
\]

and

\[
\langle v_r,v_s\rangle=0
\quad(r\ne s).
\tag{8}
\]

Pairwise orthogonal inputs are the singleton-block case.

Rank-one Gram is not sufficient. If \(x^a=\alpha_av\) with unequal \(|\alpha_a|\), then

\[
q(\alpha_av)=\frac1{1+\alpha_a^2v^2}
\]

does not share a common factor across \(a\). A scalar coordinate change could cancel all sample coefficients only if all ratios \(q(\alpha_av)/q(\alpha_bv)\) were constant in \(v\), which for arctan requires

\[
|\alpha_a|=|\alpha_b|.
\]

For nonsingular \(G\), one can strengthen this: a \(C^2\) multivariate coordinate change satisfying

\[
DT(U)\,G D_q(U)=B
\]

for a constant invertible matrix \(B\) exists only if \(G\) is diagonal. The mixed-partial integrability conditions force every off-diagonal entry of \(G^{-1}\) to vanish because \((1/q)'(u)=2u\) is nonconstant.

## Closure under the block condition

Assume (8). Define one latent first-layer preactivation per block,

\[
V^r=\langle W^{(1)},v_r\rangle.
\]

Then

\[
U^a=\varepsilon_aV^r,\qquad a\in B_r.
\]

Since \(q\) is even,

\[
q(U^a)=q(V^r).
\]

Orthogonality gives

\[
\dot V^r
=
-\|v_r\|^2q(V^r)
\sum_{a\in B_r}\varepsilon_a\chi_aA^a.
\]

Set

\[
X^r=\Theta(V^r).
\]

Then

\[
\dot X^r
=
-\|v_r\|^2
\sum_{a\in B_r}\varepsilon_a\chi_aA^a.
\tag{9}
\]

The unbounded backward fields now occur only linearly, without a state-dependent multiplier. The state

\[
(\Gamma;X^1,\ldots,X^R,C,Q)
\]

has a locally Lipschitz vector field in

\[
(L^2_R)^R\times L^2_L\times\mathfrak S_1,
\]

on sets with bounded \(\|C\|_\infty,\|Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\). All estimates from the single-sample construction acquire only finite \(m\)-dependent constants. Therefore:

- finite-width and limiting Euler errors are uniformly \(O_T(\delta)\);
- fixed-mesh Tensor-Program convergence lifts to continuous time;
- joint sample-mark and hidden-layer path laws converge in the required Wasserstein topology;
- outputs, the full \(m\times m\) kernel matrix, and loss converge uniformly;
- the state is autonomous and restartable, storing only the current fields and \(Q\).

## Verdict

For arbitrary fixed finite datasets:

- fixed-mesh Tensor-Program and operator convergence is valid;
- joint Gaussian input marks and shared matrix/adjoint reuse cause no theorem problem;
- generic uniform mesh removal in the proposed \(L^2/\mathfrak S_1\) framework is unresolved because of the first-layer product \(q(U^a)A^a\).

The existing analytic proof closes when the input Gram decomposes into orthogonal blocks of equal-magnitude sign duplicates, equivalently condition (8). Outside that class, a new weighted/Orlicz stability or structure-specific uniqueness argument is required.

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
