Quadratic homogeneity gives global normalized \(L^2\) coercivity, but not the moment control needed for the full mean-field limit.

For \(\phi(z)=z^2\), the output is multi-homogeneous of degrees
\[
(d_1,d_2,d_3)=(4,2,1)
\]
in the three parameter blocks. Therefore,
\[
\langle W^{(\ell)},\nabla_{W^{(\ell)}}f_a\rangle=d_\ell f_a.
\]
For
\[
L=\sum_a(f_a-y_a)^2,\qquad e_a=f_a-y_a,
\]
gradient flow satisfies
\[
\frac d{dt}\|W^{(\ell)}\|_F^2
=-4\lambda_\ell d_\ell\sum_a e_af_a.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\frac{\|W^{(\ell)}\|_F^2}{\lambda_\ell d_\ell}
-\frac{\|W^{(m)}\|_F^2}{\lambda_m d_m}
\]
is exactly conserved. Moreover,
\[
\left|\sum_a e_af_a\right|
\le \|e\|_2\bigl(\|e\|_2+\|y\|_2\bigr)
\le \sqrt{L(0)}\bigl(\sqrt{L(0)}+\|y\|_2\bigr),
\]
because loss is nonincreasing. Thus every weighted Frobenius norm grows at most linearly on compact time intervals.

Under the natural scaling
\[
(\lambda_1,\lambda_2,\lambda_3)\asymp(n,1,n^{-1}),
\]
this controls
\[
\frac1n\|W^{(1)}\|_F^2,\qquad
\frac1n\|W^{(2)}\|_F^2,\qquad
n\|W^{(3)}\|_F^2
\]
uniformly on compact intervals, assuming tight initial loss and normalized initial norms. It rules out finite-time Frobenius blowup and gives global existence for every finite width.

The obstruction is that this controls only second moments. Differentiating a \(2p\)-th empirical moment creates mixed terms such as
\[
\mathbb E_n\bigl[|z|^{2p-1}|P|\bigr],
\]
followed by still higher moments of the reused-adjoint fields. Balancedness supplies no closure. A vector can have bounded normalized \(L^2\) norm while one coordinate is \(O(\sqrt n)\), causing every empirical moment above order two to diverge. The invariants do not exclude such concentration.

Therefore homogeneity prevents radial blowup but does not establish uniform integrability, polynomial-traffic convergence at growing depth, or passage of the quadratic gradient vector field. No actual spiking gradient-flow orbit is established, so this is not a negative theorem; it identifies the precise missing ingredient: propagation of sufficiently high moments and nonconcentration beyond the exact balancedness identities.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 27489 | 2026-08-27T12:23:17.492Z | /root/functional_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/functional_audit
Payload:
The traffic route identifies every fixed Taylor coefficient, but it does not justify summing the time series or passing to a compact-time path limit.

For \(\phi(x)=x^2\),
\[
s_j=\sum_i B_{ji}u_i^2,\qquad
f=\sum_j c_js_j^2.
\]
As a polynomial in \((u,B,c)\), \(f\) has degree \(7\), \(\nabla f\) has degree \(6\), and the exact loss-gradient vector field
\[
-2(f-1)\nabla f
\]
contains degrees \(6\) and \(13\). Repeated time differentiation therefore creates graph polynomials whose degrees grow linearly with differentiation order, at worst by \(12\) per derivative.

At every fixed order \(r\), the resulting expressions consist of:

- iid Gaussian matrix entries;
- diagonal matrices formed from coordinatewise powers;
- Hadamard products;
- rank-one vector insertions;
- normalized sums and traces.

These are precisely the kinds of fixed graph-polynomial observables for which traffic methods are designed. Thus fixed-\(r\), fixed-moment convergence is plausible.

The missing estimate is uniform summability in \(r\). To recover a short-time solution from its Taylor series, one would need a bound comparable to
\[
\sup_n\|a_{n,r}\|_{L^2}\le C^r r!.
\]
Traffic convergence of each fixed graph polynomial supplies no such estimate. A Gaussian monomial of degree \(d\) has \(L^2\)-norm on the scale
\[
\sqrt{(2d-1)!!},
\]
which is approximately \(d^{d/2}\) up to exponential factors. With \(d\asymp r\), this growth can dominate the \(r!\) from time ordering after derivative and pairing multiplicities are included.

This is not merely evidence of real-time blow-up. Even the scalar dissipative equation
\[
\dot x=-x^{13}
\]
has the global forward solution
\[
x(t)=\frac{x_0}{(1+12t x_0^{12})^{1/12}},
\]
but its formal time coefficients contain \(x_0^{12r+1}\). For Gaussian \(x_0\), their \(L^2\)-norms grow so rapidly that the \(L^2\)-valued Taylor series has zero radius of convergence. Thus a traffic proof based on termwise time analyticity can fail even when the actual flow is perfectly dissipative and globally defined.

There is a second obstruction to strong convergence. The diagonal Gaussian marks appearing in the coordinatewise gates have operator norm
\[
\max_{i\le n}|G_i|\asymp\sqrt{\log n}.
\]
Consequently, strong random-matrix convergence results requiring uniformly bounded operator norms do not apply directly. Truncating at level \(M\) yields fixed-\(M\) traffic convergence, but polynomial ODE stability can grow like \(e^{CTM^{12}}\). Polynomial Gaussian tail control does not automatically survive this amplification.

Loss dissipation gives
\[
\frac{d}{dt}L_n
=
-4(f_n-1)^2K_n\le0,
\]
but this controls the output error and the weighted parameter-gradient energy. It does not control all high coordinate moments required by the quadratic gates, nor does it exclude rare-coordinate amplification before those coordinates materially affect the normalized loss.

Therefore an exact theorem mapping would require substantially more than a standard traffic limit:

1. fixed graph-polynomial convergence for all diagonal/Hadamard/vector operations;
2. a non-Taylor compactness or Galerkin argument;
3. width-uniform high-moment or tail propagation;
4. stability under truncation with constants compatible with Gaussian tails;
5. uniform Euler error on the same interval.

I found no traffic theorem that supplies items 2–5 for an \(n\)-dependent polynomial gradient ODE with unbounded diagonal Gaussian marks. Accordingly, traffic probability is a valid fixed-order discovery tool here, but it does not presently prove full-sequence compact-time convergence. The exact obstruction is uniform time resummation and tail stability, not identification of individual graph-polynomial coefficients.

Payload:
No width-uniform theorem was found in Hida/Kondratiev scales.

The standard dimension-free-looking estimate is Våge’s inequality for the Wick product, not ordinary multiplication. Replacing ordinary products by Wick products removes contraction terms and changes the GD dynamics.

Hida test functions are closed under ordinary pointwise multiplication only as a nuclear Fréchet algebra with loss of seminorm level. Hida distributions are not generally closed under ordinary multiplication. Creation and annihilation operators likewise lose chaos regularity.

The decisive width issue is that nuclear one-particle weights increase with the Gaussian mode index. Encoding \(n^2\) independent matrix entries therefore produces norms and constants growing with \(n\); assigning every mode uniform weight would destroy nuclearity. Generic estimates also miss the \(n^{-1/2}\) random-operator cancellation.

Ovsyannikov’s theorem gives existence time proportional to the available scale gap divided by the vector-field and initial-data constants. Since those constants grow with the Gaussian dimension here, it yields no verified width-independent interval.

Thus this route would require a new Gaussian analytic norm that is simultaneously dimension-free, closed under ordinary products including contractions, and bounded under reused \(B,B^\top\) actions. Standard Hida/Kondratiev/Gevrey theory does not provide it.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 27677 | 2026-08-27T12:29:30.521Z | /root/uniform_lift_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/uniform_lift_audit
Payload:
The factorial-chaos scale supports dimension-free operations between strictly separated indices, but its radius-loss constants are too singular for an Ovsyannikov flow theorem.

Let
\[
\|F\|_s^2=\sum_{k\ge0}(k!)^{2s}k!\|f_k\|^2,
\qquad 0<s<\tfrac12.
\]
The Wiener product formula and tensor contraction estimates give
\[
\|FG\|_{s'}\le C_{s,s'}\|F\|_s\|G\|_s,
\qquad s'<s.
\]
Likewise, normalized Gaussian aggregation decomposes as
\[
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_\ell g_\ell u_\ell
=\delta(u/\sqrt n)
+\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_\ell D_\ell u_\ell.
\]
Creation raises chaos order by one, Malliavin differentiation lowers it, and the normalized trace is controlled by the Hilbert–Schmidt Malliavin derivative. Hence these maps are dimension-free from the vector-valued \(s\)-space into any \(s'<s\) space.

The problem is sharp constant growth. Take a normalized one-dimensional pure \(k\)-th chaos. The highest-chaos term of its square implies
\[
C_{s,s'}
\ge
\sup_{k\ge1}
\binom{2k}{k}^{s'+1/2}(k!)^{-2(s-s')}.
\]
Writing \(\delta=s-s'\), Stirling’s formula gives
\[
\log C_{s,s'}
\gtrsim c\,\delta\,2^{c'/\delta}.
\]
Thus the constant grows super-exponentially as \(\delta\downarrow0\), rather than like a power \(\delta^{-a}\).

In an \(r\)-fold Picard iteration with total available radius loss \(\Delta\), some step has loss at most \(\Delta/r\). Its multiplication constant is then at least of order
\[
\exp\!\left[
c\frac{\Delta}{r}\,2^{c'r/\Delta}
\right],
\]
which overwhelms the time-simplex factor \(1/r!\). Quadratic nonlinearities already encounter this obstruction; analytic composition adds further products.

Therefore these chaos spaces prove fixed-gap continuity of products and Gaussian creation/trace operations, but they do not yield a positive-time Ovsyannikov theorem for the neural vector field. A different scale, a Wick-product formulation, structural cancellations, or orbit-specific tail bounds would be required.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 27679 | 2026-08-27T12:29:30.521Z | /root/functional_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/functional_audit
Payload:
A dimension-free chaos estimate exists for the reused Gaussian matrix, but the quadratic composition exhausts every fixed positive analytic-chaos radius. Thus a standard Hida/Ovsyannikov construction does not yield the required \(L^2/W_2\) flow.

Let
\[
F=\sum_{k\ge0}I_k(f_k),\qquad
\|F\|_\rho^2
=
\sum_{k\ge0}\rho^{2k}k!\|f_k\|^2.
\]
For a unit first-chaos variable \(g=I_1(h)\),
\[
gI_k(f_k)
=
I_{k+1}(h\widetilde\otimes f_k)
+kI_{k-1}(h\mathbin{\lrcorner}f_k).
\]
Hence, for \(0<\rho'<\rho\),
\[
\|gF\|_{\rho'}
\le
C(\rho,\rho')\|F\|_\rho,
\]
with, for example,
\[
C(\rho,\rho')
\lesssim
\left(\rho'+\rho^{-1}\right)
\left(1-\frac{\rho'^2}{\rho^2}\right)^{-1/2}.
\]
The estimate follows by bounding
\[
\sup_{k\ge0}\sqrt{k+1}\left(\frac{\rho'}\rho\right)^k
\]
and its annihilation analogue.

This estimate remains dimension-free for the normalized matrix action. If
\[
(BF)_j=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_i g_{ji}F_i,
\qquad
\|F\|_{\rho,n}^2=\frac1n\sum_i\|F_i\|_\rho^2,
\]
then
\[
\|BF\|_{\rho',n}
\le C(\rho,\rho')\|F\|_{\rho,n},
\]
and similarly for \(B^\top\). Orthogonality of the Gaussian directions controls the creation part. The row annihilation operator satisfies, on the \(k\)-th chaos,
\[
\left\|
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_i a(h_{ji})F_i
\right\|^2
\le
\frac{k}{n}\sum_i\|F_i\|^2,
\]
so its contribution is also uniform in \(n\). Thus two-sided Gaussian action is not itself the obstruction at the level of chaos norms.

Ordinary multiplication is different. Product estimates require strictly stronger input regularity than output regularity. Schematically,
\[
\|FG\|_{\rho'}
\le
C_{\rho,\rho'}\|F\|_\rho\|G\|_\rho
\]
holds only after a definite radius loss, equivalently after paying higher Gaussian \(L^p\) regularity. This follows already from Gaussian hypercontractivity:
\[
\|FG\|_2\le\|F\|_4\|G\|_4,
\]
while controlling \(L^4\) requires increasing chaos weights by a fixed factor.

For \(\phi(x)=x^2\), the loss-gradient vector field has degree as high as \(13\). One Picard substitution therefore requires several product-radius losses. Iterating Picard consumes a fixed multiplicative amount of chaos radius at every level. No fixed positive output radius survives infinitely many iterations. Expressed as an additive Ovsyannikov scale, the \(r\)-th iteration acquires growth comparable to
\[
(r!)^p,\qquad p>1,
\]
which is not canceled by the \(1/r!\) time-ordering factor.

The obstruction is real even for a dissipative scalar flow. For
\[
\dot x=-x^{13},\qquad x(0)=G
\]
with \(G\) Gaussian,
\[
x(t)=\frac{G}{(1+12tG^{12})^{1/12}}
\]
exists globally and is bounded for every \(t>0\). Nevertheless, its formal time coefficients contain
\[
G^{12r+1},
\]
whose \(L^2\)-norm grows superfactorially. The \(L^2\)-valued time-Taylor series has zero radius. Thus real-time dissipativity does not imply existence in a positive analytic-chaos radius or convergence of chaos-Picard truncations.

One can weaken the weights to Kondratiev distribution spaces so that the formal products are continuous. That does not solve the problem: those norms do not imply the \(L^2\), uniform-integrability, Wasserstein, or quadratic-kernel convergence required by the seven properties.

Therefore:

- normalized \(B\) and \(B^\top\) admit explicit, dimension-free creation-annihilation estimates;
- polynomial products admit estimates only with strict regularity loss;
- the degree-\(13\) vector field exhausts the chaos radius under infinite Picard iteration;
- a generalized Ovsyannikov theorem would at best produce a Gevrey/formal generalized solution, not the required \(L^2\) path-law limit.

A successful quadratic proof would need a nonanalytic dissipative argument propagating an entire \(L^p\) or moment hierarchy uniformly in \(n\), rather than a Wiener-chaos Taylor/Galerkin construction.

No activation-independent counterexample was found for pairwise-distinct normalized inputs.

Exact duplicate inputs with opposite labels give a genuine contradiction, but distinctness removes it. Antipodal inputs only obstruct purely even or odd activations; an activation with mixed parity distinguishes them. Initialization symmetries of orthogonal or simplex datasets are broken immediately by the label-dependent residuals.

More strongly, an analytic activation with every Hermite degree nonzero induces
\[
K(x,x')=\sum_{k\ge0}c_k\langle x,x'\rangle^k,\qquad c_k>0.
\]
On any finite set of distinct sphere points, the corresponding polynomial features separate the points, so the Gram matrix is strictly positive definite. Hence arbitrary finite \(\pm1\) labels are representable; no symmetric finite dataset forces a collision.

Multiple fixed samples also do not alter the width-power activity balances—only finite \(O(1)\) Gram factors. Thus any impossibility proof must be dynamical, not based on bias-free representational symmetry. The missing continuous-time random-operator theorem remains a proof gap, not evidence of nonexistence.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 27719 | 2026-08-27T12:30:43.903Z | /root/uniform_lift_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/uniform_lift_audit
Payload:
A naive path-space propagation-of-chaos argument fails because reuse of \(B_0\) and \(B_0^*\) creates an order-one Onsager response.

The trained matrix satisfies exactly
\[
B(t)=B_0-2\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(s)\,
D_b(s)\otimes H_b(s)\,ds.
\]
Hence
\[
S_a(t)=B_0H_a(t)
-2\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(s)D_b(s)
 \langle H_b(s),H_a(t)\rangle\,ds,
\]
and
\[
P_a(t)=B_0^*D_a(t)
-2\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(s)H_b(s)
 \langle D_b(s),D_a(t)\rangle\,ds.
\]
The trained part is governed by empirical two-time correlations. The difficult terms are the adaptive Gaussian actions \(B_0H(t)\) and \(B_0^*D(t)\).

The first feedback expansion already shows why ordinary cavity independence is false. Initially,
\[
P_i=\sum_k B_{ki}D_k.
\]
The variation of \(S_j\) through the updated \(H_i\) contains
\[
\sum_iB_{ji}q(U_i)P_i
=\sum_{i,k}B_{ji}B_{ki}q(U_i)D_k.
\]
The diagonal \(k=j\) term is
\[
D_j\sum_iB_{ji}^2q(U_i)
\longrightarrow D_j\,\mathbb E[q(U)],
\]
which is order one. Removing the reused row while treating the cavity field as independent therefore loses a leading deterministic term; it does not create an \(o(1)\) error.

A correct autonomous limit would need:

- two-time correlations \(Q_H^{ab}(s,t)\) and \(Q_D^{ab}(s,t)\);
- forward and backward Gaussian cavity processes with those covariances;
- response kernels \(R_H,R_D\);
- Volterra memory terms generated by the order-one diagonal response.

This is a dynamic mean-field/Onsager law on augmented path and response data, not a law of one-time marginals.

A rigorous derivation still fails at susceptibility control. Differentiating with respect to one Gaussian edge generates the reused multiplier
\[
(q(U)-q(\widetilde U))P.
\]
Bounded \(\phi\) and \(q\) do not control \(P\) pointwise. Uniform estimates for \(P\), its first edge derivatives, and its second derivatives are needed to retain the order-one response while proving all higher cavity remainders vanish.

Thus the obstruction is quantitative: naive Gaussian conditioning has an explicit order-one error at the first response order. A valid affirmative route would require a rigorous Onsager–Volterra DMFT theorem with uniform response and tail estimates; the current argument does not supply one.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 27745 | 2026-08-27T12:31:35.144Z | /root/functional_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/functional_audit
Payload:
The exact Malliavin hierarchy is not triangular because adaptive reuse of \(B^\top\) costs one additional derivative.

Write one backward source as
\[
P_i=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{j=1}^n g_{ji}D_j(g),
\]
where \(g_{ji}\) are standard Gaussian entries. With the usual divergence convention,
\[
P_i
=
\frac1{\sqrt n}\,\delta_i(D)
+
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_j D_{ji}D_j.
\]
The second term is the adaptive trace or Onsager term. Differentiating \(r\) times gives
\[
D^rP_i
=
\frac1{\sqrt n}\delta_i(D^rD)
+\text{contractions involving }D^{r+1}D
+\text{lower orders}.
\]
Already at \(p=2\), the divergence isometry requires both \(F\) and \(DF\). Consequently,
\[
\|D^rP\|_{L^2}
\lesssim
\|D^rD\|_{L^2(\mathcal H)}
+
\|D^{r+1}D\|_{L^2(\mathcal H^{\otimes2})}
+\cdots.
\]
Thus order \(r\) depends on order \(r+1\).

An analytic Malliavin radius can absorb this single derivative loss:
\[
\sum_{r\ge0}\frac{\rho'^r}{r!}\|D^{r+1}D\|
\lesssim
\frac1{\rho-\rho'}
\sum_{r\ge0}\frac{\rho^r}{r!}\|D^rD\|.
\]
Normalized creation, annihilation, and trace contractions can all be bounded independently of \(n\) in such a scale.

The obstruction appears when this is combined with ordinary products. The differentiated first-layer equation contains
\[
P\,D^rU.
\]
At fixed \(L^p\),
\[
\|P\,D^rU\|_{L^p}
\le
\|P\|_{L^{2p}}\|D^rU\|_{L^{2p}}.
\]
Hence the hierarchy couples
\[
(r,p)\longmapsto(r+1,2p).
\]
A factorial weight in \(r\) alone cannot close this. Moreover, the top variational equation has the form
\[
\partial_tD^rU=P\,D^rU+\text{lower orders},
\]
so its response contains
\[
\exp\!\left(\int_0^tP_s\,ds\right).
\]
Even when \(P\) is Gaussian, its \(L^p\)-moments grow like \(e^{Cp^2}\), i.e. lognormal rather than sub-Gaussian. Therefore the usual polynomial or sub-Gaussian \(p\)-weights are not invariant under the response flow.

Boundedness of \(D\) does not imply a better adaptive Gaussian bound. For example, for one selected column \(i_0\), take
\[
D_j(g)=\tanh(\kappa g_{ji_0}).
\]
Then \(D\) is bounded and analytic, with dimension-free averaged Malliavin derivative norms, but
\[
P_{i_0}
=
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_jg_{ji_0}\tanh(\kappa g_{ji_0})
\sim
\sqrt n\,\mathbb E[G\tanh(\kappa G)].
\]
Only one coordinate is large, so the empirical \(L^2\)-norm of \(P\) remains order one. Nevertheless, \(P^2\) is not uniformly integrable across coordinates. This shows that bounded gates plus averaged all-order Sobolev estimates do not exclude precisely the sparse adaptive alignment that invalidates kernel convergence. Permutation symmetry alone does not repair this: a randomly selected exceptional column gives an exchangeable version of the same phenomenon.

Therefore a complete proof would need a stronger norm that simultaneously provides:

1. analytic-radius control of the derivative loss \(r\mapsto r+1\);
2. a lognormal-or-stronger moment scale stable under \(p\mapsto2p\);
3. exclusion of sparse adaptive column alignment, hence uniform integrability of \(P^2\);
4. dimension-free continuity of the normalized divergence and trace in that stronger scale.

No such closure follows from the bounded analytic activation or the present loss estimates. The exact failed inequality is a same-space estimate for
\[
PX
\quad\text{and}\quad
\delta(D)
\]
without respectively doubling the moment exponent and adding one Malliavin derivative. Thus the proposed all-order Malliavin/Sobolev hierarchy does not yet yield a width-uniform positive-time theorem.

The Celentano–Cheng–Montanari theorem does not directly cover the trained-middle-layer flow.

Their finite-dimensional model is exactly
\[
\dot\theta_t
=-\theta_t\Lambda_t^\top
-\delta^{-1}X^\top\ell_t(X\theta_t;z),
\]
where:

- \(X\) is one fixed iid sub-Gaussian matrix;
- the number of columns of \(\theta\) is fixed;
- \(z,\theta_0\) are independent of \(X\);
- \(\ell_t\) is globally Lipschitz with globally Lipschitz Jacobian;
- \(\Lambda_t\) is deterministic, symmetric, bounded, and Lipschitz.

See [Eq. (12), Assumption 1, and Theorem 2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2112.07572).

For the neural flow,
\[
B(t)=B_0-2\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(s)
D_b(s)\otimes H_b(s)\,ds,
\]
so
\[
P_a(t)
=B_0^*D_a(t)
-2\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(s)H_b(s)
 \langle D_b(s),D_a(t)\rangle\,ds.
\]
Thus the \(U\)-equation contains both \(q(U_a)B_0^*D_a\) and an endogenous path-dependent correlation drift. This cannot be written as either \(-\theta\Lambda_t\) with prescribed deterministic \(\Lambda_t\), or \(X^\top\ell(X\theta)\) for a fixed design \(X\). The integral update of \(B\) is not uniformly finite rank and cannot be encoded with a fixed number of additional columns.

For \(\phi(u)=c+\tanh u\), the coordinate
\[
A(u)=\int^u\frac{dv}{\phi'(v)}
=\frac u2+\frac{\sinh(2u)}4
\]
removes the multiplier \(\phi'(U)\) from the \(U\)-velocity. It does not remove the trained-\(B\) memory term and introduces lognormal-growth initial coordinates. Without this transform,
\[
f(u,p)=\phi'(u)p
\]
has derivative \(\partial_uf=\phi''(u)p\), which is unbounded in \(p\), violating the paper’s global-Lipschitz-Jacobian hypothesis.

The minimal needed extension is a two-population flow with alternating \(X\) and \(X^\top\) messages, rowwise nonlinear updates, endogenous empirical correlation coefficients, and Volterra memory after eliminating \(B\). Fixed-step AMP technology may plausibly analyze such a system, but the cited continuous-time proof does not establish it: its contraction and Euler estimates rely on the global Lipschitz hypotheses that fail for the reused-adjoint multiplier.

Finally, their general theorem gives weak convergence of empirical path laws. The Gaussian strengthening concerns Wasserstein convergence of finite-dimensional distributions; it does not automatically provide path-\(W_2\), uniform integrability of velocities and kernels, or all seven requested properties.

Therefore there is no exact mapping. Extending the theorem would require a new two-population AMP reduction and new continuous-time estimates controlling the unbounded adaptive response \(P=B^*D\).

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 27973 | 2026-08-27T12:41:07.650Z | /root/tp_scope_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/tp_scope_audit
Payload:
The CCM theorem cannot be invoked to justify the proposed trained two-population flow.

Its microscopic model is specifically
\[
\dot\theta=-\theta\Lambda(t)^\top-\delta^{-1}X^\top\ell_t(X\theta;z),
\]
with fixed channel dimension, static marks \(z\), externally prescribed deterministic \(\Lambda(t)\), and globally Lipschitz \(\ell_t\) whose full space-time Jacobian is globally Lipschitz. Its continuous-time and diagonal Euler conclusions apply to this class.

The proposed dynamics depart from that class in three essential ways:

- The term \(q(U)\odot B^\top D\) applies an evolving coordinatewise gate after the transpose multiplication. It cannot be represented by the allowed deterministic channel mixing \(-\theta\Lambda^\top\), even using fixed-dimensional augmentation.
- The row-side variable \(D\) has its own dynamics and history; CCM’s row variable is only \(X\theta\), with the row message an instantaneous function \(\ell_t((X\theta)_i;z_i)\).
- Eliminating the trained rank-one part of \(B(t)\) creates endogenous empirical overlaps. CCM permits externally prescribed coefficients, so substituting deterministic limiting overlaps would assume the propagation-of-chaos conclusion that must be proved.

The proof is correspondingly non-portable: one would need a new alternating two-population AMP reduction, additional covariance and response kernels, a new fixed-point theorem and uniqueness argument, control of endogenous overlaps, and a uniform Euler-to-flow estimate. For polynomial-growth activations, CCM’s global Lipschitz assumptions also fail; even bounded activations leave bilinear gated messages that are not globally Lipschitz without localization and new moment estimates.

Thus CCM is useful as a proof blueprint, but not as an applicable external theorem. The proposed proof has a fatal theorem-scope gap unless those new results are proved independently.

The activation
\[
\phi(u)=c+\tanh u+\varepsilon\tanh^2u,
\qquad c\varepsilon\neq0,\quad |\varepsilon|<\frac12,
\]
has two valuable properties, but the cited continuous-AMP theorem does not cover the resulting network flow.

Set
\[
T^a=\tanh U^a,\qquad
H^a=c+T^a+\varepsilon(T^a)^2,
\]
and
\[
\psi(T)=(1-T^2)(1+2\varepsilon T)=\phi'(U).
\]
Because \(|\varepsilon|<1/2\), \(T\mapsto H\) is invertible on \((-1,1)\), and \(\psi\) is a bounded smooth function of either \(T\) or \(H\). Moreover,
\[
\dot T^a=(1-(T^a)^2)\dot U^a,
\]
so every coordinatewise gate in the transformed equations is bounded.

With normalized output weights \(C=nW^{(3)}\), define
\[
D^a=C\,\psi(T_2^a),\qquad
e_a=f_a-y_a.
\]
Eliminating the trained middle matrix gives the exact Volterra representation
\[
B_t
=
B_0-\frac2m\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(r)\,
D_r^b\otimes_n H_r^b\,dr.
\]
Consequently,
\[
S_t^a
=
B_0H_t^a
-\frac2m\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(r)D_r^b
\langle H_r^b,H_t^a\rangle_n\,dr,
\]
and
\[
P_t^a
=
B_0^\top D_t^a
-\frac2m\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(r)H_r^b
\langle D_r^b,D_t^a\rangle_n\,dr.
\]
The remaining equations are
\[
\dot U_i^a
=
-\frac2m\sum_bR_{ab}e_b\,\psi(T_i^b)P_i^b,
\qquad
\dot C_j
=
-\frac2m\sum_be_b\,\phi(S_j^b).
\]
Thus the exact state is intrinsically two-sorted: \(H,U\) evolve on the right population, while \(C,S,D\) evolve on the left population.

I checked the full setting, assumptions, main convergence statements, and continuous/discrete approximation sections of Celentano–Cheng–Montanari, [*The high-dimensional asymptotics of first order methods with random data*](https://arxiv.org/html/2112.07572v3). Their flow is
\[
\dot\theta
=
-\theta\Lambda_t^\top-\delta^{-1}X^\top\ell_t(X\theta;z),
\]
where:

- \(\theta\in\mathbb R^{d\times k}\) has fixed \(k\);
- \(z\) is fixed side information independent of \(X\);
- \(\ell_t(r;z)\) is a current-state rowwise function;
- \(\ell_t\) and its Jacobian are globally Lipschitz uniformly in time;
- the only evolving high-dimensional state is on one side of \(X\).

Their Theorem 1 proves existence and uniqueness of the associated one-sided DMFT; Theorem 2 proves empirical path-law convergence. For Gaussian matrices, quadratic-growth test functions receive a stronger finite-dimensional conclusion. Their discretization/AMP argument also supports a diagonal \(\eta_n\to0\) limit for flows in that class.

Our network is not in that class. The left variable \(C\) evolves and feeds back into \(D\). It is neither fixed side information nor an algebraic function of \(B_0H\). Eliminating \(C\) makes the row nonlinearity depend on its entire previous path. Representing this memory by a fixed number of columns is impossible as \(\eta\to0\). Hence the theorem cannot be invoked without proving a new two-population continuous-AMP extension.

There is, however, a rigorous universal strict-positive-definiteness result for this activation. Suppose
\[
\sum_{a=1}^m\alpha_a\phi(w\cdot x_a)=0
\quad\text{for every }w.
\]
Comparing \(w\) and \(-w\) gives separate relations
\[
\sum_a\alpha_a\tanh(w\cdot x_a)=0,
\]
and
\[
\sum_a\alpha_a\bigl(c+\varepsilon\tanh^2(w\cdot x_a)\bigr)=0.
\]
Group inputs by unoriented direction, allowing antipodal pairs. The odd relation involves the coefficient differences \(\alpha_+-\alpha_-\); the even relation involves the sums \(\alpha_++\alpha_-\).

Choose a generic \(z\) such that the numbers
\[
\beta_r=|z\cdot v_r|
\]
for the distinct unoriented directions are positive and pairwise distinct. Along \(w=tz\),
\[
\tanh(\beta t)=1-2e^{-2\beta t}+O(e^{-4\beta t}),
\]
\[
\tanh^2(\beta t)=1-4e^{-2\beta t}+O(e^{-4\beta t}).
\]
Ordering the distinct \(\beta_r\) and successively comparing the slowest decaying exponential forces every antipodal difference and every antipodal sum to vanish. Hence all \(\alpha_a=0\).

Therefore the ridge functions \(w\mapsto\phi(w\cdot x_a)\) are linearly independent for every finite pairwise-distinct set of sphere inputs. Since Gaussian measure has full support, the corresponding Gaussian feature Gram matrix is strictly positive definite.

This resolves the initialization output-kernel rank issue, including antipodal datasets. It does not prove:

- the required two-sorted DMFT convergence;
- path-\(W_2\) and kernel-observable uniform integrability;
- response-kernel uniqueness for the coupled Volterra system;
- the exact-GD joint diagonal limit;
- nonconstant total-kernel evolution for every dataset and labeling.

The activation makes the missing theorem considerably more plausible because all transformed gates are bounded and smooth. The remaining bridge is a rigorous two-sorted continuous-AMP theorem with co-evolving row variables on both sides of the reused Gaussian matrix. The cited CCM theorem does not itself supply that bridge.

The near-identity analytic-continuation route has a fatal gap as currently stated.

Finite-width analyticity in \(\alpha\), even together with a complete theorem at \(\alpha=0\), does not imply convergence for any fixed \(\alpha\ne0\). One needs a width- and step-size-independent complex neighborhood, uniform bounds there, and convergence of the entire Taylor series under a summable majorant. Fixed-order nonlinear insertions are insufficient.

There is also a functional-analytic obstruction: a coordinatewise analytic activation need not define an analytic map \(L^2\to L^2\), since
\[
D^2F(u)[h,k]=\phi''(u)hk
\]
is generally only in \(L^1\). Moving to an \(L^\infty\)-based Banach algebra encounters Gaussian extrema of order \(\sqrt{\log n}\), which can make the analyticity radius shrink with width. For sine/cosine perturbations, complexified derivatives grow exponentially with the imaginary part, so bounded real derivatives do not solve this.

Moreover, derivatives in \(\alpha\) generate adaptive diagonal and Hadamard operations interleaved with reused \(B,B^\top\). A theorem for deep-linear words does not cover these decorated nonlinear words, whose degree and moment requirements grow with derivative order. Nor does it provide uniform control over the diverging number of GD steps.

A viable repair would require proving all of the following:

- a common complex \(\alpha\)-disk with width-uniform trajectory bounds;
- convergence of every Taylor coefficient for the full decorated random-operator calculus;
- an \(n\)-independent summable coefficient majorant;
- uniform integrability of states and velocities sufficient for path-\(W_2\), kernels, and action observables;
- an Euler error controlled by macroscopic time rather than the number of steps;
- uniqueness of the resulting autonomous analytic law-level flow.

None of these follows from a deep-linear theorem. Until supplied, analytic continuation does not establish the proposed nonlinear compact-time limit.

The \(\alpha\)-expansion is borderline but not demonstrably divergent.

For
\[
\phi_\alpha(x)=x+\alpha h(x),\qquad
\phi_\alpha'(x)=1+\alpha h'(x),
\]
the troublesome multiplier becomes linear at \(\alpha=0\). For each fixed derivative order \(k\), and after fixed Picard or time discretization, the coefficient is built from finitely many applications of \(B_0,B_0^*\), coordinate maps \(h^{(j)}\), contractions, and lower-order coefficients. Fixed-\(k\) width convergence is therefore plausible once the deep-linear continuous-time limit is rigorously established.

The coefficient growth does not yield an obvious zero-radius result. A useful threshold is
\[
\frac{Z^{dk}}{k!}.
\]
Its \(L^2\) norm behaves, up to exponential factors, as
\[
k^{(d/2-1)k}.
\]
Thus \(d\le2\) is compatible with positive radius, while \(d>2\) forces zero radius. The initial reused response \(P_0=B_0^*C_0\) is effectively second-chaos/subexponential, precisely the borderline \(d=2\) case. Perturbative tree counting appears to create at most \(O(2k)\) Gaussian leaves per order \(k\); the Gaussian moment growth can therefore be balanced by \(k!\). Raw polynomial degree alone cannot prove divergence because normalized matrix sums create contractions.

A positive-radius proof would require a width-uniform estimate such as
\[
\sup_n\mathbb E\exp\!\left(
c\sup_{t\le T}L_n(t)
\right)<\infty,
\]
where \(L_n\) controls the deep-linear response \(P_0\) and its variational operators. This would permit Bell-polynomial and tree majorants. Existing normalized \(L^2\) and operator-norm bounds do not imply such exponential integrability.

Conversely, a lognormal response envelope would destroy the radius, but no such mechanism is forced. Spectral exponentials involving \(B_0B_0^*\) are controlled by
\[
e^{T\|B_0\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2},
\]
and \(\|B_0\|_{\mathrm{op}}\) is tight.

Hence fixed Taylor coefficients may possess width limits, but uniform summability in \(k\) is unproved. The missing result is a uniform subexponential estimate for the full deep-linear susceptibility hierarchy. No rigorous positive-radius theorem or superfactorial counterexample currently follows.

Let \(Y_n(t,\alpha)\) denote the normalized parameter/state vector. At every fixed \(n\),
\[
\dot Y_n=F_n(Y_n,\alpha),
\]
and for \(h=\sin\), \(\cos\), or \(\sin+\cos\), \(F_n\) is real analytic in \(\alpha\). Defining
\[
Y_{n,r}(t)=\partial_\alpha^rY_n(t,\alpha)\big|_{\alpha=0},
\]
gives the exact triangular variational system
\[
\dot Y_{n,r}
=
A_n(t)Y_{n,r}
+
G_{n,r}(Y_{n,0},\ldots,Y_{n,r-1}),
\qquad
A_n(t)=D_YF_n(Y_{n,0}(t),0).
\]
Here \(G_{n,r}\) is a finite Faà di Bruno sum whose nonlinear insertions are derivatives \(h^{(k)}\) evaluated along the deep-linear trajectory.

At \(\alpha=0\), \(\phi'=1\). Hence \(A_n(t)\) contains no random diagonal activation multiplier. On an event where the Gaussian middle matrix has bounded operator norm and the deep-linear factor Grams remain bounded, the propagator satisfies
\[
\|\mathcal U_n(t,s)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le e^{C(T)(t-s)}
\]
uniformly in \(n\). Loss dissipation and the deep-linear balancedness identities plausibly provide these factor bounds on compact time intervals. Thus every fixed \(r\) reduces to finitely many Gaussian-matrix/vector expressions, and a fixed-order tensor-program or random-operator limit is plausible.

The obstruction is uniformity in \(r\). Coordinatewise differentiation generates multilinear products, while empirical \(L^2\) has no dimension-free algebra estimate:
\[
\|v_1\cdots v_k\|_{2,n}
\not\le C^k\prod_j\|v_j\|_{2,n}.
\]
A one-coordinate spike gives a factor \(n^{(k-1)/2}\). For genuinely Gaussian-like coordinates, the corresponding product norm is instead
\[
\sqrt{(2k-1)!!}\asymp (C\sqrt{k})^k.
\]
This growth is still dominated by \(C^kk!\), so ordinary Gaussian moments do not disprove analytic dependence. The required majorant would follow from a width-uniform joint sub-Gaussian estimate for every perturbative source and response field.

That estimate is not a consequence of operator-norm bounds. Reusing \(B_0\) and \(B_0^\top\) allows the perturbative fields to depend adaptively on the same columns whose cancellation is needed. Fixed-order tensor-program convergence proves the relevant moment limit separately for every \(r\), but gives no control on how its constants grow with \(r\), nor does it exclude sparse adaptive alignment.

A complex-analytic argument faces the same issue. Since
\[
|\sin(x+iy)|,\ |\cos(x+iy)|\lesssim e^{|y|},
\]
a uniform Cauchy estimate
\[
\|Y_{n,r}\|_{2,n}\le C^rr!
\]
would follow from a width-uniform complex-\(\alpha\) neighborhood with exponential-moment control of all imaginary coordinate fields. Deterministic empirical \(L^2\) and operator bounds do not provide that exponential control.

I do not find an actual-orbit Gevrey counterexample. Simple Gaussian insertions do not suffice: derivatives such as \(G^r\) grow only like \((C\sqrt r)^r\), which is smaller than \(C^rr!\). Therefore the analytic-majorant claim remains plausible, but unproved. The exact missing estimate is a full-order adaptive tail bound, not fixed-order convergence.

There is also an activation-specific obstruction. For
\[
\phi_\alpha(x)=x+\alpha\sin x,
\]
the network is odd:
\[
f_n(-x)=-f_n(x).
\]
On the equal-label antipodal dataset \((x,+1),(-x,+1)\),
\[
L_n=1+f_n(x)^2,\qquad
\nabla L_n=2f_n(x)\nabla f_n(x).
\]
Thus any self-averaging initialization with \(f_n(0)\to0\) has a stationary limiting flow, violating feature motion and loss decrease. A CLT-sized random initial output requires the separate first-layer scaling obstruction to close that loophole. Consequently the pure sine perturbation cannot support a universal dataset theorem. A mixed choice such as \(h=\sin+\cos\) avoids this parity obstruction.

No deep-linear external theorem is needed for the conclusions above, and I did not invoke one. A complete perturbative theorem would still require:

1. compact-time deep-linear operator convergence;
2. fixed-order variational convergence;
3. a width- and order-uniform exponential tail estimate;
4. Cauchy resummation in a common complex-\(\alpha\) disk;
5. verification of the seven dynamical properties for the chosen nonzero \(\alpha\).

The third step is presently the hard unresolved bridge.

No directly applicable rigorous theorem was found.

The closest result is Celentano–Cheng–Montanari, arXiv:2112.07572v3. It proves compact-time path-law convergence and a joint Euler/width limit for
\[
\dot\theta=-\theta\Lambda(t)^\top-\delta^{-1}X^\top\ell_t(X\theta;z),
\]
but its class excludes the proposed system’s independently evolving row population, the adaptive column-side gate \(q(U)\odot B^\top D\), and endogenous overlaps from the trained rank-one matrix drift.

Discrete AMP theorems do admit alternating \(B,B^\top\) with nonlinear messages, but only for a fixed number of Onsager-corrected iterations. Here the scaling requirements force \(\eta=o(n^{-1})\), hence \(T/\eta\gg n\), so fixed-iteration and polylogarithmic-iteration results cannot establish the required continuous-time limit.

Rigorous random-neural-network and spin-glass path theorems cover either one-sided asymmetric disorder or special symmetric Hamiltonian models. Block symmetrization does not resolve this: the resulting matrix has a bipartite variance profile, exact reciprocal correlations, adaptive gates, and an endogenously trained finite-rank component.

Thus an affirmative proof still needs a new two-population continuous-time AMP/DMFT theorem, including:

- reciprocal response and Onsager kernels;
- uniqueness of the enlarged causal fixed-point system;
- endogenous overlap and low-rank-feedback control;
- localization and uniform moments for bilinear gates;
- path-\(W_2\), velocity-action, and kernel uniform integrability;
- a width-uniform Euler estimate depending on macroscopic time, not step count.

This is the central unresolved theorem-scale gap.

A universal finite-dataset obstruction is impossible for mixed-parity activations. The activation
\[
\phi(x)=\sin x+\cos x
\]
gives a strictly positive-definite feature kernel on every finite set of distinct normalized inputs.

If \(W\sim N(0,\tau^2I)\), then
\[
\mathbb E[\phi(W\!\cdot x)\phi(W\!\cdot y)]
=e^{-\tau^2+\tau^2\langle x,y\rangle}
=e^{-\frac{\tau^2}{2}\|x-y\|^2}.
\]
The Gaussian RBF kernel is strictly positive definite. Indeed,
\[
\sum_{a,b}c_ac_b e^{-\gamma\|x_a-x_b\|^2}
=C_\gamma\int
\left|\sum_a c_ae^{i\omega\cdot x_a}\right|^2
e^{-\|\omega\|^2/(4\gamma)}\,d\omega.
\]
If this vanishes, the exponential polynomial vanishes identically. Choose \(v\) such that the numbers \(\langle v,x_a\rangle\) are distinct. Restriction to \(\omega=tv\), followed by differentiation at \(t=0\), gives a Vandermonde system and hence \(c_a=0\) for every \(a\).

At the next Gaussian layer, the covariance is a positive multiple of this kernel and has constant diagonal. Applying \(\sin+\cos\) again yields
\[
K_2(a,b)
=\exp\!\left[-\frac12
 \|\Psi(x_a)-\Psi(x_b)\|^2\right],
\]
where \(\langle\Psi(x_a),\Psi(x_b)\rangle\) is the preceding covariance. The vectors \(\Psi(x_a)\) remain distinct because
\[
\|\Psi(x_a)-\Psi(x_b)\|^2
\propto
2\left(1-e^{-\gamma\|x_a-x_b\|^2}\right)>0.
\]
The same Fourier argument on their finite-dimensional span proves that \(K_2\) is strictly positive definite.

Since \(\phi\) is bounded,
\[
K_n^{(3)}=\lambda_{3,n}\sum_j h_jh_j^\top.
\]
Finite nonzero output-block activity forces \(\lambda_{3,n}n\to\kappa\in(0,\infty)\); otherwise this block either vanishes uniformly or diverges at initialization. Therefore
\[
K^{(3)}(0)=\kappa K_2
\]
is strictly positive definite. For every nonzero residual,
\[
\dot L(0)=-4e(0)^\top K(0)e(0)<0.
\]

This includes antipodal and arbitrarily near-distinct inputs. Near duplicates can make the smallest eigenvalue very small, but not zero, and dataset-dependent time intervals are allowed. Thus no pairwise-distinct finite dataset can force a universal symmetry cancellation, tangent-kernel nullity, or initial loss stasis across all admissible activations and scalings. This eliminates the dataset-obstruction route, though it does not establish the remaining compact-time convergence properties.

Payload:
The transformed tanh activation makes every scalar gate bounded, and every fixed Picard depth is tractable. It does not yield a dimension-uniform contraction in an empirical \(L^2\)-type state space.

Let
\[
T^a=\tanh U^a,\qquad
H^a=c+T^a+\varepsilon(T^a)^2,
\]
and
\[
\psi(T)=(1-T^2)(1+2\varepsilon T)=\phi'(U).
\]
With \(D^a=C\,\psi(T_2^a)\), eliminating the trained middle matrix gives
\[
B_t
=
B_0-\frac2m\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(r)D_r^b\otimes_nH_r^b\,dr,
\]
\[
S_t^a
=
B_0H_t^a
-\frac2m\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(r)D_r^b
\langle H_r^b,H_t^a\rangle_n\,dr,
\]
\[
P_t^a
=
B_0^\top D_t^a
-\frac2m\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(r)H_r^b
\langle D_r^b,D_t^a\rangle_n\,dr.
\]
The row dynamics are
\[
\dot T_i^a
=
-\frac2m(1-(T_i^a)^2)
\sum_bR_{ab}e_b\,\psi(T_i^b)P_i^b,
\]
\[
\dot C_j
=
-\frac2m\sum_be_b\,\phi(S_j^b).
\]
On finite intervals, \(C\) has a deterministic envelope obtained from
\[
|\dot C_j|
\le C_0\left(1+\langle |C|,1\rangle_n\right),
\]
and all functions of \(T\in[-1,1]\) are smooth and bounded. Thus every fixed Picard iterate consists of finitely many applications of

- \(B_0\) and \(B_0^\top\);
- bounded coordinatewise nonlinearities;
- empirical inner products;
- finite-rank Volterra updates.

Accordingly, every fixed Picard depth is compatible with a fixed-step tensor-program or graph-polynomial analysis.

The obstruction is the derivative of the Picard map. The \(T\)-equation has the form
\[
\dot T=a(T)\,P+\cdots
\]
with a nonconstant bounded \(a\). Its linearization contains
\[
\operatorname{diag}(P)a'(T)\,\delta T.
\]
In normalized empirical \(L^2\),
\[
\|\operatorname{diag}(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
=
\max_i|P_i|.
\]
Hence no width-independent contraction constant exists whenever the source coordinates have Gaussian-scale maxima. At positive time, the leading source is
\[
P=B_0^\top D+\text{finite-rank memory}.
\]
For a nondegenerate, non-adaptively aligned \(D\), its coordinates have order-one Gaussian fluctuations and
\[
\max_i|P_i|\asymp\sqrt{\log n}.
\]
The adaptive case requires a separate proof of this asymptotic, but boundedness of \(D\) cannot improve the deterministic derivative estimate.

More fundamentally, on every open empirical \(L^2\) neighborhood one has the exact spike obstruction. Choose one coordinate \(i_*\) with \(a'(T_{i_*})\neq0\), put
\[
P_{i_*}=\sqrt n,\qquad \delta T_{i_*}=n^{-1/2},
\]
and set the remaining coordinates to zero. Then
\[
\|P\|_{2,n}=1,\qquad
\|\delta T\|_{2,n}=n^{-1},
\]
but
\[
\|\operatorname{diag}(P)a'(T)\delta T\|_{2,n}
\asymp n^{-1/2}.
\]
The derivative ratio is \(O(\sqrt n)\). Thus no open Banach space whose norm is uniformly equivalent to empirical \(L^2\) can make the Picard map uniformly contractive.

A weighted norm such as
\[
\|\delta T\|_{P}^{2}
=
\langle(1+P^2)|\delta T|^2\rangle_n
\]
controls one multiplication by \(P\), but the next Picard step changes \(P\), and \(B_0\) mixes the coordinate weights. Closing the iteration requires weights containing all products of past sources. This reproduces the full response hierarchy rather than a finite reachable-state norm.

There is no immediate probabilistic divergence. Formally, the \(k\)-th response contribution resembles
\[
\frac{t^k}{k!}P^k.
\]
For Gaussian \(P\),
\[
\|P^k\|_2\asymp(C\sqrt{k})^k,
\]
so the factorial denominator is sufficient for summability. Therefore the failure of deterministic contraction does not prove that the random Picard series diverges.

The missing result is an all-depth estimate for the adaptive reused-matrix sources, for example
\[
\sup_n
\left\|
P_{n,k}
\right\|_{L^p(\text{particle}\times\text{initialization})}
\le
C^k e^{Ckp}
\]
with constants strong enough to sum the Picard tail and to give uniform integrability of \(P^2\). Fixed-depth tensor-program convergence supplies no such uniform-in-\(k\) estimate.

Thus the rigorous outcome is:

- exact bounded-state Volterra formulation: yes;
- fixed-depth tensor/traffic approximation: yes;
- dimension-uniform deterministic Picard contraction: no;
- probabilistic all-depth Picard convergence: not proved and not refuted;
- full path-\(W_2\), kernel, and exact-GD diagonal convergence: still open.

The precise remaining bridge is an adaptive all-depth tail theorem for alternating \(B_0,B_0^\top\) programs.

Payload:
Standard Hida, Kondratiev, Fock, and Malliavin analytic-scale theorems do not supply the required width-uniform ODE limit.

The main obstructions are:

- Våge-type estimates control the Wick product, whereas the network uses ordinary multiplication. Since
  \[
  \xi^2=\xi\diamond\xi+1,
  \]
  substituting Wick multiplication changes the dynamics.
- Ordinary multiplication is continuous on the Hida test Fréchet space only with loss of seminorm. This is not a fixed Banach-algebra estimate suitable for dimension-uniform Picard iteration.
- Hida norms weight Gaussian modes by eigenvalues \(\lambda_j\to\infty\). Embedding \(n^2\) matrix entries into these modes makes coordinate norms diverge with \(n\); uniformly bounded weights would destroy the required nuclear embeddings.
- Generic Gaussian norms see the normalized matrix \(B\) at Frobenius/Cameron–Martin scale \(O(\sqrt n)\), not its random-matrix operator scale \(O(1)\). Consequently, generic ODE lifespan estimates shrink with width.
- Malliavin spaces are not algebras at fixed \(L^p\): product estimates require successively higher \(p\), while differentiation through reused \(B,B^\top\) introduces sums over \(n^2\) Gaussian directions.
- Ovsyannikov theory would require a dimension-independent scale-loss constant and uniformly bounded initial analytic norm. Existing white-noise multiplication theorems do not establish either condition here.

A successful version would need a new permutation-invariant chaos norm adapted to matrix contractions, uniform ordinary-product estimates, simultaneous boundedness of \(B\) and \(B^\top\), and compactness/UI estimates for path-\(W_2\) and velocity actions. Developing this is essentially a new traffic/DMFT theorem, not an application of existing Hida theory.

Payload:
A direct time-power-series proof has zero radius in the required \(L^2\) topology, already in the minimal reused-multiplier subsystem.

Freeze a nondegenerate Gaussian source \(P\) and consider
\[
u'(t)=P(\cos u(t)-\sin u(t)),\qquad u(0)=0.
\]
Setting \(v=u+\pi/4\) gives
\[
v'=\sqrt2\,P\cos v,
\]
and therefore
\[
\sec v+\tan v=(\sqrt2+1)e^{\sqrt2Pt}.
\]
Thus
\[
u(t)=g(Pt),
\]
where \(g\) is analytic and nonpolynomial but has finite complex singularities. If
\[
g(z)=\sum_{k\ge0}c_kz^k,
\]
then
\[
\limsup_k|c_k|^{1/k}=R^{-1}>0.
\]
The \(L^2\)-valued time coefficient is \(c_kP^k\). For Gaussian \(P\),
\[
\|P^k\|_2
=\bigl(\mathbb E|P|^{2k}\bigr)^{1/2},
\qquad
\|P^k\|_2^{1/k}\asymp\sqrt{k}.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\limsup_k\|c_kP^k\|_2^{1/k}=\infty,
\]
so the \(L^2\)-valued Taylor series has radius zero.

The key point is that the Taylor coefficient \(c_k\) has already absorbed the \(1/k!\). Scalar analyticity yields only exponential decay of \(c_k\), which cannot offset Gaussian moment growth. Normalized averaging over neurons does not help: the expected empirical squared coefficient equals the typical-coordinate \(L^2\) moment.

In the full network, time dependence of \(P\) adds diagrams, so this is not a nonexistence theorem for the real-time flow. But repeated differentiation contains the frozen-source branch proportional to \(P(0)^k\). A Taylor proof would need an exact cancellation of these terms against all response diagrams, and no generic identity provides it.

Therefore Wick/time-series estimates cannot supply a uniform positive radius in path-\(W_2\) or normalized \(L^2\). Compact-time convergence must instead be proved through real-time stability, cavity/AMP, or another nonanalytic-in-time method.

A dimension-free estimate exists for the Gaussian matrix action, but the trigonometric composition destroys every positive exponential-chaos radius. This prevents a standard Fock/Ovsyannikov proof before the two-sided reuse difficulty is even reached.

Let
\[
F=\sum_{k\ge0}I_k(f_k),\qquad
\|F\|_\rho^2
=
\sum_{k\ge0}\rho^{2k}k!\|f_k\|^2,
\qquad \rho>1.
\]
For \(g=I_1(h)\), \(\|h\|=1\),
\[
gI_k(f_k)
=
I_{k+1}(h\widetilde\otimes f_k)
+kI_{k-1}(h\mathbin{\lrcorner}f_k).
\]
Consequently, for \(1<\rho'<\rho\),
\[
\|gF\|_{\rho'}
\le
C(\rho,\rho')\|F\|_\rho,
\]
where one may take
\[
C(\rho,\rho')
\lesssim
(\rho'+\rho^{-1})
\left(1-\frac{\rho'^2}{\rho^2}\right)^{-1/2}.
\]

For
\[
(BF)_j=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_i g_{ji}F_i,
\qquad
\|F\|_{\rho,n}^2=\frac1n\sum_i\|F_i\|_\rho^2,
\]
orthogonality of the creation directions and the number-operator bound for annihilation yield
\[
\|BF\|_{\rho',n}
+
\|B^\top F\|_{\rho',n}
\le
C(\rho,\rho')\|F\|_{\rho,n},
\]
uniformly in \(n\). The adaptive divergence/trace decomposition costs one additional Malliavin derivative, hence one further radius loss, but it also has the correct normalized \(n\)-scaling.

The decisive problem is composition by \(\sin\) and \(\cos\). Take one standard Gaussian \(G\) and
\[
X=G^3.
\]
Since \(X\) is finite chaos, \(X\in\mathcal H_\rho\) for every finite \(\rho\). Let
\[
Y=e^{iG^3}.
\]
Repeated Malliavin differentiation gives
\[
D^kY=e^{iG^3}P_k(G),
\]
where \(P_k\) is a polynomial of degree \(2k\) with leading coefficient \((3i)^k\). Because the probabilists’ Hermite polynomial \(H_{2k}\) is monic and has squared Gaussian norm \((2k)!\),
\[
\|D^kY\|_2
=
\|P_k(G)\|_2
\ge
3^k\sqrt{(2k)!}.
\]

For every \(F\in L^2\), the chaos/Malliavin identity is
\[
\|\Gamma(\rho)F\|_2^2
=
\sum_{k=0}^\infty
\frac{(\rho^2-1)^k}{k!}\,
\mathbb E\|D^kF\|^2.
\]
Therefore
\[
\|Y\|_\rho^2
\ge
\sum_{k=0}^\infty
\frac{(\rho^2-1)^k}{k!}\,9^k(2k)!.
\]
The ratio of successive terms is asymptotic to
\[
36(\rho^2-1)k,
\]
so the series diverges for every \(\rho>1\). Hence
\[
e^{iG^3}\notin\mathcal H_\rho
\qquad\text{for every }\rho>1.
\]

For the proposed activation,
\[
\phi=\sin+\cos,\qquad
\phi'=\cos-\sin.
\]
From \(\phi(X)\) and \(\phi'(X)\) one recovers both \(\sin X\) and \(\cos X\), hence \(e^{iX}\). Thus the pair of Nemytskii maps required by forward and backward propagation cannot map an open positive-radius Fock space into any weaker positive-radius Fock space.

This is stronger than a large Lipschitz constant: the output leaves the entire exponential-chaos scale after a single composition. Therefore no standard Ovsyannikov theorem based on
\[
\mathcal H_\rho\to\mathcal H_{\rho'},\qquad \rho'<\rho,
\]
can apply.

One could weaken the factorial weights to a Kondratiev or Gevrey scale large enough to contain \(e^{iG^3}\). That creates two new problems:

- the resulting time expansions are generally Gevrey rather than convergent analytic Picard series;
- weak Kondratiev norms do not imply the \(L^2\), Wasserstein, uniform-integrability, and quadratic-kernel convergence required by the problem.

A custom reachable-state class restricting growth along Cameron–Martin directions might avoid the \(G^3\) example, because bounded real gates can constrain actual source-ray growth. But such a class is not an open Fock scale, and no dimension-uniform completeness, product, or two-sided \(B/B^\top\) theorem has been established for it.

Thus:

- Gaussian creation/annihilation and the normalized reused matrix have the correct dimension-free radius-loss bounds;
- trigonometric Nemytskii composition fails on every positive exponential-chaos scale;
- a Fock/Ovsyannikov local existence and all-depth convergence proof cannot be completed in that scale.

The decisive failure is nonlinear composition, not the \(n\)-scaling of \(B_0\) or \(B_0^\top\).

No audited external theorem applies to the exact normalized \(B/B^\top\) dynamics.

The precise failures are:

- Continuous-time DMFT results such as Celentano–Cheng–Montanari cover only flows of the form
  \[
  \dot\theta=-\theta\Lambda(t)^\top-\delta^{-1}X^\top\ell_t(X\theta;z).
  \]
  They exclude an independently evolving row population, post-\(X^\top\) coordinate gates, and endogenous low-rank/overlap feedback.
- AMP, generalized-first-order-method, and Tensor Program theorems permit reused \(B,B^\top\) only for fixed or limited iteration counts. Here admissibility forces \(\eta=o(n^{-1})\), hence \(T/\eta\gg n\), outside those quantifiers.
- Classical propagation-of-chaos and graphon theorems use \(1/n\) law-of-large-numbers interactions. The centered \(1/\sqrt n\) Gaussian interaction has zero graphon limit although its dynamical Gaussian field remains order one.
- Rigorous random-neural-network theorems generally assume independent directed disorder; spin-glass results use special symmetric Hamiltonian structures. Neither contains arbitrary reciprocal bipartite gates and trained disorder.
- Strong operator and traffic convergence controls fixed polynomials or finite diagrams, not adaptive positive-time ODE functional calculus, path-\(W_2\), or velocity actions.
- The trained finite-rank component can be handled algebraically only after convergence of the histories and endogenous overlaps—the main missing result.

Thus the defensible conclusion is not that no theorem exists anywhere, but that none of the principal rigorous frameworks examined applies. Closing the proof requires a new reciprocal two-population continuous-time state-evolution theorem with response kernels, endogenous overlap feedback, uniform moments, path-\(W_2\)/action convergence, and a mesh error uniform over \(T/\eta\gg n\) steps.

The only quantifier inconsistency is the literal inclusion of \(T=0\). At \(T=0\),

\[
\int_0^T V^{(\ell)}(t)^2\,dt=0,\qquad
\int_0^T K^{(\ell)}(t)\,dt=0,
\]
and \(L(T)<L(0)\) is impossible. The intended statement must be read as requiring the properties for every
\[
0<T<T_*.
\]

Under that natural correction, I find no universal dataset obstruction.

Exact initial fitting would make gradient flow stationary, but the viable centered mean-field initialization has deterministic limiting output \(f(0)=0\), whereas every \(\{\pm1\}\)-valued label vector is nonzero. Exact fitting under continuous Gaussian initialization also has probability zero.

More importantly, \(\phi=\sin+\cos\) defeats every symmetry-based construction. Its Gaussian feature kernel is
\[
k(x,y)=e^{-\tau^2\|x-y\|^2/2},
\]
which is strictly positive definite on every finite set of pairwise-distinct normalized inputs. The second Gaussian layer preserves strict positive definiteness through the induced feature-space Gaussian kernel. Nonzero finite output-block activity forces
\[
\lambda_{3,n}n\to\kappa\in(0,\infty),
\]
so \(K^{(3)}(0)\) is strictly positive definite. Hence every nonzero residual satisfies
\[
\dot L(0)=-4e(0)^\top K(0)e(0)<0.
\]

Therefore antipodes, label characters, Gram nullspaces, linear dependencies, and neuron-permutation symmetries cannot force loss stasis or tangent-kernel degeneracy. Near-duplicate inputs only make the smallest eigenvalue small; dataset-dependent constants and existence times are allowed.

The scaling
\[
\sigma_{2,n}\asymp n^{-1/2},\quad
\sigma_{3,n}\asymp n^{-1},\quad
(\lambda_{1,n},\lambda_{2,n},\lambda_{3,n})
\asymp(n,1,n^{-1})
\]
is also internally consistent with all block activities when \(\eta_{0,n}=o(n^{-1})\).

Thus, after explicitly restricting to \(T>0\), no structural contradiction follows from the data, labels, symmetries, or scaling. Any genuine negative theorem must arise from failure of the compact-time reused-adjoint limit, not from a finite-dataset obstruction.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 28838 | 2026-08-27T13:06:36.256Z | /root/functional_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/functional_audit
Payload:
A rigorous cavity decomposition is available, but the standard exponential-moment bootstrap is circular.

Write
\[
B_{ji}=\frac{g_{ji}}{\sqrt n},
\qquad g_{ji}\sim N(0,1),
\]
and remove column \(i\). Let \(D^{(-i)}(t)\) denote the left process generated by the cavity system, which is independent of
\[
g_i=(g_{1i},\ldots,g_{ni}).
\]
Then
\[
Z_i(t)
=
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_jg_{ji}D_j^{(-i)}(t)
\]
is conditionally Gaussian with covariance
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[Z_i(t)Z_i(s)\mid\mathcal F^{(-i)}\right]
=
\langle D^{(-i)}(t),D^{(-i)}(s)\rangle_n.
\]

For
\[
\phi_\varepsilon(x)=1+\varepsilon(\sin x+\cos x),
\]
\[
\|\phi_\varepsilon'\|_\infty+
\|\phi_\varepsilon''\|_\infty
\le C\varepsilon.
\]
The output variable \(C\) has a deterministic compact-time envelope, hence
\[
\sup_{t\le T}\|D^{(-i)}(t)\|_n\le C_T\varepsilon.
\]
If additionally
\[
\|D^{(-i)}(t)-D^{(-i)}(s)\|_n
\le L_T|t-s|,
\]
then elementary Gaussian chaining gives
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[
\exp\left(
\theta\sup_{t\le T}|Z_i(t)|
\right)
\middle|\mathcal F^{(-i)}
\right]
\le
\exp\!\left(C_T\theta+C_T\varepsilon^2\theta^2\right).
\]
Thus the pure cavity source has the desired dimension-free exponential moment.

The true source is
\[
P_i(t)=Z_i(t)+\mathcal R_i(t),
\]
where \(\mathcal R_i\) is the column-response/Onsager correction. Differentiating the true flow with respect to the removed column gives a response size \(J_i\) satisfying, after absolute values,
\[
J_i(t)
\le
C_T\varepsilon
+
C_T\varepsilon
\int_0^t(1+|P_i(s)|)J_i(s)\,ds.
\]
Therefore
\[
J_i(t)
\le
C_T\varepsilon
\exp\!\left(
C_T\varepsilon t+
C_T\varepsilon\int_0^t|P_i(s)|\,ds
\right).
\]

This is the exact circularity. Gaussian integration by parts or concentration for \(P_i\) requires a bound on its random column-Lipschitz constant, which is controlled by \(J_i\). Even assuming the cavity source is sub-Gaussian, the response bound is lognormal. Lognormal variables have no positive exponential moment.

The scalar model
\[
P=G,\qquad
J=e^{\varepsilon TG},\qquad G\sim N(0,1),
\]
shows the logical failure sharply:
\[
\mathbb E e^{\theta J}=\infty
\qquad
\text{for every }\theta>0,
\]
however small \(\varepsilon T>0\) is. Thus the implication
\[
\text{sub-Gaussian cavity field}
\Longrightarrow
\text{exponential response moment}
\Longrightarrow
\text{sub-Gaussian corrected field}
\]
is false.

A stopping-time argument does not close with the same estimate. On
\[
\max_i\sup_{t\le T}|P_i(t)|\le M,
\]
one obtains
\[
J_i\le C_T\varepsilon e^{C_T\varepsilon M}.
\]
The conditional Gaussian maximum suggests \(M\asymp\sqrt{\log n}\), but then
\[
e^{C\varepsilon M}
=
e^{C\varepsilon\sqrt{\log n}},
\]
which is subpolynomial in \(n\) yet grows faster than every power of \(\log n\). Hence it cannot be absorbed back into the same \(\sqrt{\log n}\) stopping threshold if the Onsager correction is estimated directly by \(J_i\).

Fixed-\(p\) estimates remain possible:
\[
\|Z_i\|_{L^p}\le C_T\varepsilon\sqrt p.
\]
They can give uniform fourth moments and potentially kernel uniform integrability. They do not provide the exponential response control required for clipping removal and uniform path stability. Taking \(p\sim\log n\) encounters the same response-growth problem unless one proves an averaged trace cancellation stronger than absolute-value Gronwall.

Therefore the rigorous result is:

- the leave-one-column cavity field is conditionally Gaussian with uniform short-time exponential moments;
- the response correction satisfies an explicit exponential Gronwall estimate;
- that estimate converts Gaussian tails into lognormal response tails and cannot bootstrap itself;
- small fixed \(\varepsilon\) does not eliminate the obstruction.

A successful high-temperature proof needs a new signed, one-sided, or averaged-response identity that bounds the Onsager correction without \(e^{C\varepsilon\int|P|}\). Bounded periodic gates alone do not provide such an estimate after absolute values.

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root

Payload:
The classical path-LDP theorems do not extend directly to the deterministic two-population ODE.

For Ben Arous–Guionnet’s Langevin spin-glass LDPs and Faugeras–MacLaurin’s stochastic neural-network LDPs, nondegenerate diffusion is structural. Their proofs construct a Radon–Nikodym derivative relative to an uncoupled Gaussian diffusion using Girsanov-type arguments. At zero diffusion, path laws generated by different deterministic drifts can be mutually singular, so this density and the resulting entropy rate function disappear. Moment bounds and Arzelà–Ascoli compactness provide only subsequences; they do not identify the limit or prove uniqueness.

Dembo–Lubetzky–Zeitouni’s universality theorem likewise concerns Brownian-driven asymmetric soft-spin dynamics with independent directed disorder. It transfers an existing Gaussian-disorder limit and does not establish a deterministic zero-noise theorem. Reciprocal \(B/B^\top\) correlations and gated two-population dynamics violate its model assumptions.

The Ben Arous–Dembo–Guionnet Cugliandolo–Kurchan theorem is different: it proves compact-time convergence of correlation and response observables for spherical \(p\)-spin Langevin dynamics. Its closure uses a fixed Gaussian Hamiltonian, the spherical constraint, Gaussian integration by parts, and Itô identities. It neither gives full empirical path-law convergence nor covers arbitrary bipartite gates or learned finite-rank disorder.

An externally prescribed bounded mean-field drift might be perturbative in a nondegenerate diffusion theorem. Here the rank-one drift is endogenous and path-dependent, requiring new continuity, tightness, self-consistency, and uniqueness arguments. Therefore Brownian diffusion cannot simply be removed and replaced by finite moments plus compactness.

Finite-\(p\) compactness can support subsequential identification, but it cannot prove uniqueness of the reused-multiplier flow.

Let
\[
X=q(U)-q(\widetilde U).
\]
Since \(q\) is bounded and Lipschitz,
\[
|X|\le 2\|q\|_\infty,\qquad
\|X\|_2\le \operatorname{Lip}(q)\|U-\widetilde U\|_2.
\]
If \(P\) is uniformly bounded in \(L^p\), \(p>2\), then
\[
\|XP\|_2^2
\le \|P\|_p^2
   \|X\|_{2p/(p-2)}^2
\le C\|U-\widetilde U\|_2^{2(1-2/p)}.
\]
For the squared error \(E=\|U-\widetilde U\|_2^2\), this gives
\[
E'\le C E^{1-1/p}.
\]
Because \(1-1/p<1\) for every finite \(p\), this differential inequality is non-Osgood and does not imply uniqueness. Uniform all-\(p\) bounds of sub-Gaussian form,
\[
\|P\|_p\le C\sqrt p,
\]
instead yield
\[
E'\le CE\log(C/E),
\]
which is Osgood.

A candidate autonomous limit would require:

- covariance kernels
  \[
  Q_H^{ab}(t,s)=\mathbb E[H_a(t)H_b(s)],
  \qquad
  Q_D^{ab}(t,s)=\mathbb E[D_a(t)D_b(s)];
  \]
- forward and backward Gaussian cavity processes with these covariances;
- trained-memory terms involving
  \[
  \int e_b(s)D_b(s)Q_H^{ba}(s,t)\,ds,
  \quad
  \int e_b(s)H_b(s)Q_D^{ba}(s,t)\,ds;
  \]
- Onsager response kernels defined by functional derivatives.

For bounded \(C^3\) activation, sub-Gaussian cavity fields plus bounded memory could plausibly give existence and Osgood uniqueness. But finite-width identification still requires joint Gaussian conditioning for adaptive \(B_0H\) and \(B_0^*D\), the correct Onsager terms, and uniform sub-Gaussian bounds for messages and response derivatives.

Regarding minimum moments:

- \(p>2\) can provide square uniform integrability for kernels and velocities;
- \(p\ge4\) is convenient for finite products and time tightness;
- no finite \(p\) suffices for uniqueness.

Thus compactness plus finite-\(p\) uniform integrability is not a complete affirmative route. The necessary endpoint is an all-moment or sub-Gaussian source estimate.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 29106 | 2026-08-27T13:14:51.969Z | /root/functional_audit ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/functional_audit
Payload:
A fixed-\(p\) moment proof does not close at any finite level using the available cavity and Frobenius estimates. The obstruction is an infinite moment-doubling hierarchy.

Assume \(\phi,\phi',\phi'',\phi'''\) are bounded and \(C(0)=0\). On every compact interval, the output-coordinate equation gives a deterministic envelope
\[
\sup_{j,t\le T}|C_j(t)|\le C_T.
\]
Hence
\[
D_j^a=C_j\phi'(S_j^a),\qquad H_i^a=\phi(U_i^a)
\]
are pointwise bounded.

Writing
\[
B_t=B_0+Q_t,\qquad
Q_t=-\frac2m\int_0^t\sum_a e_a(s)D_s^a\otimes_nH_s^a\,ds,
\]
one has
\[
(Q_t^\top D_t^a)_i
=
-\frac2m\int_0^t\sum_b e_b(s)H_{s,i}^b
\langle D_s^b,D_t^a\rangle_n\,ds.
\]
This term is pointwise bounded uniformly in \(n\). Thus the only difficult part of
\[
P=B^\top D
\]
is \(B_0^\top D\).

For column \(i\), the leave-one-column decomposition gives
\[
P_i=Z_i+\mathcal R_i,
\]
where, conditionally on the cavity system,
\[
Z_i(t)=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_jg_{ji}D_j^{(-i)}(t)
\]
is Gaussian. Therefore, for every fixed \(p<\infty\),
\[
\|Z_i(t)\|_{L^p}
\le
C\sqrt p\,\|D^{(-i)}(t)\|_n
\le C_{p,T}.
\]
Consequently, defining
\[
M_p(T)
=
\sup_{n,t\le T}
\mathbb E\langle |P_t|^p,1\rangle_n,
\]
the value estimate reduces to controlling a \(p\)-moment of the column-response correction.

Let \(J_i\) schematically denote the normalized Frobenius response of the trajectory to column \(i\). Differentiating the exact equations yields a top-order term
\[
\phi''(U)\,P\,J
\]
(and an analogous term at the second hidden layer). Since \(\phi''\) is bounded, the \(p\)-energy satisfies
\[
\frac d{dt}
\mathbb E\langle |J|^p,1\rangle_n
\le
C_p\left(
1+\mathbb E\langle |J|^p,1\rangle_n
\right)
+
C_p\mathbb E\langle |P||J|^p,1\rangle_n.
\]
The final term cannot be bounded at the same moment level. Hölder gives
\[
\mathbb E\langle |P||J|^p\rangle_n
\le
M_{2p}^{1/2}
\left(
\mathbb E\langle |J|^{2p}\rangle_n
\right)^{1/2}.
\]
Thus the \(p\)-response estimate requires both the \(2p\)-source and \(2p\)-response estimates. Repeating gives
\[
p\longrightarrow2p\longrightarrow4p\longrightarrow8p\longrightarrow\cdots.
\]
Young’s inequality does not terminate the escalation: it replaces \(J^p\) by a strictly higher power such as \(J^{p^2/(p-1)}\).

The standard deterministic controls stop at order two:

- \(\|B_0\|_{\mathrm{op}}\) controls \(\|B_0^\top D\|_{2,n}\);
- loss dissipation controls weighted quadratic velocities;
- the finite-rank term \(Q^\top D\) is pointwise bounded.

None controls \(M_{2p}\) from \(M_p\), and none bounds the response multiplier \(P\) pointwise. Hence choosing \(p=8\) or \(16\) merely moves the missing estimate to \(16\), \(32\), and onward.

The velocities have the form “bounded gate times \(P\)” plus bounded finite-rank terms. Therefore their \(p\)-moment and time-translation estimates inherit the same unresolved hierarchy. For kernel convergence, one needs uniform integrability of quadratic quantities such as
\[
\phi'(U)^2P^2.
\]
A uniform \(2+\delta\) moment would suffice, but the argument above cannot prove even that without entering the infinite hierarchy.

There is no immediate counterexample showing that \(M_8(T)\) actually diverges. Indeed, the small-time expansion is favorable:
\[
C(t)=t\,c_1(S_0)+O(t^2),\qquad
D(t)=t\,d_1(S_0)+O(t^2),
\]
and hence
\[
P_i(t)
=
t\,\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_jg_{ji}d_1((B_0H_0)_j)+O(t^2).
\]
Gaussian regression gives uniformly bounded moments of every fixed order for this leading term. Thus the theorem may be true.

The exact conclusion is:

- fixed-\(p\) moments of the pure cavity source are uniformly bounded;
- the trained finite-rank contribution is pointwise bounded;
- the response correction produces a \(p\mapsto2p\) recursion;
- no finite moment hierarchy closes;
- loss energy and operator-norm bounds do not remove the escalation.

A proof requires either an all-\(p\) response estimate, or a signed cancellation/one-sided Frobenius inequality that replaces
\[
\langle |P||J|^p\rangle
\]
by a quantity controlled at order \(p\). Without such an estimate, fixed-\(p\) compactness and kernel uniform integrability remain unproved rather than disproved.

Payload:
The logarithmic-depth incremental-GFOM route is plausible, but the stated heuristic is not yet a rigorous theorem.

For a tangent recursion
\[
J_{k+1}\le (1+C\delta |P_k|)J_k+\delta A_k,
\]
one obtains
\[
J_K\lesssim
\exp\!\left(C\delta\sum_{k<K}|P_k|\right).
\]
If \((P_0,\ldots,P_K)\) is jointly Gaussian with uniformly bounded variances plus bounded drift, then
\[
\sup_K\mathbb E
\exp\!\left(
pC\delta\sum_{k<K}|P_k|
\right)<\infty
\]
for every fixed \(p\). This does not require covariance lower bounds. Even complete correlation gives only \(T|G|\). Max-coordinate control costs approximately
\[
e^{C\sqrt{\log n}}=n^{o(1)},
\]
which is potentially compatible with \(n^{-1/2}\)-scale concentration.

However, the following new results are required:

1. A quantitative AMP/GFOM theorem valid to depth \(K_n\asymp\log n\), with total error \(n^{-c+o(1)}\).

2. Joint state evolution for the tangent and response recursions.

3. Gaussian conditioning formulated through orthogonal projections rather than inverse Gram matrices, so singular and nearly singular covariance matrices are harmless.

4. Concentration and uniform \(p>2\) integrability for empirical lognormal response observables with \(n^{o(1)}\) sensitivities.

5. Uniform Euler error \(O(\delta)\) for states and responses.

6. Convergence and uniqueness of the resulting covariance-response Volterra system.

No covariance lower bound is intrinsically necessary: exactly repeated queries simply have zero Gaussian innovation. But a proof based on inverse empirical Gram matrices fails under near singularity.

There is also an important tail caveat. An exponential of the integral of a lognormal process can have infinite moments. Therefore \(P_k\) must remain Gaussian or sub-Gaussian plus bounded memory; it is insufficient to know that \(P_k\) is controlled by a previous lognormal response. The proposed DMFT has the desired Gaussian-plus-deterministic-memory form, but the finite-width induction must prove this quantitatively.

A sufficient rate regime would be
\[
K_n=c\log n,\qquad
\delta_n=T/K_n,\qquad
R_n=C\sqrt{\log n},
\]
with cumulative stability \(n^{o(1)}\), one-step errors \(n^{-1/2+o(1)}\), and truncation probability \(n^{-A}\) for sufficiently large \(A\).

Thus I found no decisive counterexample. But fixed-depth AMP and the informal product estimate do not suffice: the route requires a new logarithmic-depth, tangent-augmented, singular-covariance GFOM theorem.

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER

Payload:
The temporal Jensen step is valid and does not require independence. The unresolved issue is the empirical Onsager mean, not accumulation over \(T\log n\) steps.

Let \(\delta=1/\log n\), \(K=\lceil T/\delta\rceil\), and suppose a response satisfies
\[
J_{k+1}
\le
\left(1+C\delta(1+|P_k|)\right)J_k+C\delta.
\]
Then
\[
J_k
\le
(J_0+CT)
\exp\left(
CT+C\delta\sum_{\ell<k}|P_\ell|
\right).
\]
Since \(K\delta\le T+\delta\),
\[
\exp\left(qC\delta\sum_{\ell<K}|P_\ell|\right)
=
\exp\left(
qCT\frac1K\sum_{\ell<K}|P_\ell|+O(\delta)
\right)
\le
\frac1K\sum_{\ell<K}e^{qCT|P_\ell|+O(\delta)}
\]
by convexity. Therefore
\[
\sup_{\ell<K}\mathbb E e^{qCT|P_\ell|}<\infty
\]
would imply
\[
\sup_{n,k<K}\mathbb E J_k^q<\infty.
\]
This part of the proposed bootstrap is rigorous and dependence-free.

For an adaptive Gaussian matrix, conditioning on all previous queries gives an exact decomposition
\[
P_k=M_k+Z_k,
\]
where \(Z_k\) is an independent Gaussian innovation in the orthogonal complement of the previous query span. However, only the innovation is automatically controlled. The conditional mean \(M_k\) is the projection onto the span of all previous right and left messages.

Using increments makes the raw conditioning system ill-conditioned. If
\[
\Delta H_k=H_k-H_{k-1}=O(\delta),
\]
then the \(K\times K\) covariance Gram of these increments has trace \(O(K\delta^2)=O(\delta)\), so its smallest eigenvalue is at most \(O(\delta^2)\). Its pseudoinverse norm is therefore at least \(O(\delta^{-2})\), and can be much larger because increments of a smooth path become nearly linearly dependent. For an analytic path, high-index covariance eigenvalues may decay much faster than any power. A singular dataset Gram introduces additional exact redundant directions.

Large pseudoinverse coefficients can cancel in the final projected vector, so this does not imply that \(M_k\) is large in empirical \(L^2\). But empirical \(L^2\)-boundedness is insufficient for
\[
\mathbb E e^{\theta|M_{k,i}|}.
\]
The Jensen response estimate needs coordinate exponential moments.

AMP rewrites this unstable projection mean as an Onsager/Volterra memory term. In the present two-sided system, its coefficients are empirical averages of Jacobian responses. Schematically,
\[
M_k
=
\delta\sum_{\ell<k}\widehat R_{k\ell}\,\mathcal H_\ell,
\qquad
\widehat R_{k\ell}
=
\frac1n\sum_i J_{k\ell,i}.
\]
Bounded response moments imply
\[
\mathbb E|\widehat R_{k\ell}|^q\le C_q,
\]
but they do not imply a uniform exponential moment or concentration around a bounded deterministic coefficient. Markov’s inequality only yields bounds with thresholds growing with \(n\). Inserting such a random mean into
\[
\mathbb E e^{\theta|P_k|}
\]
returns the same response-tail problem.

Thus the induction is circular:

\[
\text{bounded Onsager mean}
\Rightarrow
\text{Gaussian exponential moment for }P
\Rightarrow
\text{bounded response moments}
\Rightarrow
\text{moment bound for Onsager mean},
\]
but the last implication does not recover the exponential/concentration bound required at the first step.

There is also a separate approximation bridge. The \(\delta=1/\log n\) program is a coarse auxiliary discretization, while exact GD uses a much finer admissible mesh. Comparing them requires a uniform stability estimate. The proposed response bound would provide it, but only after the Onsager induction has closed.

Therefore:

- \(T\log n\) temporal accumulation is not fatal;
- Gaussian innovations remain exact under adaptive conditioning;
- near-singular increment Grams make raw conditioning coefficients unstable;
- the stable Onsager representation requires concentration of empirical response traces;
- fixed response moments alone do not provide the exponential moment needed to restart the induction.

A successful proof needs a quantitative growing-iteration AMP theorem formulated directly in stable Volterra variables, with high-probability concentration of empirical response coefficients for \(K=O(\log n)\). The proposed self-contained induction does not yet establish that result.

Reeves’s coupling construction itself tolerates singular covariance, but the published error bounds do not.

The constructive theorem permits a Cholesky factor with zero diagonal and handles exactly dependent queries by inserting unused orthogonal directions. Moreover, if \(f_t=\Delta h_t\), normalization removes \(\Delta\) from the Gram–Schmidt direction \(q_t\), while the triangular factors \(R\) and \(\Omega\) retain the \(\Delta\) scale. Thus the exact decomposition leaves room for a genuinely incremental estimate.

The proved bounds lose this structure in two places:

- They assume \(\Sigma\) positive definite and whiten by \(\Omega^{-1}\) and \(\Sigma^{-1}\), producing explicit dependence on \(\kappa(\Sigma)\). Fine-time covariance is nearly singular, so this is fatal.
- Their stability estimate gives
  \[
  (1+4L_f+L_g)^{K-1}.
  \]
  With Markov memory \(g_t=x_{t-1}+O(\Delta)\), \(L_g\approx1\), yielding \(2^K\) instead of the physically appropriate \(e^{CT}\).

A possible repair would remain in unwhitened coordinates, prove an incremental discrete Grönwall estimate, and control \(R-\Omega\) directly. Near singular covariance, Cholesky dependence is generally only \(1/2\)-Hölder, potentially converting \(n^{-1/2}\) covariance error into \(n^{-1/4}\). Because covariance fluctuations carry a \(\Delta^2\) factor, this might still be adequate for \(K\asymp\log n\), but the required causal estimate and its \(K\)-dependence are not proved.

Additional unresolved issues are pseudoinverse instability in the Onsager coefficients, causal rather than optimal Gaussian coupling near rank changes, and extension from symmetric GOE recursion to the rectangular bipartite \(B/B^\top\) system.

So the source offers a credible proof mechanism, but no existing condition-number-free small-step theorem. A substantial new projection-level analysis is required.

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
