Payload:
The \(L=3\) hidden-layer extension is affirmative. The exact scaling and the complete small-time activity argument are as follows.

Let the fixed dataset be

\[
x_1,\dots,x_p\in\mathbb R^d,\qquad
\|x_a\|^2=d,\qquad x_a\ne x_b\ (a\ne b),
\]

with arbitrary \(y_a\in\{\pm1\}\), and use

\[
\phi(x)=\sin x+\cos x,\qquad
b:=\sup_{r\ge0}\|\phi^{(r)}\|_\infty=\sqrt2.
\]

The network is

\[
U_i^a=w_i^\top x_a,\qquad H_i^a=\phi(U_i^a),
\]

\[
S_j^a=\sum_iB_{ji}H_i^a,\qquad J_j^a=\phi(S_j^a),
\]

\[
T_k^a=\sum_jA_{kj}J_j^a,\qquad G_k^a=\phi(T_k^a),
\]

\[
f_a=\sum_kc_kG_k^a,\qquad
\mathcal L=\frac1p\sum_a(f_a-y_a)^2.
\]

Initialize independently by

\[
w_{ir}\sim N(0,d^{-1}),\qquad
B_{ji},A_{kj}\sim N(0,n^{-1}),\qquad
c_k\sim N(0,n^{-4}).
\]

Use

\[
\boxed{
\lambda_{w,n}=\frac nd,\qquad
\lambda_{B,n}=\lambda_{A,n}=1,\qquad
\lambda_{c,n}=\frac1n.
}
\]

For the exact-GD diagonalization one may take, very conservatively,

\[
\eta_{0,n}=e^{-e^n}.
\]

Then every \(\eta_{0,n}\lambda_{\ell,n}\to0\).

## 1. Exact normalized flow

Set

\[
C=nc,\qquad \langle u,v\rangle_n=n^{-1}u^\top v,\qquad
\gamma=\frac2p,\qquad e_a=f_a-y_a,
\]

and

\[
R_{ab}=\frac{x_a^\top x_b}{d}.
\]

Define the backward fields

\[
D_3^a=C\odot\phi'(T^a),
\]

\[
P_2^a=A^\top D_3^a,\qquad
D_2^a=\phi'(S^a)\odot P_2^a,
\]

\[
P_1^a=B^\top D_2^a.
\]

Direct differentiation gives the exact finite-width gradient-flow equations

\[
\dot U^a
=
-\gamma\sum_b
R_{ab}e_b\,
\phi'(U^b)\odot P_1^b,
\tag{1}
\]

\[
\dot B
=
-\gamma\sum_b e_bD_2^b\otimes_n H^b,
\tag{2}
\]

\[
\dot A
=
-\gamma\sum_b e_bD_3^b\otimes_n J^b,
\tag{3}
\]

\[
\dot C=-\gamma\sum_b e_bG^b.
\tag{4}
\]

The four NTK blocks are exactly

\[
\Theta^{(4)}_{ab}
=
\langle G^a,G^b\rangle_n,
\]

\[
\Theta^{(3)}_{ab}
=
\langle J^a,J^b\rangle_n
\langle D_3^a,D_3^b\rangle_n,
\]

\[
\Theta^{(2)}_{ab}
=
\langle H^a,H^b\rangle_n
\langle D_2^a,D_2^b\rangle_n,
\]

\[
\Theta^{(1)}_{ab}
=
R_{ab}
\left\langle
\phi'(U^a)P_1^a,\,
\phi'(U^b)P_1^b
\right\rangle_n.
\tag{5}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\dot f=-\gamma\Theta e,\qquad
\dot{\mathcal L}=-\frac4{p^2}e^\top\Theta e.
\tag{6}
\]

Thus the proposed scaling makes every block finite; none has been artificially reweighted after taking the limit.

## 2. Initial Gaussian kernels

For standard jointly Gaussian \(X,Y\) with covariance \(\rho\),

\[
\mathbb E[\phi(X)\phi(Y)]
=
\mathbb E[\phi'(X)\phi'(Y)]
=
e^{-1+\rho}.
\tag{7}
\]

Hence the three initialization kernels are

\[
Q_1=e^{-1+R},\qquad
Q_2=e^{-1+Q_1},\qquad
Q_3=e^{-1+Q_2},
\tag{8}
\]

with entrywise exponentials.

The first matrix is

\[
(Q_1)_{ab}
=
\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|x_a-x_b\|^2}{2d}\right).
\]

It is strictly positive definite for every finite family of distinct inputs. A direct proof is

\[
v^\top Q_1v
=
c_d\int_{\mathbb R^d}
\left|\sum_av_ae^{i\omega^\top x_a}\right|^2
e^{-d\|\omega\|^2/2}\,d\omega,
\]

which can vanish only if the exponential polynomial is identically zero, hence only if \(v=0\).

If \(Q\) is positive definite, then

\[
e^{-1+Q}
=
e^{-1}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\frac{Q^{\circ r}}{r!}
\]

is positive definite: every Schur power is positive semidefinite, and the \(r=1\) term is positive definite. Therefore

\[
Q_1,Q_2,Q_3>0.
\tag{9}
\]

This remains valid when the input Gram \(R\) is singular, including antipodal or arbitrarily close distinct inputs.

At initialization,

\[
U\sim N(0,R),\qquad
S\sim N(0,Q_1),\qquad
T\sim N(0,Q_2),
\]

and

\[
\mathbb E[G^aG^b]=(Q_3)_{ab}.
\]

Since \(C(0)\to0\), \(f(0)=0\) in the limit, and

\[
\dot{\mathcal L}(0)
=
-\frac4{p^2}y^\top Q_3y<0.
\tag{10}
\]

## 3. Nested cavity chain and positivity of every hidden block

Let

\[
g(T)=\sum_a y_a\phi(T_a),
\qquad
\Psi=\frac12\mathbb E[g(T)^2].
\]

Define

\[
(v_3)_a
=
y_ag(T)\phi'(T_a),
\qquad
M_3=\mathbb E[v_3v_3^\top].
\tag{11}
\]

### Lemma 1: \(M_3>0\)

For \(z\ne0\),

\[
z^\top M_3z
=
\mathbb E\left[
g(T)^2
\left(\sum_a z_ay_a\phi'(T_a)\right)^2
\right].
\]

The law \(N(0,Q_2)\) has full support. If the last expectation vanished, analyticity would imply

\[
g(t)\sum_a z_ay_a\phi'(t_a)\equiv0.
\]

But

\[
\mathbb E[g(T)^2]=y^\top Q_3y>0,
\]

so \(g\not\equiv0\). The second analytic factor must therefore vanish identically. Differentiating in \(t_c\),

\[
z_cy_c\phi''(t_c)\equiv0.
\]

Since \(\phi''=-\phi\not\equiv0\), every \(z_c=0\), a contradiction.

### First transpose cavity

For a representative second-layer neuron, Gaussian conditioning for the reused \(A,A^\top\) pair gives

\[
\Pi_3
=
\xi_3+\Lambda_3\phi(S),
\tag{12}
\]

where

\[
\xi_3\sim N(0,M_3),\qquad
\xi_3\perp S,
\]

and

\[
(\Lambda_3)_{ac}
=
\mathbb E[\partial_{T_c}(v_3)_a].
\tag{13}
\]

This is obtained directly by deleting one column of \(A\). The deleted-column-independent sum gives \(\xi_3\); Gaussian integration by parts in the deleted column gives (13); the Taylor remainder is \(O(n^{-1/2})\) in \(L^2\), because all derivatives of \(\phi\) are bounded.

Define

\[
v_2=\phi'(S)\odot\Pi_3,\qquad
M_2=\mathbb E[v_2v_2^\top].
\tag{14}
\]

### Lemma 2: \(M_2>0\)

Conditioning on \(S\),

\[
\operatorname{Cov}(v_2\mid S)
=
D_{\phi'(S)}M_3D_{\phi'(S)}.
\]

Every coordinate \(S_a\) has a continuous Gaussian law and the zeros of \(\phi'\) are discrete. Thus \(D_{\phi'(S)}\) is invertible almost surely. Since \(M_3>0\),

\[
z^\top M_2z
\ge
\mathbb E\!\left[
z^\top
D_{\phi'(S)}M_3D_{\phi'(S)}
z
\right]>0
\]

for \(z\ne0\).

### Second transpose cavity

The reused \(B,B^\top\) pair gives

\[
\Pi_2
=
\xi_2+\Lambda_2\phi(U),
\tag{15}
\]

where

\[
\xi_2\sim N(0,M_2),\qquad
\xi_2\perp U,
\]

and

\[
(\Lambda_2)_{ac}
=
\mathbb E[\partial_{S_c}(v_2)_a].
\tag{16}
\]

Set

\[
v_1=\phi'(U)\odot\Pi_2,
\qquad
M_1=\mathbb E[v_1v_1^\top].
\tag{17}
\]

### Lemma 3: \(M_1>0\), even if \(R\) is singular

Conditionally on \(U\),

\[
\operatorname{Cov}(v_1\mid U)
=
D_{\phi'(U)}M_2D_{\phi'(U)}.
\]

Each marginal \(U_a\sim N(0,1)\), so all diagonal entries of \(D_{\phi'(U)}\) are nonzero almost surely. Joint nonsingularity of \(U\) is unnecessary. Hence \(M_1>0\).

### Limiting parameter energies

A direct cavity evaluation of the three squared gradient norms yields

\[
\mathcal E_A
=
\sum_{a,b}(Q_2)_{ab}(M_3)_{ab},
\tag{18}
\]

\[
\mathcal E_B
=
\sum_{a,b}(Q_1)_{ab}(M_2)_{ab},
\tag{19}
\]

\[
\mathcal E_w
=
\sum_{a,b}R_{ab}(M_1)_{ab}
=
\operatorname{tr}(RM_1).
\tag{20}
\]

The first two are positive because \(Q_2\circ M_3\) and \(Q_1\circ M_2\) are positive definite. The last is positive because \(R\ge0\), \(R\ne0\), and \(M_1>0\). Therefore

\[
\boxed{\mathcal E_w,\mathcal E_B,\mathcal E_A>0.}
\tag{21}
\]

There is no cancellation between layers in this argument.

## 4. Small-time geometry

Let \(h=(w,B,A)\), and let \(M_h\) denote the block mobility

\[
M_h=\operatorname{diag}\!\left(\frac ndI_w,I_B,I_A\right).
\]

Since \(C(0)=0\) and \(e(0)=-y\),

\[
C(t)=\gamma t\,g(T)+O_{L^2}(t^2).
\tag{22}
\]

Moreover,

\[
\dot h(t)
=
\gamma^2t\,M_h\nabla\Psi(h_0)+O(t^2),
\tag{23}
\]

and

\[
h(t)-h_0
=
\frac{\gamma^2t^2}{2}
M_h\nabla\Psi(h_0)+O(t^3).
\tag{24}
\]

Equations (22)–(24) follow node by node from (1)–(4), not from an initialization-linearized network.

For the hidden NTK blocks,

\[
y^\top\Theta^{(1)}(t)y
=
\gamma^2t^2\mathcal E_w+O(t^3),
\]

\[
y^\top\Theta^{(2)}(t)y
=
\gamma^2t^2\mathcal E_B+O(t^3),
\]

\[
y^\top\Theta^{(3)}(t)y
=
\gamma^2t^2\mathcal E_A+O(t^3).
\tag{25}
\]

For the output block,

\[
y^\top\Theta^{(4)}(t)y
=
2\Psi(h(t))
=
2\Psi(h_0)
+
\gamma^2t^2
(\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B+\mathcal E_A)
+O(t^3).
\tag{26}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
y^\top[\Theta(t)-\Theta(0)]y
=
2\gamma^2t^2
(\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B+\mathcal E_A)
+O(t^3)>0
}
\tag{27}
\]

for all sufficiently small \(t>0\).

All four parameter blocks are therefore active.

## 5. All three hidden preactivations move

Let

\[
V_U=\lim_{t\downarrow0}\frac{\dot U(t)}{\gamma^2t},\qquad
V_S=\lim_{t\downarrow0}\frac{\dot S(t)}{\gamma^2t},\qquad
V_T=\lim_{t\downarrow0}\frac{\dot T(t)}{\gamma^2t}.
\]

Chain rule in the mobility Hilbert spaces gives

\[
\langle\nabla_U\Psi,V_U\rangle=\mathcal E_w>0,
\tag{28}
\]

\[
\langle\nabla_S\Psi,V_S\rangle
=
\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B>0,
\tag{29}
\]

\[
\langle\nabla_T\Psi,V_T\rangle
=
\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B+\mathcal E_A>0.
\tag{30}
\]

Thus \(V_U,V_S,V_T\) are all nonzero. In particular,

\[
\dot U(t)=\gamma^2tV_U+O_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

\[
\dot S(t)=\gamma^2tV_S+O_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

\[
\dot T(t)=\gamma^2tV_T+O_{L^2}(t^2).
\tag{31}
\]

Therefore each layer has finite positive integrated squared velocity and nonzero displacement on every sufficiently short nonzero interval.

## 6. Nonlinearity survives

Every initial one-sample marginal of \(U,S,T\) is \(N(0,1)\), because all three covariance matrices have unit diagonal.

For \(Z\sim N(0,1)\),

\[
\inf_{\alpha,\beta}
\mathbb E|\phi(Z)-\alpha Z-\beta|^2>0,
\]

since equality would make the analytic non-affine function \(\sin z+\cos z\) affine on \(\mathbb R\). The regression residual is continuous under \(W_2\) convergence while the variance remains bounded away from zero. Hence it stays positive in all three hidden layers for sufficiently short time.

## 7. The two-matrix convergence theorem reduces exactly to one GFOM matrix

This is the important \(L=3\) convergence bridge.

Define the single rectangular Gaussian matrix

\[
\mathcal A_0=[\,B_0,\ A_0^\top\,]\in\mathbb R^{n\times2n}.
\tag{32}
\]

All its entries are iid \(N(0,n^{-1})\). Its right side has two deterministic species: hidden layers \(1\) and \(3\); its left side is hidden layer \(2\).

The four necessary reused-matrix queries are exactly

\[
\mathcal A_0(H,0)=B_0H,
\]

\[
\mathcal A_0^\top J
=
(B_0^\top J,\ A_0J),
\]

\[
\mathcal A_0(0,D_3)=A_0^\top D_3,
\]

\[
\mathcal A_0^\top D_2
=
(B_0^\top D_2,\ A_0D_2).
\tag{33}
\]

Coordinatewise species masks retain the indicated component and discard the unused one.

The trained operator is

\[
\mathcal A(t)=[B(t),A(t)^\top],
\]

and satisfies

\[
\dot{\mathcal A}
=
-\gamma\sum_a e_a
\left[
D_2^a\otimes_n H^a,\,
J^a\otimes_n D_3^a
\right].
\tag{34}
\]

Thus every current-matrix query reduces exactly to a query of \(\mathcal A_0\) or \(\mathcal A_0^\top\), plus finitely many normalized inner products.

I independently checked the full asymmetric-GFOM theorem and its hypotheses in Han’s primary arXiv source. The theorem permits:

- rectangular proportional dimensions;
- iid sub-Gaussian entries with variance \(1/n\);
- coordinate-dependent row-separate maps;
- deterministic species masks;
- arbitrary covariance rank in its Gaussian state evolution.

One Euler cycle is serialized as two GFOM pairs per sample:

\[
(H^a,0)\xrightarrow{\mathcal A_0}S^a
\xrightarrow{\mathcal A_0^\top}T^a,
\]

\[
(0,D_3^a)\xrightarrow{\mathcal A_0}P_2^a
\xrightarrow{\mathcal A_0^\top}P_1^a.
\]

Hence the \(L=2\) fixed-mesh identification applies without importing an unproved “two independent matrix” theorem. The two matrices are disjoint blocks of the one matrix to which the actual theorem applies.

The current autonomous limit is equivalently the state

\[
(U,C,\mathcal B,\mathcal A),
\]

with bounded operators

\[
\mathcal B:\mathcal H_1\to\mathcal H_2,
\qquad
\mathcal A:\mathcal H_2\to\mathcal H_3,
\]

and equations (1)–(4). It retains neither initialization matrices nor their history.

The cutoff, source-response, cutoff-removal, mesh-Cauchy, path-tightness and exact-GD diagonal estimates from the \(L=2\) proof acquire only a fixed factor of two in the number of Gaussian queries per sample. The single-matrix embedding shows that no new probabilistic theorem is needed.

## Audit conclusion

The scaling, NTK normalization, strict-positive-definiteness argument, both transpose-response terms, singular-\(R\) case, all three feature velocities, all four NTK blocks, kernel motion, loss decrease and surviving nonlinearity pass the audit.

The key potentially fatal issue—whether two independently reused matrices require an unavailable multi-matrix continuous-time theorem—is removed exactly by the rectangular embedding (32)–(33).

Payload:
Audit verdict: the L3 compact-time limit is provable with the rectangular embedding, but the proof is complete only if the triangular response lemma and uncut uniqueness estimate are included explicitly.

Let

\[
\mathcal A_0=[B_0,A_0^\top]:V_1\oplus V_3\to V_2 .
\]

It is an \(n\times2n\) matrix with iid \(N(0,n^{-1})\) entries. Each sample requires exactly two asymmetric-GFOM stages:

\[
(H^1,0)\xrightarrow{\mathcal A_0}S
 \xrightarrow{\mathcal A_0^\top}(\ast,T),
\]

\[
(0,D^3)\xrightarrow{\mathcal A_0}P^2
 \xrightarrow{\mathcal A_0^\top}(P^1,\ast).
\]

The trained finite-rank memories are exactly

\[
\begin{aligned}
S^a&=\mathcal A_0(H^{1,a},0)
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}e_{\ell b}D^2_{\ell b}
 \langle H^1_{\ell b},H^{1,a}\rangle_n,\\
T^a&=\operatorname{pr}_3\mathcal A_0^\top H^{2,a}
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}e_{\ell b}D^3_{\ell b}
 \langle H^2_{\ell b},H^{2,a}\rangle_n,\\
P^{2,a}&=\mathcal A_0(0,D^{3,a})
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}e_{\ell b}H^2_{\ell b}
 \langle D^3_{\ell b},D^{3,a}\rangle_n,\\
P^{1,a}&=\operatorname{pr}_1\mathcal A_0^\top D^{2,a}
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}e_{\ell b}H^1_{\ell b}
 \langle D^2_{\ell b},D^{2,a}\rangle_n.
\end{aligned}
\]

Thus Han’s asymmetric GFOM theorem applies literally: aspect ratio \(1:2\), independent centered sub-Gaussian entries, and coordinate-dependent species masks. After smooth cutoffs of \(P^1,P^2,C\), all row maps satisfy its global Lipschitz/\(C^3\) assumptions. Conditioning on the independent initialization marks is legitimate because the theorem can be applied on each conditional probability space.

The potentially fatal term

\[
D^2=\phi'(S)P^2
\]

is controlled only in top-down order. If \(J\) is the aggregate source-Jacobian and

\[
K_r=\sup_q\|P^r_q\|_{\psi_2},
\]

Han’s exact response identities give

\[
K_2\le C T J,
\tag{A}
\]

because both the innovation and every response in \(P^2=A^\top D^3\) carry

\[
D^3=C\phi'(T),\qquad \|C\|_\infty=O(T).
\]

Next,

\[
K_1
\le
C\bigl[K_2+(K_2+T)J+TK_2^2\bigr].
\tag{B}
\]

Indeed, the centered innovation in \(P^1=B^\top D^2\) has variance at most \(C K_2^2\), while

\[
\partial_S D^2
=
\phi''(S)P^2+\phi'(S)\partial_SP^2,
\]

and the cross-source derivative \(\partial_SP^2\) contains \(D^3\) or \(\partial C\), hence is \(O(TJ)\). Independence of the two column species is essential here.

Separating slow-state derivatives from the finitely many within-cycle queries gives

\[
J\le
C_0\,
\mathbb E\exp\!\left[
C_0h\sum_{k,a}
\bigl(1+|P^1_{ka}|+|P^2_{ka}|\bigr)
\right].
\]

Jensen’s inequality, without any independence assumption between times, yields

\[
J
\le
2C_0
\exp\!\left[
C_0T+C_0T^2(K_1^2+K_2^2)
\right].
\tag{C}
\]

Starting with the bootstrap \(K_1,K_2\le1\), (C) bounds \(J\) by a numerical \(J_0\). Choosing \(T>0\) so that

\[
CTJ_0\le\frac14,
\qquad
C\!\left[
CTJ_0+(CTJ_0+T)J_0+T(CTJ_0)^2
\right]\le\frac14
\]

improves both bootstrap bounds. A first-exit argument therefore proves, uniformly in the cutoff and mesh,

\[
K_2\le C T,\qquad K_1\le C T.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\mathbb E\!\left[
(P^r)^2\mathbf 1_{\{|P^r|>R\}}
\right]
\le C e^{-cR^2/T^2},
\qquad r=1,2.
\tag{D}
\]

This is the required all-source closure; a symmetric estimate involving only \(\max(K_1,K_2)\) would be circular.

For the joint diagonal limit, take

\[
h_n=(\log\log n)^{-\gamma},\qquad
R_n=a\log\log\log n,\qquad 0<\gamma<1.
\]

There are \(O((\log\log n)^\gamma)\) Han stages. Its explicit error bound tends to zero because

\[
-\frac{\log n}{C_0^{\,O((\log\log n)^\gamma)}}
\]

dominates every positive polylogarithmic term. The cutoff Euler error satisfies

\[
\operatorname{err}_{\rm Euler}
\le
C h_ne^{CR_nT}\to0
\]

after decreasing \(T\). From (D), cutoff removal satisfies

\[
\operatorname{err}_{\rm cut}
\le
Ce^{CR_nT-cR_n^2/T^2}\to0.
\]

The adaptive empirical scalars are handled by coupling the actual recursion to the conditional deterministic oracle:

\[
d_{k+1}\le(1+Ch_nR_n)d_k+Ch_n\zeta_n,
\]

so there is no erroneous \(\zeta_n/h_n\) loss.

Finally, exact GD is compared directly with the finite-width ODE. On the short-time bounded region the finite-dimensional Jacobian grows at most polynomially in \(n\), so an explicit choice such as

\[
\eta_{0,n}=e^{-n^{10}}
\]

makes the accumulated Euler defect vanish while preserving
\(\eta_{0,n}\lambda_n^{(\ell)}\to0\).

For uniqueness of the uncut action flow, cutoff uniqueness alone is insufficient. One must use the sub-Gaussian estimate

\[
\mathbb E\!\left[
X^2|q(U)-q(V)|^2
\right]
\le
CK^2 E\log\frac{C}{E},
\qquad
E=\mathbb E|U-V|^2,
\]

valid for sub-Gaussian \(X\) and bounded Lipschitz \(q\). The resulting Osgood inequality has divergent reciprocal integral and therefore forces equality of two solutions. This supplies autonomy and restartability of the GNS current-operator state.

Hence I find no fatal obstruction. The L3 width/continuous-time theorem is unconditional once (A)–(D), oracle coupling, cutoff removal, and the Osgood uniqueness argument are actually written into the proof. Omitting any of those bridges would leave it conditional.

Audit verdict: the \(L=3\) construction is viable; I found no fatal scaling, NTK, Gaussian-kernel, or small-time activity error. It becomes a rigorous affirmative theorem once the following points are explicitly included.

- The exact scaling is correct:
  \[
  \lambda_w=n/d,\qquad \lambda_B=\lambda_A=1,\qquad \lambda_c=1/n,\qquad C=nc.
  \]
  All four NTK blocks and
  \[
  \dot{\mathcal L}=-\frac4{p^2}e^\top\Theta e
  \]
  have the claimed normalization.

- The single-matrix reduction is exact:
  \[
  \mathcal A_0=[B_0,A_0^\top]\in\mathbb R^{n\times2n}
  \]
  has iid \(N(0,1/n)\) entries. Han’s asymmetric state-evolution theorem applies at aspect ratio \(1:2\). Physical averages on either right species are recovered by multiplying its \(1/(2n)\) test average by \(2\).

- Han’s theorem applies only to row-separate maps. Every adaptive empirical output, error, and overlap must first be replaced by its conditional deterministic state-evolution oracle. Coupling the actual and oracle recursions then gives
  \[
  d_{k+1}\le (1+C_Rh)d_k+C_Rh\,\zeta_n.
  \]
  The factor \(h\) is essential; it prevents a spurious \(\zeta_n/h\) loss.

- In the two-stage serialization, only the left Gaussian innovations from the two stages are orthogonal, because their right inputs occupy disjoint species. The corresponding right innovations can be correlated and must retain their cross-covariances.

- The all-source response induction must be displayed. Cutting off both
  \[
  D^2=\phi'(S)P^2
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  \phi'(U)P^1
  \]
  yields cutoff-independent sub-Gaussian estimates for \(P^2,P^1\) on a sufficiently short interval. Cutting only \(P^1\) is insufficient.

- To identify the original uncut finite-width dynamics, use
  \[
  \begin{aligned}
  \phi'(S)P-\phi'(S_R)\tau_R(P_R)
  ={}&\phi'(S)(P-P_R)\\
  &+[\phi'(S)-\phi'(S_R)]P_R\\
  &+\phi'(S_R)[P_R-\tau_R(P_R)].
  \end{aligned}
  \]
  Thus only the cutoff trajectory’s tails are required. Splitting at \(|P_R|=R\) gives
  \[
  d'(t)\le C(1+R)d(t)+Ce^{-cR^2},
  \]
  hence
  \[
  d(T)\le Ce^{CRT-cR^2}\to0.
  \]
  This both links the actual network to the uncut limit and proves uniqueness in the reachable sub-Gaussian action-state class.

- Conditional state evolution must be converted to a deterministic full-sequence limit using the law of large numbers for the independent initialization marks. Countably many reachable probes are handled by finite unions followed by a summable metric tail.

- If no explicit finite-width Jacobian degree is computed, replace \(\eta_{0,n}=e^{-n^{10}}\) by the completely safe
  \[
  \eta_{0,n}=e^{-e^n}.
  \]
  Once the short-time Jacobian is shown polynomial in \(n\), this makes the accumulated Euler defect vanish.

- Include \(w\), or equivalently \(U\) together with the frozen component of \(w\) orthogonal to the data span, in the limiting state if claiming convergence of every first-layer parameter update.

The activity calculation itself passes: \(Q_1,Q_2,Q_3\) are strictly positive definite for every finite set of distinct normalized inputs; \(M_3,M_2,M_1\) are positive definite; and
\[
\mathcal E_A,\mathcal E_B,\mathcal E_w>0.
\]
Consequently,
\[
y^\top[\Theta(t)-\Theta(0)]y
=
2\gamma^2t^2(\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B+\mathcal E_A)+O(t^3)>0,
\]
while
\[
\dot{\mathcal L}(0)=-\frac4{p^2}y^\top Q_3y<0.
\]
All three hidden feature laws move, all four parameter blocks are active, and the non-affine activation survives.

Therefore the result is affirmative, but any presentation omitting the oracle coupling, all-source tail lemma, or finite-width uncut-to-cutoff comparison is not yet a complete proof.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 32116 | 2026-08-27T19:21:08.536Z | /root/audit_algebra_activity ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/audit_algebra_activity
Payload:
Audit result for the finite-width algebra and activity sector: it passes after making three proof obligations explicit. I found no incorrect scaling, sign, NTK coefficient, initialization kernel, or leading activity coefficient.

The corrections needed in the written proof are:

1. State a precise multi-sample definition of “block activity.”
2. Prove the cavity-SPD lemma rather than citing “conditioning.”
3. Replace the asserted \(O(t^r)\) expansions by a local integral-remainder lemma.

The corrected details follow.

### 1. Exact normalization

With \(C=nc\), \(\gamma=2/p\), and \(R_{ab}=d^{-1}x_a^\top x_b\),

\[
f_a=\langle C,H^{3,a}\rangle_n.
\]

Direct differentiation gives

\[
\nabla_{A}f_a=D^{3,a}\otimes_nH^{2,a},
\qquad
\nabla_Bf_a=D^{2,a}\otimes_nH^{1,a},
\]

and

\[
\nabla_{w_i}f_a
=
\frac1n\phi'(U_i^a)P_i^{1,a}x_a,
\qquad
\nabla_cf_a=H^{3,a}.
\]

Consequently, with

\[
\lambda_w=\frac nd,\qquad
\lambda_A=\lambda_B=1,\qquad
\lambda_c=\frac1n,
\]

the displayed ODE is exactly correct:

\[
\dot U^a
=
-\gamma\sum_bR_{ab}e_b
\phi'(U^b)P^{1,b},
\]

\[
\dot B=-\gamma\sum_be_bD^{2,b}\otimes_nH^{1,b},
\]

\[
\dot A=-\gamma\sum_be_bD^{3,b}\otimes_nH^{2,b},
\]

\[
\dot C=-\gamma\sum_be_bH^{3,b}.
\]

The four NTK blocks are also exactly

\[
\Theta^{(4)}_{ab}
=
\langle H^{3,a},H^{3,b}\rangle_n,
\]

\[
\Theta^{(3)}_{ab}
=
\langle H^{2,a},H^{2,b}\rangle_n
\langle D^{3,a},D^{3,b}\rangle_n,
\]

\[
\Theta^{(2)}_{ab}
=
\langle H^{1,a},H^{1,b}\rangle_n
\langle D^{2,a},D^{2,b}\rangle_n,
\]

\[
\Theta^{(1)}_{ab}
=
R_{ab}
\langle\phi'(U^a)P^{1,a},
       \phi'(U^b)P^{1,b}\rangle_n.
\]

Therefore

\[
\dot f=-\gamma\Theta e,
\qquad
\dot{\mathcal L}
=-\frac4{p^2}e^\top\Theta e.
\]

For a multi-sample theorem, “block \(\ell\) is active” should be stated as

\[
\int_0^T e(t)^\top\Theta^{(\ell)}(t)e(t)\,dt>0.
\]

This is stronger and less ambiguous than merely saying that the block matrix is nonzero. The proof below establishes it.

### 2. Initialization kernels

For jointly standard Gaussian \(X,Y\) with correlation \(\rho\),

\[
\mathbb E[(\sin X+\cos X)(\sin Y+\cos Y)]
=e^{-1+\rho},
\]

and the same identity holds for \(\phi'=\cos-\sin\). Thus

\[
Q_1=e^{-1+R},\qquad
Q_2=e^{-1+Q_1},\qquad
Q_3=e^{-1+Q_2}.
\]

The proof that these are strictly positive definite is valid.

For \(Q_1\),

\[
(Q_1)_{ab}
=
\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|x_a-x_b\|^2}{2d}\right).
\]

If \(v^\top Q_1v=0\), the Fourier representation of the Gaussian kernel gives

\[
\sum_av_ae^{i\omega^\top x_a}=0
\]

for almost every, hence every, \(\omega\). Choose a direction \(u\) for which \(u^\top x_a\) are pairwise distinct. Restriction to \(\omega=su\) gives a finite linear combination of exponentials with distinct frequencies, forcing \(v=0\).

If \(Q>0\), then

\[
e^{-1+Q}
=
e^{-1}\sum_{r=0}^\infty\frac{Q^{\circ r}}{r!}>0,
\]

because \(Q^{\circ r}\ge0\) by the Schur product theorem and the \(r=1\) summand is strictly positive definite. Hence \(Q_1,Q_2,Q_3>0\).

### 3. Correct cavity-SPD lemma

Let

\[
g(T)=\sum_ay_a\phi(T_a),
\qquad
(v_3)_a=y_ag(T)\phi'(T_a),
\qquad
M_3=\mathbb E[v_3v_3^\top].
\]

Then \(M_3>0\). Indeed, if \(z^\top M_3z=0\), then

\[
g(T)\sum_az_ay_a\phi'(T_a)=0
\]

almost surely. Since \(Q_2>0\), \(T\) has full support. The product is analytic and therefore vanishes on all of \(\mathbb R^p\). Since \(g\not\equiv0\),

\[
\sum_az_ay_a\phi'(t_a)\equiv0.
\]

Differentiating in \(t_c\) gives

\[
z_cy_c\phi''(t_c)\equiv0.
\]

Because \(\phi''=-\phi\not\equiv0\), \(z_c=0\) for every \(c\).

The transpose cavity decomposition has the precise form

\[
\Pi_3=\xi_3+\Lambda_3\phi(S),
\qquad
\xi_3\sim N(0,M_3),
\qquad
\xi_3\perp S.
\]

It follows from the Gaussian row-regression identity: after removing the regression of a matrix column on the finite vector of forward fields, the residual column is independent of those fields; its summed covariance converges to the second moment \(\mathbb E[v_3v_3^\top]=M_3\). The regression part gives

\[
(\Lambda_3)_{ac}
=
\mathbb E[\partial_{T_c}(v_3)_a].
\]

Define

\[
v_2=\phi'(S)\odot\Pi_3,
\qquad
M_2=\mathbb E[v_2v_2^\top].
\]

Conditionally on \(S\),

\[
\operatorname{Cov}(v_2\mid S)
=
D_{\phi'(S)}M_3D_{\phi'(S)}.
\]

Every coordinate \(S_a\) has a continuous \(N(0,1)\) law, while the zero set of \(\phi'\) is discrete. Hence \(D_{\phi'(S)}\) is invertible almost surely. For \(z\ne0\),

\[
z^\top M_2z
\ge
\mathbb E\!\left[
(D_{\phi'(S)}z)^\top
M_3(D_{\phi'(S)}z)
\right]>0.
\]

Thus \(M_2>0\).

The second cavity decomposition is

\[
\Pi_2=\xi_2+\Lambda_2\phi(U),
\qquad
\xi_2\sim N(0,M_2),
\qquad
\xi_2\perp U.
\]

Consequently,

\[
v_1=\phi'(U)\odot\Pi_2,
\qquad
M_1=\mathbb E[v_1v_1^\top]
\]

satisfies \(M_1>0\). This does not require \(R>0\): for every nonzero \(z\), at least one \(z_a\ne0\), and \(\phi'(U_a)\ne0\) almost surely because \(U_a\sim N(0,1)\).

Therefore

\[
\mathcal E_A=\operatorname{tr}(Q_2M_3)>0,
\]

\[
\mathcal E_B=\operatorname{tr}(Q_1M_2)>0,
\]

\[
\mathcal E_w=\operatorname{tr}(RM_1)>0.
\]

For the last assertion, \(R\ge0\) and \(R\ne0\), while \(M_1>0\); hence \(M_1^{1/2}RM_1^{1/2}\) is a nonzero positive-semidefinite matrix and has positive trace.

### 4. Rigorous small-time remainder lemma

The displayed proof should not simply assert differentiability of the action law. The following integral argument suffices.

On the already established bounded short-time region, the forward/backward maps are locally Lipschitz in the controlled \(L^2\) fields. At \(t=0\),

\[
C(0)=0,\qquad e(0)=-y,
\]

and every hidden velocity vanishes.

Let \(g_0=\sum_ay_a\phi(T_0^a)\). From the \(C\)-equation,

\[
\|C(t)\|_2\le Ct.
\]

All hidden velocities are bounded by \(C\|C(t)\|_2\); hence

\[
\|h(t)-h_0\|\le Ct^2,
\qquad h=(w,B,A).
\]

Also \(f(t)=O(t)\), so \(e(t)+y=O(t)\). Substitution back into the equations gives

\[
C(t)=\gamma tg_0+O_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

and

\[
\dot h(t)
=
\gamma^2t\,M_h\nabla\Psi(h_0)+O_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

where

\[
\Psi(h)=\frac12\mathbb E
\left(\sum_ay_a\phi(T^a(h))\right)^2.
\]

Integrating,

\[
h(t)-h_0
=
\frac{\gamma^2t^2}{2}
M_h\nabla\Psi(h_0)+O_{L^2}(t^3).
\]

All remainders here follow directly from the integral equations and local Lipschitz estimates; no unproved \(C^3\) differentiability of the limiting action state is required.

This yields

\[
y^\top\Theta^{(3)}(t)y
=
\gamma^2t^2\mathcal E_A+O(t^3),
\]

\[
y^\top\Theta^{(2)}(t)y
=
\gamma^2t^2\mathcal E_B+O(t^3),
\]

\[
y^\top\Theta^{(1)}(t)y
=
\gamma^2t^2\mathcal E_w+O(t^3).
\]

Moreover,

\[
y^\top\Theta^{(4)}(t)y
=
2\Psi(h(t))
=
2\Psi(h_0)
+
\gamma^2t^2
(\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B+\mathcal E_A)
+
O(t^3).
\]

Therefore

\[
y^\top[\Theta(t)-\Theta(0)]y
=
2\gamma^2t^2
(\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B+\mathcal E_A)
+
O(t^3)>0
\]

for sufficiently small \(t>0\).

Since \(e(t)=-y+O(t)\), each hidden block also satisfies

\[
e(t)^\top\Theta^{(\ell)}(t)e(t)
=
\gamma^2t^2\mathcal E_\ell+O(t^3)>0,
\qquad \ell=1,2,3.
\]

The output block is strictly active already at zero:

\[
e(0)^\top\Theta^{(4)}(0)e(0)
=
y^\top Q_3y>0.
\]

This proves the corrected block-activity condition.

### 5. Feature motion and loss

Let \(V_U,V_S,V_T\) be the coefficients of
\(\dot U/(\gamma^2t)\), \(\dot S/(\gamma^2t)\), and
\(\dot T/(\gamma^2t)\). Chain rule in the three parameter groups gives

\[
D_U\Psi[V_U]=\mathcal E_w,
\]

\[
D_S\Psi[V_S]=\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B,
\]

\[
D_T\Psi[V_T]
=
\mathcal E_w+\mathcal E_B+\mathcal E_A.
\]

All three right-hand sides are positive, so none of \(V_U,V_S,V_T\) is zero. The integral estimates above therefore give both positive integrated velocity and nonzero \(L^2\) displacement for all three hidden preactivations.

At initialization,

\[
f(0)=0,
\qquad
\dot{\mathcal L}(0)
=
-\frac4{p^2}y^\top Q_3y<0.
\]

Equation

\[
\dot{\mathcal L}
=
-\frac4{p^2}e^\top\Theta e\le0
\]

then ensures that once the loss decreases on an initial interval, it remains strictly below its initial value at every later time in the common existence interval.

### 6. Nonlinearity

For \(G\sim N(0,1)\),

\[
\inf_{a,b}\mathbb E|\phi(G)-aG-b|^2>0.
\]

Otherwise continuity and full support would imply
\(\phi(x)=ax+b\) for all \(x\), impossible for
\(\phi(x)=\sin x+\cos x\).

Because \(\phi\) is bounded, this least-squares residual is continuous under \(W_2\) convergence as long as the variance stays bounded away from zero. Every hidden marginal begins as \(N(0,1)\), so nonlinearity remains positive on a nonzero initial interval.

### Remaining localization wording

The exact-GD comparison must be stated on a high-probability localization event controlling the initial Gaussian operator norms and polynomial coordinate maxima. On that event, the finite-dimensional vector field and its derivative are bounded by \(n^q\) for some deterministic \(q\). A bootstrap then gives

\[
\sup_{t\le T}
\|\theta_n^{\mathrm{GD}}(t)-\theta_n^{\mathrm{ODE}}(t)\|
\le
C_T e^{-e^n}n^q e^{n^qT}\to0.
\]

The event has probability tending to one. Without this localization sentence, the original Euler-error paragraph was incomplete, though its conclusion and chosen step size were correct.

Thus this audited sector is valid with the preceding insertions. It does not independently certify the separate all-source Han/state-evolution and cutoff-removal portions; those require their own audits.

Payload:
External-theorem audit verdict: the Han invocation is not rigorous as written. The block construction is valid, but several required bridges are absent.

What the primary source actually proves:

- The asymmetric GFOM has the form
  \[
  u^r=A F_r(v^{<r})+G_r^1(u^{<r}),\qquad
  v^r=A^\top G_r^2(u^{\le r})+F_r^2(v^{<r}),
  \]
  with row-separate maps.
- Its averaged state-evolution theorem requires \(A=A_0/\sqrt m\), independent centered sub-Gaussian entries, proportional dimensions, globally Lipschitz maps with uniformly bounded values at zero, and pseudo-Lipschitz tests.
- It concludes an \(L^q\) bound between empirical averages and finite-\(n\) state-evolution expectations:
  \[
  \bigl(K\Lambda\Lambda_\psi\log N
  (1+\|u^0\|_\infty+\|v^0\|_\infty)\bigr)^{C_0r^5}
  N^{-1/C_0^r}.
  \]
- It does not itself provide an \(n\)-independent action state, continuous-time convergence, adaptive empirical-scalar maps, cutoff removal, or restartability.

The following parts of the previous invocation are correct:

1. With
   \[
   \mathbb A_0=[B_0,A_0^\top]\in\mathbb R^{n\times2n},
   \]
   its entries are genuinely independent \(N(0,1/n)\). Taking Han’s row dimension \(m=n\) and column dimension \(N=2n\) gives exactly \(A=A_0/\sqrt m\) and aspect ratio \(m/N=1/2\).

2. The direct-sum embedding correctly realizes
   \[
   B_0H^1,\quad A_0H^2,\quad A_0^\top D^3,\quad B_0^\top D^2
   \]
   as alternating \(\mathbb A_0\) and \(\mathbb A_0^\top\) queries.

3. Han imposes no nonsingularity condition on the Gaussian state-evolution covariances.

4. Species-restricted empirical averages are legitimate: use a test function that vanishes on one \(n\)-coordinate species and multiply Han’s right average by \(2\). This factor must not be inserted into the state-evolution covariance or response sums.

The missing or incorrect parts are as follows.

### 1. The projected identities do not define a Han GFOM

Both direct-sum components must be retained. With deterministic oracle errors and overlaps, the two stages for cycle \(k\), sample \(a\), must be defined by

\[
F^1=(H_{ka}^1,0),
\]

\[
G^1
=
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}
\bar e_{\ell b}D_{\ell b}^2
\overline{\langle H_{\ell b}^1,H_{ka}^1\rangle},
\]

\[
G^2=H_{ka}^2,
\]

\[
F^2
=
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}\bar e_{\ell b}
\begin{pmatrix}
H_{\ell b}^1
\overline{\langle D_{\ell b}^2,H_{ka}^2\rangle}\\
D_{\ell b}^3
\overline{\langle H_{\ell b}^2,H_{ka}^2\rangle}
\end{pmatrix},
\]

followed by

\[
F^1=(0,D_{ka}^3),
\]

\[
G^1
=
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}
\bar e_{\ell b}H_{\ell b}^2
\overline{\langle D_{\ell b}^3,D_{ka}^3\rangle},
\]

\[
G^2=D_{ka}^2,
\]

\[
F^2
=
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}\bar e_{\ell b}
\begin{pmatrix}
H_{\ell b}^1
\overline{\langle D_{\ell b}^2,D_{ka}^2\rangle}\\
D_{\ell b}^3
\overline{\langle H_{\ell b}^2,D_{ka}^2\rangle}
\end{pmatrix}.
\]

The unused first component after the first stage and unused second component after the second stage cannot simply be discarded; they remain in the GFOM history. The previous proof never supplied these four maps or proved recursively that they reproduce the oracle Euler scheme.

### 2. Han cannot be applied directly to the actual network

Outputs, errors and Gram coefficients are global empirical functions of the matrix-dependent state. Hence the actual network maps are not row-separate.

A valid proof requires:

- a deterministic oracle recursion in which every such scalar is replaced by a prescribed state-evolution expectation;
- application of Han only to that oracle recursion;
- an induction coupling the actual and oracle schemes.

The asserted inequality

\[
d_{k+1}\le(1+C_Rh)d_k+C_Rh\zeta_n
\]

was not proved. To make it true, \(d_k\) must include accumulated rank-memory errors. Query errors themselves are generally \(C_R(d_k+\zeta_n)\), without an \(h\); the \(h\) appears only after they enter a slow update or accumulated rank-one memory. This enlarged-error induction is repairable but absent.

### 3. Independent initialization marks require an explicit conditioning lemma

The proposed formal Han initial vectors are zero, while \(w(0)\) and \(C(0)\) are used as external row marks. This is permissible only after proving:

> If an independent sigma-field \(\mathcal S\) determines the row maps, and conditional on every \(\mathcal S\)-realization those maps satisfy Han’s hypotheses with the same deterministic constants, then Han’s conditional estimate remains valid after conditioning on \(\mathcal S\).

For \(w(0)\), uniformity follows from bounded derivatives of \(\phi\). For \(C(0)\), one needs a smooth deterministic saturation and a high-probability argument showing it is inactive.

Even after this conditioning, Han’s target is a finite-\(n\), mark-dependent state evolution. A separate fixed-stage law-of-large-numbers induction is needed to prove that its covariance, response and oracle coefficients converge to deterministic limits. That induction was missing.

### 4. Global regularity was asserted, not verified

The averaged theorem requires global Lipschitzness and bounded map values at zero. If derivative-based state evolution is used, the relevant maps must also satisfy the stated \(C^3\) bounds.

The proof specified only \(\|\tau_R'\|_\infty\). It must choose, for example,

\[
\tau_R(x)=R\tau(x/R),
\]

with a fixed \(C^\infty\) cutoff satisfying

\[
\|\tau_R^{(j)}\|_\infty\le c_jR^{1-j},
\qquad 1\le j\le3.
\]

The same is needed for the \(C\)-saturation. One must then bound every one of the four GFOM maps above, including its value at zero and derivatives through order three.

For fixed \(R,h,T\), these bounds are obtainable because \(H^1,H^2,D^3,D_R^2\) and the two slow updates are bounded. They were not supplied.

### 5. The growing-iteration application is unsupported

Although Han’s estimate is nonasymptotic and may in principle be evaluated at \(r=r_n\), the previous proof did not establish:

- the claimed
  \[
  \log\Lambda_n\le Cs_n(1+R_n)^3;
  \]
- corresponding bounds for every test-function constant;
- uniform concentration of the external-mark conditional state evolution;
- uniform actual-oracle coupling over \(s_n\) stages.

Thus the displayed choice \(h_n=(\log\log n)^{-1/2}\) is not justified.

The clean repair is to avoid growing Han iterations entirely:

1. Fix \(R<\infty\) and an auxiliary mesh \(h>0\).
2. Apply Han for the resulting fixed number \(2p\lceil T/h\rceil\) of stages.
3. Prove convergence of the fixed-mesh oracle and actual schemes.
4. Use a dimension-uniform cutoff Euler estimate and send \(h\downarrow0\).
5. Use the proved cutoff-tail estimate and send \(R\uparrow\infty\).
6. Finally compare exact GD with the finite-width ODE using the prescribed \(\eta_{0,n}\).

This is a three-\(\varepsilon\) proof of convergence along the full \(n\)-sequence; it is not an illicit iterated model limit.

### 6. Han does not prove the action-state conclusion

The paragraphs invoking “enumerating probes,” a GNS completion and restartability do not follow from Han alone. A separate lemma must prove:

- simultaneous convergence of every finite family of typed probes;
- boundedness of the induced operator on the quotient;
- passage of
  \[
  \langle \mathbb A v,u\rangle=\langle v,\mathbb A^*u\rangle;
  \]
- closure under the rank-one evolution;
- convergence in the countable action metric;
- uniqueness determined solely by the current action state.

None of these conclusions is asserted by the cited theorem.

### 7. Source-level qualification

The primary arXiv source formally states the needed asymmetric averaged state-evolution theorem. However, its averaged proof is omitted as “largely similar,” and its asymmetric AMP ingredient is described as a variant/easy extension of another theorem rather than proved completely in the source. Therefore:

- if formally stated external theorems are accepted, Han may be imported after all hypotheses above are verified;
- if the requested standard requires auditing the proof of every imported theorem itself, the Han preprint does not by itself close that obligation.

Overall, the matrix embedding and intended GFOM route survive the audit, but the previous response’s claim that “no unresolved probabilistic, limit-exchange, closure, or activity gap remains” is false. The Han-dependent portion is repairable using fixed auxiliary meshes, exact four-map encoding, oracle coupling, side-mark conditioning and a separate action-state closure theorem; those proofs still have to be supplied before the \(L=3\) theorem can be called rigorous.

Verdict: the preceding \(L=3\) theorem is not proved as written. I found no contradiction to the proposed scaling, but the central width-to-continuous-time bridge contains several substantive gaps. The claim that “no unresolved gap remains” must be withdrawn.

## 1. External theorem audit

The narrow invocation of Han’s asymmetric averaged state-evolution theorem is potentially valid:

- \(\mathbb A_0=[B_0,A_0^\top]\) is \(n\times 2n\) with iid \(N(0,1/n)\) entries.
- In Han’s notation the row dimension is \(m=n\), the column dimension is \(2n\), so the aspect ratio is admissible.
- Conditioning on the independent \(U_0,C_0\) marks is allowed, provided they enter through deterministic row-dependent maps with uniform Lipschitz/value bounds.
- Species masks convert Han’s \(1/(2n)\) right averages into the desired \(1/n\) block averages.
- Singular state-evolution covariances are not excluded by the theorem.

But Han’s theorem supplies only empirical state evolution for an already specified deterministic row-separate GFOM. It does not itself prove:

- replacement of adaptive empirical outputs and overlaps by deterministic oracle values;
- the proposed all-source response estimates;
- convergence of a growing-step recursion unless the \(n\)-dependent map constants are explicitly verified;
- an operator/GNS limit;
- an ODE limit, cutoff removal, path-law convergence, uniqueness, or restartability.

Thus the theorem was used beyond its stated conclusion.

## 2. The all-source lemma fails audit

The displayed implications

\[
J_{k+1}
\le
J_k\!\left[1+Ch\Bigl(1+\sum_a(|P_k^{1,a}|+|P_k^{2,a}|)\Bigr)\right],
\]

\[
K_2\le C_0T(1+J)+C_0TJ K_2+C_0T^3,
\]

\[
K_1\le
C_0\!\left[K_2+(K_2+T)J+TK_2^2\right]
\]

cannot be certified from the proof.

Specifically:

1. \(J_k\) was never defined as a precise norm over typed Gaussian sources, source times, sample indices, and the two right species.

2. The exact derivative recursion was not written. It must include derivatives of \(U,C,H^1,S,H^2,T,H^3,D^3,P^2,D^2,P^1\), every rank-one memory, and every cross-stage response.

3. Only a few within-cycle responses were mentioned. The complete historical response matrix and all cross-species terms were not enumerated.

4. No induction proves that every off-cycle response contains exactly one factor \(h\), nor that no current-cycle unbounded field occurs without such a factor.

5. The claimed \(\psi _2\) closure requires a deterministic \(\ell^1\)-bound on all response coefficients in the representation
   \[
   P=\text{Gaussian innovation}+\sum_r c_r X_r.
   \]
   That coefficient bound was not derived.

6. The generalized-Hölder calculation presupposes the very uniform \(\psi _2\) bounds \(K_1,K_2\) it is intended to establish. A valid simultaneous first-exit induction is possible in principle, but none was supplied.

7. Consequently,
   \[
   \mathbb E[P^2\mathbf 1_{\{|P|>R\}}]\le Ce^{-cR^2/T^2}
   \]
   is unsupported.

These are not presentation omissions. This lemma is the essential new estimate on which cutoff removal and continuous-time identification depend. It is plausible, but presently unproved.

A sufficient corrected lemma would have to state and prove, uniformly in \(R\ge1\) and \(0<h\le h_0\),

\[
\sup_{kh\le T_*,a}
\bigl(
\|P_k^{1,a}\|_{\psi _2}
+
\|P_k^{2,a}\|_{\psi _2}
\bigr)
\le C T_*,
\]

and

\[
\sup_{kh\le T_*}
\sum_{\substack{\text{all earlier}\\\text{typed sources }s}}
\left(
\mathbb E|\partial_sX_k|
+
|\mathbb E\partial_sX_k|
\right)
\le C T_*,
\]

with the source index set, aggregation norm, initialization, and complete recursion specified explicitly. The earlier argument does not prove this lemma.

## 3. Oracle replacement is incomplete

For fixed \(h,R\), one can plausibly compare the adaptive finite-width recursion with a deterministic-oracle GFOM by

\[
d_{k+1}\le(1+C_Rh)d_k+C_Rh\,\zeta_{n,k}.
\]

However, the proof must first:

- list every oracle scalar;
- show that the actual-minus-oracle scalar is Lipschitz in \(d_k\);
- apply Han to each oracle scalar;
- control all scalar errors simultaneously;
- handle the conditional iid side marks and then remove the conditioning.

None of this induction was supplied. Han cannot be applied directly to maps containing the random empirical errors and overlaps.

## 4. Cutoff removal and uniqueness are not established

The algebraic split

\[
\begin{aligned}
\phi'(S)P-\phi'(S_R)\tau_R(P_R)
={}&\phi'(S)(P-P_R)\\
&+[\phi'(S)-\phi'(S_R)]P_R\\
&+\phi'(S_R)[P_R-\tau_R(P_R)]
\end{aligned}
\]

is correct. But the resulting estimate requires the missing uniform empirical tail bound.

There is a second issue: the asserted global cutoff Lipschitz bound

\[
L_R\le C(1+R)
\]

is not true on the entire cutoff state space merely from the definition of the cutoff. It is valid only on an invariant region where \(\|A\|_{\rm op}\), \(\|B\|_{\rm op}\), \(\|C\|_\infty\), and \(\|D^2\|_2\) have already been bounded independently of \(R\). That invariant-region estimate was not proved.

Likewise, comparison with cutoff solutions does not prove uniqueness among arbitrary finite-energy solutions. It proves uniqueness only in a class having a common uniform-integrability or sub-Gaussian tail estimate. Such a solution class must be stated, and every candidate solution must be shown to belong to it.

Conditional on those missing facts, the correct comparison would be

\[
D_R'(t)
\le C(1+R)D_R(t)
+C\sum_{\ell=1}^2
\|P_R^\ell(t)\mathbf1_{\{|P_R^\ell(t)|>R/2\}}\|_2,
\]

and hence

\[
D_R(T)
\le
Ce^{CRT}e^{-cR^2/T^2}\longrightarrow0.
\]

At present, the hypothesis producing the last term is unproved.

## 5. The mesh argument should be replaced

The simultaneous choice

\[
h_n=(\log\log n)^{-1/2},
\qquad
R_n=a\log\log\log n
\]

requires a proved \(n\)-dependent bound on the global Lipschitz constants of every serialized GFOM map. The assertion

\[
\log\Lambda_n\le Cs_n(1+R_n)^3
\]

was not derived from the actual maps.

A safer rigorous quantifier order is:

1. Fix \(R<\infty\) and \(h>0\).
2. Apply Han for the resulting fixed finite number of stages.
3. Couple different fixed meshes on the same finite-width matrix and prove
   \[
   d_{\rm act}(\mathfrak S_{h,R},\mathfrak S_{h',R})
   \le C_R(h+h').
   \]
4. Let \(h,h'\downarrow0\).
5. Use the uniform-in-\(R\) tail lemma to let \(R\to\infty\).
6. Only afterward compare exact GD to the finite-width ODE.

This fixed-\((h,R)\) \(\varepsilon\)-argument is enough for full-sequence joint convergence and avoids needing a growing-iteration extension of Han.

## 6. Action-law/GNS closure is asserted, not proved

To obtain an actual autonomous state, one must construct it.

A valid construction would:

- enumerate a countable typed algebra containing rational linear combinations, bounded Lipschitz cylinder functions, products needed by the dynamics, and applications of \(\mathbb A,\mathbb A^*\);
- prove convergence and projective consistency of every finite joint probe law;
- realize those laws on probability spaces \(\Omega_1,\Omega_2,\Omega_3\);
- quotient by \(L^2\)-null vectors and complete;
- pass the finite-width estimate
  \[
  \|\mathbb A_nv\|_2\le M\|v\|_2
  \]
  to the dense probe algebra;
- prove that \(\mathbb A\) extends boundedly and that
  \[
  \langle\mathbb Av,u\rangle
  =
  \langle v,\mathbb A^*u\rangle;
  \]
- prove that the nonlinear coordinate maps and rank-one updates are closed on this realized state;
- prove uniqueness in a specified tail-controlled solution class.

The earlier paragraphs do not establish these points. In particular, Gram convergence alone gives a Hilbert space but not the probability algebra needed for pointwise nonlinearities.

## 7. Exact-GD diagonalization is repairable

The choice

\[
\eta_{0,n}=e^{-e^n}
\]

does dominate every fixed polynomial-width local Lipschitz bound. But a rigorous Euler comparison still needs:

- a high-probability stopping region;
- polynomial bounds on the vector field and its derivative there;
- proof that both the ODE and Euler iterates remain inside that region;
- an explicit stopped Euler estimate;
- removal of the stopping event.

This part is routine relative to the missing response lemma and appears repairable.

## 8. Path-\(W_2\) convergence is incomplete

Mesh-point convergence alone does not imply convergence in
\(W_2(C([0,T];\mathbb R^p))\).

The needed tightness estimate is

\[
\frac1n\sum_i
\omega(z_i,\delta)^2
\le
\delta
\int_0^T
\frac1n\sum_i|\dot z_i(t)|^2\,dt.
\]

For \(W_2\), one must additionally prove uniform integrability of

\[
\frac1n\sum_i\sup_{t\le T}|z_i(t)|^2.
\]

That requires higher-moment or tail bounds for the velocities. Again, this ultimately depends on the missing all-source estimate.

## Final bridge assessment

- The combined-operator reduction is sound.
- Han’s theorem is a plausible fixed-mesh probabilistic input and its basic normalization matches.
- The exact-GD step can likely be repaired.
- The all-source response/tail theorem, oracle induction, cutoff removal, action-state construction, uniqueness, and path-\(W_2\) passage are not proved.
- I found no decisive counterexample showing the proposed theorem is false.
- I also found no way to certify the stated all-source inequalities from the supplied argument.

Therefore the correct audited status is:

\[
\boxed{\text{The affirmative \(L=3\) theorem remains unproved, not disproved.}}
\]

A new audit cannot pass cleanly until the uniform all-source response-tail lemma and the six downstream bridge lemmas above are supplied in full.

The Han-dependent part can be repaired completely for every fixed auxiliary mesh \(h>0\) and cutoff \(R<\infty\). It does not, on the present argument, justify the growing-stage choice \(h=h_n\downarrow0\).

## 1. Exact fixed-\((h,R)\) oracle GFOM

Let

\[
\mathbb A_0=[B_0,A_0^\top]:
\mathbb R^n\oplus\mathbb R^n\to\mathbb R^n.
\]

Write the two right species as type \(1\) and type \(3\). Use the physical norms

\[
\|x\|_s^2=\frac1n\sum_i x_i^2,
\qquad
\|(x,z)\|_{1\oplus3}^2=\|x\|_1^2+\|z\|_3^2.
\]

Choose fixed \(C^\infty\) functions

\[
\tau_R(x)=R\tau(x/R),
\qquad
\chi_B(x)=B\chi(x/B),
\]

where \(\tau,\chi\) are odd, equal to the identity on \([-1,1]\), bounded, and have bounded derivatives of every order. Thus

\[
\|\tau_R^{(j)}\|_\infty\le c_jR^{1-j},
\qquad
\|\chi_B^{(j)}\|_\infty\le c_jB^{1-j}.
\]

Use

\[
D^{2,a}=\phi'(S^a)\tau_R(P^{2,a}),
\qquad
D^{3,a}=\chi_B(C)\phi'(T^a),
\]

and use \(\tau_R(P^{1,a})\) in the \(U\)-update.

For each cycle \(k\) and sample \(a\), perform two asymmetric-GFOM stages. A bar over an inner product denotes the deterministic infinite-population state-evolution expectation of that inner product.

### First stage

Set

\[
F_{ka,1}^{\langle1\rangle}
=
(H_{ka}^1,0),
\]

\[
G_{ka,1}^{\langle1\rangle}
=
-\gamma h
\sum_{\ell<k,b}
\bar e_{\ell b}D_{\ell b}^2
\,
\overline{\langle H_{\ell b}^1,H_{ka}^1\rangle}.
\]

Then the left iterate is \(S_{ka}\). Set

\[
G_{ka,1}^{\langle2\rangle}=H_{ka}^2=\phi(S_{ka}),
\]

and

\[
F_{ka,1}^{\langle2\rangle}
=
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}\bar e_{\ell b}
\begin{pmatrix}
H_{\ell b}^1\,
\overline{\langle D_{\ell b}^2,H_{ka}^2\rangle}\\[1mm]
D_{\ell b}^3\,
\overline{\langle H_{\ell b}^2,H_{ka}^2\rangle}
\end{pmatrix}.
\]

The second component of the resulting right iterate is \(T_{ka}\). Its first component is not physically used, but must remain in the GFOM history.

### Second stage

Set

\[
F_{ka,2}^{\langle1\rangle}
=
(0,D_{ka}^3),
\]

\[
G_{ka,2}^{\langle1\rangle}
=
-\gamma h
\sum_{\ell<k,b}
\bar e_{\ell b}H_{\ell b}^2
\,
\overline{\langle D_{\ell b}^3,D_{ka}^3\rangle}.
\]

The resulting left iterate is \(P_{ka}^2\). Define

\[
G_{ka,2}^{\langle2\rangle}
=
D_{ka}^2
=
\phi'(S_{ka})\tau_R(P_{ka}^2),
\]

and

\[
F_{ka,2}^{\langle2\rangle}
=
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}\bar e_{\ell b}
\begin{pmatrix}
H_{\ell b}^1\,
\overline{\langle D_{\ell b}^2,D_{ka}^2\rangle}\\[1mm]
D_{\ell b}^3\,
\overline{\langle H_{\ell b}^2,D_{ka}^2\rangle}
\end{pmatrix}.
\]

The first component of the resulting right iterate is \(P_{ka}^1\). Again, the unused second component remains in the history.

After all \(2p\) stages of cycle \(k\), define coordinatewise

\[
U_{k+1}^a
=
U_k^a
-
\gamma h\sum_b
R_{ab}\bar e_{kb}
\phi'(U_k^b)\tau_R(P_{kb}^1),
\]

\[
C_{k+1}
=
C_k-\gamma h\sum_b\bar e_{kb}H_{kb}^3.
\]

These variables are decoder functions of the right GFOM history and independent external marks. The physical state remains frozen during the \(2p\) query stages.

The deterministic coefficients are constructed recursively. Before a left iterate is generated, every coefficient used in \(F^{\langle1\rangle},G^{\langle1\rangle}\) depends only on already constructed state-evolution laws. After the current left Gaussian law is constructed, the current overlaps used in \(F^{\langle2\rangle}\) are determined. There is therefore no fixed-point or anticipative definition.

This is now exactly of Han’s asymmetric form

\[
u^r=\mathbb A_0F_r^{\langle1\rangle}(v^{<r})
     +G_r^{\langle1\rangle}(u^{<r}),
\]

\[
v^r=\mathbb A_0^\top G_r^{\langle2\rangle}(u^{\le r})
     +F_r^{\langle2\rangle}(v^{<r}).
\]

## 2. Verification of Han’s hypotheses

Condition on the independent side marks

\[
\mathcal S_n
=
\sigma\{w_i(0),C_j(0):i,j\le n\}.
\]

Replace \(C_j(0)\) temporarily by a smooth clipping at level \(1\). Since

\[
C_j(0)=nc_j(0)\sim N(0,n^{-2}),
\]

\[
\Pr\!\left(\max_j|C_j(0)|>1\right)
\le 2n e^{-n^2/2}\to0.
\]

The \(w_i(0)\)-marks occur only inside bounded functions
\(\phi(w_i(0)^\top x_a+\cdot)\) and their derivatives, so no clipping of \(w_i(0)\) is needed for the row-map regularity.

Conditional on \(\mathcal S_n\):

- the maps above are deterministic;
- they are row-separate;
- the formal Han initial vectors may be chosen as zero;
- \(\mathbb A_0\) remains independent of the side marks.

For fixed

\[
K_h=\left\lceil T/h\right\rceil,
\qquad
s=2pK_h,
\]

define recursively

\[
L_0=2,
\qquad
L_{r+1}
=
C_{p,R,B,T}(1+L_r)^3,
\qquad 0\le r<s.
\]

Faà di Bruno’s formula and the displayed cutoff bounds show inductively that every row decoder and every one of the four maps at stage \(r\) has:

- absolute value at zero at most \(L_r\);
- all derivatives through order three at most \(L_r\);
- global Lipschitz constant at most \(L_r\).

The recursion terminates because \(s<\infty\), and

\[
\Lambda_{h,R,T}:=\max_{r\le s}L_r<\infty
\]

is deterministic and independent of \(n\).

Moreover,

\[
\mathbb A_0=\frac{G}{\sqrt n},
\qquad
G\in\mathbb R^{n\times2n}
\]

has iid \(N(0,1)\) entries. Han’s row dimension is \(m=n\), its column dimension is \(N=2n\), and

\[
\frac mN=\frac12.
\]

Thus the normalization, independence, sub-Gaussian and aspect-ratio hypotheses hold exactly.

The conditioning extension is immediate but should be stated:

> If \(\mathcal S\) is independent of \(A\), and conditional on every \(\mathcal S\)-realization the maps satisfy Han’s hypotheses with the same deterministic constants, then Han’s conditional \(L^q\) estimate holds conditional on \(\mathcal S\). Integrating the conditional estimate gives the unconditional one.

This follows simply by applying the deterministic-map theorem under each regular conditional law.

## 3. Deterministic side-mark state evolution

For fixed \(s\), the conditional finite-\(n\) state evolution still depends on the side marks. Its deterministic limit follows by induction over the \(s\) stages.

At a given stage, all new covariance and response coefficients are finite averages of bounded continuous functions of:

- the iid type-1 mark \(w_i(0)\);
- the clipped type-3 mark \(C_j(0)\);
- finitely many previously constructed Gaussian coordinates;
- previously converged deterministic coefficients.

Assume all coefficients through stage \(r-1\) converge. The strong law applied separately to the two right species shows that the covariance and response sums at stage \(r\) converge to their population expectations.

If a limiting covariance is singular, choose the canonical positive semidefinite square root. The map

\[
\Sigma\longmapsto\Sigma^{1/2}
\]

is continuous on finite-dimensional positive semidefinite matrices. Coupling the Gaussian vectors as \(\Sigma_n^{1/2}G\) and using bounded dominated convergence proves convergence of every needed Gaussian expectation. No inverse covariance appears.

This closes the induction. Consequently, for every scalar in the finite ledger of outputs and overlaps,

\[
\bar s_{n,r}\longrightarrow \bar s_r.
\]

Applying Han’s averaged theorem to products and smooth tail tests then gives, conditionally and hence unconditionally,

\[
\zeta_n
:=
\max_{r\le s}
\max_{q\in\mathcal Q}
|\widehat q_{n,r}-\bar q_r|
\ \xrightarrow{\Pr}\ 0,
\]

where \(\mathcal Q\) contains precisely the output and six overlap types appearing in the four maps.

Thus deterministic side-mark/oracle passage is valid for fixed \(h,R\).

## 4. Actual–oracle coupling

Let \(X_k\) be the actual cutoff Euler state and \(\widehat X_k\) the oracle state. Define the learned rank parts

\[
R_k^B
=
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}
e_{\ell b}D_{\ell b}^2\otimes_nH_{\ell b}^1,
\]

\[
R_k^A
=
-\gamma h\sum_{\ell<k,b}
e_{\ell b}D_{\ell b}^3\otimes_nH_{\ell b}^2,
\]

and define \(\widehat R_k^B,\widehat R_k^A\) similarly using oracle vectors and \(\bar e_{\ell b}\).

Set

\[
\delta_k
=
\max_a\|U_k^a-\widehat U_k^a\|_1
+\|C_k-\widehat C_k\|_3
+\|R_k^B-\widehat R_k^B\|_{\rm op}
+\|R_k^A-\widehat R_k^A\|_{\rm op}.
\]

On the event \(\|\mathbb A_0\|_{\rm op}\le4\), sequential application of the four query formulas gives

\[
q_k
\le
C_{R,T}(\delta_k+\zeta_n),
\]

where \(q_k\) is the maximum \(L^2\)-difference of
\(S,T,P^2,P^1,H^r,D^r\) during cycle \(k\).

The \(\zeta_n\) term occurs because applying \(\widehat R_k^B\) or \(\widehat R_k^A\) produces empirical oracle overlaps, whereas the oracle GFOM uses their deterministic limits.

For normalized rank-one operators,

\[
\|u\otimes_nv-u'\otimes_nv'\|_{\rm op}
\le
\|u-u'\|\,\|v\|
+
\|u'\|\,\|v-v'\|.
\]

All cutoff factors are uniformly bounded. Moreover,

\[
|e_{ka}-\bar e_{ka}|
\le
C_{R,T}(\delta_k+q_k+\zeta_n).
\]

Therefore the slow-state and rank-memory updates give

\[
\delta_{k+1}
\le
(1+C_{R,T}h)\delta_k
+
C_{R,T}h\zeta_n.
\]

Since \(\delta_0=0\), discrete Grönwall yields

\[
\max_{kh\le T}\delta_k
\le
C_{R,T}e^{C_{R,T}T}\zeta_n
\xrightarrow{\Pr}0.
\]

This proves pointwise fixed-\((h,R)\) identification of the actual cutoff Euler network with the deterministic Gaussian oracle.

## 5. Action-algebra closure

A precise closure construction is available.

For each type, form the countable probe algebra generated by:

- the external marks \(U_0^a,C_0\) and constants;
- rational linear combinations;
- the coordinate maps \(\phi,\phi',\tau_R,\chi_B\);
- \(\mathbb A_0\), \(\operatorname{pr}_1\mathbb A_0^\ast\), and
  \(\operatorname{pr}_3\mathbb A_0^\ast\);
- all finite compositions of these operations.

For every finite family of probes, the preceding fixed-stage Han argument gives convergence of all same-type Gram matrices and joint coordinate laws. Quotient each probe span by its limiting zero seminorm and complete it, producing

\[
\mathcal H_1,\qquad \mathcal H_2,\qquad\mathcal H_3.
\]

The Gaussian operator-norm estimate and the rank-update estimate give, with probability tending to one,

\[
\|\mathbb A_n(t)\|_{\rm op}
\le 4+C_{R,T}T.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\|\mathbb A p\|_{\mathcal H_2}
\le
(4+C_{R,T}T)\|p\|_{\mathcal H_1\oplus\mathcal H_3}
\]

on the dense probe domain, so \(\mathbb A\) extends uniquely to a bounded operator. The finite-width identity

\[
\langle\mathbb A_np,q\rangle
=
\langle p,\mathbb A_n^\ast q\rangle
\]

passes to the Gram limit; hence the limiting transpose operation is the Hilbert adjoint.

Lipschitz coordinate maps descend to the quotient because

\[
\|\psi(X)-\psi(Y)\|_2
\le
\|\psi\|_{\rm Lip}\|X-Y\|_2.
\]

The cutoff vector field is therefore an autonomous locally Lipschitz map on

\[
(\mathcal H_1)^p
\times
\mathcal H_3
\times
\mathcal B(\mathcal H_1\oplus\mathcal H_3,\mathcal H_2).
\]

For example,

\[
\|\delta D^2\|_2
\le
\|\phi''\|_\infty\|\tau_R\|_\infty\|\delta S\|_2
+
\|\phi'\|_\infty\|\tau_R'\|_\infty\|\delta P^2\|_2,
\]

and the corresponding \(D^3\), operator-action and rank-one bounds are analogous. Picard–Lindelöf therefore gives a unique cutoff action-state flow. The construction is equivariant under Hilbert-space isomorphisms, so its isomorphism class is autonomous and restartable.

A countable weighted metric on probe laws then gives full action-state convergence by first truncating to finitely many probes and applying the fixed-mesh result.

## Final verdict

After these repairs:

- Han genuinely applies to the fixed-\((h,R)\) oracle;
- deterministic side-mark state evolution is justified for every fixed finite stage count;
- the actual cutoff Euler scheme is coupled to that oracle;
- the operator-action state admits a closed autonomous realization.

Han does not yet cover the proposed \(h_n\)-dependent growing number of stages. Doing so would additionally require quantitative uniform bounds for the row maps, tests, side-mark state-evolution induction and oracle coupling. Those were not proved.

The rigorous route is therefore:

\[
\text{fix }R,h
\;\longrightarrow\;
n\to\infty
\;\longrightarrow\;
h\downarrow0
\;\longrightarrow\;
R\uparrow\infty,
\]

with the \(h\)-Euler and \(R\)-cutoff errors uniform in \(n\). Combined with the separate uniform exact-GD/finite-width-ODE estimate, this is a valid three-\(\varepsilon\) proof of convergence along the actual full diagonal sequence \(n\to\infty\), \(\eta_{0,n}\to0\).

The all-source lemma can be repaired. The earlier schematic inequality with an \(O(1)\) term in the \(P^2\) bound was too weak; the correct leading term is \(O(T)\). Here is a self-contained formulation and proof.

## Uniform all-source lemma

Let \(p\) be fixed, \(\phi=\sin+\cos\), and let \(\tau_R\in C^\infty\) satisfy

\[
|\tau_R(x)|\le |x|,
\qquad
|\tau_R'(x)|\le1,
\qquad
\tau_R(x)=x\quad (|x|\le R).
\]

Consider the deterministic-oracle version of the cutoff Euler recursion, with physical step \(h\), \(Nh\le T\), and

\[
D^{3,a}=C\phi'(T^a),
\qquad
D^{2,a}=\phi'(S^a)\tau_R(P^{2,a}),
\]

while \(\tau_R(P^{1,a})\) is used in the \(U\)-update.

There are constants \(T_0,c,C>0\), depending only on \(p\) and the bounds

\[
\|\phi\|_\infty,\quad
\|\phi'\|_\infty,\quad
\|\phi''\|_\infty,
\]

such that, for every \(R\ge1\), every \(h>0\), every \(Nh\le T_0\), and every oracle cycle \(k\le N\),

\[
\max_a
\left(
\|P_k^{1,a}\|_{\psi_2}
+
\|P_k^{2,a}\|_{\psi_2}
\right)
\le CT,
\tag{A}
\]

and

\[
\mathbb E\!\left[
|P_k^{r,a}|^2
\mathbf1_{\{|P_k^{r,a}|>u\}}
\right]
\le
CT^2
\left(1+\frac{u^2}{T^2}\right)
e^{-cu^2/T^2},
\qquad r=1,2.
\tag{B}
\]

All constants are independent of \(h,R,N\) and of singularities in the joint Gaussian covariance.

### Formal source indices

Order the two matrix queries for every sample and physical cycle lexicographically. A micro-index is

\[
r=(k,a,s),
\qquad
0\le k<N,\quad
1\le a\le p,\quad
s\in\{1,2\}.
\]

Let \(Z_r\) and \(Y_r\) denote the formal left and right Gaussian coordinates in asymmetric state evolution. They may be correlated, including perfectly correlated. Formal partial derivatives are nevertheless taken in the ambient coordinates of the deterministic decoder maps.

For a scalar decoder \(X\), define

\[
|D_{<k}X|_1
=
\sum_{\substack{r=(\ell,a,s)\\ \ell<k}}
\left(
|\partial_{Z_r}X|
+
|\partial_{Y_r}X|
\right).
\tag{C}
\]

No covariance inverse or independent-source representation is used.

The two query pairs are

\[
F_{ka1}=(H_k^{1,a},0),
\qquad
G_{ka1}=H_k^{2,a},
\]

\[
F_{ka2}=(0,D_k^{3,a}),
\qquad
G_{ka2}=D_k^{2,a}.
\tag{D}
\]

The exact asymmetric state-evolution decoder has the triangular form

\[
L_r
=
Z_r+\sum_{q<r}\alpha_{rq}G_q+\ell_r,
\]

\[
R_r
=
Y_r+\sum_{q\le r}\beta_{rq}F_q+r_r,
\tag{E}
\]

where

\[
\alpha_{rq}
=
\mathbb E[\partial_{Y_q}F_r],
\qquad
\beta_{rq}
=
\mathbb E[\partial_{Z_q}G_r],
\tag{F}
\]

with the appropriate species masks. Those masks have operator norm one and do not affect the bounds. The terms \(\ell_r,r_r\) are precisely the learned rank-one memories. In physical notation,

\[
L_{ka1}=S_k^a,\qquad
R_{ka1}^{(3)}=T_k^a,
\]

\[
L_{ka2}=P_k^{2,a},\qquad
R_{ka2}^{(1)}=P_k^{1,a}.
\]

All oracle errors and empirical overlaps in \(\ell_r,r_r\) are deterministic. Hence they are not differentiated in (C).

### Weighted reachable-history norm

Define

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal S_k
={}&
\sum_a |D_{<k}U_k^a|_1
+
|D_{<k}C_k|_1\\
&+
h\sum_{\ell<k,a}
\Big(
|D_{<k}H_\ell^{1,a}|_1
+
|D_{<k}H_\ell^{2,a}|_1\\
&\hspace{45mm}
+
|D_{<k}D_\ell^{3,a}|_1
+
|D_{<k}D_\ell^{2,a}|_1
\Big).
\end{aligned}
\tag{G}
\]

This includes every source history reachable through either a slow parameter update or a learned rank-one matrix update. Histories are not truncated.

Within cycle \(k\), let \(\mathcal W_k\) be the sum of the corresponding full derivative norms of

\[
S_k^a,\ H_k^{2,a},\ T_k^a,\ D_k^{3,a},\
P_k^{2,a},\ D_k^{2,a},\ P_k^{1,a},
\qquad a=1,\ldots,p,
\]

including the current-cycle sources.

### Exact derivative table

Put

\[
H=\|\phi\|_\infty,\qquad
J=\|\phi'\|_\infty,\qquad
J_2=\|\phi''\|_\infty.
\]

The nonlinear nodes satisfy, pathwise,

\[
|DH^{1,a}|\le J|DU^a|,
\]

\[
|DH^{2,a}|\le J|DS^a|,
\]

\[
|DD^{3,a}|
\le
J|DC|+J_2|C|\,|DT^a|,
\]

and, crucially,

\[
|DD^{2,a}|
\le
J|DP^{2,a}|
+
J_2|P^{2,a}|\,|DS^a|.
\tag{H}
\]

The last inequality is uniform in \(R\) because

\[
|\tau_R(P^2)|\le|P^2|,
\qquad
|\tau_R'(P^2)|\le1.
\]

Differentiating (E) gives exactly

\[
DL_r
=
dZ_r+\sum_{q<r}\alpha_{rq}DG_q+D\ell_r,
\]

\[
DR_r
=
dY_r+\sum_{q\le r}\beta_{rq}DF_q+Dr_r.
\tag{I}
\]

There are no derivatives of \(\alpha,\beta\), because they are deterministic oracle coefficients.

The rank memories are the four exact terms

\[
h\sum_{\ell<k,b}
D_{\ell b}^2
\langle H_{\ell b}^1,H_k^{1,a}\rangle,
\]

\[
h\sum_{\ell<k,b}
D_{\ell b}^3
\langle H_{\ell b}^2,H_k^{2,a}\rangle,
\]

\[
h\sum_{\ell<k,b}
H_{\ell b}^2
\langle D_{\ell b}^3,D_k^{3,a}\rangle,
\]

\[
h\sum_{\ell<k,b}
H_{\ell b}^1
\langle D_{\ell b}^2,D_k^{2,a}\rangle.
\tag{J}
\]

Their scalar overlaps are deterministic in the oracle recursion. Under the bootstrap

\[
\max_{\ell<k,a,r}\|P_\ell^{r,a}\|_{\psi_2}\le1,
\tag{K}
\]

they are bounded respectively by constants times

\[
TK_2,\qquad T^2,\qquad T^3,\qquad TK_2^2,
\tag{L}
\]

where

\[
K_r=\max_{\ell\le k,a}\|P_\ell^{r,a}\|_{\psi_2}.
\]

Applying (H) and (I) successively through the \(2p\) acyclic microstages gives

\[
\mathcal W_k
\le
C_p
\left(1+\sum_a|P_k^{2,a}|\right)
(1+\mathcal S_k).
\tag{M}
\]

This is a finite induction over the ordered nodes. Its only potentially unbounded multiplier is the second term in (H); all remaining multipliers are bounded by \(H,J,J_2\), the fixed number of samples, or the overlap bounds in (L).

### Slow-history recursion

Differentiate the \(C\)- and \(U\)-updates and append the four new history factors in (G). Since the oracle errors are deterministic,

\[
\mathcal S_{k+1}
\le
\mathcal S_k
+
Ch
\left(
1+\sum_a(|P_k^{1,a}|+|P_k^{2,a}|)
\right)
(1+\mathcal S_k).
\tag{N}
\]

Thus, with

\[
X_k=1+\sum_a(|P_k^{1,a}|+|P_k^{2,a}|),
\]

\[
1+\mathcal S_k
\le
\exp\left(Ch\sum_{\ell<k}X_\ell\right).
\tag{O}
\]

No independence between the \(X_\ell\) is used.

Under (K), generalized Hölder gives, for every fixed \(q\ge1\),

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb E
\exp\left(qCh\sum_{\ell<k}X_\ell\right)
&\le
\prod_{\ell<k}
\left(
\mathbb E e^{qChkX_\ell}
\right)^{1/k}\\
&\le
\exp(C_qT),
\end{aligned}
\tag{P}
\]

because \(kh\le T\) and each \(X_\ell\) has uniformly bounded sub-Gaussian norm. Also,

\[
\left\|
h\sum_{\ell<k}X_\ell
\right\|_{L^4}
\le CT.
\]

Using \(e^x-1\le xe^x\) for \(x\ge0\), (O)–(P) yield

\[
\|\mathcal S_k\|_{L^2}\le CT.
\tag{Q}
\]

### Historical response mass

Let

\[
\Gamma_k
=
\sum_{\substack{r\text{ in cycle }k\\q\text{ in cycles }<k}}
\bigl(|\alpha_{rq}|+|\beta_{rq}|\bigr).
\]

Equations (F), (H), and the fact that every dependence on an earlier cycle is contained in \(\mathcal S_k\) give

\[
\Gamma_k
\le
C\,
\mathbb E
\left[
\left(1+\sum_a|P_k^{2,a}|\right)
\mathcal S_k
\right].
\]

By Cauchy–Schwarz and (Q),

\[
\Gamma_k\le CT.
\tag{R}
\]

This proves, rather than assumes, that all off-cycle response mass is \(O(T)\). Individual off-cycle paths contain an \(h\)-weighted slow-update or rank-memory edge, while summing \(O(T/h)\) histories produces the \(O(T)\) bound.

### Closing the sub-Gaussian bootstrap

First bound \(C\) and the errors. Since \(\phi\) is bounded,

\[
|C_k|
\le
\gamma pHT
\left(1+H\sup_{\ell\le k}\|C_\ell\|_\infty\right).
\]

For \(T\le(2\gamma pH^2)^{-1}\),

\[
\|C_k\|_\infty\le2\gamma pHT,
\qquad
\max_a|e_{k,a}|\le2.
\tag{S}
\]

Hence

\[
|D_k^{3,a}|\le C T.
\tag{T}
\]

The centered Gaussian innovation in \(P^{2,a}\) has variance

\[
\mathbb E|D^{3,a}|^2\le C T^2,
\]

and therefore sub-Gaussian norm \(O(T)\).

Its same-cycle response terms are \(O(T)(1+K_2)\): every current derivative of \(D^3=C\phi'(T)\) contains the factor \(C=O(T)\). Historical responses contribute at most

\[
C\Gamma_k(1+K_2).
\]

The learned \(A^\top\)-rank memory is \(O(T^3)\). Thus

\[
K_2
\le
C\left[
T+TK_2+\Gamma_k(1+K_2)+T^3
\right].
\tag{U}
\]

For \(P^1\), the centered innovation covariance is

\[
\mathbb E[D^{2,a}D^{2,b}],
\]

so its sub-Gussian scale is at most \(CK_2\). The same-cycle response coefficients are bounded by

\[
C(K_2+T):
\]

the derivative with respect to \(P^2\) is bounded by \(J\), while the derivative with respect to \(S\) is bounded by the expectation of

\[
J_2|P^2|+O(T).
\]

Historical responses contribute \(C\Gamma_k\), and the learned \(B^\top\)-rank memory is bounded by \(CTK_2^2\). Therefore

\[
K_1
\le
C\left[
K_2+T+\Gamma_k+TK_2^2
\right].
\tag{V}
\]

Using (R), under bootstrap (K),

\[
K_2\le C_2T,
\qquad
K_1\le C_1T.
\]

Choose

\[
T_0
\le
\min\left\{
\frac1{2\gamma pH^2},
\frac1{2C_1},
\frac1{2C_2}
\right\}.
\]

Then the right sides improve the bootstrap bound \(1\) to \(1/2\). Since \(P^1=P^2=0\) when \(C=0\) at the limiting initialization, discrete induction over \(k\) proves (A) for every cycle.

Finally, if \(\|X\|_{\psi_2}\le CT\), integration of the sub-Gaussian tail gives

\[
\mathbb E[X^2\mathbf1_{\{|X|>u\}}]
\le
CT^2
\left(1+\frac{u^2}{T^2}\right)
e^{-cu^2/T^2},
\]

which is (B).

### Audit conclusion

There is no obstruction in this lemma once the response histories are measured by the weighted norm (G). The important corrections are:

- the \(P^2\) innovation and all its current-cycle responses are \(O(T)\), not \(O(1)\);
- singular and repeated Gaussian innovations must not be replaced by independent sources;
- formal derivatives are taken in the ambient source coordinates, while generalized Hölder handles arbitrary temporal correlations;
- both \(P^2\) and \(P^1\) must be cut off;
- the only unbounded local derivative is controlled by
  \[
  |DD^2|
  \le J|DP^2|+J_2|P^2||DS|.
  \]

With these corrections, the claimed uniform response/tail estimate is valid.

I found no counterexample to the proposed all-source estimate. The mechanism appears structurally correct, but the earlier proof did not establish it. The exact status is:

\[
\boxed{\text{The bridge is repairable if—and only if—the source-path lemma below is proved explicitly.}}
\]

The downstream cutoff, action-state, uniqueness, and path-law arguments can then be closed.

## 1. Uniform deterministic energy bounds

These bounds do not require state evolution and hold for the finite-width cutoff dynamics, its Euler discretization, and the limiting recursion.

Let \(\|\cdot\|_n\) be normalized Euclidean norm. On the event
\(\|A_0\|_{\mathrm op}+\|B_0\|_{\mathrm op}\le M\) and
\(\|C_0\|_\infty=o(1)\), boundedness of \(\phi,\phi'\) gives

\[
\|C(t)\|_\infty\le C t+o(1),
\]

because

\[
\frac{d}{dt}\|C\|_\infty
\le C\bigl(1+\|C\|_\infty\bigr).
\]

Next,

\[
\|\dot A\|_{\mathrm op}
\le
C\sum_a\|D^{3,a}\|_n\|H^{2,a}\|_n
\le Ct,
\]

so

\[
\sup_{t\le T}\|A(t)\|_{\mathrm op}\le M+CT^2.
\]

Since

\[
P^{2,a}=A^*D^{3,a},
\]

we obtain

\[
\|P^{2,a}(t)\|_n\le Ct.
\]

Because \(|\tau_R(x)|\le |x|\),

\[
\|D_R^{2,a}(t)\|_n
\le \|\phi'\|_\infty\|P^{2,a}(t)\|_n
\le Ct.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\|\dot B\|_{\mathrm op}\le Ct,
\qquad
\sup_{t\le T}\|B(t)\|_{\mathrm op}\le M+CT^2,
\]

and

\[
\|P^{1,a}(t)\|_n
=
\|B^*D_R^{2,a}\|_n
\le Ct.
\]

Thus, uniformly in \(R\) and the auxiliary mesh \(h\),

\[
\boxed{
\|C\|_\infty+\|P^1\|_2+\|P^2\|_2\le CT,
\qquad
\|A\|_{\rm op}+\|B\|_{\rm op}\le C .
}
\tag{E}
\]

This part is rigorous and repairs an important circularity in the previous argument.

## 2. Exact source-path structure

Serialize each physical cycle as

\[
F_{k1}=(H_k^1,0),\quad U_{k1}=S_k,\quad
G_{k1}=H_k^2,\quad V_{k1}=T_k,
\]

\[
F_{k2}=(0,D_k^3),\quad U_{k2}=P_k^2,\quad
G_{k2}=D_k^2,\quad V_{k2}=P_k^1.
\]

The Han response representation then has the following exact structural facts:

- \(u\)-responses multiply either \(H^2\), which is bounded, or \(D^2\), whose \(\psi _2\)-norm is controlled by that of \(P^2\).
- \(v\)-responses multiply either \(H^1\), which is bounded, or \(D^3\), which is \(O(T)\) in \(L^\infty\).
- Within one physical cycle the only potentially nonzero direct coefficients are
  \[
  \beta_{11}=\mathbb E\phi'(S),
  \]
  \[
  \alpha_{21}=\mathbb E[C\phi''(T)],
  \]
  \[
  \beta_{22}
  =
  \mathbb E[\phi'(S)\tau_R'(P^2)],
  \]
  and
  \[
  \beta_{21}
  =
  \mathbb E\!\left[
  \phi''(S)\tau_R(P^2)
  +
  \phi'(S)\tau_R'(P^2)
  \alpha_{21}\phi'(S)
  \right],
  \]
  with the appropriate species masks.
- Every path from a source in an earlier physical cycle to a current query crosses either a slow Euler update or a learned-rank memory term and therefore contains a factor \(h\).

The last assertion follows by inspecting the acyclic graph

\[
(U_k,C_k)
\to H_k^1\to S_k\to H_k^2\to T_k
\to D_k^3\to P_k^2\to D_k^2\to P_k^1
\to(U_{k+1},C_{k+1}),
\]

together with the four learned-rank memories, all of which have coefficient \(h\).

## 3. The precise missing source-path lemma

Let \(\partial_s\) denote differentiation with respect to any typed Gaussian source from cycle \(s\). A complete chain-rule induction over the preceding DAG must establish

\[
|\partial_sX_k|
\le
Ch(1+|P_s^2|)
\prod_{r=s}^{k-1}
\left[
1+Ch\left(
1+\sum_a(|P_r^{1,a}|+|P_r^{2,a}|)
\right)
\right]
\tag{SP}
\]

for \(s<k\), for every current node \(X_k\) that enters an Onsager coefficient. For sources from the same cycle, the leading \(h\) is omitted, but only the four direct coefficients listed above occur.

Assuming

\[
K=\sup_{k,a}
\left(
\|P_k^{1,a}\|_{\psi_2}
+
\|P_k^{2,a}\|_{\psi_2}
\right),
\]

generalized Hölder, without any independence assumption, gives

\[
\mathbb E
\exp\!\left(
Ch\sum_{r,a}(|P_r^{1,a}|+|P_r^{2,a}|)
\right)
\le
2\exp\!\left(CpT+Cp^2T^2K^2\right).
\]

Summing (SP) over all earlier typed sources therefore yields the total historical response mass

\[
\Gamma
\le
CpT(1+K)
\exp\!\left(CpT+Cp^2T^2K^2\right).
\tag{R}
\]

The earlier proof never supplied the induction proving (SP). That is the one genuinely open proof obligation.

## 4. Orlicz closure once (SP) is available

The exact Gaussian-plus-response representation gives

\[
K_2
\le
CT+C\Gamma(1+K_2).
\tag{O2}
\]

Indeed:

- the \(P^2\) Gaussian innovation has standard deviation
  \(\|D^3\|_2\le CT\);
- the immediate \(\alpha_{21}H^2\) term is \(O(T)\);
- historical stage-one response bases are bounded \(H^2\);
- historical stage-two response bases are \(D^2\), with
  \(\|D^2\|_{\psi_2}\le CK_2\);
- the learned-rank correction is \(O(T^3)\).

Likewise,

\[
K_1
\le
C\bigl(
K_2+T+TK_2^2+\Gamma
\bigr).
\tag{O1}
\]

Here the Gaussian innovation has scale \(\|D^2\|_2\le CK_2\), the two immediate responses are \(O(K_2+T)\), all historical response bases are bounded \(H^1\) or \(O(T)\) fields \(D^3\), and the learned-rank correction is \(O(TK_2^2)\).

Let \(K\) reach \(1\) for the first time. Equations (R), (O2), and (O1) imply

\[
K\le C T e^{CT}<\frac12
\]

when \(T_*>0\) is sufficiently small. This contradicts first exit. Reapplying the inequalities then gives

\[
\boxed{
\sup_{h,R,k h\le T_*}
\bigl(
\|P_k^1\|_{\psi_2}
+
\|P_k^2\|_{\psi_2}
\bigr)
\le C T_* .
}
\tag{SG}
\]

Thus (SP) is sufficient to prove the desired uniform tail estimate.

## 5. Downstream bridge once (SG) holds

Han’s averaged theorem applied at fixed \(h,R\), using smooth pseudo-Lipschitz tail majorants, transfers (SG) to

\[
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
\Pr\!\left[
\sup_{kh\le T}
\frac1n\sum_i
(P_{k,i}^\ell)^2
\mathbf1_{\{|P_{k,i}^\ell|>r\}}
>
Ce^{-cr^2/T^2}
\right]=0.
\]

Euler-to-ODE comparison transfers this to all \(t\in[0,T]\).

On the invariant region (E), the cutoff vector field has stability constant \(C(1+R)\), not because it is globally \(C(1+R)\)-Lipschitz, but because \(A,B,D^2,C\) already obey \(R\)-independent bounds. Comparing uncut and cutoff systems gives

\[
D_R'(t)
\le
C(1+R)D_R(t)
+
C\sum_{\ell=1}^2
\|P_R^\ell
\mathbf1_{\{|P_R^\ell|>R/2\}}\|_2.
\]

Therefore

\[
D_R(T)
\le
Ce^{CRT-cR^2/T^2}\longrightarrow0.
\]

The correct limit order is fixed \(R,h\), then \(n\to\infty\), then \(h\downarrow0\), and finally \(R\to\infty\). No growing-step invocation of Han is needed.

Uniqueness holds in the explicitly defined class satisfying (E) and (SG): apply the same truncation estimate to two solutions and let \(R\to\infty\). Time shifts remain in this class, so uniqueness gives the semigroup/restartability property.

## 6. Action state and path laws

For the action state, enumerate the countable typed algebra generated by rational linear combinations, bounded Lipschitz cylinder maps, \(\mathbb A,\mathbb A^*\), and the rank-one operations. Finite joint laws converge by fixed-mesh Han. Projective consistency gives probability spaces; the finite-width operator inequality passes to the \(L^2\) quotient, producing a bounded operator and its adjoint. Cross-mesh finite-width Euler estimates make the limiting probe laws Cauchy.

For a hidden path \(z_i\),

\[
\omega(z_i,\delta)^2
\le
\delta\int_0^T|\dot z_i(t)|^2\,dt.
\]

Hence

\[
\frac1n\sum_i\omega(z_i,\delta)^2
\le
\delta\int_0^TV_n(t)^2\,dt.
\]

The source-tail estimate also applies to the finite DAGs defining the velocities, giving uniform integrability of

\[
\frac1n\sum_i\sup_{t\le T}|z_i(t)|^2.
\]

Mesh-point convergence plus these two estimates yields
\(W_2(C([0,T];\mathbb R^p))\) convergence.

## Decisive repair verdict

The proposed bridge has no identified structural counterexample. The deterministic energy bounds, Orlicz closure, cutoff removal, action realization, uniqueness, restartability, and path-\(W_2\) passage can all be completed as above.

But the theorem still cannot be declared proved until (SP) is established by a literal induction over every typed source and every node of the serialized two-stage recursion. That induction was absent from the original response and remains the precise unresolved obligation.
