### Nondegeneracy and immediate activity at \(C(0)=0\)

Write \(p\) for the number of samples and \(\gamma=2/p\). All inner products below are the normalized width-limit inner products. At initialization, a typical coordinate of the three preactivations has laws
\[
U=(U_a)_{a\le p}\sim N(0,R),\qquad
S\sim N(0,Q_1),\qquad
T\sim N(0,Q_2),
\]
and
\[
H_{1a}=\phi(U_a),\qquad H_{2a}=\phi(S_a),\qquad
H_{3a}=\phi(T_a),\qquad \phi(z)=\sin z+\cos z.
\]
The matrix \(R\) may be singular.

#### Strict positivity of the forward kernels

If \(X,Y\) are centered unit Gaussians with covariance \(r\in[-1,1]\), possibly jointly singular, their characteristic function gives
\[
\mathbb E[\phi(X)\phi(Y)]
 =e^{-1+r}.
\]
Since \(\phi'(z)=\cos z-\sin z\), the same calculation gives
\[
\mathbb E[\phi'(X)\phi'(Y)]=e^{-1+r}.
\tag{1}
\]

We use the following elementary strictness fact. If \(K\) is positive semidefinite, \(K_{aa}=1\), and \(K_{ab}<1\) for \(a\ne b\), then the entrywise matrix
\[
\mathscr E(K)_{ab}=e^{-1+K_{ab}}
\]
is positive definite. Indeed, represent \(K_{ab}=z_a\cdot z_b\) by distinct unit vectors \(z_a\). For any \(c\in\mathbb R^p\),
\[
c^\top \mathscr E(K)c
=e^{-1}\sum_{m=0}^\infty \frac1{m!}
 \left\|\sum_a c_a z_a^{\otimes m}\right\|^2.
\tag{2}
\]
If this vanished, all tensor sums would vanish. Choose \(v\) outside the finite union of hyperplanes
\((z_a-z_b)^\perp\), so that the numbers \(\lambda_a=v\cdot z_a\) are pairwise distinct. Contracting the identities for \(m=0,\ldots,p-1\) with \(v^{\otimes m}\) gives
\[
\sum_a c_a\lambda_a^m=0,\qquad m=0,\ldots,p-1.
\]
The Vandermonde matrix is invertible, so \(c=0\).

Because the \(x_a\) have equal norm and are distinct, Cauchy–Schwarz gives \(R_{ab}<1\) for \(a\ne b\), without any quantitative separation assumption. Applying (2) successively yields
\[
Q_1\succ0,\qquad Q_2\succ0,\qquad Q_3\succ0.
\tag{3}
\]
Thus arbitrarily close but distinct inputs cause no loss of strictness. The smallest eigenvalues can, however, tend to zero as inputs coalesce, so no data-uniform lower bound or data-uniform short-time interval follows.

We shall also use this Schur-product strengthening: if \(A\succ0\), \(B\succeq0\), and \(B_{aa}>0\) for all \(a\), then \(A\circ B\succ0\). To see this, write
\(B=\sum_k b^k(b^k)^\top\). Then
\[
x^\top(A\circ B)x
=\sum_k (\operatorname{diag}b^k\,x)^\top
 A(\operatorname{diag}b^k\,x).
\]
If \(x\ne0\), choose \(a\) with \(x_a\ne0\); since
\(B_{aa}=\sum_k(b_a^k)^2>0\), at least one term is strictly positive.

#### The two reused transposes: innovation plus response

The following form of Gaussian regression is needed; replacing a reused transpose by an independent Gaussian is incorrect.

Consider a Gaussian layer
\[
Z_i=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_j W_{ij}h_j,\qquad
Q_n=\frac1n\sum_jh_jh_j^\top,
\]
and a transpose call
\[
P_{j,a}=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_i W_{ij}F_a(Z_i,\Xi_i),
\]
where \(\Xi_i\) is independent auxiliary randomness. Conditional Gaussian regression, valid also when \(Q_n\) is singular, gives
\[
W_{ij}
=\frac1{\sqrt n}h_j^\top Q_n^+Z_i+\eta_{ij},
\]
where \(\eta_{ij}\) is conditionally centered and independent of \(Z_i\). Hence the width limit is
\[
P_a=\zeta_a+
h^\top Q^+\mathbb E[ZF_a(Z,\Xi)],
\qquad
\mathbb E[\zeta_a\zeta_b]=\mathbb E[F_aF_b],
\tag{4}
\]
with \(\zeta\) independent of the lower-layer variables. The second moment on the right of (4) is deliberately uncentered.

There is no hidden nonsingularity assumption in (4). If \(Q=\mathbb E[hh^\top]\), then \(h\in\operatorname{ran}Q\) almost surely: for \(v\in\ker Q\),
\(\mathbb E(v\cdot h)^2=0\). Moreover, if \(F\) is differentiable, the singular Gaussian Stein identity
\[
\mathbb E[ZF]=Q\,\mathbb E[\nabla F]
\tag{5}
\]
follows by writing \(Z=L\xi\), \(\xi\) standard Gaussian, and integrating by parts in \(\xi\). Consequently the response in (4) equals
\[
\sum_c h_c\,\mathbb E[\partial_cF_a].
\tag{6}
\]

Define the initial readout direction
\[
g(T):=\sum_c y_c\phi(T_c),
\qquad
d^3_a:=g(T)\phi'(T_a),
\qquad
G^3_{ab}:=\mathbb E[d^3_ad^3_b].
\tag{7}
\]
Applying (4) to \(A_0^\top d^3_a\) yields the first reused adjoint
\[
p^2_a=\zeta^2_a+\sum_c M^3_{ac}\phi(S_c),
\qquad
\zeta^2\sim N(0,G^3),\quad \zeta^2\perp S,
\tag{8}
\]
where
\[
M^3_{ac}
=\mathbb E[\partial_{T_c}d^3_a]
=y_cQ_{3,ac}-\delta_{ac}(Q_3y)_a.
\tag{9}
\]
Here we used \(\phi''=-\phi\) and (1). Put
\[
d^2_a:=\phi'(S_a)p^2_a,\qquad
G^2_{ab}:=\mathbb E[d^2_ad^2_b].
\tag{10}
\]
A second application of (4), now to \(B_0^\top d^2_a\), gives
\[
p^1_a=\zeta^1_a+\sum_cM^2_{ac}\phi(U_c),
\qquad
\zeta^1\sim N(0,G^2),\quad \zeta^1\perp U,
\tag{11}
\]
with
\[
M^2_{ac}
=\mathbb E[\partial_{S_c}d^2_a]
=Q_{2,ac}M^3_{ac}
-\delta_{ac}\sum_kQ_{2,ak}M^3_{ak}.
\tag{12}
\]
Finally define
\[
q^1_a:=\phi'(U_a)p^1_a,\qquad
G^1_{ab}:=\mathbb E[q^1_aq^1_b].
\tag{13}
\]

All three \(G^\ell\) are positive definite. For \(G^3\), if
\(\lambda\ne0\), then
\[
\lambda^\top G^3\lambda
=\mathbb E\!\left[
g(T)^2\Big(\sum_a\lambda_a\phi'(T_a)\Big)^2
\right].
\tag{14}
\]
Both factors are nonzero real-analytic functions: varying a coordinate with \(y_a\ne0\) shows \(g\not\equiv0\), and varying one with \(\lambda_a\ne0\) shows the second factor is not identically zero. Their product is therefore nonzero on an open set. Since \(Q_2\succ0\), \(T\) has a strictly positive density on \(\mathbb R^p\), and (14) is strictly positive.

The innovation terms in (8) and (11) rule out every possible response cancellation:
\[
G^2
=Q_2\circ G^3+
\mathbb E\!\left[
\big(\phi'(S)\odot \rho^2(S)\big)
\big(\phi'(S)\odot \rho^2(S)\big)^\top
\right]\succ0,
\tag{15}
\]
where \(\rho^2_a(S)=\sum_cM^3_{ac}\phi(S_c)\), and
\[
G^1
=Q_1\circ G^2+
\mathbb E\!\left[
\big(\phi'(U)\odot \rho^1(U)\big)
\big(\phi'(U)\odot \rho^1(U)\big)^\top
\right]\succ0.
\tag{16}
\]
The first summands in (15)–(16) are already positive definite. This also proves (16) when \(R\) is singular; no density of the joint vector \(U\) is used. Equivalently, conditional on \(U\), the variance contributed by \(\zeta^1\) is
\[
(\lambda\odot\phi'(U))^\top
G^2(\lambda\odot\phi'(U))>0
\]
almost surely for every \(\lambda\ne0\), because every marginal \(U_a\sim N(0,1)\) and
\(\phi'(U_a)=0\) has probability zero.

#### Small-time expansion and hidden-layer activity

All expansions below hold in the limiting normalized \(L^2\) space. Boundedness of \(\phi\) and its derivatives, finiteness of \(p\), and finite Gaussian moments justify the differentiations and the \(o(\cdot)\) remainders.

At \(t=0\), \(f_a=0\) and \(e_a=-y_a\). Therefore
\[
C(t)=\gamma t\,g+O(t^2).
\tag{17}
\]
Using the two reused adjoints above,
\[
\begin{aligned}
D_{3a}(t)&=\gamma t\,d^3_a+o(t),&
P_{2a}(t)&=\gamma t\,p^2_a+o(t),\\
D_{2a}(t)&=\gamma t\,d^2_a+o(t),&
P_{1a}(t)&=\gamma t\,p^1_a+o(t).
\end{aligned}
\tag{18}
\]

Define
\[
\begin{aligned}
v^1_a&:=\sum_b y_bR_{ab}q^1_b,\\
v^2_a&:=\sum_b y_bQ_{1,ab}d^2_b
       +B_0\!\left[\phi'(U_a)v^1_a\right],\\
v^3_a&:=\sum_b y_bQ_{2,ab}d^3_b
       +A_0\!\left[\phi'(S_a)v^2_a\right].
\end{aligned}
\tag{19}
\]
Direct substitution into the stated flow gives
\[
\dot U_a(t)=\gamma^2t\,v^1_a+o(t),\qquad
\dot S_a(t)=\gamma^2t\,v^2_a+o(t),\qquad
\dot T_a(t)=\gamma^2t\,v^3_a+o(t).
\tag{20}
\]
In particular,
\[
Z^\ell_a(t)-Z^\ell_a(0)
=\frac{\gamma^2t^2}{2}v^\ell_a+o(t^2),
\qquad (Z^1,Z^2,Z^3)=(U,S,T).
\tag{21}
\]

Set
\[
L_1:=R\circ G^1,\qquad
L_2:=Q_1\circ G^2,\qquad
L_3:=Q_2\circ G^3.
\tag{22}
\]
By the Schur argument above,
\[
L_1\succ0,\qquad L_2\succ0,\qquad L_3\succ0.
\tag{23}
\]
For \(L_1\), this uses only \(R\succeq0\), \(R_{aa}=1\), and \(G^1\succ0\), so singularity of \(R\) causes no difficulty. Let
\[
E_\ell:=y^\top L_\ell y>0.
\tag{24}
\]

The possible cancellations in the transported terms in (19) are excluded by exact adjoint identities:
\[
\sum_a y_a\langle q^1_a,v^1_a\rangle=E_1,
\tag{25}
\]
\[
\sum_a y_a\langle d^2_a,v^2_a\rangle=E_2+E_1,
\tag{26}
\]
and
\[
\sum_a y_a\langle d^3_a,v^3_a\rangle=E_3+E_2+E_1.
\tag{27}
\]
For example, the transported part of (26) equals
\[
\sum_a y_a
 \left\langle d^2_a,B_0[\phi'(U_a)v^1_a]\right\rangle
=\sum_a y_a\langle p^1_a,\phi'(U_a)v^1_a\rangle
=E_1.
\]
The same calculation with \(A_0^\top d^3_a=p^2_a\) proves (27). Thus every \(v^\ell\) is nonzero.

Since \(\phi'(Z^\ell_a)=0\) has probability zero for every \(a,\ell\), multiplication by \(\phi'(Z^\ell)\) cannot annihilate a nonzero \(L^2\) vector. Hence
\[
w^\ell_a:=\phi'(Z^\ell_a)v^\ell_a
\]
is nonzero, and
\[
\dot H_{\ell a}(t)
=\gamma^2t\,w^\ell_a+o(t),
\qquad
H_{\ell a}(t)-H_{\ell a}(0)
=\frac{\gamma^2t^2}{2}w^\ell_a+o(t^2).
\tag{28}
\]
Consequently, for every sufficiently small \(t>0\),
\[
\sum_a\|\dot H_{\ell a}(t)\|^2
=\gamma^4t^2\sum_a\|w^\ell_a\|^2+o(t^2)>0,
\tag{29}
\]
and
\[
\sum_a\|H_{\ell a}(t)-H_{\ell a}(0)\|^2
=\frac{\gamma^4t^4}{4}
 \sum_a\|w^\ell_a\|^2+o(t^4)>0.
\tag{30}
\]
The velocities are zero at \(t=0\); the correct assertion is immediate activity for every sufficiently small \(t>0\).

Equation (21) also gives, for every sample and layer,
\[
\operatorname{Var}Z^\ell_a(t)=1+O(t^2).
\tag{31}
\]
Thus all individual hidden preactivation variances remain, say, at least \(1/2\) on a sufficiently short interval. No claim that the full covariance of \(U(t)\) is positive definite is possible when \(R\) is singular.

#### Positivity of all four NTK blocks

The four block kernels are
\[
\begin{aligned}
K^C_{ab}(t)
 &=\langle H_{3a},H_{3b}\rangle,\\
K^A_{ab}(t)
 &=\langle D_{3a},D_{3b}\rangle
   \langle H_{2a},H_{2b}\rangle,\\
K^B_{ab}(t)
 &=\langle D_{2a},D_{2b}\rangle
   \langle H_{1a},H_{1b}\rangle,\\
K^U_{ab}(t)
 &=R_{ab}
   \langle\phi'(U_a)P_{1a},\phi'(U_b)P_{1b}\rangle .
\end{aligned}
\tag{32}
\]
Equations (18) and (21) imply
\[
\begin{aligned}
K^C(t)&=Q_3+O(t^2),\\
K^A(t)&=\gamma^2t^2L_3+o(t^2),\\
K^B(t)&=\gamma^2t^2L_2+o(t^2),\\
K^U(t)&=\gamma^2t^2L_1+o(t^2).
\end{aligned}
\tag{33}
\]
Therefore, for every sufficiently small \(\tau>0\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_0^\tau K^C(t)\,dt
 &=\tau Q_3+o(\tau)\succ0,\\
\int_0^\tau K^A(t)\,dt
 &=\frac{\gamma^2\tau^3}{3}L_3+o(\tau^3)\succ0,\\
\int_0^\tau K^B(t)\,dt
 &=\frac{\gamma^2\tau^3}{3}L_2+o(\tau^3)\succ0,\\
\int_0^\tau K^U(t)\,dt
 &=\frac{\gamma^2\tau^3}{3}L_1+o(\tau^3)\succ0.
\end{aligned}
\tag{34}
\]
Thus “positive” can be taken in the strong Loewner sense. Entrywise positivity is not true in general and should not be asserted.

#### The total NTK cannot be constant

Let \(\theta=(W^1,B,A)\) denote all hidden parameters and
\[
g(\theta)=\sum_a y_aH_{3a}(\theta),\qquad
\mathcal E(\theta)=\frac12\|g(\theta)\|^2.
\]
The three components of \(\nabla_\theta\mathcal E(\theta_0)\) are precisely the leading hidden gradients built from \(q^1,d^2,d^3\), and
\[
\|\nabla_\theta\mathcal E(\theta_0)\|^2
=E_1+E_2+E_3=:E_*>0.
\tag{35}
\]
Equations (17)–(18) give
\[
\theta(t)=\theta_0+\frac{\gamma^2t^2}{2}
 \nabla_\theta\mathcal E(\theta_0)+o(t^2).
\tag{36}
\]
Since
\[
y^\top K^C(t)y=\|g(\theta(t))\|^2,
\]
Taylor expansion along (36) yields
\[
y^\top K^C(t)y
=y^\top Q_3y+\gamma^2t^2E_*+o(t^2).
\tag{37}
\]
On the other hand,
\[
y^\top(K^U+K^B+K^A)(t)y
=\gamma^2t^2E_*+o(t^2).
\tag{38}
\]
Thus the readout variation and the hidden blocks have the same positive sign:
\[
y^\top K(t)y
=y^\top Q_3y+2\gamma^2t^2E_*+o(t^2).
\tag{39}
\]
No cancellation is possible, and the total NTK is nonconstant.

#### Residuals and strict loss decrease

From (17),
\[
f(t)=\gamma t\,Q_3y+O(t^2),
\qquad
e_a(t)=-y_a+\gamma t(Q_3y)_a+O(t^2).
\tag{40}
\]
Hence, after shortening the interval if necessary,
\[
|e_a(t)|\ge\frac12,\qquad
-y_ae_a(t)\ge\frac12
\tag{41}
\]
for every \(a\). In particular every squared residual remains strictly positive.

For
\[
\mathcal L(t)=\frac1p\sum_a e_a(t)^2,
\]
the kernel gradient-flow identity is
\[
\dot e=-\gamma K(t)e,\qquad
\dot{\mathcal L}(t)
=-\gamma^2 e(t)^\top K(t)e(t).
\tag{42}
\]
Because \(K^C(t)\) remains positive definite by (3) and continuity, and \(e(t)\ne0\) by (41),
\[
\dot{\mathcal L}(t)<0
\]
throughout a sufficiently short nontrivial interval. In particular,
\[
\dot{\mathcal L}(0)=-\gamma^2y^\top Q_3y<0.
\]

If “the residuals stay positive” is meant literally as \(e_a(t)>0\), that statement is false: \(e_a(0)=-y_a\), so it is negative whenever \(y_a=1\). The valid conclusions are (41), positivity of each squared residual, and strict positivity of the residual norm.

## Compact-time continuum bridge for \(L=3\)

Write
\[
\langle x,z\rangle_n=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n x_i z_i,\qquad
(u\otimes_n v)x=u\langle v,x\rangle_n,
\]
and let \(m=\sup_{j\ge0}\|\phi^{(j)}\|_\infty=\sqrt2\) for
\(\phi=\sin+\cos\). Let \(\tau_R(0)=0\), \(\|\tau_R'\|_\infty\le1\),
\(\tau_R(x)=x\) on \([-R,R]\), and \(\|\tau_R\|_\infty\le \kappa R\).
The first two properties imply
\[
|\tau_R(x)|\le |x|. \tag{1}
\]

For \(a\in[p]\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
&H_1^a=\phi(U^a),\quad S^a=BH_1^a,\quad H_2^a=\phi(S^a),\\
&T^a=AH_2^a,\quad H_3^a=\phi(T^a),\quad
f_a=\langle C,H_3^a\rangle_n,\quad e_a=f_a-y_a,\\
&D_3^a=C\phi'(T^a),\quad P_2^a=A^\top D_3^a,\\
&D_2^a=\phi'(S^a)\tau_R(P_2^a),\quad P_1^a=B^\top D_2^a .
\end{aligned} \tag{2}
\]
The clipped flow is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\dot U^a&=-\gamma\sum_b\mathsf R_{ab}e_b\phi'(U^b)\tau_R(P_1^b),\\
\dot B&=-\gamma\sum_b e_bD_2^b\otimes_n H_1^b,\qquad
\dot A=-\gamma\sum_b e_bD_3^b\otimes_n H_2^b,\\
\dot C&=-\gamma\sum_b e_bH_3^b,\qquad \gamma=2/p.
\end{aligned} \tag{3}
\]
The uncut flow is obtained by replacing both \(\tau_R\)'s by the identity. Initially,
\[
(U_{0i}^1,\ldots,U_{0i}^p)\stackrel{\rm iid}{\sim}N(0,\mathsf R),
\quad A_{0ij},B_{0ij}\stackrel{\rm iid}{\sim}N(0,1/n),
\quad C_0=0, \tag{4}
\]
with no nondegeneracy assumption on \(\mathsf R\).

The exact low-rank identities
\[
\begin{aligned}
A_t&=A_0-\gamma\sum_b\int_0^t e_b(s)D_3^b(s)\otimes_nH_2^b(s)\,ds,\\
B_t&=B_0-\gamma\sum_b\int_0^t e_b(s)D_2^b(s)\otimes_nH_1^b(s)\,ds
\end{aligned} \tag{5}
\]
will always be used instead of estimating trained matrices by Frobenius norms.

### 1. The Gaussian-response representation and the uniform \(\psi _2\) bound

For an Euler mesh \(h\), expand (5) as a finite sum and apply the scalar Tensor-Program rule only to \(A_0,B_0\). If \(W\) denotes either initial Gaussian matrix, the scalar rule is
\[
\begin{aligned}
Z^{Wx}
 &=\widehat G_W^+(x)
   +\sum_{W^\top y\prec Wx} Z^y\,
      \mathbb E\!\left[\partial_{\widehat G_W^-(y)}Z^x\right],\\
Z^{W^\top y}
 &=\widehat G_W^-(y)
   +\sum_{Wx\prec W^\top y} Z^x\,
      \mathbb E\!\left[\partial_{\widehat G_W^+(x)}Z^y\right], \tag{6}
\end{aligned}
\]
where
\[
\mathbb E[\widehat G_W^\pm(x)\widehat G_W^\pm(z)]
   =\mathbb E[xz].
\tag{7}
\]
The sums are over earlier named matrix-multiplication lines in the compiled Euler DAG. Formula (6), together with population moments in place of \(\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_n\), is a self-contained definition of the clipped OMFP Euler recursion.

There is no covariance-invertibility hypothesis in (6). Indeed, for any finite list of Gaussian sources with covariance \(K\succeq0\), let
\[
\mathcal H_K=\overline{\mathbb R^\Lambda/\ker K},
\qquad
\langle [u],[v]\rangle_{\mathcal H_K}=u^\top Kv,
\]
and realize the sources as \(G_\lambda=\mathcal W([e_\lambda])\) for an isonormal process \(\mathcal W\) on \(\mathcal H_K\). Then
\[
DG=\sum_\lambda \partial_\lambda G\,[e_\lambda],\qquad
\mathbb E[G_\lambda F]=\mathbb E\langle DF,[e_\lambda]\rangle_{\mathcal H_K}. \tag{8}
\]
Thus neither a Cholesky factor nor \(K^{-1}\) is used. Equivalently, one may prove all estimates for \(K+\varepsilon I\) and let \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\); the estimates below contain no negative power of \(\varepsilon\).

First note that \(C\) and the errors are bounded independently of \(h,R\). If
\[
c_k=\|C_k\|_\infty,\qquad Y=\max_a|y_a|,
\]
then the Euler recursion gives
\[
c_{k+1}\le (1+\gamma hp m^2)c_k+\gamma hp mY.
\]
Consequently, for \(kh\le T\),
\[
c_k\le c_T:=\frac Ym\bigl(e^{\gamma pm^2T}-1\bigr),\qquad
\max_a|e_{k,a}|\le E_T:=Y+mc_T, \tag{9}
\]
and hence
\[
\|D_{3,k}^a\|_\infty\le d_T:=mc_T\le K T \quad (T\le1). \tag{10}
\]

The needed response estimate is the following.

**Gaussian-response lemma.** There are \(K<\infty\) and \(T_*>0\), depending only on \(p,\mathsf R,y\) and \(m\), such that for every \(0<h\le T\le T_*\), every \(R\ge1\), and every Euler index \(kh\le T\), the all-source Cameron–Martin/response ledger satisfies
\[
\mathfrak I_k\le
K\exp\!\left\{KT+KT^2(q_{1,k}^2+q_{2,k}^2)\right\}, \tag{11}
\]
where
\[
q_{i,k}=\max_{\ell\le k,\ a\le p}\|P_{i,\ell}^a\|_{\psi_2}.
\]
Moreover, if
\[
\begin{aligned}
r_{3,k}
 &=\max_a\sum_{A_0H_2\prec A_0^\top D_{3,k}^a}
       \left|\mathbb E\,\partial_{\widehat G_A^+(H_2)}
                    D_{3,k}^a\right|,\\
r_{2,k}
 &=\max_a\sum_{B_0H_1\prec B_0^\top D_{2,k}^a}
       \left|\mathbb E\,\partial_{\widehat G_B^+(H_1)}
                    D_{2,k}^a\right|,
\end{aligned}
\]
then
\[
r_{3,k}\le KT\,\mathfrak I_k,\qquad
r_{2,k}\le K\bigl(q_{2,k}+T\bigr)\mathfrak I_k. \tag{12}
\]

Here \(\mathfrak I_k\) is the sum, over all named Gaussian sources, of the corresponding Cameron–Martin influences of all fields up to time \(kh\), together with the absolute response coefficients appearing in (6). It is an overcomplete all-source norm; therefore it remains meaningful when the Gaussian covariance is singular.

To prove the lemma, differentiate the compiled Euler DAG. The only identities involving unbounded fields are
\[
\begin{aligned}
D[\phi'(U)\tau_R(P_1)]
 &=\phi''(U)\tau_R(P_1)DU
   +\phi'(U)\tau_R'(P_1)DP_1,\\
DD_2
 &=\phi''(S)\tau_R(P_2)DS
   +\phi'(S)\tau_R'(P_2)DP_2,\\
DD_3
 &=\phi'(T)DC+C\phi''(T)DT. \tag{13}
\end{aligned}
\]
Every earlier-time dependence carries a factor \(h\), every activation and derivative of an activation is bounded by \(m\), and (1) removes \(R\) from the value estimates. Topological induction over one Euler step therefore gives, pathwise,
\[
J_k\le K\left(1+h\sum_{\ell<k}
   \Bigl[1+\sum_a(|P_{1,\ell}^a|+|P_{2,\ell}^a|)\Bigr]J_\ell\right), \tag{14}
\]
for the pointwise all-source derivative sum \(J_k\). Discrete Grönwall yields
\[
J_k\le K\exp\!\left(
Kh\sum_{\ell<k}\Bigl[1+\sum_a(|P_{1,\ell}^a|+|P_{2,\ell}^a|)\Bigr]\right). \tag{15}
\]
If \(\|X_\ell\|_{\psi_2}\le q\), convexity and the sub-Gaussian exponential-moment bound give
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb E\exp\!\left(\lambda h\sum_{\ell<k}|X_\ell|\right)
&\le \max_{\ell<k}\mathbb E e^{\lambda T|X_\ell|}\\
&\le 2e^{K\lambda^2T^2q^2}. \tag{16}
\end{aligned}
\]
Taking \(L^2\) norms in (15) proves (11). In (13), \(D_3\) contains either the factor \(C=O(T)\), or \(DC\), which is a time sum with factor \(h\); this proves the first inequality in (12). The two terms in \(DD_2\) are bounded respectively by \(|P_2||DS|\) and \(|DP_2|\). The \(B\)-forward influence of \(P_2=A^\top D_3\) is \(O(T)\mathfrak I_k\), because \(D_3\) and its relevant responses are \(O(T)\). Cauchy–Schwarz then proves the second inequality. These are precisely all the unbounded gates, so the induction is complete.

Now use (5) and (6) in the two adjoints. They give exact decompositions
\[
P_{2,k}^a=G_{2,k}^a+\rho_{2,k}^a,\qquad
P_{1,k}^a=G_{1,k}^a+\rho_{1,k}^a, \tag{17}
\]
where the centered Gaussian families satisfy
\[
\mathbb E(G_{2,k}^a)^2=\mathbb E(D_{3,k}^a)^2,\qquad
\mathbb E(G_{1,k}^a)^2=\mathbb E(D_{2,k}^a)^2, \tag{18}
\]
and, since the transpose-response companions in (6) are \(H_2,H_1\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\rho_{2,k}^a\|_\infty
 &\le m r_{3,k}
   +\gamma hp\sum_{\ell<k}E_Tm\,d_T^2,\\
\|\rho_{1,k}^a\|_\infty
 &\le m r_{2,k}
   +\gamma hp\sum_{\ell<k}E_Tm
        \max_b\|D_{2,\ell}^b\|_2\|D_{2,k}^a\|_2. \tag{19}
\end{aligned}
\]
Since \(\|D_2\|_2\le m\|P_2\|_2\), (18)–(19) imply
\[
\begin{aligned}
q_{2,k}&\le Kd_T+mr_{3,k}+KTd_T^2,\\
q_{1,k}&\le Kq_{2,k}+mr_{2,k}+KTq_{2,k}^2. \tag{20}
\end{aligned}
\]

This closes without circularity. Let \(k_*\) be the first index at which
\(q_{1,k}+q_{2,k}>1\). Before \(k_*\), (11) is bounded by a constant. Equations (10), (12), and the first line of (20) first give \(q_{2,k_*}\le KT\); only after that estimate is available, the second line of (12) gives \(r_{2,k_*}\le KT\), and the second line of (20) gives \(q_{1,k_*}\le KT\). Choosing \(T_*>0\) so that \(2KT_*<1\) contradicts the definition of \(k_*\). Therefore
\[
\sup_{\substack{0<h\le T\le T_*\\R\ge1\\kh\le T}}
\max_{a\le p}\bigl(
\|P_{1,k}^{a,h,R}\|_{\psi_2}
+\|P_{2,k}^{a,h,R}\|_{\psi_2}\bigr)
\le Q_T,\qquad Q_T=KT. \tag{21}
\]
The same calculation gives a uniform all-source Cameron–Martin influence bound for \(P_1,P_2\). Notice that (21) is a statement about the OMFP scalar variables, not about finite-width coordinates.

### 2. Fixed-\(R\) Euler stability

On
\[
\mathcal G_{n,K}=\{\|A_0\|_{\rm op}+\|B_0\|_{\rm op}\le K\},
\]
whose probability tends to one exponentially fast for any fixed \(K>4\), use (5) to eliminate \(A_t,B_t\). For example,
\[
\begin{aligned}
S_t^a
 &=B_0H_{1,t}^a-\gamma\sum_b\int_0^t
 e_b(s)D_{2,s}^b\,
 \langle H_{1,s}^b,H_{1,t}^a\rangle_n\,ds,\\
P_{1,t}^a
 &=B_0^\top D_{2,t}^a-\gamma\sum_b\int_0^t
 e_b(s)H_{1,s}^b\,
 \langle D_{2,s}^b,D_{2,t}^a\rangle_n\,ds, \tag{22}
\end{aligned}
\]
with analogous formulas for \(T,P_2\). The identities
\[
\|u\otimes_n v\|_{\rm op}=\|u\|_n\|v\|_n \tag{23}
\]
and \(|\tau_R|\le\kappa R\) show that, on every fixed-\(R\) bounded-moment ball, the vector field and the algebraic signal map are Lipschitz with a constant \(L_R\) independent of \(n\). Comparing two Euler schemes, or an Euler scheme with the ODE, gives
\[
d_{k+1}\le(1+L_Rh)d_k+K_Rh^2, \tag{24}
\]
where \(d_k\) is the sum of the normalized \(L^2\) distances of all fields in (2), plus the parameter actions appearing in (22). Hence
\[
\max_{kh\le T}d_k\le e^{L_RT}\bigl(d_0+K_RT h\bigr). \tag{25}
\]
This is the required dimension-free fixed-\(R\) stability.

### 3. Exact Tensor-Program tail transfer

For fixed \(h,R\), the clipped Euler computation is a finite
\(\mathrm{Netsor}^{\top+}\) program. Its initial data satisfy Setup E.2 of Tensor Programs III, including when \(\mathsf R\) is singular. Every vector-defining nonlinearity is linearly bounded after the DAG is unwound: \(H_j\) are bounded, \(D_3\) is linear in \(C\), and the two otherwise unbounded adjoint gates are saturated. Scalar moments are quadratically bounded.

If rank stability is verified, Tensor Programs III, Theorem A.7—the \(\mathrm{Netsor}^{\top+}\) extension of Theorem A.2—therefore gives, for every quadratically bounded test \(\Psi\),
\[
\mathbb E\left|
 \frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\Psi(Z_{n,i}^{h,R})
 -\mathbb E\Psi(Z^{h,R})\right|\longrightarrow0. \tag{26}
\]
Rank stability is not automatic for \(\mathrm{Netsor}^{\top+}\) programs: an error scalar can converge to zero without being identically zero at finite width. Thus Theorem A.7 must not be cited without this verification. In the present smooth setting the same conclusion follows without rank stability: Theorem E.15 gives almost-sure convergence for pseudo-Lipschitz tests, while the operator-norm and normalized-\(\ell_2\) argument in the proof of Theorem A.2 gives uniform integrability. This promotes the convergence to \(L^1\) and proves (26).

Apply (26) to
\[
\Psi_M(x)=|x-\tau_M(x)|^2.
\]
It is pseudo-Lipschitz and quadratically bounded. From (21),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\mathbb E\frac1n\sum_i
 |P_{j,n,i}^{h,R}-\tau_M(P_{j,n,i}^{h,R})|^2
 &=\mathbb E|P_j^{h,R}-\tau_M(P_j^{h,R})|^2\\
 &\le KQ_T^2e^{-M^2/(KQ_T^2)},\qquad j=1,2. \tag{27}
\end{aligned}
\]
This is the finite-width clipped-tail transfer; it uses an exact theorem applicable to the actual finite program, not a heuristic iid-coordinate assertion.

Combining (25), the finite-grid convergence furnished by (26), and the second-moment convergence gives, for every fixed \(R\),
\[
\mu_n^R\longrightarrow\mu^R
\quad\text{in }W_2\bigl(C([0,T];\mathbb R^{(9p+1)}),\|\cdot\|_\infty\bigr), \tag{28}
\]
where \(\mu_n^R=n^{-1}\sum_i\delta_{\mathcal Z_{n,i}^R(\cdot)}\) is the empirical path law of the neuron-indexed fields in (2).

### 4. Removing the cutoff

Let \(1\le R\le R'\) and couple the two OMFP solutions using the same initial isonormal sources. The common-isometry coupling
\[
\|\widehat G_W(x)-\widehat G_W(\tilde x)\|_2
   =\|x-\tilde x\|_2 \tag{29}
\]
again uses no covariance inverse.

The only cutoff-dependent estimates are
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\phi'(S^R)\tau_R(P^R)-\phi'(S^{R'})
       \tau_{R'}(P^{R'})\|_2
&\le m\|P^R-P^{R'}\|_2
  +m\kappa R\|S^R-S^{R'}\|_2\\
&\quad+2m\|P^{R'}\mathbf1_{\{|P^{R'}|>R\}}\|_2, \tag{30}
\end{aligned}
\]
and the identical estimate at the \(U,P_1\) gate. In all inner products generated by (5), one factor is controlled by the uniform \(L^2\) consequence of (21); this is why only one power of \(R\), rather than \(R^2\), occurs. Thus, for the sum \(D_{R,R'}\) of the coupled \(L^2\) field distances, its upper Dini derivative satisfies
\[
D_{R,R'}'(t)\le K(1+R)D_{R,R'}(t)+K\varepsilon_R, \tag{31}
\]
where
\[
\varepsilon_R
 =\sup_{\rho\ge R}\sup_{t\le T,a,j}
 \|P_{j,t}^{a,\rho}\mathbf1_{\{|P_{j,t}^{a,\rho}|>R\}}\|_2
 \le KQ_Te^{-R^2/(KQ_T^2)}. \tag{32}
\]
Consequently,
\[
\sup_{t\le T}D_{R,R'}(t)
 \le KT\,e^{K(1+R)T}\varepsilon_R
 \xrightarrow[R\to\infty]{}0, \tag{33}
\]
uniformly in \(R'\ge R\). The clipped solutions are therefore Cauchy in path \(L^2\). Their limit solves the uncut equations, because
\[
\|\tau_R(P^R)-P\|_2
 \le\|P^R-P\|_2+2\|P^R\mathbf1_{\{|P^R|>R\}}\|_2\to0. \tag{34}
\]
Fatou applied to the exponential-square moments preserves (21) for the uncut preclips.

The same comparison works between two uncut solutions in the class
\(\sup_{t,a,j}\|P_j^a(t)\|_{\psi_2}<\infty\): insert \(\tau_R\) in both equations, apply (31), and let \(R\to\infty\). Hence the uncut solution is unique in that class.

Restarting at \(t_0\) is valid after retaining the full enlarged OMFP state—trained \(A,B,C,U\) together with the Gaussian-response ledger. The marginal law of \((U,S,T,P_1,P_2)\) alone is not Markovian and is insufficient for a restart. With the enlarged state, the preceding bootstrap starts from the finite values \(Q_{t_0}\) and \(\mathfrak I_{t_0}\), and gives a positive continuation time depending only on these two quantities. This proves restartability, though not a global-in-time bound preventing their possible blow-up.

At finite width, compare the uncut path with its \(R\)-clipped path by splitting
\[
P^\infty-\tau_R(P^R)
 =(P^\infty-P^R)+(P^R-\tau_R(P^R)). \tag{35}
\]
Thus the forcing tail is that of the clipped path, not the uncut finite-width path. Expanding the matrices as in (22) gives
\[
(D_n^{\infty,R})'(t)
 \le K(1+R)D_n^{\infty,R}(t)
   +K\sum_{a,j}
     \|P_{j,n}^{a,R}-\tau_R(P_{j,n}^{a,R})\|_n. \tag{36}
\]
By (27)–(28), the last term converges to its population counterpart. Hence
\[
\lim_{R\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}
 \sup_{t\le T}D_n^{\infty,R}(t)=0. \tag{37}
\]
The triangle inequality using (28), (33), and (37) proves
\[
\mu_n^\infty\longrightarrow\mu^\infty
\quad\text{in path }W_2. \tag{38}
\]

### 5. Exact GD versus the finite-width ODE

Let \(\Theta_n=(U^1,\ldots,U^p,B,A,C)\), and let
\(\dot\Theta_n=F_n(\Theta_n)\) denote the uncut finite-width ODE. GD with the same normalized layer metrics and step \(\eta\) is exactly
\[
\Theta_{n,k+1}=\Theta_{n,k}+\eta F_n(\Theta_{n,k}), \tag{39}
\]
so it is the explicit Euler scheme for this ODE.

Let \(\mathcal E_n\) be the event that every underlying standard Gaussian used in (4) has absolute value at most \(n\). Since there are \(O(n^2)\) such variables,
\[
\mathbb P(\mathcal E_n^c)\le Kn^2e^{-n^2/2},
\]
and therefore \(\mathcal E_n\) eventually holds almost surely. On \(\mathcal E_n\), the sequential structure of (3) gives, for each fixed \(T\), exponents \(q_0,q_1<\infty\), independent of \(n\), such that
\[
\sup_{t\le T}\|\Theta_n(t)\|\le n^{q_0},\qquad
\sup_{\|\Theta\|\le 2n^{q_0}}
 \bigl(\|F_n(\Theta)\|+\|DF_n(\Theta)\|\bigr)\le n^{q_1}. \tag{40}
\]
Indeed \(C\) is bounded by (9), \(A\) grows by a bounded rank-\(p\) term, \(P_2\) is linear in \(A,C\), \(B\) is then polynomially bounded through \(D_2\), and finally \(P_1,U\) are polynomially bounded through \(B,D_2\). The degree is fixed because \(p\) and the depth are fixed.

If \(M_n,L_n\le n^{q_1}\) denote the bounds in (40), the usual Euler recursion gives, for the piecewise-linear interpolation,
\[
\sup_{t\le T}\|\Theta_n^\eta(t)-\Theta_n(t)\|
 \le M_n\eta\left(1+\frac{e^{L_nT}-1}{2}\right). \tag{41}
\]
A first-exit bootstrap keeps the Euler path inside the ball used in (40). With
\[
\eta_n=e^{-e^n}, \tag{42}
\]
the right-hand side of (41) is at most
\[
\exp\{-e^n+Tn^{q_1}+O(\log n)\}\longrightarrow0 \tag{43}
\]
almost surely. Thus exact GD at step \(\eta_n\) and the finite-width ODE have the same limiting path law, and (38) transfers to GD.

### Scope of the result

Two stronger interpretations are false or unsupported:

1. A cutoff-uniform \(\psi_2\) bound cannot hold at finite width. Already for \(n=p=1\), at the first nontrivial uncut Euler step,
   \[
   P_1=\gamma h y\,A_0B_0\,
   \phi'(S_0)\phi(T_0)\phi'(T_0).
   \]
   Periodicity provides arbitrarily large regions on which the bounded factor is separated from zero, so this variable has a product-Gaussian lower tail and is not sub-Gaussian. Uniformity in \(R\) for the clipped variables would imply a sub-Gaussian uncut limit by Fatou. Thus (21) is necessarily an infinite-width OMFP statement.

2. Tensor Programs III Theorem A.7 cannot be invoked literally without rank stability. The stated assumptions do not provide it. Smoothness permits the no-rank workaround via Theorem E.15 plus the uniform-integrability argument of Theorem A.2; without either that workaround or a separate rank-stability proof, the claimed mean tail transfer has a genuine gap.

NOT CLEAN.

## Fatal issues

1. **The all-source lemma is not a coherent proved statement.**

   - \(I_q(X)=\|\partial X/\partial\widehat Z_q\|_2\) and \(I(X)\) are deterministic numbers. Thus the stated \(J_k\), built from these quantities and deterministic response coefficients, is deterministic. But (4.5)–(4.8) treat \(J_k\) as a pointwise random derivative envelope and estimate \(\mathbb E J_k^2\) using the random \(P_{r,\ell}\). If a pointwise envelope was intended, it must be defined before taking \(L^2\) norms and re-proved.
   - “Every path has a unique last cross-cycle edge” does not establish (4.5). A transpose call introduces response terms indexed by all preceding opposite-orientation calls, and learned-rank terms contain vectors, overlaps, and errors from many cycles. No node-by-node recurrence enumerates these edges or shows that multiplicities remain \(O(1)\) as \(k\sim T/h\).
   - The extra \(h\) in (4.1) does not itself control the unweighted absolute response-coefficient sums later used in (4.9). The relation between the weighted source derivative sum and those coefficient sums is never proved.
   - The two estimates (4.9) are the decisive step and are only prose. The discussion of the two immediate product-rule terms does not enumerate indirect routes through \(C,T,H_3,e\), learned \(A,B\) memories, and earlier response corrections. There is no derivation showing that every such term has respectively \(T\) or \(q_2+T\), with constants uniform in \(h\).
   - At singular Grams, only the response element in the span is invariant; the \(\ell^1\) mass of coefficients is not invariant under redundant representations and can be unstable near degeneracy. If the intended coefficients are the syntax-dependent derivatives in (2.5), that representation and its uniform bound must be proved explicitly. Merely “retaining redundant innovations” does not do this.
   - The first-crossing bootstrap is circular as written. At \(k_*\), the hypothesis \(q\le1\) used for (4.8), the \(J_{k_*}\) estimate, and hence (4.9), has already failed. A within-cycle stopped induction might repair this, but the assertion that the estimates apply “before and at” \(k_*\) is unjustified.

   Consequently neither (4.2) nor the tail bound (4.12) has been established.

2. **The primary tensor-program theorem does not supply the missing uniform estimate.**

   The primary result is for each fixed finite program. Theorem 2.10 gives almost-sure empirical convergence for polynomially bounded nonlinearities/tests; Theorem A.2 gives \(L^1\) convergence for linearly bounded nonlinearities and quadratically bounded tests; Lemma L.11 proves rank stability inside a fixed parameterless program. It provides no estimate uniform over programs whose length grows like \(1/h\), no uniform response-coefficient bound, and no cutoff-uniform derivative estimate. See [Yang, Tensor Programs III](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.10685).

3. **The finite-width tail transfer (5.2) does not follow from the preceding results.**

   - The oracle empirical residual converges in mean, but actual–oracle coupling (3.7) is only in probability. Passing a quadratic residual through that coupling requires uniform integrability that is not proved.
   - The ODE/Euler estimate (5.1) holds only on an operator-norm event. No bad-event moment estimate is supplied, yet (5.2) is an unconditional expectation bound.
   - Moving from grid values to the time integral of a quadratic residual likewise needs quantitative moment control, not just the in-probability \(O(h)\) comparison.

   This is load-bearing: without (5.2), cutoff removal fails.

4. **The cutoff comparison is asserted in an undefined metric.**

   \(D_{n,R}\) and \(S_{n,R}\) are never defined. A proof of (5.4) must specify whether \(D\) contains instantaneous normalized \(L^2\) norms, learned-rank operator norms, matrix-action probes, scalar overlaps, or their squares, and then check every term in the \(U,C,A,B,S,T,P_1,P_2\) equations. Constants must be controlled on and off the initialization operator-norm event. The phrase following (5.3) is not such a derivation.

   Without a defined complete space and norm, the claims that \(X_R\) is Cauchy, that its limit solves the uncut equations, and that (5.5) identifies the finite-width uncut ODE are not justified.

5. **Existence, uniqueness, and autonomy in the GNS topology are not proved.**

   - Fixed-\(R\) uniqueness of a coupled finite-width/Lipschitz construction is not automatically uniqueness of an action-law trajectory.
   - No vector field is defined on the metric space (3.8), nor is continuity, closedness under limits, or completeness of the admissible bounded-action states shown.
   - Two abstract action-law solutions need not come with the common coordinatewise coupling required by the \(D\)-comparison.
   - Saying that the current GNS state contains the actions does not prove a restart/semigroup theorem. One must show that future derivatives of every probe are determined continuously by the current state and that the uniqueness argument applies from arbitrary \(t_0\).

6. **The \(W_2(C)\) and uniform-integrability paragraph is insufficient.**

   - \(W_2\) on path space requires convergence of the second moment of the sup norm, not merely fixed-time or fixed-grid second moments.
   - (5.6) can support the standard argument only after uniform bounds for empirical integrated squared velocities are proved. No such finite-width, mesh-uniform, cutoff-uniform bound is given.
   - Velocities of \(S,T\) contain current matrix actions, so (4.12), even if valid, controls too little by itself.
   - One needs an explicit compact-containment/modulus argument, finite-grid weak convergence, approximation of the sup-norm moment by grid maxima, and tail control for the uncut finite-width laws.
   - The statement that “the same uniform integrability passes every kernel block … and squared velocity” is unsupported. Therefore the \(L^2\) small-time expansions in Section 7 and all conclusions relying on passage of velocities/kernels are also unproved.

## Repairable but currently missing

- In the general-\(\sigma_r\) statement, (2.3) and the innovation covariance in (2.4) omit the factor \(\sigma_r^2\). This does not affect the variance-one \(A_0,B_0\) application but makes the quoted general rule inaccurate.
- The fixed-\(R\) mesh estimate (5.1) needs a concrete state norm, uniform trajectory bounds, and a standard Euler consistency calculation; comparing two meshes also requires compiling their joint program.
- The exact-GD step is plausible but not proved at the requested level. One must give explicit polynomial recurrences, specify the growing-dimensional norm, derive \(q_0,q_1\), prove the Euler iterates remain in the \(2n^{q_0}\) ball by a stopped bootstrap, and include the local truncation factor involving both \(\|F_n\|\) and \(\|DF_n\|\). The super-exponential step would then dominate the resulting \(\exp(\mathrm{poly}(n))\) bound.

The theorem may be salvageable, but Sections 4–5 currently replace all load-bearing continuum estimates with assertions rather than proofs.

NOT CLEAN.

Primary-source check: arXiv:2009.10685v3, Theorem 2.10, says: “Fix a NETSOR⊤ program… Assume all nonlinearities φ used in Nonlin are polynomially bounded. Then for any fixed k and any polynomially bounded ψ,” with
\[
n^{-1}\sum_\alpha\psi(h^1_\alpha,\ldots,h^k_\alpha)
 \xrightarrow{\rm a.s.}\mathbb E\psi(Z^{h^1},\ldots,Z^{h^k}).
\]
Its Setup 2.2 requires independent \(N(0,\sigma_W^2/n)\) matrices and iid coordinate slices from a joint Gaussian law; the proof does allow singular initial covariance and removes rank stability for a fixed parameterless program. Theorem A.2 gives \(L^1\) convergence only when all Nonlin maps are linearly bounded and the test is quadratically bounded. Thus it covers the fixed-\((R,h,K_0)\) oracle, but gives no uniformity as \(K_0\sim h^{-1}\to\infty\), no operator-norm convergence, and no uniform control of response-coefficient masses. [Primary v3 PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.10685v3)

Fatal gaps in the submitted proof:

1. **The all-source lemma is internally inconsistent and unproved.** In (4.1), \(I_q(X)=\|\partial X/\partial\widehat Z_q\|_2\), hence \(I_q,I\), and the subsequently defined \(J_k\) are deterministic numbers. Equations (4.5)–(4.8), however, treat \(J_k\) as a random variable: the right side of (4.5) contains random \(X_\ell\), and the proof takes \(\mathbb EJ_k^2\). Changing \(I_q\) to a pointwise derivative would be a substantive rewrite, not a notational correction.

2. **Even after such a rewrite, (4.5) and especially (4.9) are assertions, not proved estimates.** No formal source graph, induction, or accounting of response paths is supplied. At singular covariance, the Tensor Programs theorem supplies a jointly Gaussian, possibly redundant \(\widehat Z\)-family and an invariant *total response*; it does not bound the sum of absolute symbolic response coefficients uniformly as the number of calls grows like \(h^{-1}\). The claim that every old-source path carries \(h\), and that all redundant-call coefficient masses are bounded independently of \(h,R\), is precisely the missing theorem. Since (4.2)/(4.12) is the sole uniform estimate used for \(h\downarrow0\), cutoff removal, uniform integrability, velocity passage, and \(W_2\) path convergence, the main limit theorem is not established.

3. **The GNS construction does not prove the claimed action law.** Exact transpose identities establish adjointness on the algebraic probe domain; they do not imply that \(A,B\) extend to bounded operators on the completed Hilbert spaces. A uniform finite-width operator inequality would be needed and must pass to every probe. Moreover, the grammar is stated to use only bounded coordinate maps, while the dynamics require linearly unbounded maps such as \((s,p)\mapsto\phi'(s)p\). Most importantly, no state space, vector field on its equivalence classes, or comparison between different GNS realizations is defined. The cutoff comparison on one coupled initialization therefore proves neither uniqueness of an abstract action-law solution nor the asserted autonomous/restartable property.

4. **The compact-time bridges are only claimed.** The distances \(d\) in (5.1) and \(D_{n,R}\) in (5.4) are never defined, and the displayed inequalities are not derived. Operator differences generate terms such as \(\Delta B^\top\Delta D_2\), so a linear inequality of the form (5.4) requires a bootstrap/localization argument. Also, (3.7) gives actual-to-oracle convergence only in probability, whereas (5.2) transfers an expectation of an unbounded quadratic residual; no uniform-integrability estimate for the coupling error or bad operator-norm event is given. These are necessary independently of the formal tail bound.

5. **The \(W_2(C([0,T]))\) conclusion is asserted beyond what (5.6) proves.** Equation (5.6) can give modulus tightness once uniform velocity bounds are established, but weak path-law convergence and convergence/uniform integrability of the squared supremum norm must still be proved. The sentence claiming that fixed-grid convergence and uniform integrability “therefore” give \(W_2\) convergence supplies neither argument, and the required uniform integrability currently depends on the invalid Section 4 lemma.

Repairable errors/gaps:

6. **The stated reused-matrix formulas omit variance factors.** For entries \(N(0,\sigma_r^2/n)\),
\[
\sigma_{\rm cond}^2=\frac{\sigma_r^2}{n}\|P_Y^\perp h\|^2,\qquad
\mathbb E[\widehat W^s[X]\widehat W^s[X']]
 =\sigma_r^2\mathbb E[XX'],
\]
and the derivative response is
\[
\sigma_r^2\sum_iY_i\,\mathbb E\frac{\partial X}
 {\partial\widehat W^{-s}[Y_i]}.
\]
Equations (2.3)–(2.5) omit these factors. The network uses \(\sigma_r=1\), so specialization repairs the application, but the theorem as stated is false for general \(\sigma_r\).

7. **The fixed-mesh coupling is too compressed to verify.** In (3.6), \(\delta_k\), the learned-memory error, and \(\zeta_{n,k}\) are not defined precisely; the text also literally says \(\delta_k\) includes the operator norms of the learned memories rather than their differences, which would make (3.7) false. A finite induction should be possible, but it must track each vector, scalar coefficient, rank-memory difference, the \(C_0\) localization, and moment control.

8. **The ODE/GD comparison needs norm-specific bounds.** The polynomial ball and Jacobian estimates in (5.7) are only asserted, and (5.8) additionally needs a bootstrap showing Euler iterates remain in that ball plus control of the piecewise interpolation. The triangular structure makes this plausibly repairable.

The exact normalization (1.1)–(1.3), the singular-\(R\) fixed-program Tensor Programs application, and the initialization/response/strict-positivity calculations in Sections 6–8 appear algebraically correct, conditional on the missing existence, uniform-limit, and GNS bridges above.

NOT CLEAN.

The calculations in Sections 1 and 6–8 are correct conditional on the existence and strong \(L^2\)-continuity claims of Sections 2–5:

- All \(n,d,\gamma\) factors in (1.1)–(1.3) check out.
- For unit-variance jointly Gaussian \(X,Y\),
  \[
  \mathbb E[\phi(X)\phi(Y)]
  =\mathbb E[\phi'(X)\phi'(Y)]=e^{-1+\operatorname{Cov}(X,Y)},
  \]
  so \(Q_1,Q_2,Q_3\) are correct.
- The response matrices are correctly recomputed as
  \[
  M^3_{ac}=y_cQ_{3,ac}-\delta_{ac}(Q_3y)_a,
  \]
  \[
  M^2_{ac}=Q_{2,ac}M^3_{ac}
  -\delta_{ac}\sum_kQ_{2,ak}M^3_{ak}.
  \]
  The independent innovation covariances \(G^3,G^2\) are also correct.
- The strict-positive-definiteness arguments are valid for every collection of distinct RMS-normalized inputs, even when \(R\) is singular. In particular, the Gaussian RBF argument gives \(Q_1\succ0\), Schur powers give \(Q_2,Q_3\succ0\), and the row-factor argument proves \(R\circ G^1\succ0\).
- For every \(y\in\{\pm1\}^p\), \(g\not\equiv0\), \(G^1,G^2,G^3\succ0\), and hence \(E_\ell=y^\top L_\ell y>0\).
- The leading feature velocities, displacements, kernel coefficients, and loss derivative are correct:
  \[
  \dot Z^\ell(t)=\gamma^2t\,v^\ell+o_{L^2}(t),\qquad
  Z^\ell(t)-Z^\ell(0)=\tfrac12\gamma^2t^2v^\ell+o_{L^2}(t^2),
  \]
  \[
  K^{A,B,U}(t)=\gamma^2t^2L_{3,2,1}+o(t^2),
  \]
  \[
  y^\top K(t)y
  =y^\top Q_3y+2\gamma^2t^2(E_1+E_2+E_3)+o(t^2),
  \]
  \[
  \mathcal L'(0+)=-\gamma^2y^\top Q_3y<0.
  \]
  There is no missing factor of two in (7.8) or (7.9).

The full theorem is nevertheless not established because these conclusions rely on the following unproved obligations.

1. The all-source lemma is internally inconsistent.

   In (4.1), \(I_q(X)=\|\partial X/\partial\widehat Z_q\|_2\) is deterministic, so the subsequently defined \(J_k\) is apparently deterministic. But (4.5)–(4.8) treat \(J_k\) as a random pointwise Jacobian envelope driven by the random \(P_{r,\ell}\). More importantly, the load-bearing response bounds (4.9) are asserted by prose rather than derived. No complete enumeration controls all historical opposite-orientation calls uniformly when \(k\asymp T/h\), and absolute coefficient mass is not invariant under redundant representations at singular Grams.

   Repair: define a pointwise Jacobian envelope first, prove a node-by-node recursion in an invariant Gaussian-source basis, derive (4.9) with all historical multiplicities, and use a within-cycle stopped bootstrap.

2. The tail transfer and cutoff removal are not proved.

   The oracle–actual comparison is only in probability, whereas (5.2) is an unconditional quadratic expectation estimate. Uniform integrability on the bad operator-norm event and between Euler grid points is not supplied. Moreover, \(D_{n,R}\) and \(S_{n,R}\) in (5.4) are never defined, and no termwise estimate proves that all matrix-memory, overlap, and adjoint terms satisfy the displayed linear inequality with constants uniform in \(n\).

   Repair: define the complete comparison norm, prove the differential inequality component by component, and supply uniform moment estimates before passing expectations and time integrals.

3. \(W_2(C)\), velocity convergence, and the strong expansions remain unsupported.

   Inequality (5.6) yields path-space tightness only after a uniform finite-width bound on integrated squared velocities. No such cutoff-, mesh-, and width-uniform estimate is proved, nor is uniform integrability of the sup norm established. Path-law \(W_2\) convergence alone also does not pass squared velocities.

   Consequently (7.1)–(7.4), although algebraically correct, still require proof that current matrix actions are strongly \(L^2\)-continuous and, for example,
   \[
   A(t)-A_0=O_{\rm op}(t^2),\qquad B(t)-B_0=O_{\rm op}(t^2),
   \]
   in the limiting action class.

4. Uniqueness and autonomy do not follow from (5.5).

   That estimate concerns the specifically coupled finite-width solution. It does not compare two arbitrary GNS action-law solutions, which need not share a coordinatewise coupling or the tail estimates used in the cutoff argument. Merely recording current actions in the GNS state does not prove a restart/semigroup theorem.

   Repair: define the vector field on a complete admissible action-state space, show it is closed and locally unique there, and prove that future derivatives of every probe are determined by the current state.

5. The theorem’s quantifiers and observables require clarification.

   Literally “for every \(T<T_*\)” includes \(T=0\), contradicting \(\mathcal L(T)<\mathcal L(0)\). It should say \(0<T<T_*\). The interpolation must be explicitly defined as continuous, such as polygonal, to produce laws in \(C([0,T])\). “Layer displacement” should also be stated quantitatively: the proof establishes aggregate positivity
   \[
   \sum_a\mathbb E|Z_a^\ell(T)-Z_a^\ell(0)|^2>0,
   \]
   not explicitly positivity for every sample \(a\). Likewise “positive time integral” should be written as
   \[
   \int_0^T K^\ell(t)\,dt\succ0.
   \]

6. The general Gaussian-program rule is misstated.

   For matrices with entries \(N(0,\sigma_r^2/n)\), (2.3) and the innovation covariance following (2.4) omit the factor \(\sigma_r^2\). This does not affect the present \(A_0,B_0\), whose variance parameter is one, but the stated supporting theorem is not correct as written.

Thus Sections 1 and 6–8 contain no detected sign, scaling, covariance, response-matrix, or small-time coefficient error, but the continuum estimates needed to make those formal calculations conclusions about the claimed GD limit are not proved.

The proposed trigonometric-energy repair does not close. The exact obstruction is the gate derivative
\[
D_S\!\left[\phi'(S)P\right]\xi=-\phi(S)P\,\xi,
\]
which is an unbounded multiplication operator on \(L^2\) once \(P\) has a Gaussian innovation. The rotation identity preserves each activation pair’s norm, but not distances between trajectories.

Therefore, the report still needs a genuinely new uniform aggregate-adjoint/Orlicz theorem for dense reused-matrix histories. The fixed-program tensor theorem does not provide this. In particular, the report’s all-source lemma is not rigorous enough to imply its sub-Gaussian tail estimate, so cutoff removal, uniqueness, and full compact-time convergence remain unproved.

- After repairing those appendix-level issues, the fixed-finite-mesh population-oracle invocation is rigorous. It supplies no mesh-uniform or continuum-time theorem.

    +\kappa^2\mathbb E[q(A_0)^2H_0^2]>0.
  \]

Independent recomputation gives:

\[
\partial_{A_i}f_n=n^{-1}q(A_i)P_i,\quad
\partial_{B_{ji}}f_n=n^{-1}D_jH_i,\quad

\]
and hence exactly
\[
\dot A=-2e\,q(A)P,\quad
\dot B=-2e\,D\otimes_nH,\quad
\dot C=-2eG.
\]
The effective raw rates \(n^{-1},n^{-2},n^{-3}\), all three kernel blocks, and
\[

are correct. Equation (1.6) is also correct.

\[

B_0^\top u(s)=a_nH+\sqrt{\nu_n}P_H^\perp\Gamma,
\]
with
\[

=\mathbb Eu'(Z)=\kappa,\qquad \nu_n\to\nu.
\]
Since the removed one-dimensional projection is \(o_{L^2_n}(1)\),
\[
\Xi=\sqrt\nu\,\Gamma+\kappa H_0
\]
is the correct reused-adjoint law.

All small-time coefficients recompute as stated, using the corrected \(d\):
\[

P=2t\Xi+O(t^2),
\]
\[
A-A_0=2t^2q(A_0)\Xi+o(t^2),\quad
B-B_0=2t^2u\otimes H_0+o(t^2),
\]
\[
S-Z=2t^2\mathcal Ru+o(t^2).
\]

\[
\|\dot A\|_2^2=16dt^2+o(t^2),\qquad
\|\dot S\|_2^2=16\|\mathcal Ru\|_2^2t^2+o(t^2),

\]
Thus the claimed strict positivity, motion, kernel nonconstancy, and loss decrease follow on one common sufficiently short interval. More explicitly,
\[

\quad

\]
with analogous positive formulas for \(S\) and \(K^B\); \(K^C\) already has leading integral \(k_3T\).

The variance and nonlinear-regression arguments are sound. Initial variances are \(1\) and \(m>0\); \(W_2\)-continuity preserves positivity for short time. Formula (9.13) is the affine-projection residual, is strictly positive for either full-support Gaussian, and is continuous under \(W_2\). These facts support a single \(T_*>0\) working for every \(0<T\le T_*\).

- The entire width identification depends on the external reused-Gaussian theorem (3.1), including its singular-covariance form. “The full primary proof was checked” is not an internal proof, so this dependency cannot be independently certified from the sole input.

- The passage from Cauchy finite-probe records to a jointly consistent time-indexed realization satisfying the Bochner integral equation, and the alignment needed to apply the operator-norm uniqueness estimate, is outlined rather than proved.
- The truncation argument for \(BQ\), uniform velocity-energy convergence, and its transfer to exact GD omit the quantitative limit interchange required for the stated convergence mode.

The activity theorem is algebraically supported, but the full convergence theorem is not self-containedly proved by this manuscript alone.

I read the full [Tensor Programs III v3 primary proof](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.10685v3): Setup 2.2, Theorem 2.10, Box 1/Remarks 2.11–2.12, Appendix K.5, and Appendix L. The external-theorem specialization is accurate:

- Singular initial Gaussian covariance is allowed.
- The program and nonlinearities must be fixed as \(n\to\infty\) and polynomially bounded.
- The conclusion is almost-sure empirical convergence for polynomially bounded tests.
- Appendix L genuinely proves the singular case through simultaneous Moments/CoreSet induction, zero stability, rank stability, exact dependence when the innovation vanishes, and a rank-\(n-O(1)\) projected-Gaussian strong law otherwise.
- ZHat/ZDot retains all reused-transpose responses.
- The population oracle, for fixed \(\delta,N\), is a valid fixed program: \(C_0=0\) is a permissible degenerate Gaussian, all coefficients are deterministic and \(n\)-independent, and all coordinate maps are polynomially bounded. Appendix A.1/A.2 is not needed.

Two proof defects remain.

1. Major but repairable: [Section 5](/tmp/L2_FULL_AUDITED_PROOF.md:245) records only one-time finite joint laws. The GNS completions therefore have no specified common identification across different \(t\). Consequently the Bochner integral
\[
B(t)=B_0-2\int_0^t e(s)D(s)\otimes H(s)\,ds
\]
is not yet defined in one fixed \(\mathcal B(\mathcal H_R,\mathcal H_L)\): cross-time inner products/joint laws and strong measurability are absent. The grammar also records the current \(B\), not the \(B_0\)-actions subsequently used in (5.2). Thus the claimed genuine integral equation, uniqueness, and restartable semigroup do not follow as written.

A repair is to record all rational-time, time-tagged probe joint laws and \(B_0,B_0^*\) actions, then extend by uniform \(L^2\) continuity; alternatively construct the initial cyclic GNS space once, solve the Banach-space ODE there, and identify its Euler laws with the population oracle.

2. Local repair needed in [Section 4](/tmp/L2_FULL_AUDITED_PROOF.md:223): (4.3) is not literally the finite-\(n\) Euler scheme because empirical Gram factors have been replaced by population expectations. Define
\[
\bar B_k^{(n)}
=B_0-2\delta\sum_{r<k}\bar e_r\bar D_r\otimes_n\bar H_r.
\]
Then, for example,
\[
\bar B_k^{(n)}\bar H_k-\bar S_k
=-2\delta\sum_{r<k}\bar e_r\bar D_r
\bigl(\langle\bar H_r,\bar H_k\rangle_n-\mathbb E[\bar H_r\bar H_k]\bigr),
\]
with the analogous adjoint residual. These residuals must be inserted before deriving (4.4). The “learned rank memory” for the oracle is otherwise undefined, and \(\zeta_{n,k}\) must be a nonnegative sum of absolute empirical deviations. Once written this way, Theorem 2.10 does control every residual and the coupling argument is sound.

No contradiction to the theorem was found, but the common-time operator/action-law realization is a missing essential bridge.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 35017 | 2026-08-28T15:09:34.882Z | /root/l2_blind_activity_r1 ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/l2_blind_activity_r1
Payload:
A clean fixed-space continuum construction is available, conditional only on the reused-Gaussian theorem in Section 3. It also pinpoints what Section 5 must add: saturation, a uniform operator bound, and current-rooted—not history-tagged—states.

### Lemma 1: simultaneous cyclic/GNS realization

Let \(\mathcal P_R,\mathcal P_L\) be the countable formal probe families obtained by jointly unrolling the population-oracle Euler schemes for every dyadic mesh and dyadic time, then closing under:

- rational linear combinations and scalar inner products;
- the coordinate maps used by the vector field;
- \(B_0\) and \(B_0^*\);
- a countable dense family of bounded Lipschitz cylinder maps.

Every finite subfamily is one fixed tensor program sharing the same \(A_0,B_0\). Thus (3.1) gives consistent limiting finite joint laws. Kolmogorov extension gives canonical same-side probability spaces. Let \(\mathcal H_R,\mathcal H_L\) be the \(L^2\)-closures of the named coordinates.

For a formal right probe \(v\), define \(B_0v\) by its named left probe. On the almost-sure product coupling,
\[
\|B_n v_n\|_n\le M_0\|v_n\|_n
\]
eventually. Passing to limiting norms gives
\[
\|B_0v\|_{\mathcal H_L}\le M_0\|v\|_{\mathcal H_R}.
\]
Hence zero-norm relations are respected and \(B_0\) extends boundedly. The finite identity
\[
\langle u,B_nv\rangle_n=\langle B_n^\top u,v\rangle_n
\]
shows that the named transpose action extends as its Hilbert adjoint.

Every population-oracle Euler scheme now lives on this one fixed pair of spaces and has the same \(B_0\). Its unrolled equations are exactly (4.3).

### Lemma 2: fixed-space action ODE

On
\[
\mathcal E=\mathcal H_R\times
\mathcal B(\mathcal H_R,\mathcal H_L)\times\mathcal H_L
\]
write \(B=B_0+K\) and define
\[
H=h(X),\quad S=BH,\quad G=\arctan S,\quad
D=Cq(S),\quad e=\langle C,G\rangle-1,
\]
\[
\dot X=-2eB^*D,\qquad
\dot K=-2eD\otimes H,\qquad
\dot C=-2eG. \tag{*}
\]

On sets where \(\|B\|_{\rm op}\le M_B\) and \(\|C\|_\infty\le M_C\), this vector field is Lipschitz in
\[
\|X-\widetilde X\|_2+
\|K-\widetilde K\|_{\rm op}+
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2.
\]
A Picard argument works on the closed class of paths satisfying the common \(L^\infty\) bound on \(C\); an \(L^\infty\)-bounded ball is closed under \(L^2\) convergence. The \(C\)-component of the integral map preserves such a ball on a sufficiently short interval because \(G\) is bounded.

The solution is global. The \(L^2\) Nemytskii chain rule gives, almost everywhere,
\[
\dot e=-2eK_{\rm tan},
\]
where
\[
K_{\rm tan}
=\|G\|_2^2+\|H\|_2^2\|D\|_2^2
 +\langle B^*D,q(J(X))^2B^*D\rangle\ge0.
\]
Hence \(|e(t)|\le |e(0)|\), followed by
\[
\|C(t)\|_\infty\le \|C(0)\|_\infty+2a_0|e(0)|t,
\]
\[
\|\dot B\|_{\rm op}
\le2a_0|e(0)|\|C\|_2,\qquad
\|\dot X\|_2
\le2|e(0)|\|B\|_{\rm op}\|C\|_2.
\]
No finite-time blow-up is possible.

Standard Banach-space Euler error estimates now give
\[
\sup_{t\le T}
\bigl(
\|X^\delta-X\|_2+
\|B^\delta-B\|_{\rm op}+
\|C^\delta-C\|_2
\bigr)\le C_T\delta.
\]
Since \(z^\delta\) is exactly the population oracle on the simultaneous GNS space, the Cauchy action law constructed in Section 5 is the law of this genuine solution. This avoids trying to infer an integral equation merely from a collection of limiting records.

### Lemma 3: uniqueness and restartability on action laws

Define the state at time \(t\) by the complete grammar rooted at the current triple
\[
(X(t),C(t),B(t)),
\]
not by retaining absolute-time history tags.

If two such rooted action laws agree, mapping each named probe to its counterpart is isometric on the rational probe spans and extends to unitaries
\[
U_R:\mathcal H_R\to\widetilde{\mathcal H}_R,\qquad
U_L:\mathcal H_L\to\widetilde{\mathcal H}_L.
\]
Saturation gives
\[
U_LB=\widetilde B\,U_R,\qquad
U_RB^*=\widetilde B^*U_L,
\]
and makes the unitaries commute with every required coordinate map. They also transport rank-one operators:
\[
U_L(D\otimes H)U_R^{-1}
=(U_LD)\otimes(U_RH).
\]
Thus (*) is equivariant. Fixed-space uniqueness implies that equivalent initial action laws have equivalent trajectories, so the flow descends to action-law equivalence classes.

For any \(s\ge0\), \(t\mapsto z(s+t)\) solves the same autonomous equation with initial state \(z(s)\). Therefore
\[
\Phi_t(\Phi_s[z_0])=\Phi_{t+s}[z_0].
\]
This proves restartability without referring to \(B_0\) or the past.

### Necessary topology caveat

The weighted finite-record topology alone does not ensure a bounded limiting operator. For example, on \(\ell^2\) with named basis \(e_k\), let
\[
B_ne_k=
\begin{cases}
k e_k,&k\le n,\\
0,&k>n.
\end{cases}
\]
Every fixed finite family of probe records stabilizes, so the record sequence is Cauchy, but its formal limit \(Be_k=ke_k\) is unbounded. Even with a uniform bound, \(B_n=P_{\operatorname{span}\{e_n\}}\) converges on every fixed probe while \(\|B_n\|_{\rm op}=1\), so the topology does not control operator-norm convergence.

Accordingly, Section 5 must restrict to saturated action laws carrying a common operator bound. The manuscript’s particular sequence has that bound from (2.13) plus the bounded learned rank memory, so the obstruction is repairable.

Absolute rational-time tags may be used to construct the common GNS space, but they must be discarded when defining the state \(\mathfrak S(t)\). Otherwise the object contains history, and (5.4) does not follow merely from autonomy of the current triple.

NOT CLEAN.

I found no counterexample to the concrete mean-field dynamics, and the Tensor Programs III theorem is applicable after repairing its printed proof. However, the submitted proof is not yet fully rigorous.

1. The action-law bridge is undefined/incomplete.

   - In (5.12), neither “current-rooted probe record” nor \(d_{\rm record}\) is defined.
   - Equations (5.5) establish residual control only for the dynamical \(H_k,D_k\) calls. Convergence for an arbitrary nested record containing further \(B,B^*\) calls requires a structural induction and the analogous Gram residuals.
   - In §4.2, equality of current probe laws gives isometries only on the current cyclic probe closures. Surjective unitaries on all of \(\mathcal H_R,\mathcal H_L\), and hence the asserted full intertwining of \(B\), require proof that current-rooted probes remain dense at every \(t\).
   - Repair: define a countable typed probe language and its record metric explicitly, prove fixed-mesh convergence by induction on probe depth, then either work on the cyclic closures or prove density using backward Euler approximation of the autonomous ODE. Only afterward may the \(2^{-j}\)-metric enumeration and law-level semigroup argument be invoked.

2. The exact-GD observable bridge is unfinished.

   Section 6 proves uniform kernel/output/loss convergence for the auxiliary flow. Section 7 proves state closeness, velocity-energy convergence, and path-law convergence for GD, but does not actually derive uniform convergence of the exact-GD \(K_n^A,K_n^B,K_n^C,f_n,L_n\). State \(L^2\) closeness alone is insufficient for \(K^A\) because of the unbounded \(P\) factor. Repair: reuse (7.9) and the uniform square-tail estimate at GD nodes, extend it between nodes using the \(P\)-time modulus, and repeat the fixed-\(M\) truncation from (6.12)–(6.18).

3. Tensor Programs III v3’s printed proof has literal errors in the transpose-reuse argument.

   In `proofs.tex` lines 1578–1617, Case 2 defines
   \(U=(Z^{u^1},\dots,Z^{u^k})\), but the Gaussian-conditioning and conditional-independence claims require
   \(U=(\widehat Z^{u^1},\dots,\widehat Z^{u^k})\). As written, the independence assertion is false: conditioning on a sum of a Gaussian component and another random component generally couples them. Line 1613 must likewise contain \(Z^{v^i}\), not \(\widehat Z^{v^i}\). With these substitutions, the adjunction proof closes.

4. The source’s high-moment count drops a factor \(n\).

   The one-collision/weak-correlation contribution is
   \[
   n^{-(p-1)/4}n^{2p-1}B''_{2p}
   =n^{(7p-3)/4}B''_{2p},
   \]
   not \(n^{1.75(p-1)}B''_{2p}\). After normalization it is
   \(n^{-(p+3)/4}B''_{2p}\), which is still \(O(n^{-3/2})\) for \(p\ge6\). Thus the Master Theorem survives, but the printed derivation needs correction. Its conditional-variance display also needs \(\operatorname{Var}(\widehat Z^g)=\sigma_A^2\mathbb E(Z^h)^2\) and \(\mathring\sigma^2\), not \(\mathbb E(Z^g)^2\) and \(\mathring\sigma\).

5. Minor but exact issues:

   - The specialized theorem in §3 should state mutual independence of the initial Gaussian matrix and initial vectors.

Verdict: the broad “no \(O(1)\)-field/PDE closure” reading is false. Section 8 supports only a width-uniform, state-universal, finite numerical graph-moment no-go.

### Precisely recoverable no-go

Let \(\delta_n\) be the feature-time derivation from [§2, lines 54–67](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:54). A rigorous version of [lines 895–919](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:895) is:

- \(k<\infty\) is independent of width.
- Each coordinate \(g_j\) is a fixed finite linear combination of typed, fully contracted, same-time graph monomials in \(a,R,B,x\); hence the union of their connected component types and their maximal degree is finite.
- \(f\) is one of the coordinates.
- One fixed \(F:\mathbb R^k\to\mathbb R^k\), independent of \(n\), satisfies
  \[
  \delta_n g(\theta)=F(g(\theta))
  \]
  for every sufficiently large \(n\) and every state \(\theta\), not merely for Gaussian initialization or its orbit.
- For the analytic version, \(F\) must be an ambient real-analytic germ on a neighborhood of \(g(0)\), and the identity must hold for all sufficiently small states. Analyticity only on the realized orbit/image is insufficient.

No such closure exists when \(F\) is polynomial; the same proof extends to the stated ambient-germ analytic class after Taylor expansion and degree scaling.

### The graph obstruction is correct in that class

Put \(T=RB\), \(C=T^*T=B^*R^*RB\), and retain only the branch of the derivation that differentiates \(a,x\), leaving \(R,B\) untouched. Define
\[
A_j=a^*TC^jx,\quad
X_j=x^*C^{j+1}x,\quad
Y_j=a^*(TT^*)^{j+1}a.
\]
Then
\[
\delta_0A_j=X_j+Y_j,\qquad
\delta_0X_j=\delta_0Y_j=2A_{j+1},
\]
so
\[
\delta_0^{\,2m-1}f
=4^{m-1}(X_{m-1}+Y_{m-1}).
\]
Consequently the full \(\delta^{2m-1}f\) contains
\[
4^{m-1}x^*C^m x
\]
with positive coefficient. All differentiation rules in (2.2) have positive coefficients, so other branches cannot cancel it. This strengthens the unquantified “positive coefficient” assertion at [lines 905–915](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:905).

Let \(\mathcal S\) be the finite set of connected graph types appearing as components of the \(g_j\). Polynomial expressions in \(g\) contain only disjoint unions of members of \(\mathcal S\). For \(m\) large, the connected path
\[
\Gamma_m=x^*(B^*R^*RB)^m x
\]
is not in \(\mathcal S\). Stable contraction-graph independence therefore gives
\(\delta^{2m-1}f\notin\mathbb R[g_1,\dots,g_k]\), contradicting closure.

Two missing formal points should be added to the file:

1. The graph-independence assertion at [lines 913–919](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:913) must be a stable-range statement: distinct typed contraction graphs are independent once \(n\) exceeds a bound depending on graph degree. At fixed \(n\), Cayley–Hamilton relations invalidate the claim.

2. For analytic \(F\), subtract coordinate constants, expand \(F\) at \(g(0)\), and scale the raw state by a scalar. At each finite raw degree, only finitely many Taylor monomials contribute, and their connected components still belong to \(\mathcal S\). Without this ambient-germ interpretation, the one-sentence analytic argument is incomplete.

### What Section 8 does not rule out

It does not rule out:

- a fixed number of continuum fields or kernels;
- infinitely many moments packaged as a generating function or graph-indexed field;
- closures restricted to the one Gaussian-initialized orbit;
- fixed-width or width-dependent finite closures;
- non-contraction indexed fields;
- pathological encodings, as acknowledged at [lines 921–925](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:921).

Accordingly, “failure of finite scalar moment lists” at [lines 962–980](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:962) must always retain “width-uniform and state-universal.”

### Counterexamples to broader readings

1. **\(O(1)\) scalar PDE/IDE fields.**  
   The file itself gives one: \(Q(w,w')\) and \(e\) obey the cubic kernel equation (6.16), with a fixed word domain, at [lines 760–771](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:760). The expanded scalar-field system (6.15) is at [lines 711–758](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:711). Thus any claim excluding a fixed number of bounded-degree scalar continuum fields is directly false. Such fields contain infinitely many scalar degrees of freedom, so the finite-set graph argument does not apply.

2. **A one-scalar analytic closure on the initialized orbit.**  
   The feature curve has \(f'(s)=K(s)>0\) and range \(\mathbb R\), by [lines 818–834](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:818), with \(f'(0)=4\) by [lines 941–955](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:941). The polynomial Banach-space flow is analytic, so
   \[
   \kappa(u)=K(f^{-1}(u))
   \]
   is real analytic along the orbit. Hence
   \[
   f'=\kappa(f),\qquad
   \dot f=2\eta(y_\star-f)\kappa(f)
   \]
   is an exact one-scalar autonomous analytic closure for that particular initialized trajectory. It is excluded only by the essential “state-universal” qualifier.

3. **Fixed-width closure.**  
   At \(n=1\), set
   \[
   A=a^2,\quad U=R^2,\quad V=B^2,\quad X=x^2,\quad f=aRBx.
   \]
   Then feature time closes polynomially:
   \[
   A'=U'=V'=X'=2f,
   \]
   \[
   f'=UVX+AVX+AUX+AUV.
   \]
   Thus the no-go is false at fixed width; uniformity over unbounded widths is indispensable.

The three narrower non-identifiability examples are correct:

- the free-MP calculations \(\tau(X^2Y^2)=4\), \(\tau(XYXY)=3\), and commutator norm \(2\) at [lines 841–858](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/identity_compiler/linear_gaussian_program/depth3_unfrozen_readout_closure/THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md:841);

Those examples prove that the named summaries do not determine even the instantaneous kernel. They do not prove a universal impossibility for enriched continuum PDE states.

=== OPERATOR1 ROLLOUT LINE 36556 | 2026-08-28T17:54:52.212Z | /root/pde_no_go_route ===
Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/pde_no_go_route
Payload:
The broad PDE no-go is false. A fixed finite number of fields with a fixed finite-order polynomial local PDE can still carry infinitely many scalar coordinates in its spatial jet. The sharp boundary is finite contraction support across the entire jet.

## 1. Natural local-PDE class

Let \(U^{(n)}=(U^{(n)}_1,\dots,U^{(n)}_q)\) be scalar fields on a fixed open \(\Omega\subset\mathbb R^d\), with \(q,d\) independent of \(n\). An exact state-universal local realization of the feature derivation \(\mathscr D_n\) consists of

\[
\mathscr D_n U^{(n)}_\alpha(z)
 =
F_\alpha\!\left(z,j^rU^{(n)}(z)\right),
\tag{A}
\]

where each \(F_\alpha\) is one fixed differential polynomial, of fixed finite order \(r\) and degree \(p\), and a fixed finite-order local readout

\[
f_n=G\!\left(j^\rho U^{(n)}(z_*)\right).
\tag{B}
\]

All coefficients, \(q,d,r,p,\rho,z_*\), are independent of \(n\) and of the state. The identities hold on a state-open set for every sufficiently large \(n\).

For a finite set \(S\) of connected typed contraction graphs, let

\[
\mathcal A_S^{(n)}
=
\operatorname{span}
\left\{P_G^{(n)}:
\text{every connected component of }G\text{ belongs to }S
\right\}.
\]

It is a unital algebra because multiplication is disjoint union.

Call the encoder finite-contraction-jet if one finite \(S\), independent of \(n,\alpha,\beta\), satisfies

\[
\partial_z^\beta U^{(n)}_\alpha(z_*;\,\cdot)\in\mathcal A_S^{(n)}
\qquad\text{for every }\alpha,\beta.
\tag{C}
\]

A uniform representation

\[
U^{(n)}_\alpha(z)
 =\sum_{\deg G\le D}c_{\alpha,G}(z)P_G^{(n)}
\]

automatically satisfies (C), since only finitely many graph types have degree at most \(D\).

## 2. PDE no-go under finite jet support

No realization (A)–(C) exists.

Introduce jet variables \(u_{\alpha,\beta}\) and total spatial derivatives \(D_z^\beta\). The evolutionary prolongation of \(F\) is

\[
\operatorname{pr}F
 =
\sum_{\alpha,\beta}
D_z^\beta F_\alpha\,
\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{\alpha,\beta}}.
\]

Because \(\mathscr D_n\) acts only on the tensor state and commutes with spatial differentiation, (A) and the chain rule give, for every differential polynomial \(H\),

\[
\mathscr D_n H(jU^{(n)})
 =
(\operatorname{pr}F H)(jU^{(n)}).
\]

Hence

\[
\mathscr D_n^\ell f_n
 =
(\operatorname{pr}F)^\ell G
 \left(j^{\rho+\ell r}U^{(n)}(z_*)\right).
\tag{D}
\]

For each fixed \(\ell\), the right-hand side is a finite polynomial in finitely many spatial jets. By (C) and the algebra property,

\[
\mathscr D_n^\ell f_n\in\mathcal A_S^{(n)}
\qquad\text{for every }\ell.
\tag{E}
\]

But the document proves that, for every \(m\ge1\),

\[
\mathscr D_n^{\,2m-1}f_n
\quad\text{contains with positive coefficient}\quad
4^{m-1}P_{L_m}^{(n)},
\]

where

\[
P_{L_m}^{(n)}
 =
x^{\mathsf T}(B^{\mathsf T}R^{\mathsf T}RB)^m x.
\]

The \(L_m\) are connected and pairwise nonisomorphic. Choose \(m\) with \(L_m\notin S\). No element of \(\mathcal A_S^{(n)}\) can contain \(L_m\), since multiplication only forms disjoint unions and never creates a new connected component.

Coefficient comparison is legitimate in sufficiently large width: passing from unrestricted contractions to injectively labelled contractions is a triangular partition-lattice transformation, and a bijectively labelled monomial uniquely determines its typed graph. Thus every fixed finite family of graph polynomials is independent in the stable range. This contradicts (E).

The same proof works if the PDE identity is assumed only on a nonempty state-open set: after substitution, both sides are polynomials in the tensor entries, so equality on that open set is a polynomial identity.

## 3. Quantitative form of the obstruction

Suppose instead that every individual spatial jet is a finite contraction polynomial, but there is no common finite \(S\). Then any exact order-\(r\) PDE realization must satisfy:

\[
\boxed{
\text{for every }m,\text{ some jet of order }
|\beta|\le \rho+(2m-1)r
\text{ contains }L_m.
}
\]

Indeed, (D) constructs \(\mathscr D_n^{2m-1}f_n\) from precisely those jets. Since \(L_m\) is connected, a product of graph polynomials can produce it only if one of its factors already contains \(L_m\). In particular, the contraction degree of the encoded spatial jet must be unbounded; since \(\deg L_m=4m+2\), it grows at least linearly along a subsequence of jet orders.

Thus “finite differential order” does not imply “finite moment state.” Repeated time differentiation accesses jets of orders

\[
\rho,\quad \rho+r,\quad \rho+2r,\quad\dots,
\]

which form an infinite scalar hierarchy.

## 4. Decisive counterexample to any broader no-go

Let \(\Phi_n^s\) denote the local feature-time flow of \(\mathscr D_n\). Define one scalar field

\[
E_n(\theta)(z)=f_n(\Phi_n^z\theta).
\tag{F}
\]

Evolve it by the fixed local PDE

\[
\partial_s u=\partial_z u,
\qquad
u(0,z)=E_n(\theta)(z).
\tag{G}
\]

Then

\[
u(s,z)=f_n(\Phi_n^{s+z}\theta),
\qquad
u(s,0)=f_n(\Phi_n^s\theta).
\]

Moreover,

\[
E_n(\Phi_n^s\theta)(z)=u(s,z),
\]

so this is autonomous and restartable, not merely a fit from time zero. The PDE uses one field, one spatial dimension, one derivative, and a degree-one polynomial differential expression, all independent of \(n\).

It is valid on a fixed neighborhood uniformly in \(n\). In cyclic form the feature flow is \(C'=(C^{\mathsf T})^3\). On \(\|C\|\le2\varepsilon\),

\[
\|(C^{\mathsf T})^3\|\le8\varepsilon^3,\qquad
\|(C^{\mathsf T})^3-(D^{\mathsf T})^3\|
 \le12\varepsilon^2\|C-D\|.
\]

The Picard map is therefore invariant and contractive on a fixed time interval \(T=c\varepsilon^{-2}\), independently of matrix dimension. Thus (F)–(G) works on a fixed state neighborhood and fixed spacetime neighborhood.

Its spatial jets are exactly

\[
\partial_z^kE_n(\theta)(0)=\mathscr D_n^k f_n(\theta).
\]

Consequently the odd jets contain every \(L_m\). The single field has hidden the entire infinite ordered-word hierarchy in its Taylor jet.

This construction is state-universal—the same encoder formula works for every state—but it is scientifically vacuous as compression: its initial spatial profile is the future observable trajectory. Equivalently, its formal initial germ is

\[
E_n(\theta)(z)
 =
\sum_{k\ge0}\frac{z^k}{k!}\mathscr D_n^k f_n(\theta),
\]

so even when described entirely by same-time contractions, it stores infinitely many moments of unbounded degree.

## 5. Physical-time extension

The finite-jet-support no-go also applies directly to the physical derivation

\[
\mathscr X_n=2\eta(y-f_n)\mathscr D_n.
\]

For \(y\ne0\), tensor-degree grading shows that the lowest-degree part of \(\mathscr X_n^\ell f_n\) is

\[
(2\eta y)^\ell\mathscr D_n^\ell f_n;
\]

terms using \(-f_n\mathscr D_n\) have four additional tensor degrees and cannot cancel it. For \(y=0\),

\[
\mathscr X_n^\ell f_n=(-2\eta)^\ell Q_\ell,
\qquad
Q_{\ell+1}=f_n\mathscr D_nQ_\ell,
\]

and positivity of the graph differentiation coefficients shows that \(Q_\ell\) contains \(f_n^\ell\mathscr D_n^\ell f_n\). At \(\ell=2m-1\), this graph has \(L_m\) as a connected component. The same finite-\(S\) contradiction follows.

The transport construction likewise works for the physical flow.

Hence the sharp verdict is:

\[
\boxed{\text{No fixed finite-contraction-jet local PDE closure}}
\]

but

\[
\boxed{\text{finite field count + finite PDE order alone admits a one-field realization}.}
\]

The exact missing hypothesis is finite initialization/jet provenance. Spatial Taylor jets are already infinitely many scalar coordinates.
