Payload:
The proposed universal lift is false. The ODE theorem obstructs finite numerical dimension and a finite contraction-graph alphabet; a scalar field has infinitely many modes and an infinite spatial jet. Partial derivatives can carry exactly the graph ladder used in the ODE contradiction.

### Natural meanings of “allow partial derivatives”

| Interpretation | Verdict |
|---|---|
| A fixed finite-order equation using only time derivatives, e.g. \(f^{(p)}=F(f,\ldots,f^{(p-1)})\) | **No-go remains true.** Adjoin \(f,\mathscr Df,\ldots,\mathscr D^{p-1}f\) as finitely many contraction coordinates and obtain a forbidden companion ODE. |
| Fields constrained to be spatially constant, to a fixed finite-mode ansatz, or to a finite closed jet | **No-go remains true.** This is a finite-dimensional ODE in disguise. |
| One scalar PDE on the full finite-width state space | **Universal impossibility is false.** If \(Z'=F_n(Z)\), then \(u_t=F_n(Z)\cdot\nabla_Zu,\ u(0,Z)=f_n(Z)\), has \(u(t,Z)=f_n(\Phi_t^n Z)\). It is a one-field, first-order, state-universal Koopman PDE. Its spatial dimension grows with \(n\). |
| A fixed number of fields on a fixed continuum domain, with arbitrary profiles and a fixed local finite-order PDE | **False.** A scalar transport equation already suffices via an orbit/jet encoding below. |
| A fixed number of fields with integral, kernel, pseudodifferential, or other nonlocal terms | **False a fortiori.** The theorem’s positive kernel IDE is already such a counterexample. |
| A finite PDE whose domain, number of modes, coefficients, or derivative order may depend on \(n\) | **False.** Every finite-dimensional ODE embeds into one scalar local PDE of finite, but dimension-dependent, order. |
| A fixed local polynomial/analytic finite-jet PDE whose field values are uniformly bounded-degree complete-contraction moments, with a finite-jet evaluation readout | **True.** This is the defensible corrected PDE no-go proved below. |
| A “non-orbit-fitted” PDE without a precise restriction on the encoding | **Not a mathematical claim yet.** Allowing all instantaneous iterates \(\mathscr D_n^mf_n\) is already enough to defeat it; a finite graph-alphabet condition is one precise repair. |

### Fixed scalar local PDE defeating the ladder

Let \(\Phi_\xi^n\) be the feature flow. Define the current field encoding

\[
E_n(Z)(\xi)=f_n(\Phi_\xi^n Z).
\]

Along \(Z(t)=\Phi_t^nZ_0\),

\[
U(t,\xi)=E_n(Z(t))(\xi)
       =f_n(\Phi_{\xi+t}^nZ_0)
\]

satisfies the fixed scalar first-order PDE

\[
\partial_tU=\partial_\xi U,\qquad f_n(Z(t))=U(t,0).
\]

It is autonomous and restartable because
\(E_n(\Phi_t^nZ)=S_tE_n(Z)\), where \(S_tu(\xi)=u(\xi+t)\). Moreover,

\[
\partial_\xi^rE_n(Z)(0)=\mathscr D_n^rf_n(Z).
\]

Thus the connected graph
\[
x^{\mathsf T}(B^{\mathsf T}R^{\mathsf T}RB)^m x
\]
appearing in \(\mathscr D_n^{2m-1}f_n\) is simply stored in the
\((2m-1)\)-st spatial derivative. The ODE proof has no contradiction once an infinite jet is available. Locally this construction works for every finite-width state; globally it works whenever the forward flow is global.

This encoding is orbit-built, but that is precisely why an unrestricted PDE claim is false.

### One scalar profile stores an arbitrary finite-dimensional state

Flatten the finite-width state into \(Z=(Z_1,\ldots,Z_N)\). Choose fixed pairwise disjoint smooth bumps \(\psi_i\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)\) with \(\psi_i(i)=1\). Then

\[
e_N(Z)(r)=\sum_{i=1}^N Z_i\psi_i(r)
\]

is an injective encoding into one scalar field, since \(e_N(Z)(i)=Z_i\).

It can encode the entire dynamics with the same fixed transport PDE. Set

\[
E_N(Z)(s,r)
 =\sum_{i=1}^N [\Phi_s(Z)]_i\psi_i(r).
\]

Then

\[
U_t=U_s,\qquad
U(t,s,r)=E_N(Z_0)(s+t,r),
\]

and \(U(t,0,i)=[\Phi_t(Z_0)]_i\). Hence one scalar field on a fixed two-dimensional domain stores and evolves arbitrary finite-dimensional states, for arbitrary \(N\); all model dependence is in the initial profile.

If orbit-built initialization is disallowed but nonlocality is allowed, choose orthonormal \(\psi_i\), put
\(c_i(u)=\int u\psi_i\), and use

\[
u_t(r)=\sum_{i=1}^N
F_i(c_1(u),\ldots,c_N(u))\psi_i(r).
\]

Starting from \(u(0)=\sum Z_i\psi_i\), its modal coefficients satisfy exactly \(\dot Z=F(Z)\).

### Even local finite-order PDEs suffice when order may grow

For any \(N\)-dimensional ODE \(\dot Z_i=F_i(Z)\), encode

\[
u(t,x)=\sum_{i=0}^{N-1} Z_i(t)\frac{x^i}{i!}.
\]

For \(0\le i<N\), define from the \((N-1)\)-jet at \(x\)

\[
T_i(x,J^{N-1}u)
 =\sum_{q=0}^{N-1-i}\frac{(-x)^q}{q!}
   \partial_x^{i+q}u(x).
\]

The binomial identity gives \(T_i=Z_i\) on the polynomial ansatz. Therefore

\[
u_t
 =\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}
 F_i(T_0,\ldots,T_{N-1})\frac{x^i}{i!}
\]

is a scalar local PDE of order \(N-1\) whose invariant polynomial manifold reproduces the ODE exactly. Thus “a finite PDE for every width” is also too weak; the order must be uniform.

### Corrected local-PDE no-go

A clean statement supported by the graph-ladder proof is:

> Fix \(q,d,r,s,D<\infty\), independent of \(n\), a spatial domain \(\Omega\subset\mathbb R^d\), and finitely many evaluation points. Suppose fields \(U_{\alpha,n}(x;Z)\), \(1\le\alpha\le q\), satisfy:
>
> 1. For every \(x\), each \(U_{\alpha,n}(x;\cdot)\) is a linear combination, with smooth state-independent \(x\)-coefficients, of typed same-time complete-contraction graphs of tensor degree at most \(D\), uniformly in \(n\).
> 2. Along feature flow they obey a fixed local differential-polynomial system
>    \[
>    \mathscr D_nU_{\alpha,n}
>      =F_\alpha(x,J_x^rU_n).
>    \]
> 3. \(f_n\) is a fixed polynomial of finitely many jet evaluations
>    \(\partial^\beta U_{\alpha,n}(x_\ell)\), \(|\beta|\le s\).
>
> Then no such system exists for all sufficiently large \(n\).

Proof: bounded tensor degree gives a finite set \(\mathcal A\) of possible contraction graphs. Let \(\mathcal S\) be the finite set of connected component types occurring in \(\mathcal A\).

The state derivation commutes with spatial differentiation:

\[
\mathscr D_n(\partial^\beta U_{\alpha,n})
 =\partial^\beta F_\alpha(x,J^rU_n).
\]

Induction therefore expresses every \(\mathscr D_n^k f_n\) as a differential polynomial in finitely many spatial jets of \(U_n\). Every such jet is still a linear combination of graphs in \(\mathcal A\). Products of contractions are disjoint unions, so every graph in the resulting expansion has all connected components in \(\mathcal S\).

But for every \(m\),

\[
\mathscr D_n^{\,2m-1}f_n
\]

contains with positive coefficient the connected graph

\[
\Gamma_m
=x^{\mathsf T}(B^{\mathsf T}R^{\mathsf T}RB)^m x.
\]

Its size tends to infinity, so for sufficiently large \(m\),
\(\Gamma_m\notin\mathcal S\). Stable graph independence then contradicts the PDE-derived expansion in sufficiently large dimension.

The same proof covers analytic germs: subtract zero-state constants, scale all tensors by \(\lambda\), and compare the finite homogeneous coefficient of the degree of \(\Gamma_m\). Only finitely many Taylor monomials contribute at that degree, and none can create a new connected component type.

The sharp conclusion is therefore:

\[
\text{no fixed local finite-jet PDE built from a finite bounded-degree contraction alphabet,}
\]

not

\[
\text{no PDE with \(O(1)\) scalar fields.}
\]

Locality, finite differential order, point evaluation, autonomy, and restartability do not individually rescue the broad claim; the transport encoding satisfies all of them. The indispensable restriction is that the spatial profile and its jets may not hide infinitely many new contraction types.

Verdict: the broad PDE no-go is false, but the clean counterexample is an exact analytic embedding, not automatically a robust PDE closure.

Let \(V_n=\mathscr D_n\) be feature flow and \(L_nO=DO[V_n]\). For any scalar observable \(O\), define

\[
\Psi_{n,O}(\theta;\xi)
=\sum_{m\ge 0}(L_n^mO)(\theta)
\frac{(|\xi|^2/4)^m}{(m!)^2},
\qquad \xi\in\mathbb R^2 .
\]

Polynomial ODE solutions are locally complex analytic. Hence, at every state, Cauchy estimates give
\[
|(L_n^mO)(\theta)|\le M\,m!R^{-m},
\]
so the series is entire in \(\xi\). Since
\[
\Delta_\xi \frac{(|\xi|^2/4)^m}{(m!)^2}
=
\frac{(|\xi|^2/4)^{m-1}}{((m-1)!)^2},
\]
every feature-flow orbit \(\theta(s)\) satisfies the exact identity
\[
\partial_s\Psi_{n,O}=\Delta_\xi\Psi_{n,O},
\qquad
\Psi_{n,O}(s,0)=O(\theta(s)).
\]

Thus, with \(O=f_n\), the entire iterated contraction ladder \(\{\mathscr D_n^mf_n\}_{m\ge0}\) is encoded in one scalar field obeying the ordinary heat equation on the fixed domain \(\mathbb R^2\). The PDE has differential order \(2\), polynomial degree \(1\), and is independent of \(n\). The same construction with the physical vector field \(2\eta(y-f_n)\mathscr D_n\) encodes physical time.

To encode every contraction graph, enumerate them as \(O_\nu\), with \(O_0=f\), and use a passive variable \(w\):
\[
U_\theta(w,\xi)=
\sum_{\nu\ge0}a_\nu w^\nu\Psi_{n,O_\nu}(\theta;\xi).
\]
Weights such as \(a_\nu=\exp[-(\nu+d_\nu)^2]\), where \(d_\nu\) is graph degree, dominate the exponential-in-degree bounds on each bounded state neighborhood. Then \(U\) is one scalar field on fixed \(\mathbb R^3\) and
\[
U_s=\Delta_\xi U.
\]
Every \(O_\nu\) is recovered from the \(w\)-Taylor coefficient at \(\xi=0\). Appending all raw parameter-coordinate observables makes the encoder injective, giving an exact all-state embedding for every finite width.

This does not contradict Theorem 1: the initial field stores infinitely many unbounded-degree Lie derivatives. The connected path ladder is present in its Taylor coefficients, so there is neither finite numerical state nor a uniform degree bound.

The same construction applies to the limiting trace-class IDE because its vector field on \(\mathfrak S_1\times\mathbb R\) is analytic. It therefore gives an exact field embedding on the whole affine trace-class state class, not merely the Gaussian orbit. However:

- Full finite-width coordinate encoders do not naturally converge to the Fock-space coordinate encoder.
- For rooted-word observables, the theorem yields convergence of every fixed \((\nu,m)\) coefficient. This gives a rigorous product-topology Gaussian closure.
- Compact-open or Sobolev convergence of the complete analytic fields needs a uniform hierarchy-tail estimate not proved in the source.

The important flaw is well-posedness. The initial heat field generally grows like \(e^{c|\xi|^2}\). The heat Cauchy problem is locally unique in a prescribed analytic/Gaussian-growth class, but not in unrestricted smooth classes; rapidly growing heat solutions can be nonunique. The construction rigorously proves
\[
D\mathcal E_n(\theta)V_n(\theta)=\Delta\mathcal E_n(\theta)
\]
and gives a locally restartable flow on the invariant encoded manifold. It does not by itself provide a globally well-posed PDE on an open, width-uniform Banach space of arbitrary fields.

There is also no uniform local analytic interval over all \(n\) and all states: the Cauchy radius depends on the state and width. At Gaussian initialization, the source operator-norm and finite-rank cubic bounds should give a positive interval uniform in \(n\) with probability tending to one, and similarly locally along compact physical-time intervals, but that complex-time estimate is not actually established in the supplied theorem. It must not be claimed without an additional majorant argument.

Transport gives an even simpler formal encoding,
\[
T_\theta(s)=O(\Phi_s\theta),\qquad T_t=T_s,
\]
but its initial profile already contains the future orbit and is trajectory playback. The heat/Bessel encoder is better: each coefficient is a current-state contraction polynomial, though it still front-loads the entire infinite hierarchy and unbounded exact precision.

Two exact obstructions delimit more natural generating functions:

1. A monomial code in finitely many commuting variables cannot turn word concatenation into multiplication injectively, since \(ab\) and \(ba\) receive the same exponent sum.

2. A positional code makes prefixing act by \(F(z)\mapsto z^aF(z^b)\). No finite-order differential operator equals \(F(z^b)\) on all analytic \(F\): testing \(F=z^m\) at \(0<z<1\) gives a polynomial in \(m\) on the differential-operator side and \(z^{(b-1)m}\) on the composition side.

So the audited conclusion is:

\[
\boxed{\text{Exact one-field local heat embedding exists}}
\]

but only as an infinite-jet/hierarchy representation. A stronger no-go remains plausible only after requiring a finite-complexity causal encoder, an open well-posed field state space with uniform norms, or a direct bounded-order symbol calculus for word composition.

NOT CLEAN. The negative theorem is essentially sound, but there are three exact defects.

1. Physical-time theorem is false unless \(\eta\ne0\) is stated.

If \(\eta=0\), then \(\mathscr X_n=0\), and the closure
\[
U_n(\zeta;\theta)=f_n(\theta),\qquad U_t=0
\]
satisfies the stated bounded-contraction requirements. Repair: explicitly fix \(\eta>0\).

With \(\eta>0\), both label cases are correct:

- For \(y\ne0\), every occurrence of \(-f_n\mathscr D_n\) raises degree four more than \(y\mathscr D_n\), so the unique lowest component of \(\mathscr X_n^kf_n\) is \((2\eta y)^k\mathscr D_n^kf_n\).

- For \(y=0\), \(Q_{k+1}=f_n\mathscr D_nQ_k\) has nonnegative graph coefficients and contains \(f_n^{k+1}\mathscr D_n^{k+1}f_n\). At \(k=2m-1\), the relevant graph is the disjoint union of \(k\) copies of the \(f_n\)-graph with \(\Gamma_m\), so it cannot belong to the finite component alphabet when \(\Gamma_m\notin\mathcal S\).

2. The transport counterexample is not a well-posed restartable PDE on the claimed fixed spatial domain.

The encoder
\[
E_n(\theta)(s)=f_n(\Phi_n^s\theta)
\]
is defined only for \(s\) in the state’s local flow interval. Feature flow is not generally complete: already a scalar invariant direction gives \(c'=c^3\), which blows up in finite time. Hence \(E_n(\theta)\) is not a profile on a fixed half-line.

The estimate (10.6) is arithmetically correct: for \(\|\mathcal C(0)\|\le\varepsilon\) and \(T=(24\varepsilon^2)^{-1}\),
\[
\varepsilon+8\varepsilon^3T=\tfrac43\varepsilon<2\varepsilon,
\qquad
12\varepsilon^2T=\tfrac12.
\]
But it gives only a common finite interval. On a finite interval, right translation \(u(t,s)=u_0(t+s)\) is defined only on a shrinking triangular region unless boundary data are added; it does not yield a semigroup on one fixed field space and therefore does not establish the claimed restartability. Also, a half-line is inconsistent with the earlier assumption that \(\Omega\) is open while the readout uses \(s=0\).

Repair: either

- weaken Theorem 1(4) to an exact local-germ transport representation, dropping the fixed-domain well-posed-semigroup claim; or
- assume a complete underlying flow and use a whole-line profile; or
- supply a different entire-profile encoding with a precisely stated function space and well-posedness theorem.

The phrase “stores the entire ordered-word hierarchy” should also be weakened: this profile stores the full output Lie-derivative jet, which contains unbounded graph types, but does not separately recover every ordered-word observable.

3. The operator-IDE extension is not self-contained.

The final paragraph invokes “the relevant autonomous flow” without defining the operator IDE or proving its existence, uniqueness, and restartability. That is the only silent research-level lifting. Delete this extension or include the trace-class vector field and its local Picard estimates.

The analytic-germ argument is otherwise valid, provided it is made explicit that every analytic \(F_\alpha\) and \(G\) is Taylor-expanded about the complete zero-state jet \(jU_n(\zeta;0)\). Merely expanding at the origin of jet space would be insufficient when encoder jets have nonzero constant components; recentering is what ensures only finitely many Taylor monomials contribute to each \(\lambda\)-degree. No external research theorem is needed for the negative proof.

1. **The half-line transport realization is not globally defined or restartable for the feature flow.**  
   \(E_n(\theta)(s)=f_n(\Phi_n^s\theta)\) is only defined on a uniform finite \(s\)-interval. The feature flow is not forward complete: on the invariant rank-one ray \(a=R=B=x=q>0\), one has \(q'=q^3\), hence blow-up at \(s=(2q(0)^2)^{-1}\). Thus no neighborhood of zero supplies these orbit profiles on a half-line. The half-line well-posedness argument applies to arbitrary \(C^1\) data, not to a globally defined encoder satisfying the semiconjugacy (10.4). On a bounded interval, whole-domain well-posedness also requires boundary data.

   **Consequence:** The construction decisively refutes a **local-germ/output PDE no-go**, but not an all-time fixed-domain semigroup claim.

   **Repair:** Formulate part 4 on profile germs, or choose fixed nested intervals and state
   \[
   U(t,s)=f_n(\Phi_n^{t+s}\theta_0)
   \quad\text{only where }|t|+|s|<\tau,
   \]
   with restartability on that local flow tube. Remove the half-line claim unless flow completeness is assumed. Also define the cyclic block operator \(\mathcal C\), which is currently absent; once defined, (10.6) does give an \(n\)-uniform local \(\tau\).

2. **“State-universal” is valid only in the output-semiconjugacy sense.**  
   The encoder \(E_n(\theta)\) need not be injective and generally identifies gauge-related network states with the same output orbit. It therefore does not represent the full current network/mean-field state.

   **Consequence:** It refutes the broad claim only when “represent the dynamics” means the output closure formalized by (3.3)–(3.4). It does not refute a claim requiring faithful state encoding or recovery of all current observables.

   **Repair:** Replace “state-universal realization” by “state-universal output realization” throughout, or separately construct an injective field encoding and specify its decoder.

3. **Section 11 lacks the uniformity needed for stable graph independence.**  
   “Every individual spatial jet is a finite contraction polynomial” permits its graph support to vary with \(n\) and have unbounded size. Then the stable-width threshold also varies with \(n\), and finite-\(n\) Cayley–Hamilton identities can change whether an expansion literally “contains \(\Gamma_m\).” The asserted necessity is therefore not invariant under the stated hypothesis.

   **Repair:** Require that for every fixed jet cutoff \(J\),
   \[
   \bigcup_{n,\alpha,\ |\beta|\le J}
   \operatorname{supp}_{\rm graph}
   (\partial^\beta U_{\alpha,n})
   \]
   is finite, equivalently that a degree bound \(D_J<\infty\) exists independently of \(n\). With this repair, the bound \(\rho+(2m-1)r\) and the stable-independence argument are valid.

NOT CLEAN. The central boundary theorem is repairable, but five exact gaps remain.

1. **Stable graph independence: quotient leaves the declared class.**  
   In (5.1), identifying index vertices can create vertices of valence \(>2\), whereas Section 3 defines complete contractions by pairwise index contractions. For example, merging the two summed indices in \((a^{\mathsf T}a)^2\) produces \(\sum_i a_i^4\), which is not a pairwise complete contraction. Thus the asserted unitriangular \(P_G\)-to-\(I_G\) transformation is not defined within the stated graph class.

   Repair: enlarge an auxiliary class to the finite quotient-closure of typed incidence networks, prove its injective contractions independent by the unique labelled-monomial argument, then expand \(P_G\) in that basis. Equivalently, choose a graph of maximal index-vertex count in \(\sum c_GP_G\); its \(I_G\) term cannot arise from a nontrivial quotient, eliminate it, and descend. This proves Lemma 2 as stated.

2. **The prolongation formula needs total derivatives.**  
   In (7.2), \(\partial_\zeta^\beta F_\alpha\) is wrong if interpreted as ordinary differentiation with jet variables held fixed. It must be the total derivative
   \[
   D_i=\partial_{\zeta_i}
   +\sum_{\gamma,\delta}
   u_{\gamma,\delta+e_i}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{\gamma,\delta}},
   \qquad D^\beta F_\alpha.
   \]
   Otherwise, for \(u_t=u_x\) and \(H=u_x\), (7.2) would give zero instead of \(u_{xx}\). With multiple readout points, separate jet copies \(u_{\alpha,\beta}^{(j)}\) and sum over \(j\) are also required. After this repair, (7.3)–(7.4) and the order bound
   \[
   \operatorname{ord}(\mathbf T_F^kG)\le \rho+kr
   \]
   are correct.

3. **The analytic-germ statement lacks a jet-space basepoint/domain.**  
   The \(F_\alpha\) are functions on finite jet space, not on network-state space. Because (3.2) permits an empty-graph constant whose value may depend on \(n\) and \(\zeta\), the zero network need not map to the zero jet. “Analytic near the zero network state” therefore does not specify where the fixed analytic germs are defined.

   Repair: either require all zero-state field jets to vanish and take germs at zero jet, or require fixed analytic functions on neighborhoods containing every realized zero-state jet. Then recenter
   \[
   v_{\alpha,\beta}=u_{\alpha,\beta}
   -\partial^\beta U_{\alpha,n}(\zeta;0)
   \]
   before Taylor expansion. With this and total prolongation, the fixed-\(\lambda^N\) argument is valid: only finitely many centered Taylor monomials contribute, and all retain the finite connected alphabet.

4. **Section 11 needs a homogeneous-degree projection.**  
   There is no common degree bound there, and graph supports may depend on \(n\), so Lemma 2 cannot be invoked directly on the entire participating family. The conclusion is still valid after projecting the equality to tensor degree
   \[
   N=4m+2.
   \]
   Only graph terms of degree at most \(N\) can contribute; these belong to one fixed finite universe independent of \(n\), so stable independence applies. Since graph multiplication is disjoint union, producing the connected \(\Gamma_m\) forces one participating jet itself to have a \(\Gamma_m\) term. This yields the stated order bound \(\rho+(2m-1)r\).

5. **The transport realization is only local, not the claimed half-line realization.**  
   The profile \(E_n(\theta)(s)=f_n(\Phi_n^s\theta)\) need not exist on a half-line. Already at \(n=1\), with \(a=R=B=x=c>0\), the feature flow gives \(c'=c^3\), which blows up in finite positive time. Thus the half-line well-posedness paragraph does not apply to the constructed initial profile. Also, \(\mathcal C\) in (10.5) is undefined in this self-contained artifact.

   Repair: state Theorem 1(4) as a fixed **local spacetime-germ** realization. Define the cyclic block matrix explicitly, take \(\|\mathcal C(0)\|\le\varepsilon\), construct the flow on \((-T,T)\), and use the transport equation on a smaller fixed diamond. If a global fixed-domain profile is intended, introduce and justify a globally complete cutoff extension of the feature vector field.

The connected ladder (6.3)–(6.6), positivity/no-cancellation argument, polynomial open-set continuation, and the physical-time degree arguments all recompute correctly. After the repairs above, the principal no-go and the infinite-spatial-jet obstruction are valid.
